# 1763 Kyiv-Pechersk Lavra Services to the Venerable Fathers of the Kyiv Caves (Служби преподобним Отцям Печерським, Київ: Лавра, 1763) <a id="1763-kyiv-pechersk-lavra-services-to-the-venerable-fathers-of-the-kyiv-caves"></a>

*(Binding — Front Cover)*
Eighteenth-century calfskin binding over heavy boards. The outer perimeter is framed with a blind-tooled double fillet border. In the center is an embossed ornamental cartouche featuring the Dormition of the Most Holy Theotokos and the Venerable Fathers of the Kyiv Caves. Signs of historical shelf-wear, natural patina, and age-related surface cracking.

*FRONT MATTER, TITLE PAGE, AND INTRODUCTORY APPARATUS:*

To the glory of the Holy, Consubstantial, Life-Creating, and Undivided Trinity: of the Father, and of the Son, and of the Holy Spirit:
By the command of the Most Pious, Great Sovereign, our Empress CATHERINE ALEXEYEVNA,[^2] Autocrat of All Russia:
under Her Successor, the Right-Believing Sovereign Tsesarevich and Grand Duke PAUL PETROVICH:[^3]
And with the blessing of the Most Holy Governing SYNOD, this Book: SERVICES to the Venerable Fathers of the Caves, whose relics repose incorrupt in the Near and Far Caves, was printed in the Holy Kyiv-Pechersk LAVRA, in the Stauropegion of the same Most Holy SYNOD:
under Archimandrite ZOSIMAS:[^4]
in the year from the creation of the world, 7271,[^5] and from the Nativity according to the flesh of God the Word, 1763.[^6]

*(Frontispiece Engraving: Synaxis of All Kyiv Caves Fathers)*
Full-page copperplate engraving: The Synaxis of All Venerable Fathers of the Kyiv Caves gathered before the Great Church of the Dormition, with the Protection of the Most Holy Theotokos above.
*Troparion, Tone 4:*
As a pillar of fire and a radiant sun shining forth upon the Caves Mount, we bring to Thee in prayers, O Christ, the great Anthony with the entire assembly of the God-bearing Fathers; for their sake, we pray Thee: grant grace unto our monasteries, and unto our souls great mercy.

*Kontakion, Tone 3:*
Having gathered the choir of fasters in mind, beholding them with noetic eyes, come, O people, let us honor them with praises, and venerating their relics, bring gifts with love; for they are intercessors with God and pray-ers for our souls.

*(Frontispiece Engraving: St. Anthony of the Caves and Scenes of His Life)*
Full-page copperplate engraving: Venerable Anthony of the Caves surrounded by biographical cartouches depicting his monastic path.
*Above the icon:*
Having departed from worldly turmoil through withdrawal from the world, thou didst eagerly follow Christ; and having lived an angelic life, thou didst reach the quiet haven of the Holy Mountain of Athos. Whence, with the blessing of the fathers, having come unto the mountain of Kyiv, and having laboriously completed thy life there, thou didst enlighten thy fatherland, and didst show thyself a guide for a multitude of monastic flocks unto the heavenly chamber. These hast thou brought unto Christ; pray Him, O Anthony our father, that He may save our souls.

*Below the icon:*
Having bound thyself to Christ, beloved above all from thy youth, thou didst follow Him with all thy soul's love; counting the corruptible things of the world as nothing, thou didst make a cave in the earth, and therein didst valiantly struggle against the snares of the invisible enemy, shining forth like a radiant sun unto all the ends of the earth. Whence rejoicing, thou didst pass over unto the heavenly chambers. And now standing before the Master's throne with the angels, remember us who honor thy memory, that we may cry unto thee: Rejoice, O Anthony our father!

*SERVICE TO THE VENERABLE FATHERS OF THE NEAR CAVES:*

*Month of September.*
*Service to the Venerable Fathers whose incorrupt relics repose in the Near Cave,*
*Which is chanted following the Apodosis of the Feast of the Elevation of the Precious Cross, on the first Saturday.*
*On Friday evening, at Great Vespers: "Blessed is the man..." the fifth kathisma.*
*At "Lord, I have cried," stichera on 6, Tone 4. Podoben: "Having set aside" (Всѐ ѿло́жше):*
*Rejoicing with the dawn of spiritual songs, we convene all the assemblies of festival-lovers to praise the union of the laborious, God-bearing Fathers, and as a foundation for this we place great and marvelous Anthony, and with him God-wise Theodosius as armor; and all who pray, come, asking with their prayers: for behold, another from thy God comes with swift aid to the people, not being unmindful of the memory of the innocent, who honor the gathering of their fellow-servants.*[^8]

*Service to the Venerable Fathers of the Caves.*
*...ye sang, and what struggles ye endured: exhausting your flesh with vigilance, fasting, and tears, overcoming the pride of the enemy with prayers and humility; and having shone forth in a mortal body like angels, ye removed unto the heavenly mansions: whence visit in this present day those who praise your honored assembly of ascetics in God, and ask of Christ God that we may obtain great mercy.*
*Both now... Theotokion according to the tone: seek at the end of this book. Then, Entrance: Prokeimenon of the day, and three readings for the Venerable:*
*Reading from the Wisdom of Solomon (Chapter 3):*[^9]
The souls of the righteous are in the hand of God, and no torment shall touch them. In the eyes of the foolish they seemed to die, and their departure was accounted an affliction, and their going from us utter destruction; but they are in peace. For though they be punished in the sight of men, their hope is full of immortality. And having been a little chastened, they shall be greatly rewarded; for God proved them, and found them worthy for Himself. As gold in the furnace He tried them, and received them as a whole burnt offering. And in the time of their visitation they shall shine forth, and shall run like sparks through the stubble. They shall judge nations, and have dominion over peoples, and the Lord shall reign in them unto the ages. They that put their trust in Him shall understand truth, and the faithful in love shall abide with Him; for grace and mercy are in His venerable ones, and visitation in His elect.
*Then, Chapter 5:*[^10]
The righteous live unto the ages, and their reward is in the Lord, and the care of them is with the Most High. Therefore shall they receive the kingdom of majesty, and the crown of comeliness from the hand of the Lord; for with His right hand shall He cover them, and with His arm shall He shield them. He shall take His zeal for complete armor, and shall arm creation for vengeance against enemies. He shall put on righteousness as a breastplate, and shall put on *Of the Near Caves.*
...judgment unfeigned as a helmet. He shall take holiness as an invincible shield, and He shall sharpen severe wrath for a sword; and the world shall fight with Him against the senseless. Right-aiming arrows of lightning shall go forth, and as from a well-drawn bow of the clouds they shall fly unto the mark; and hailstones full of wrath shall be cast as from a stone-bow. The water of the sea shall rage against them, and the rivers shall drown them abruptly. A spirit of power shall stand up against them, and like a whirlwind shall sift them. And lawlessness shall lay waste all the earth, and evildoing shall overturn the thrones of the mighty. Give ear therefore, O ye kings, and understand; learn, ye judges of the ends of the earth. Give ear, ye that rule multitudes, and that boast yourselves over nations of peoples: for dominion is given you from the Lord, and power from the Most High.
*Reading from the Wisdom of Solomon (Chapter 4):*[^11]
Though the righteous happen to die early, yet shall he be in rest. For honorable old age is not measured by length of time, nor reckoned by number of years; but wisdom is gray hair unto men, and an unspotted life is mature age. He pleased God and was beloved of Him; and living among sinners, he was translated. He was taken away, lest evil change his understanding or deceit beguile his soul. For the fascination of wickedness darkens good things, and the roving of desire perverts the guileless mind. Having reached perfection in a short time, he fulfilled long years; for his soul was pleasing to the Lord: therefore He hastened to take him from the midst of wickedness. The people saw and did not understand, nor did they lay this in their mind: that grace and mercy are in His venerable ones, and visitation in His elect.
*At the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 1. Podoben: "O all-praised martyrs" (И҆́же до́блыя):*
Come, O people, let us first honor the sacred choir of the God-bearing Fathers who were glorified in the holy cave as good soldiers of Christ; for behold, they come from the noetic Sun with swift aid unto the people, bringing from their fullness, and imparting untold grace unto those who honor their assembly with reverence.
*Verse: The righteous cried, and the Lord gave ear unto them.* (Ps. 33:18 [LXX])[^12]
The monastic gathering upon the most sacred firmament, which like noetic stars hath illumined the honored yoke: having come together today in the holy temple, O people, stand bearing candles, and cry out with a suppliant voice: Rejoice, O God-bearing and most blessed ones, intercessors for those who sing of your union with love!

*Service to the Venerable Fathers of the Caves.*
*Verse: Be glad in the Lord, and rejoice, ye righteous.* (Ps. 31:11 [LXX])[^13]
Rejoice, ye choirs of Christ-loving people, sanctify this celebration today with us, and weaving crowns of praise, calling together upon all by name who have blossomed in the holy cave and illumine with the rays of their grace the souls of those who magnify them with love and faithfully celebrate their memory!
*Glory: Tone 6:*
Planting their cross like a spear, the monastic gathering of everlasting glory convened, and there on the Caves Mount the God-marked host of the Venerable Fathers, bearing invincible armor against the enemy, by which they cast down the demonic hosts unto the end; and having arrived in the heavenly habitation, they received crowns of victory from Christ God and our Savior, and unceasingly pray for our souls.
*Both now... Theotokion:*
*Thou art the true vine:*
*Troparion, Tone 4:*
As a pillar of fire and a radiant sun shining forth upon the Caves Mount, we bring to Thee in prayers, O Christ, the great Anthony with the entire assembly of the God-bearing Fathers; for their sake, we pray Thee: grant grace unto our monasteries, and unto our souls great mercy.
*Glory: Both now... Theotokion:*
*The mystery hidden from the ages:*
*At Matins, following the First Stikhologia, Sessional Hymn, Tone 4. Podoben: "Thou hast appeared" (Ꙗ҆ви́лсѧ є҆сѝ):*
Through many tribulations ye traversed the path of this life, and having reached the boundary of noetic desire, ye settled in the chamber of divine glory; and now rejoicing with the King of all and God, pray, that we who honor your assembly may also become partakers of your joy, O all-blessed fathers.
*Glory: Both now... Theotokion:*
O full of grace, intercede by thy prayers, and entreat for our souls a multitude of compassions and cleansing of transgressions for all who hymn thee as the pure Mother and Virgin.
*Following the Second Stikhologia, Sessional Hymn, Tone 3:*
Having heard of the Lamb's wedding feast in the heavenly bridal chamber, and the calling of the King on high having gone forth unto all, ye abandoned all worldly attachments, O God-bearing fathers; and having cleansed your robes by tearful labors, ye entered into the feast of Christ God; but before the doors of that entrance are shut, pray, that we also, having entered through repentance, may become partakers with you.
*Glory: Both now... Theotokion:*
Beholding ever the bridal chamber adorned with everlasting glory, we have a desire to be renewed into it, but having no wedding garment, we are ashamed, lest we be exposed by the angels and cast out. Thou art the garment of boldness for the naked, O Virgin Mary: adorn us and lead us within the Kingdom of Christ!

*Of the Near Caves.*
*Then, "Praise ye the name of the Lord": Polyeleos.*
*Meglynarion (Magnification):*
We magnify you, O venerable Pechersk fathers of ours, and we honor your holy memory; for ye pray for us to Christ our God.
*Verse 1: Rejoice in the Lord, O ye righteous; praise is meet for the upright.* (Ps. 32:1 [LXX])[^14]
*Verse 2: They that sow in tears shall reap in joy.* (Ps. 125:5 [LXX])[^15]
*Verse 3: The venerable shall boast in glory, and they shall rejoice upon their beds.* (Ps. 149:5 [LXX])[^16]
*Verse 4: Be glad in the Lord, and rejoice, ye righteous, and glory, all ye that are upright in heart.* (Ps. 31:11 [LXX])[^17]
*Glory: Both now... Alleluia, thrice.*
*Following the Polyeleos, Sessional Hymn, Tone 8. Podoben: "The Wisdom" (Премꙋ́дрости):*
Following the calling of your Lord, taking the cross into your hands as a staff, ye feared not the terror that stood in the darkness, but through hope ye passed over unto the tranquil haven, and having found the reward of your struggles, ye rejoice in the unapproachable light; therefore do we honor you, O all-blessed fathers, as ever praying to Christ God to grant remission of sins unto those who celebrate your holy memory with love.
*Glory: Both now... Theotokion:*
Having thee, O Virgin Bride of God, as the only one blessed among women, and the boast and ornament of Adam, invincible help in battles, confirmation for the weak, and speedy comfort for those in affliction, we fall down today in thy most honored temple and offer our supplications unto thee: that thou mayest pray to Christ God to grant remission of sins unto those who piously worship thy most pure childbirth.
*Gradual Hymn (Anabathmoi), 1st Antiphon of Tone 4.*
*Prokeimenon: Wondrous is God in His saints, the God of Israel.* (Ps. 67:36 [LXX])[^18]
*Verse: In the congregations bless ye God, the Lord from the springs of Israel.* (Ps. 67:27 [LXX])[^19]
*Let every breath praise the Lord.*
*Verse: Praise God in His saints.*
*Then, the Gospel according to Matthew, pericope 10 (Matt. 4:23–5:1):*[^20]
*At that time, Jesus was followed by great multitudes...*
*Following Psalm 50, Glory:*
Through the prayers of our venerable Pechersk fathers, O Merciful One, blot out the multitude of our transgressions.
*Both now...*
Through the prayers of the Theotokos, O Merciful One, blot out the multitude of our transgressions.
*Then:*
*Have mercy on me, O God, according to Thy great mercy, and according to the multitude of Thy compassions blot out my transgression.* (Ps. 50:3 [LXX])[^21]
*Sticheron, Tone 8:*
*Who shall declare the mighty acts of the Lord?* (Ps. 105:2 [LXX])[^22]
*Canon.*

*Service to the Venerable Fathers of the Caves.*
*CANON*
*To the Theotokos, with the Heirmos, on 6; and to the Venerable Fathers, on 8. Tone 8.*
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
*Let us send up a song, O ye people, unto our marvelous God, Who delivered Israel from bondage, singing a song of victory and crying aloud: Let us sing unto Thee, the only Master.*
*Troparia:*
In the memory of Thy venerable ones, O my Christ, grant help from on high, and inspire and make me wise through Thy Holy Paraclete, that I may offer a hymn of praise unto Thee, the only Master.
The assembly of the God-bearing Fathers hath gathered in God; come, O people, in veneration, bringing with yourselves living sacrifices; for these summon us, that we may offer glory unto the King of all, the only Master.
Those whom the East and the Russian land brought forth, and whom the Caves Mount adorned like trees planted by God: having gladdened the people with their fruits, let us honor them eagerly, and offer a song unto the Master.
Thou art a noetic paradise, O holy Caves Mount, having multiplied the noetic trees, the blessed fathers, whom we are unable to count one by one: for all together we offer praise and song unto the only Master.
A fruitful tree, an olive branch, and an adornment brought unto this mountain is great Anthony, by whose fruits gladdening our souls, we offer a song unto Thee, O Christ, the Master of all.
Stephen, full of faith, and builder in piety of the church founded by God, and Nikon, who adorned it with icons, shone forth like great luminaries; honoring them, we offer a song unto Thee alone, O Christ, Master of all.
*Glory:*
Offering glory unto Thee, O Most Holy Trinity, we bring the marvelous Barlaam as an intercessor, who despised the glory of wealthy lineage: for whose sake cast my glory down into the dust in this world, that there I may behold glory in Thee, Master of all.
*Both now... Theotokion:*
O thou who didst restore the human race unto its primal nobility through thy holy childbirth: having been dishonored by demonic deeds, restore me unto noetic glory, that I may ever sing to thee: Rejoice, O pure Virgin Mother, the glory of the Orthodox!
*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
*There is none holy like the Lord, and none righteous like our God, Whom all creation hymnet: there is none righteous above Thee, O Lord.*
*Troparia:*
Though praise is not comely in my lips, yet do I offer it unto thee, O God-bearing Ephraim; for having ceased to serve an earthly prince, thou didst struggle for the Heavenly King, and didst receive the reward of thy labors.

*Of the Near Caves.*
I honor Isaiah, magnificent in the gift of wonderworking, and I praise him who held the archiepiscopal see of Rostov, praying that by his intercessions he may join me, a straying sheep, unto the flock of the Shepherd on high, and lead me into His fold.
An adornment of the hierarchs and of healers, with praises I honor blessed Damian; for burning with love toward all, he healed with oil and prayer: by his loving-kindness, heal me also, O Master, Lover of mankind!
It is meet to honor thee, Jeremiah, with praises; for honored from on high with the gift of clairvoyance, thou wast a foreseer of human secrets by divine revelation: remember me, O blessed one, who am in forgetfulness of countless sins, and be a guide unto my repentance.
Matthew, who didst behold the secret incursions of the demons and didst correct those who gave occasion to impassioned assaults: as knowing my lack of amendment with noetic eyes, direct me, that I may magnify thee who hast become venerable.
Good shepherd and luminary of Suzdal, Simon, together with Nikon the recluse, as zealous imitators of the angelic Lavra and zealous promoters of monastic correction: do ye, our fathers, correct me who have fallen, through your instruction.
*Glory:*
Thy true servants preserved Thy glory in their souls throughout this life, O Holy Trinity; therefore were they counted worthy of everlasting glory from Thee, of which may we also not be deprived, as we present their assembly in prayer unto Thee today.
*Both now... Theotokion:*
O thou who art ineffable joy of the venerable through the Word, make me a partaker of their joy, and deprive me not of their glory; for all things are possible for thee in God, and whatsoever thou wilt, thou dost accomplish, O Mother, Bride of God!
*Sessional Hymn, Tone 8:*
Daring to honor the assembly of the God-bearing Fathers, the noetic stars that illumine the Russian land, but having a soul darkened by passions, I am at a loss in mind: dispel the darkness of unstable thoughts, that I may honorably sing a hymn unto you, O most blessed fathers!
*Glory: Both now... Theotokion:*
Rejoice, O Mother of the Unwaning Star, and dawn of the mystical day; for through thee have we beheld the Light and Sun of true righteousness, illumined by Whose rays we ever hymn thee, O God-blessed Maiden!
*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
*The Prophet, seeing God, beheld Thee from the overshadowing mountain, O Word, about to become incarnate from the only Theotokos, and with fear he glorified Thy power.*
*Troparion incipit:*
*From the mountain Service to the Venerable Fathers of the Caves.*
*Troparia:*
From the mountain, according to divine tradition, the God-bearing Fathers appeared upon the earth as luminaries, and illumined the Russian land with the brightness of their life: which we honoring, marvel, and glorify Thy power, O Christ.
We marvel that being in the flesh, having taken up a struggle above nature, they were likened unto the angels; for having winged their souls toward divine love, they preferred nothing in the world above it, whence even in feeble flesh they received Thy power, O Christ.
Stephen, strong in fasting, and Anastasius, through boldness toward God, obtained all things in their petitions; blessing them honorably, I glorify Thy power, O my Christ.
Isaac, deceived by the darkness in the light, and having deceived the darkness by the light of correction, so that even now the sons of darkness fear his name: through such a one we pray Thee, O Christ, for Thy venerable one, that we may ever glorify Thy power.
I dare to honor the struggles of Ilya of Murom, the invincible warrior: who had the wound of a weapon in his hand, but having a deeper wound in his heart through love toward Thee, O Christ, He is crowned all the more thereby, glorifying Thy power.
Nikon's well-grounded strength through temptations, in that he endured much from the infidels; but having acquired humility and salvation the more through patience: by his prayers may we also be saved, glorifying Thy power, O all-powerful Christ.
*Glory:*
In one essence we glorify the tri-hypostatic Godhead, Father and Son and Most Holy Spirit; and we hymn Thee as the One God, and venerating in the memory of our venerable fathers, we glorify Thy power.
*Both now... Theotokion:*
Having holy hope, I fear not the assaults of the demons, and having acquired boldness concerning thee, I oppose satanic terrors, O Sovereign Lady Virgin; for thou art the good thunder terrifying the enemies.
*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
*Deliver me from the mist of passions, O Lover of mankind, as from deepest night, and count my spirit worthy, I pray, to awake early unto the light of the day of Thy commandments, O Christ.*
*Troparia:*
Having set aside all the mist of passions and harmful desire, Thy venerable ones, O Lord, ran with patience unto the contest set before them: therefore were they honorably illumined by grace, walking in the light of Thy commandments, O Savior.
We glorify the memory of Titus the priest, who trampled the wrathful demon through humility, and of Lawrence the recluse, who had boldness against evil spirits; for these preserved Thy commandments, and glorify Thee in Thy light, O Savior.

*Of the Near Caves.*
Even if I dare to praise Sisoes who shone forth in fasting, yet according to his worth I cannot bless him; unto Thee alone therefore do I flee: grant me strength from Thy gifts, O Savior, to praise Thy favorite.
Theophilus shall appear excellent in wonders; for while yet in this life he astonished many through wonderworking, and now hath he received a greater gift, to be likened unto the angels, because he kept Thy commandments, O Savior.
We honor Basil and Theodore with the martyrs, as slandered through God, who innocently endured severe torment: the one receiving his end pierced by an arrow, and the other by fire and smoke; yet with one mind they are glorified in Thy Kingdom, O Savior.
Prokhor is praised in his wonderworking and labors, for like a bird he fed upon uncultivated herbs, and was accounted as a sower of bread made from orache, and turning ashes into salt, distributed it; marveling at his wonderworking, we glorify Thee, O Savior.
*Glory:*
Led by the Spirit, John was called a son of the Most High, and was illumined by fasting; for he fed upon the Word of God rather than food: therefore, as though winged, he lightly traversed this life, having received Thy power from Thee, O Savior.
*Both now... Theotokion:*
All thy glory, O daughter of the King, is within: clothed and adorned, thou standest before the royal throne; whence shining brighter than the sun, look down upon us with the rays of thy grace, that we may ever sing of thy power, O Virgin Mother.
*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
*Held fast by many sins, O Lover of mankind, receive me who fall down before Thy compassions, as the Prophet, O Lord, and save me.*
*Troparia:*
I am held fast by love to praise your assembly, O God-bearing fathers; for ye are praised by God and men, because in this life ye despised the glory of this world, that ye might fulfill the commandments of Christ.
What tongue shall declare your glory, O venerable ones, which ye acquired through many labors, and completed by grace! All the signs manifest your marvelous benefactions and healings, by which despise not us who are infirm, that we may be able to fulfill the commandments of Christ.
O marvelous Agapitus, who impartest healings unexpectedly, and Helladius ever with the other Abraham: having much boldness toward the Master of all, despise not your servants, but pray *Service to the Venerable Fathers of the Caves.*
...pray, that we may be delivered from the snares of the enemy, and always do the will of God.
Calling unto him for many years, and even after death showing love to his brother through movement, O Theophilus, washing away defilement with tears while glorifying: as undivided here, so also there, pray together unto God, that we also may have true love toward Him, and unfeigned love toward our neighbor.
Damian, who healest spiritual passions with prayer and oil, with marvelous Paul: despise not us who faint with spiritual passions, but with the healed gift of your prayers crown our wounded hearts, that we may offer acceptable prayers unto God.
O obedient Nectarius, with Sergius of one mind with thee: having yielded your souls unto the yoke of the obedience of Christ, bow down our stubbornness by your prayer beneath the yoke, that we may eagerly fulfill His commandments.
The glory of the city of Smolensk, pastor Mercury, well tending the sheep of the Chief Shepherd, and in spirit being on the mountain undivided from the choir of the Pechersk venerable ones: whence after death having received wonderworking in the tomb, and reposing in body in the sacred cave, in soul he rejoices with the venerable ones in everlasting glory.
*Glory: Both now... Theotokion:*
Having no ascent unto the heavenly habitations from my works, I flee unto thee, O Sovereign Lady Theotokos; for thou art the Heavenly Door and the Ladder seen by the Prophet: by thy grace grant ascent in my heart, and open unto me the doors of thy mercy!
*Kontakion, Tone 2:*
Having gathered the choir of fasters in mind, beholding them with noetic eyes, come, O people, let us honor them with praises, and venerating their relics, bring gifts with love; for they are intercessors with God and pray-ers for our souls.
*Ikos:*
Who can praise Thy saints one by one, O Blessed One! If I count them, they are multiplied more than the sand; but do Thou, O Master Christ, Who countest the multitude of the stars and callest them all by their names, receive their supplications for us, and grant me Thy compassions upon Thy people: for we know that they have great boldness toward Thy majesty, and intercede with Thee as ever pray-ers for our souls.
*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
*Thou Who didst dew the children in the furnace, and didst preserve the Virgin uncorrupt after childbirth: blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers.*
*Troparia:*
Thou Who hast counted me worthy to offer a childlike song, O God-adorned choir of Pechersk fathers, through your instruction may I sing passionlessly with Christ-loving people: Blessed is the God of our fathers!

*Of the Near Caves.*
Pimen the Much-Ailing, who not only passed through this life in illness with thanksgiving, but didst also grant holy rest unto others: relieve me of sickness, that I may sing: Blessed is the God of our fathers!
Pure gold and silver tried sevenfold with gifts, O righteous favorite, thou wast a doer of the righteous, and giving all into the working of noetic virtues, thou didst pass over: by which having purified thy soul, thou didst present it unto God like gold, and singest: Blessed is the God of our fathers!
O glorious Nestor, who didst reveal unto us by thy writing the times and years of the most marvelous Church, the lives and virtues of the first fathers, and wast a zealous emulator of them in deed: together with them, bless thee now, singing: Blessed is the God of our fathers!
Eustratius, beauty of martyrs, thou didst appear as a second Elijah the charioteer: but he was taken as unto heaven, whereas thou didst ascend into the very heavenly gates in a fiery chariot, a voice calling thee from the majesty of glory, where with a voice of rejoicing thou singest: Blessed is the God of our fathers!
Glorious in gifted wonders, O blessed fathers, Helladius and Luke the steward, as having been good workers of the blessed commandments, now have ye received retribution from the torrent of delight, and joyfully sing: Blessed is the God of our fathers!
*Glory:*
The Spirit having come, bears witness with our spirit, O God-bearing fathers, that ye are children of God and heirs of Christ, Whom ye served, and with Whom ye are glorified, singing: Blessed is the God of our fathers!
*Both now... Theotokion:*
The Holy Spirit came upon thee, and the power of the Most High having overshadowed thee, showed thee to be a pure Mother both in and after childbirth: through whom being delivered from alien bondage, all generations bless thee, for the blessed God of our fathers hath blessed thee.
*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
*Hymn the Lord Who was glorified on the holy mountain and revealed the mystery of the Ever-Virgin in the bush by fire unto Moses, and exalt Him above all unto all ages.*
*Troparia:*
The God-bearing Fathers, the great luminaries whom Thou didst cause to shine upon the holy Pechersk Mount, whence they extended their gracious rays and illumined all the Russian land with wonders: therefore do we hymn the Lord, and exalt Him above all unto the ages.
*Not my much-suffering, but the holy cave reveals your relics Service to the Venerable Fathers of the Caves.*
...relics, like precious pearls, O favorites of God, and ye enrich with healings of wonders all who come unto you with faith and who exalt the Lord with praises unto the ages.
Matthew, the foreseer of things to come, and John, most radiant above others in much suffering, and Moses, the mirror of purity, like precious stones hidden in the cave, are revealed through wonders unto all who come unto them with faith and who exalt the Lord with praises unto the ages.
Ephraim the eunuch, the noetic dove, who brought for all an olive branch of the solitary rule into the holy monastery, and handed it down adorned with elder-like manners, together with the glorious and clairvoyant Jeremiah: let these be ever to praise the Lord unto the ages.
Mark the grave-digger showed himself a mirror unto the labor-loving, a teacher unto the weeping, a raiser of the dead: whose word they obeyed with fear as the voice of a lion; and now is he terrible unto demons, for he lives with the Lord unto the ages.
Gregory showed himself a wonderworker not of Caesarea, but of the Pechersk Lavra, who through prayer held thieves motionless with the fruit for three days and three nights; and having finished his life by drowning in water, he now rejoices with Sergius, his companion, upon the bed of repose, singing unto the Lord unto the ages.
*We bless the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit: the Lord.*
Glory be unto Erasmus, who adorned the Pechersk church with icons, and unto Onesimus, renowned for utility, unto whom is numbered Matthew of the day, who had an entrusted gift: in honor of which three we glorify the name of the Trinity unto all ages.
*Both now...*
Who does not desire ever to glorify thee, O Virgin of wonders? He is an alien unto his own salvation; for thy glory is extended unto the salvation of all who glorify thee, O Mary, Bride of God!
*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
*The childbirth of the Ever-Virgin revealed upon the mountain unto the Lawgiver in the fire and the bush for the salvation of us the faithful, with unceasing hymns do we magnify.*
*Troparia:*
Upon the holy mountain, in the dark cave, ye pitched your tents, O neighboring fathers, and enriched it with virtues and wonders, like fruitful trees, from which those who come with faith receive the fruits of healings, and magnify you with praises.
Spyridon, lily of guilelessness, and Nicodemus, named after victory, both holy prosphora-bakers, who made pure loaves as an offering unto God and made themselves a sweet-smelling sacrifice: unto them is numbered Anatolius also, all of whom we magnify.

*Of the Near Caves.*
Angel-like Alypius, with Gregory of Byzantium, most skilled iconographers: with the paints of good deeds amend our unskillful works, that having stood before Christ radiant in form, we may magnify you with glory.
Pimen the faster, sweet-smelling flower, and Ephraim the disciple: these are the good olive tree, anointed by the gift of whose grace, the faithful give thanks, and in doxologies ever magnify the Creator of all.
Onuphrius the lover of silence, and Isaiah the wilderness-loving turtledove, with blessed Sylvester: a three-stranded cord against the demons, having utterly choked their passionate suggestions inflicted by them, have gifts to assist others also; therefore do all magnify them.
Vladimir, equal-to-the-apostles, with God-wise Roman and David, and with them Nikola Sviatosha, a quaternity of Russian princes, are sung; and upon them, as upon a noetic chariot, God hath rested; therefore do all magnify them.
*Glory:*
Glory to the martyrs, branch-bearing Barbara, and Euphrosyne of Polotsk, who gave singing, and Juliana, who bestowed healings upon the infirm, the oil of grace: for the sake of these honored virgins, save all, O Holy Trinity, who magnify Thee with adoration!
*Both now... Theotokion:*
Rejoice, O radiant glory of the saints, most pure Virgin! Rejoice, O ship for those who wish to be saved in this tempest-tossed life! Rejoice, O rock that gave drink to those thirsting for everlasting blessings! This "Rejoice" do we all offer unto thee, and with songs do we magnify thee.
*Exapostilarion (Photogogic):*
With songs let us sing of the hierarchs of the Russian land: among whom first Michael, Hilarion, Peter, Alexius, and Jonah; Photius and Cyprian, Ephraim, Nicholas, and Niphont; Macarius the hieromartyr, Leontius, and Symeon; and the entire pastoral assembly do we bring in prayer: for their sake, O Christ, save our souls!
*Theotokion:*
O true Mother of the spotless Lamb and the Shepherd on high, I am a sheep of thy holy fold, but through empty deeds I have turned aside into foul iniquities: guide me, I pray thee, and save my soul from the noetic wolves!
*At the Praises (Lauds): Stichera.*
*Tone 2. Podoben: "When from the Tree" (Егда̀ ѿ дре́ва):*
With a single-minded soul and a single-minded heart, O all-blessed fathers who beheld all things, having cast off all worldly attachments and taken up your cross according to the word of the Lord, to follow through voluntary poverty Christ God Who became poor for our sake: by Whom now to be enriched with imperishable *Service to the Venerable Fathers of the Caves.*
...treasures in Heaven: send down from them unto us also, that we too may be enriched, and through your intercession receive great mercy from the Master of all.
Rejoice, O choir of God-chosen fathers; for having lived an angelic life single-mindedly in this world, ye set your dwelling in the dark cave, and having drawn near unto God by many labors, ye received repose from Him; but as standing before Him in prayer, look down upon us with prayer, and by intercession appease Christ the Lover of mankind, that we may find forgiveness of sins and obtain mercy on the day of judgment, as we fall down before you in prayer.
Today hath shone forth brighter than the sun the all-festal memory of the God-bearing fathers, and illumines the festal gathering with the rays of grace of manifold wonders; therefore do we also, O all-blessed ones, run with faith unto you, and fall down before your relics, asking in our necessities for your speedy visitation: despise not our supplication, but as having boldness before the Benefactor, entreat great mercy for us.
*Glory: Tone 8.*
The hymnographic gifts of great honors, O our all-blessed Pechersk fathers, do we offer unto you with love, saying: Rejoice, O adornment of the Russian land! Rejoice, O boast of monastics! Rejoice, O good warriors of the Heavenly King! Rejoice, O radiant stars of the noetic firmament! Rejoice, O citizens of Sion on high! Rejoice, O myrrh-breathing flowers of paradise! Rejoice, O our protectors in afflictions and intercessors for salvation! And as having boldness before the Good One, fervently pray for our souls!
*Both now... Theotokion:*
O Sovereign Lady, receive the supplications of thy servants...
*Great Doxology, and Dismissal.*
*At the Liturgy, the Beatitudes from the Canon, Odes 3 and 6.*
*Prokeimenon, Tone 7: Precious in the sight of the Lord is the death of His venerable ones.* (Ps. 115:6 [LXX])[^23]
*Verse: What shall I render unto the Lord for all that He hath rendered unto me?* (Ps. 115:3 [LXX])[^24]
*Epistle to the Corinthians, pericope 172 (2 Cor. 4:6–15).*[^25]
*Alleluia, Tone 6: Blessed is the man that fears the Lord, he shall greatly delight in His commandments.* (Ps. 111:1 [LXX])[^26]
*Verse: His seed shall be mighty upon earth.* (Ps. 111:2 [LXX])[^27]
*Gospel according to Matthew, pericope 43 (Matt. 11:27–30).*[^28]
*Communion Verse: Rejoice in the Lord, O ye righteous.* (Ps. 32:1 [LXX])[^29] [Woodcut Engraving: The Venerable Damian the Healer, full length, with hagiographical border scenes of the Venerable Jeremiah and Matthew; below: An Angel came to Damian in the likeness of Theodosius, promising the Kingdom]

# MONTH OF OCTOBER: ON THE 5TH DAY

*Memory of Our Venerable Fathers Damian the Presbyter, Matthew, and Jeremiah the Clairvoyant:*
*At Vespers, on "Lord, I have cried": Stichera, on 6. Tone 4. Podoben: "As One Valiant" (Ꙗ҆́кѡ до́блѧ):*
Held fast by spiritual and bodily ailments, with the oil of thy mercy, as having a sacred anointing from on high, O Damian our companion, speedily heal me who cry unto thee from the heart; and through warm prayers unto God, grant humility unto my mind, and cleanse the festering wounds of my soul, that with clean *Service to the Venerable Fathers of the Caves.*
...clean lips I may offer praise unto God Who is glorified in His saints.
Having illumined thy soul by the grace of the Spirit received, O all-praised Jeremiah, thou didst receive a gift from God, to announce things future as though present; and likening thyself unto the marvelous prophet, unerringly didst thou foretell all things, and didst foresee the secrets of the human heart: by thine illumination enlighten our hearts also, and ask from Christ God great mercy!
Matthew, preacher of the Word of God, thou didst follow Him, O marvelous one; for as that Evangelist boldly reproved the unrepentant people, so also didst thou, O blessed one, put to shame the craftily woven snares of the subterranean cunning Ethiopians; and now we pray thee, O father, noetically cleanse our minds, drive away the demonic legions, and deliver our souls from afflictions!
*Glory: Tone 2.*
Come, O ye people, let us honor with praises the three most blessed fathers, intercessors before the Trinity: Damian, Jeremiah, and Matthew; and let us offer doxology unto God Who was in their minds, Who gave His favorites as a help unto the Russian land and as intercessors for our souls.
*Both now... Theotokion:*
*At the Aposticha, all from the Octoechos.*
*Glory: Tone 6.*
Bright and radiant hath shone forth today your memory, O our blessed fathers; for brightly do ye shine with the rays of wonders upon those who come unto you and ask of you warm protection; therefore do we also fall down before your relics, eagerly kissing them, and we glorify God Who gave you such great gifts, with which enrich us also, and entreat great mercy from Christ!
*Both now... Theotokion:*
*Troparion, Tone 4:*
Having illumined your hearts with the light of the commandments of Christ, ye drove away the passionate gloom; therefore did ye three-numbered fathers, Damian, Jeremiah with Matthew, become a dwelling of the Trinity, from Whom having received grace, ye heal the infirm, announce things to come, and being partakers with the angels, pray unto Christ God to grant us communion with the saints!
*CANON*
*Tone 2. Ode 1. Heirmos:*
*Unto God the Savior, Who guided the people in the sea with dry feet, and drowned Pharaoh with all his host, unto Him alone let us sing: for He hath been glorified.*
*Troparia:*
Unto God the Savior let us offer praise, glorifying His favorite, all-blessed Damian, the wise physician of the infirm; and let us cry aloud unto Him Who gave such gifts: for He hath been glorified!

*To Damian, Jeremiah, and Matthew.*
With unceasing zeal didst thou imitate the angel-like life of thy teacher, instructed by whom thou wast adorned with virtue, abiding unceasingly in praise throughout all nights without sleep, and crying unto God: for He hath been glorified!
Standing now with the rest before God the Savior, as having boldness, pray through grace to establish the hearts of those who bless thee with faith, O divinely inspired Jeremiah, and who cry unto the Savior: for He hath been glorified!
*Glory:*
The all-seeing eye of God, beholding thy pure heart, O blessed Matthew, gave thee the gift to know the secrets of men and to reveal them for the profit of all, that guided by thee we may sing unto God: for He hath been glorified!
*Both now... Theotokion:*
O noetic Sun, holy and consecrated Virgin, illumine our minds darkened by passions and pleasures, that enlightened by thee, we may sing unto God: for He hath been glorified!
*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
*By the power of Thy Cross, O Christ, establish my mind, that I may hymn and glorify Thy saving Ascension.*
*Troparia:*
Strengthened by the power of God, thou wast gentle and silent, O blessed one, ever with warm prayers unto God, and exercising thyself in diligent reading of the Holy Scriptures, and thereby being established in the Lord.
The word of God was thy food and drink, with which thou didst feed thy soul, O venerable one; while thy body was fed only with bread and water, and upon this didst thou nourish thyself until the day of thy death, subduing unto the spirit all rising passions.
Having completed thy years in ripe old age, O Jeremiah, thou didst receive the gift to foresee the secrets of the heart, and in private didst reprove the cunning thoughts of men, and didst instruct them to beware of the counsel of the devil.
*Glory:*
Having come unto the first-founded Pechersk fathers, thou didst dwell with them, and living as a monk didst cut off all bodily lusts; therefore didst thou receive a gift from God, openly to behold the faces of the subterranean Ethiopians, O marvelous Matthew!
*Both now... Theotokion:*
The cunning demons who transform themselves into manifold forms and work in our members, do thou, O Theotokos, terrify and drive away by the visitation of thy countenance, that without disturbance we may sing of thy saving deliverance!

*Service to the Venerable Fathers of the Caves.*
*Sessional Hymn, Tone 8:*
As having received twofold grace from God, Thou healest twofold passions: spiritual ones through diligent prayers, and bodily ones through anointing with holy oil; do Thou heal us who suffer from both ailments, and drive away the passions that oppress us, and pray unto Christ God that those who honor thy holy dormition may obtain everlasting life.
*Glory: Both now... Theotokion:*
All-spotless Bride, spacious dwelling of the Word of God: cleanse me who am a foul dwelling of passions, and show me forth as a radiant habitation of virtues, drive away the darkness of iniquities, and by thy prayers count me worthy to partake of the Unwaning Light, that I may sing of thine ineffable mercy!
*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
*I heard the report of the power of the Cross, that Paradise was opened thereby, and I cried aloud: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!*
*Troparia:*
Thou didst appear as a sweet-smelling cluster of the vineyard of the Mother of God, watered by the dew of the teachings of the venerable fathers, and didst distil the wine of healings unto all who flee unto thee with faith, O Damian, servant of God.
Walled about with the weapon of faith and the flame of love, O blessed one, thou didst drive away the enemies who harm men with diseases; for thy diligent prayer, passing through the clouds, drove away sicknesses, O Damian worthy of wonder.
As a foreseer of human thoughts, thou didst observe those turning their backs unto the enemies and wishing to depart from this holy enclosure, and with words mixed with love thou didst instruct them, O Jeremiah, to stand firmly in the contest, and to be unshakable against the enemies.
*Glory:*
With hidden eyes, O Matthew, thou didst once behold in church a demon walking about in the likeness of a warrior, and casting sticky flowers upon the brothers' cloaks, so that, darkened with sleep, they would depart before the blessing; therefore didst thou instruct them to depart without laziness and to wall themselves about with the weapon of the Cross.
*Both now... Theotokion:*
Encompassed by demonic legions and assailed by flattering passions, strengthen me, O Mother of God, and grant me grace to conquer their deceptive wiles, that I may flee beneath thy pure help!
*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
*Rising early, we cry unto Thee, O Lord: Save us! For Thou art our God, and beside Thee we know no other.*
*Troparia:*
Seeing those who came in sickness, thy blessed instructor Theodosius commanded thee to offer prayer; and thou, being humble and obedient *To Damian, Jeremiah, and Matthew.*
...obedient, didst count thyself unworthy of the gift of healing; yet fulfilling the command of thy father, with prayer and oil thou didst bestow health upon the sick.
As a good athlete, thou didst walk well along the path of life, confirming thyself through monastic struggles; and when thou didst attain unto thy end, having completed what was lacking through sickness, purer than gold thou didst depart unto painless repose.
Through the clairvoyance given unto thee by God, thou didst see wondrously, O blessed one; without hesitation thou didst declare unto everyone, and thy word was fulfilled in truth; therefore, as the glory of prophets and composer of hymns, we worthily bless thee, O Jeremiah.
*Glory:*
Thou didst never go forth from the church before the conclusion of the singing, but wast always the last, having an insatiable desire to abide in the doxology of God; therefore God glorified thee before angels and men, O God-bearing Matthew!
*Both now... Theotokion:*
Knowing thee to be the holy mountain of God and the house of glory of the Most High, O pure Virgin, falling down in humility I pray: lead me up unto the height of a virtuous life, and make me a pure temple for the reception of divine gifts!
*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
*Cleanse me, for the abyss of my sins seethes within me; yet I cried aloud unto Thee, O Lover of mankind, and Thy right hand saved me, O Lord.*
*Troparia:*
Having lived upon earth along the right paths, at thine end thou didst mingle thy prayer unto the Crowner with tears, asking of His goodness that He deprive thee not of the Heavenly Kingdom and the fellowship of thy father, the venerable Theodosius.
Desiring not to be deprived of partaking of the hymn-singing with the unapproachable light, with humility thou didst send up prayers unto Christ, and didst obtain what thou didst ask; for the wise Creator informed thee by marvelous incorruption, so that with good hope thou mightest depart to rest in the repose of the righteous.
In deep old age thy youth was spiritually renewed, O venerable Jeremiah; for having left behind the old bodily vesture, thou didst depart in soul into a new habitation, unto the ancient prophets, to behold the glory of God.
*Glory:*
Sitting once beneath the church bell, thou didst behold a great throng of gloomy demons, and having questioned them, didst learn that they were going unto a certain brother; therefore thou thyself didst lovingly instruct many of the brethren how to beware of them: teach us also, therefore, O blessed one, that they may not enter into *Service to the Venerable Fathers of the Caves.*
...into the doors of our hearts!
*Both now... Theotokion:*
Deliver me, O Sovereign Lady, from the assaults of demonic passions, lest those cunning throngs enter into the house of my soul; bar the noetic doors of my heart with fear, and grant me to watch and take heed unto my salvation!
*Kontakion, Tone 2:*
As the ancient prophets, having received the grace of prophecy, unerringly foresaw by the Spirit and foretold things about to come to pass, so also in our life did ye foretell the evils coming upon us because of our sins; and now, that evils may not overtake us, entreat from Christ God the amendment of our deeds and great mercy!
*Ikos:*
Having imitated your namesakes, and having lived angelically upon earth, ye were counted worthy of great gifts, O Jeremiah and Matthew; for foretelling things to come, ye brought many unto correction, put all the demons to shame, and astonished men; and now, living after this temporal life in the endless one with the choirs of angels, ask from Christ God the amendment of our deeds and great mercy!
*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
*Thou Who didst save the hymn-singing children in the fiery furnace: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers.*
*Troparia:*
An angel of God stood before thee, O master, father Damian, in the likeness of the venerable Theodosius, and falling upon thy breast kissed thee lovingly, as one beloved of God, and together with angels, and thou didst sing: Blessed is the God of our fathers!
Seeking an apparition, behold the wonder: "He unto Whom they prayed to the Lord hath sent me to inform thee that according to thy petition it shall be unto thee, and thou shalt be settled in the Kingdom of God with the saints." Having heard these words, joyfully didst thou cry aloud, O Damian: Blessed is the God of our fathers!
What is sweeter, and what is greater, than to live with the saints unto the ages? Where Christ lives in the Unwaning Light, there also is a place of repose prepared for thee, O Damian; pray that we also may obtain it who sing: Blessed is the God of our fathers!
Numbered in the choir of the saints, in honor with the prophets, in kinship with the angels, thou wast taught of God, O Jeremiah; praying unto Whom thou standest before Him, that we also may honor Him who sing: Blessed is the God of our fathers!
*Glory:*
Having love for true salvation, O Matthew, thou didst reveal all hidden things for the profit of all, and didst instruct those coming unto thy cell to weep over their sins *To Damian, Jeremiah, and Matthew.*
...sins, and to sing: Blessed is the God of our fathers!
*Both now... Theotokion:*
Delivered through thee from all demonic assaults, O most pure one, unceasingly unto Him Who was born of thee do we cry: Blessed is the God of our fathers!
*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
*Him Who was begotten of the Father before the ages as Son and God, and in these latter times was incarnate of the Virgin Mother: praise Him, O ye priests, and exalt Him above all, O ye people, unto all ages.*
*Troparia:*
When he who was sent unto thee from the Father in brightness appeared, thou didst recognize him to be thy instructor, the God-bearing Theodosius; and having called him, thou didst make known that he stood speaking, and what he promised thee, O Damian; and he marveled at what was told, and exalted the Lord above all unto the ages.
With joy didst thou ask concerning the promises from thine instructor and father, saying: "Shall it be, O father, as thou hast promised me upon my petition?" And he answered: "I know not, child, of what thou askest me; for an angel of God was he who promised thee, and he shall settle thee there where he sings with the saints unto the Lord unto the ages."
Joyful and filled with good hope at the certainty of the promise, thou didst depart unto the radiant places, escorted by radiant angels, with whom also thou singest unto the Lord unto the ages.
All thy knowledge was for the instruction of those being saved, O venerable Matthew; not even the abbot rejected thine instruction; through which being elevated he quickly attained blessedness, where the righteous exalt the Lord above all unto the ages.
*Glory:*
After many revelations thou didst depart in deep old age, O Matthew, to stand with uncovered face before the throne of God; pray also for us, O father, that through thee we also may be counted worthy to behold the Lord, and to exalt Him with praises unto the ages!
*Both now... Theotokion:*
Being one in essence of the Trinity, Christ the Word took up His abode in thy womb, O pure Virgin, and appeared in the flesh consubstantial with us: Whom blessing, we exalt Him above all unto the ages!
*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
*Thee Who beyond mind and word art the Mother of God, Who in time didst ineffably bear the Timeless One, we the faithful magnify with one mind.*
*Troparia:*
What shall I call thee, this blessed place? A heavenly firmament, whereon the trinity of fathers shines like stars through wonders, and illumines those who celebrate their honored memory and devoutly bless them with hymns!
Having passed from the temporal light unto the eternal, O God-bearing ones, by your radiant light our hearts *Service to the Venerable Fathers of the Caves.*
...illumine our hearts, and entreat by your prayers that, walking in the light of the Lord's commandments, we may with you behold the Unwaning Light.
Having received the grace of healings from God, O holy ones, while yet in this life: speedily heal us who are held fast by passions; for with faith we flee unto your relics, and praying we magnify you with hymns.
*Glory:*
Let us glorify God Who glorified His saints, Damian, Jeremiah, and Matthew, who having lived in their bodies like angels glorified Him; Whom we also pray that, honoring their memory today and magnifying them with praises, He deprive us not of His heavenly glory together with them.
*Both now... Theotokion:*
Remembering my end, when the time shall come for me to be loosed from the bonds of the flesh, I tremble, for the day of judgment is coming: from which who shall deliver me, if not thou, O pure Virgin? Therefore do I ever flee unto thee with praise, that through thine intercession we may find mercy on the day of judgment.
*Theotokion:*
Thy temple here is holy, and marvelous in truth; for being thyself the temple of God's glory, by thy providence thou didst build it, and didst adorn it with the angel-like life of the holy fathers: transform our unsightly appearance into goodly beauty, that the King may desire the beauty of our souls, and pour out His great mercy upon us.
*Exapostilarion (Photogogic):*
Having lived holily upon earth, ye are holy in heaven also, and with the angelic choirs ye stand before God: from Whom having received the reward of your labors, forget us not, but taking us by the hand, guide us into the right way, that through your intercession we may find mercy on the day of judgment.

*To Damian, Jeremiah, and Matthew.*
*At the Praises (Lauds): Stichera.*
*Tone 3. Podoben: "Thou hast Given a Sign" (Да́лъ є҆сѝ зна́менїе):*
A radiant reception-vessel of the All-Holy Spirit were ye, O all-blessed fathers, and enlightened by Him, in God-beholding manner ye were enriched with the gift of prophecy, and acquired foreknowledge of things to come and true understanding; therefore do we pray you: pray for us unto Jesus the Lover of mankind and Savior of our souls.
As true prophets, by unerring indications ye foretold all things about to come to pass, and proclaimed them, O hymn-singing fathers: Matthew and Jeremiah, with Damian worthy of wonder; for we know that the Holy Spirit enlightens pure hearts and grants the grace of revelation; therefore we glorify Jesus the Lover of mankind and Savior of our souls Who glorified you.
Kindled with divine love and desire of the blessings to come, ye willingly traversed the narrow path, counting its afflictions as nothing; therefore now rejoicing in a place of refreshment, give of your gifts unto those who honor you with love, and pray for us, O Damian and Jeremiah with Matthew, unto Jesus the Lover of mankind and Savior of our souls!
*Glory: Tone 6.*
Come, O ye faithful, let us bless the trinity of venerable fathers: the one as having received the gift of healings, the other as filled with true prophecy, and the other as clairvoyant of unseen things; honoring the day of whose feast, we glorify our God, and we pray that He grant peace unto the world and save our souls!
*Both now... Theotokion:*
O Theotokos, thou art the true vine [Woodcut Engraving: The Venerable Nikola Sviatosha, Prince of Chernihiv, in monastic vesture; below: Dormition of the Venerable Nikola Sviatosha; The Fathers visit the brethren upon his dormition]

---

# MONTH OF OCTOBER: ON THE 14TH DAY

*Memory of Our Venerable Father Nikola Sviatosha, Prince of Chernihiv:*
*At Vespers, Blessed is the man: the 1st antiphon.*
*On "Lord, I have cried": Stichera, on 6. Tone 8. Podoben: "O Most Marvellous Wonder" (Ѽ пресла́внагѡ чꙋдесѐ):*
O God-wise, blessed Nikola: seeing that the fashion of this world passes away, and that all things are subject to corruption, thou didst quickly exchange the fashion of thy glory, didst leave behind the diadem and the sceptre, and looking toward the Heavenly Kingdom, didst choose to walk the narrow path; and now dwelling in the heavenly mansions *To Nikola Sviatosha.*
...dwelling in the heavenly mansions, pray unto Christ God to grant us the blessedness of the everlasting Kingdom.
O venerable Nikola: desiring according to thy name to acquire a victorious life also, thou didst conquer this world, didst leave behind the throne of princely rule, and didst count wealth, glory, and ease as refuse; and clothed in the poor monastic habit, didst follow Christ Who became poor for our sake: Whom now standing before, pray diligently for us, that He grant peace and great mercy unto our souls!
O holy father Nikola most blessed: foreseeing with thy noetic eye that kings and princes are deprived of their glory and go down naked into the grave, thou didst fear the fragile authority of this age, following in the footsteps of Christ, for in humility thou didst serve Him; therefore didst thou obtain unplundered blessedness, where remember us also who honor thy memory!
*Glory: Tone 2.*
Foreseeing Thy Kingdom, O Christ God, and Thy dominion which abides unto the ages, Thy favorite Nikola cast away the sceptre and diadem, and departed from manifold cares, fleeing unto the tranquil life of monastics: he became a true and faithful servant, having served Thee, his Savior, in holiness and righteousness.
*Both now... Dogmatic Theotokion: The shadow of the law hath passed away... Then, the Entrance; Prokeimenon of the day; and 3 Readings for Venerable Ones (see fol. 1v).*[^30]
*At the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 1. Podoben: "Of the Heavenly Ranks" (Небе́сныхъ чинѡ́въ):*
Kindled with divine desire, ever-memorable father Nikola, thou didst despise worldly glory, and having loved the monastic life, wast of one mind and manner with the venerable fathers; with whom now dwelling, pray for those who honor thy dormition!
*Verse: Blessed is the man that fears the Lord.* (Ps. 111:1 [LXX])[^31]
Thou didst wholly enslave thyself unto Christ, winging all thy desire toward Him; for the sake of Whose love thou didst renounce this world, having loved Him Who loved the human race: therefore we bless thee with hymns, and with love venerate the shrine of thy relics.
*Verse: Blessed are all they that fear the Lord.* (Ps. 127:1 [LXX])[^32]
O thou who lookest upon all thy children who now honor thee and the people who guard thee: disappoint not our hope in thy protection, O father, but mightily intercede for all, and assist in perils and tribulations, granting what is profitable through thy prayers!

*Service to the Venerable One.*
*Glory: Tone 5.*
O venerable father, thou gavest no bound unto thy repose until thou hadst cleansed the passions of thy heart: fulfilling Christ's commandments, thou didst abandon home, kinsmen, and friends; fleeing unto the tranquil haven of the Mother of God, thou didst bring low all thy greatness and glory beneath the feet of all; thou didst blossom in fasting, didst mortify the body, and being ever directed by the Spirit of God, didst show thyself a son and heir of the Ever-Existing Light: where now dwelling, pray that we also may find mercy on the day of judgment.
*Both now... Theotokion:*
O thou who art full of grace, intercede through thy prayers, and entreat for our souls a multitude of compassions and the cleansing of our many transgressions, we pray thee.
*At Matins, on "God is the Lord": Troparion, Tone 2:*
Having abandoned the fatherland and the glory of thy princely rule, thou didst eagerly follow Christ, the Prince of humility, O our father Nikola; therefore didst thou receive from Him an everlasting kingdom and glory in Heaven, where rejoicing, remember us who honor thy memory with faith!
*Glory: Both now... Theotokion: All beyond comprehension...*
*Following the 1st Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 4. Podoben: "Thy Tomb" (Гро́бъ тво́й):*
The Russian people are filled with gladness, and thy memory makes joyful, O blessed one, having thee as a speedy helper in afflictions and deliverance in all sicknesses; therefore, as having received grace from God to drive away diseases by a word, disappoint us not, and assist even now all who honor thy venerable dormition.
*Glory: Both now... Theotokion:*
Knowing thee to be a fountain of healings, O Virgin, in warmth of heart we run unto thee, thy sinful servants: do thou, having motherly care for us, forget not those afflicted with painful tribulations; but stretching forth thy hands, pray unto thy Son, that we may receive speedy relief from them.
*Following the 2nd Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 4. Podoben: "Thou hast Appeared" (Ꙗ҆ви́лся є҆сѝ):*
Having hated the passing things in the light of earth, O blessed one, and fervently loved the ever-existing things in Heaven, which after thy many labors thou didst inherit: where dwelling, pray ever unto Christ for all of us who honor thy memory in hymns.

*To Nikola Sviatosha.*
*Glory: Both now... Theotokion:*
By thy holy childbirth, O Virgin, thou didst renew the nature of earthborn men that was corrupted by passions, and didst raise it from death unto incorruptible life: therefore, as is our duty, O pure Mother, we ever bless thee, as being blessed by all generations of the faithful.
*Following the Polyeleos, Sessional Hymn, Tone 4. Podoben: "Being Commanded Mystically" (Пове́лѣнное та́йнѡ):*
Let thy holy forefather rejoice today, the sovereign Vladimir sitting upon the height of the throne, for from his root a good branch hast thou budded forth, O most blessed Nikola: bearing upon thyself spiritual fruit as an example unto all who wish to be saved and to follow Christ in humility, even as thou didst, O venerable father; and now as thou rejoicest in glory, the fruit of thy monastic labors abides unto the ages.
*Glory: Both now... Theotokion:*
Let David the hymnographer rejoice today, for from his root hath grown up for us the noetic tree, the Virgin Mary, bearing the fruit, Christ our God: by Whom the ends of the whole world are nourished and become partakers of everlasting life; therefore do all we the faithful devoutly bless her as Mother of God.
*Sticheron following the Gospel, Tone 6:*
Great was the humility of thy life, O our father; for having left behind all earthly things—lineage and the throne of thy princely rule—thou didst love Christ alone, and serving Him with a pure conscience, didst live angelically upon earth: thou didst drive away demonic legions, having ascended unto the height of virtues. Blessed art thou who didst so work for Christ, and we, having known thy labors, bless thee with love!
*CANON*
*To the Venerable One.*
*Tone 4. Ode 1. Heirmos:*
*I shall open my mouth, and it will be filled with the Spirit, and I shall pour forth a word unto the Queen Mother, and I shall appear radiantly keeping festival, and rejoicing I shall sing her wonders.*
*Troparia:*
Open my lips, O my Christ, send down the Paraclete, that I may be able to sing the versification of Thy favorite, the height of virtues, unto which in purity of heart he ascended through humility.
In truth wast thou named a favorite, for being full of holiness, thou didst depart from every irreverent action, and having winged thy mind toward the vision of God, didst know all fragile things in the world to be transient.
Thou didst remember those who were before thee: from how great a glory they vainly fell away, and were harvested with dishonor by the sickle of death: from which that thou mightest be delivered *Service to the Venerable One.*
...that thou mightest be delivered, thou didst abandon the princely throne on earth, and didst receive in Heaven a throne that abides ever with the saints.
*Glory:*
Thou didst emulate blessed Joasaph of India; for as he abandoned an earthly kingdom and sought the heavenly, so also didst thou, O blessed one, having abandoned the burdensome princely rule, receive an everlasting one.
*Both now... Theotokion:*
Deprive me not of the Kingdom on high, O Sovereign Lady Who rulest over heavenly and earthly beings; for seeking this, I have departed, being conquered by the passions that ever war against me; yet unto thee do I flee with unwavering hope.
*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
*Establish spiritually Thy hymnographers, O Theotokos, living and ungrudging fountain, who have formed a choir for thee; in thy divine glory count them worthy of crowns of glory.*
*Troparia:*
That thou mightest be a servant of God alone, thou didst not wish to serve the world, departing from which, like a second Abraham, thou didst leave behind thy home and land; and having come, as into Haran, unto the most honored Pechersk monastery of the Mother of God, thou didst see in active deed the actions and visions of the blessed fathers ascending unto Heaven.
Having once been master of thine own princely realm, for the love of thy Lord thou didst make thyself a servant, serving the servants of Christ in the kitchen: that thou mightest follow Him Who for our sake was obedient unto death.
Clothed in purple and holding a sceptre in hand, thou wast not ashamed to carry firewood upon thy shoulders and to chop it with thine own hands: that thou mightest yield all thy members in servitude unto the Master on high, from Whom thou didst receive as reward everlasting life.
*Glory:*
Living in turbulent worldly wisdom, thy brothers, O blessed one, thought thy work in the kitchen to be dishonorable; therefore they tried to pervert thee, that thou shouldest leave this behind; but loving this obedience, thou didst ask for thyself yet another year to abide therein.
*Both now... Theotokion:*
Persuade me, O my Sovereign Lady, who obey not the commandments of my Lord nor preserve the rules of obedience, to work them; and strengthen me in the remaining time to amend myself completely in both: lest having finished my life in slothfulness, I receive punishment according to my deeds with the unprofitable servants.
*Sessional Hymn, Tone 8:*
As a true hearer of the Gospel word, a lover of monks, and an emulator of the venerable, come, O brethren, with hymnographic praises let us sing of blessed Nikola: for he enslaved his body *To Nikola Sviatosha.*
...enslaved his body unto God, that he might free the soul from passions; and having shone forth like the sun with the rays of his virtues, he illumines all of us who sing: Glory to God Who calls His servants unto everlasting salvation!
*Glory: Both now... Theotokion:*
Show me to be a citizen of the Kingdom on high, O pure Virgin, who flee unto thy protection and ask of thee warm intercession: that having found grace through thee, I may sing: Glory to God Who saves the human race!
*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
*He Who sits in glory upon the throne of the Godhead, in a light cloud, Jesus the Most Divine, is come with an incorruptible hand, and hath saved those who cry aloud: Glory to Thy power, O Christ!*
*Troparia:*
Formerly sitting in glory upon a throne, with nobles standing before thee, thou wast not ashamed, O blessed one, to sit at the gates of the toll-gatherers for three years, faithfully guarding their entrance against those wandering idly.
Whereas formerly at table those fond of delicacies stood before thee, and many servants served thee as a lord: afterward thou didst not abhor making thyself a servant unto the servants of Christ, serving them and setting forth food given by God.
As one who has ascended by steps unto the height of noetic glory has repose after labors: so also didst thou, O venerable one, by the counsel of the abbot and brethren having passed through all obediences in good order, wast constrained to live in silence with peace in the cell, and to take heed unto thy salvation.
*Glory:*
As a labor-loving servant multiplying the talent for his Master, thou didst give no rest unto thy much-labored body, but with thine own hands didst plant an orchard: and the brethren enjoyed the fruits of thy labors.
*Both now... Theotokion:*
Do thou, O Theotokos, arouse me who am sluggish unto virtuous struggle, and grant me to plant a spiritual orchard with noetic trees, to water it with tears of compunction, and to bring forth a hundredfold fruit unto my Master, that I also with His faithful servants may receive from His hands the reward of my labors.
*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
*All creation was amazed at thy divine glory: for thou, O Virgin who knewest not wedlock, didst hold in thy womb the God of all, and didst bear the Timeless Son, granting peace unto all who hymn thee.*
*Troparia:*
All marveled at thy perseverance, O blessed one, for thou wast never seen idle, but hadst work in thy hands, and in thy lips ever the Jesus Prayer: and by this didst thou wound the demon who attacks through slothfulness.

*Service to the Venerable One.*
That thou mightest become a partaker of the heavenly food, thou didst cast away the love of delicacies: for thou didst taste of nothing else, save only a little of the common food at the appointed time at table; and thus didst thou preserve the purity of soul and body.
After thy many monastic labors, thou didst receive a gift from God, to heal diseases by a single word and to assist in afflictions: I pray thee, change also my sickness into health speedily!
*Glory:*
With craftily woven nets the enemy sought to turn thy purchased soul from the good path of labor-loving, through the malice of a certain physician who had once served thee; but with patience thou didst persuade him that he should take heed not unto thee, but unto his own salvation.
*Both now... Theotokion:*
In heart and soul have I gone astray, and cast away the grace given me, walking in unbecoming paths: but before I fall into the hands of the enemies, do thou, O Mother of God, direct me unto my former blessedness: for I am a sheep of thy saved fold.
*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
*Celebrating this divine and all-honored feast of the Mother of God, come, O God-wise, let us clap our hands, and glorify God Who was born of her.*
*Troparia:*
Reaching forth with divine desire toward the love of God, thou didst sweetly endure all afflictions that came upon thee, O blessed one, considering what was spoken: that the sufferings of this present time are not worthy to be compared with the glory that shall be revealed in us.[^33]
When Peter the physician urged thee to cease from monastic labors, chastely didst thou answer many times, O holy one: "Cease turning me away from my humble endeavor; for neither affliction, nor famine, nor distress shall separate me from the love of Christ,[^34] for Whose sake I endure all things that come upon me!"
That thou mightest find Christ the Everlasting Life, thou didst mortify thy body with much abstinence and labor, and wast not deprived of what thou didst desire: for behold, thou now livest with the saints in the heavenly courts!
*Glory:*
Having Christ as the only Physician, thou didst never permit thyself to be medically treated in sickness, but didst say with the Apostle: *For to me to live is Christ, and to die is gain;*[^35] and by a word didst thou thyself heal others of their diseases.
*Both now... Theotokion:*
By thy visitation heal my soul that faints from many sins, O Sovereign Lady, and water me with the water of thy grace: that being freed from spiritual and bodily diseases, I may ever work for thee, my Sovereign Lady, with zeal.

*To Nikola Sviatosha.*
*Kontakion, Tone 4:*
Having counted all the good things of this world and corruptible riches as nothing, thou didst enrich thyself with many wonders and signs from Christ God: standing before Whom in gladness, remember us who honor thy memory with love, that we may cry unto thee: Rejoice, O Nikola most marvelous!
*Ikos:*
Unto thee who didst live angelically upon earth, and now rejoicest in the heavens with the angelic ranks in the everlasting light, O blessed one, we offer these praises in hymns:
Rejoice, thou who didst despise nobility of lineage and princely rule!
Rejoice, thou who didst trample upon riches and vainglory!
Rejoice, poor and needy one, who, leaving all, didst follow the Lord!
Rejoice, thou who didst cast down pride and cleave unto humility!
Rejoice, thou who didst pass through all obediences for many years!
Rejoice, thou who didst abide in them with all humility!
Rejoice, thou who didst not abhor the kitchen and the gate!
Rejoice, thou who didst reject living with thy brothers, the princes!
Rejoice, thou who didst thyself help in infirmity, and didst instruct the physician!
Rejoice, thou who through thy prayers didst intercede for the salvation of the physician, and didst foresee thine own death and his!
Rejoice, thou who after thy death didst bestow healing upon thy brother!
Rejoice, marvelous and speedy helper unto all who call upon thee warmly!
Rejoice, O Nikola most marvelous!
*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
*The God-wise ones served not the creature rather than the Creator; but manfully trampling upon the threat of fire, they rejoiced, singing: Blessed art Thou, O all-hymned Lord and God of our fathers!*
*Troparia:*
"From how great a glory hast thou departed, O prince!" Peter said unto thee, and reproached thee, O blessed one, saying that thou wast working for the servants and drawing near unto death; but thou didst answer him: "I have not destroyed my honor, but have rather been glorified by these things, following Christ Who came to serve all, unto Whom also I cry: Blessed is the God of our fathers!"
When Peter wished to heal thee in sickness, he himself fell sick, whom thou didst heal not with herbs, but with prayer, and didst instruct him not to hope in the art of physicians, but to trust only in God, crying: Blessed art Thou, O all-hymned God of our fathers!
Completing thine obedience among the brethren, thou didst foresee thine own end after three months: therefore thou didst call Peter, and didst persuade him to take upon himself the monastic habit in thy place, and to serve God instead of thee, Who is blessed unto the ages.

*Service to the Venerable One.*
Having heard of the departure of thy life, Peter fell down with tears at thy feet, O venerable one, weeping that he was being deprived of his master; but thou, having raised him up, didst comfort him, and didst promise that he should not sorrow over this, but cry: Blessed is the God of our fathers!
*Both now... Theotokion:*
Having no amendment of my life on account of foul deeds, who shall not bewail me? For I depart on an unknown path; I have turned aside from the way of the commandments of thy Son. Therefore with tears I call upon thee, O Sovereign Lady: show me the way, and lead me in truth, as I sing: All-hymned art thou, O Theotokos, and blessed from generation to generation!
*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
*The childbirth of the Theotokos saved the pious children in the furnace: then prefigured, but now in deed accomplished; it stirs up the whole world to sing unto Thee: Praise the Lord, O ye works, and exalt Him above all unto all ages.*
*Troparia:*
Having lived in pious wisdom, and noetically foreseeing thy departure, O venerable Nikola, thou didst prepare for thyself a place of repose, which thou didst bestow upon Peter at his supplication, saying: "Be it unto thee, if thou wilt, and even as the Lord wills, Who is exalted above all unto the ages!"
Having boldness before God, and unwavering hope in God, thou didst receive the gift of clairvoyance, through which knowing that after three days Peter would depart unto God, thou didst command him to prepare for departure; and according to thy word he arose, having received the Holy Mysteries, and departed rejoicing and singing: Exalted is God above all unto the ages!
Having lived for thirty years after the death of that physician, and having faithfully served the Lord, thou didst pour forth as a fountain fruits, with which thou didst depart, O blessed one, unto the reposed fathers,[^36] singing unto the Lord, and exalting Him above all unto the ages.
*We bless the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit: the Lord.*
Having heard of thy glorious and honored dormition, the people gathered from the whole city to give thee the last kiss, and they buried thee with tears, who wast borne with the joy of angels into the bosom of Abraham, that there with all the saints thou mayest stand before the Lord unto the ages.
*Both now... Theotokion:*
Temporal life is drawing to a close; woe is me! How shall I be presented, the most wretched of all men? The Judge waits, the books are opened, and according to my deeds it is certain that I must expect to inherit evils; yet before I depart hence, together with Thine obedient favorite Nikola, pray diligently unto Thy Son that I may inherit a portion of the good things!

*To Nikola Sviatosha.*
*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
*Let every earthborn mortal leap for joy, enlightened by the Spirit, and let the nature of the bodiless minds celebrate, honoring the sacred festival of the Mother of God, and cry aloud: Rejoice, O all-blessed Theotokos, pure Ever-Virgin!*
*Troparia:*
Let every earthborn mortal imitate the life of the God-praised father; for he despised temporal glory upon earth, and having now entered into eternal glory he rejoices, having an immovable throne in the heavenly mansions, where he also prays for us, that we may be heirs of the blessed life.
"Whosoever loves Me, follows Me, that he may be with Me": having heard these divine words,[^37] O most blessed father, having loved God, thou didst follow Him, and didst acquire nothing in the world above His love; therefore now thou livest with Christ in unapproachable light.
He Who said: "Them that glorify Me will I glorify,"[^38] fulfilled the promise upon thee, O father; for not only did He glorify thee thyself with gift-bestowals, but also thy cross, headrest, and hairshirt are glorified by thy brother, and remembered even unto this day, for glorious things were wrought through them.
*Glory:*
From generation to generation thy memory is glorified, O venerable father; the Church praises thy labors and ascetic deeds together with us, and the shrine containing thy relics in the holy cave reveals them, and convokes all to pray unto thee now, as a fervent intercessor and protector in our infirmity.
*Both now... Theotokion:*
Glorified by all generations, and honored by the All-Holy Trinity, unto us who praise thee in hymns, O pure Virgin, be an intercessor and a speedy protector against the assaults of enemies, that we may ever sing unto thee: Rejoice, O Theotokos, Ever-Virgin!
*Exapostilarion:*
