Thou wast a temple of the All-Holy Spirit, and the Father coming with the Son made an abode in thee, and through communion in the divine things showed thee to be a citizen of paradise: wherein now being nourished from the fertile tree, remember also us who with faith venerate the shrine of thy relics, O venerable father!
*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
A most holy temple wast thou, O Virgin, unto the Word Who was incarnate of thee; alas, make me, a temple of great sinning, to be a temple of the lawful commandments of thy Son through thine intercession, that having become a dwelling of God's grace, I may worthily hymn thee, as one who canst do all things through God, O Maiden of God!

*Service to the Venerable One.*
*At the Praises, Stichera, Tone 1. Podoben: "Of the Heavenly Ranks" (Небе́сныхъ чинѡ́въ):*
The great accomplishments of the fasting labors of the great Caves fathers drew thee, O blessed Nikola, unto the fasting company, whose life thou didst zealously emulate; and now living with them in Heaven, pray for those who hymn thee. *(Twice)*
Upon the holy mountain, where the Mother of God placed her abode, thou didst blossom like a leafy palm tree; by the branches of thy labors, cover from affliction all who call upon thee for help, and grant healing from thy fruits unto those who piously honor thy most honorable memory, O all-blessed Nikola!
Today at thy memory, O venerable one, the Caves Lavra rejoices, and the honorable assembly of monastics, having come unto thy tomb, spiritually keeps festival in psalms, praising God Who dwells in unapproachable light and is praised in the saints, with whom thou also now livest, receiving the reward from Him.
*Glory: Tone 2:*
Being a goodly branched tree, like a cedar in Lebanon,[^39] thou wast planted in the house of the Mother of God, O most blessed father Nikola, and nourished at the living waters from the teachings of the Caves fathers; by thy shade overshadow the people who flee unto thee, protect them from barbarian invasions, casting down their uprisings; for the Lord set thee as prince in the land of Rus'; therefore falling down we pray: preserve the realm of Rus' in peace, and guard well this monastery by thy prayers!
*Both now... Theotokion:*
Save thy servants from perils, O most holy Theotokos Virgin; for next to God we all flee unto thee, as unto an unbreakable wall and intercession. *The Great Doxology, and Dismissal.*

[Woodcut Engraving: The Venerable Nestor the Chronicler of the Kyiv Caves writing at a desk; a monastic brother reading in the foreground; Kyiv-Pechersk Lavra buildings visible through the window]
*In the Month of October, on the 27th Day:*
*Service to the Venerable Nestor the Chronicler of the Caves.*
*At Vespers, on "Lord, I have cried", Stichera, Tone 4. Podoben: "As One Valiant among Martyrs" (Ꙗ҆́кѡ до́блѧ):*
Having recorded the times and years of memorable deeds, the ascetic struggles and labors of the God-bearing fathers, O blessed Nestor, thou didst burn with love for noetic desire, and having shown thyself a good disciple, thou didst follow in the footsteps of the founders; and while yet in this life thou didst choose *Service to the Venerable One.*
...the good portion, which is also with thee now: make us also partakers thereof by thy prayers, who zealously hymn thy memory.
Having unfeignedly written in a book the writings of divine words and the ascetic struggles of those who lived angelically, thou didst write in the book of thy heart the memory of the future recompense; therefore thou hast thy name written in the Book of Life. But, O most blessed father, pray for us, that we also may be found written in the Book of Life, who honor the commemoration of thy memory.
Desiring to possess the understanding of spiritual wisdom, O venerable one, thou didst come unto the great fathers, from whom thou didst learn heavenly comprehensions; therefore having lived angelically in the flesh, thou didst shine forth as a noetic morning; and now being in the day and a partaker of the light, cease not to pray diligently, that those who hymn thy most honorable memory with faith may be delivered from snares and perils.
*Glory: Tone 6:*
Having prepared thy heart as a tabernacle for the Holy Spirit, O Nestor our father, thou didst adorn thy soul with purity and humility, with the choice of voluntary poverty, with vigilance, obedience, and unceasing supplication; and with the reed of thy chastity thou didst write the God-befitting lives of the original Caves fathers; having unceasing desire toward God, thou didst find Him Whom thou didst seek. And now, as thou hast boldness before God, pray that our souls may be saved.
*Both now... Theotokion or Stavrotheotokion.*
*At the Aposticha, Stichera from the Octoechos.*
*Glory: Tone 5:*
Thou didst fulfill the prophetic saying, O venerable Nestor, proclaiming unto us by writing the works of God, saying with David in the psalm: "I remembered the works of the Lord, and Thy wonders from the beginning, O God,"[^40] which recount the things that took place in the beginning of the monastery of Thy Mother; make known unto the latter generation Thy power in the venerable ones: having learned these things from thy writings, O most blessed one, we kiss thy labors, we bless thy memory with hymns, and unto Him Who is in the highest we offer psalmody: "Thou art the God Who workest wonders!"[^41]
*Both now... Theotokion according to the tone, or Stavrotheotokion.*
*Canon to the Same.*
*Tone 4. Ode 1. Heirmos:*
*The choirs of Israel with unwetted feet passed through the Red Sea and the watery deep; and beholding the enemy captains and horsemen submerged therein, with gladness they sang: Let us sing unto our God, for He is glorified!*
*Troparia:*
O monastic choirs, in the yearly memory of the God-bearing father hasten zealously unto the holy cave, and venerating with love the shrine of the relics of Nestor wise in God, sing unto God, for He is gloriously glorified in His saints!

*To Nestor the Chronicler.*
Unto me, who unite myself unto the monastic choirs and dare this day to hymn thy memory, though I am more sinful than all men, grant help through thy prayers, O holy Nestor; for God is marvelously glorified in thee!
Desiring to be numbered among the angelic choirs, thou didst join thyself, O father, unto the choirs of those who lived like angels in the flesh; taught by them the angelic life, thou didst ascend unto noetic desire; therefore do we honor thee, for God is marvelously glorified in thee!
*Both now... Theotokion:*
O ye choirs of the saints and venerable fathers, standing together with the angelic choirs, glorify her who bore the Word Who is holier than all the holy; and as ye glorify the Mother of God today, help also us earthborn mortals; for she is marvelously glorified by all!
*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
*The bow of the mighty hath grown weak, and the weak have girded themselves with strength; therefore my heart is established in the Lord.*
*Troparia:*
The resolve of thy mind was drawn like a bow, O father, for living in this vale of weeping, thou didst ever have an ascent in thy heart unto God; help also me, who lead my life ever in sorrows, O Nestor, pray for me, that my heart may rejoice in the Lord!
Being seventeen years of age, having embraced poverty, thou didst lofty-mindedly receive the stature of a perfect man; for thou didst shine forth as with the gray hairs of old age through good deeds; direct these things also in me, O blessed one, and my heart shall rejoice in the Lord!
*Glory:*
Thou didst acquire profound purity and humility, O father, as two wings unto the mountain of monastic virtues, whence having observed that the fashion of this world passes away in the twinkling of an eye, thou didst wing thy heart all the more into the love of God.
*Both now... Theotokion:*
Having given birth unto the Orchard of purity, by His fruit thou hast richly nourished those who believe thee to be the Mother of God; unto me also who with faith bow down before thee, O unwedded Bride of God, grant to taste at least a little of this fruit, that my sorrowful heart may rejoice!
*Sessional Hymn, Tone 4:*
Clad in virtuous ways, and having in youth partaken in a God-knowing manner of all things, thou didst mortify the lusts of the flesh by strict fasting; thou didst quicken the spirit through hope in eternal good things; having lived angelically upon earth, thou now dwellest with the angels; therefore do we honor thee, O blessed Nestor!

*Service to the Venerable One.*
*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
An ineffable image of fearful wonders do I behold in thee, O most pure Virgin, for thou didst conceive God, and didst contain the Incomprehensible One in thy womb; but how this mystery came to pass, I examine not, but rather believe, and with fear bless thee, crying: Rejoice, spacious dwelling of the uncontainable God!
*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
*I have heard of Thy glorious dispensation, O Christ God, that Thou wast born of the Virgin, to deliver from deception those who cry: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!*
*Troparia:*
Having heard the glorious things handed down on the Caves mountain, thou didst come thither, O venerable one; and seeing the grace of God descending with fire and dew at the beginning of the founding of the church, thou didst cry aloud with fear: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!
Making a greater and more perfect tabernacle in thy soul for the Holy Spirit, by the affectionate reception of the commandments from the God-bearing fathers thou didst gild it, O father Nestor; therefore, as into a beautiful bridal chamber, the Father with the Son and the Spirit came and made an abode in thee.
*Glory:*
Having received the teaching of the God-bearing fathers like sweet milk, thou didst fulfill the word in deed, O blessed one, and wast filled with wisdom from on high; reading which also in thy writings, we gladden our souls with the knowledge of God's grace that came to pass on these hills.
*Both now... Theotokion:*
Sweet milk of repentance, and heavenly nectar of compunction, dost thou grant, O pure Maiden, unto those who bring hymns with love unto thee; which I also, gripped with fear, yet moved festive-wise, bring unto thee, O Sovereign Lady, and pray: sweeten my heart, O thou who didst bear my sweet Jesus Christ!
*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
*O Thou Who didst spread out the light, the firstborn light, so that in the light the works hymn Thee, O Christ Creator: in Thy light guide our paths!*
*Troparia:*
Having removed thyself from the love of the deceptive world, with divine desire thou didst seek, O Nestor, to follow the footsteps, ways, and teachings of the great fathers; grant that we also may cleave unto these, and in the light of their deeds, through thy prayers, guide our paths!
Encompassed with deep humility, O venerable one, thou didst cast down and trample underfoot the high-walking enemy of vainglory; through thine intercession before God, O father, grant humility also unto us, and in it guide our paths!
*Glory:*
Having directed thy life angelically, all regarded thee as an angel of God; therefore Thou wast counted worthy to receive the rank of the diaconate *To Nestor the Chronicler.*
...to receive the rank of the diaconate, wherein having well accomplished thy ministry, guide also our paths in truth, O Nestor!
*Both now... Theotokion:*
Having borne the Way of life, O all-holy Virgin, as one compassionate thou guidest onto paths of righteousness those walking on the paths of temptations; and for me who go astray, having given a good guide unto the wandering, in His light guide my paths!
*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
*When I was afflicted, I cried unto the Lord, and the God of my salvation heard me.*
*Troparia:*
Having ordered thy rank in holiness and righteousness, thou didst make thyself wholly a temple for the Holy Spirit; and having entered into the temple not made with hands, thou standest before the throne of the Most High: help me also who desire to be there, by thy prayers, O father, I pray thee, the suppliant of my salvation!
Thou wast a beautiful bud of the celestial Cultivator of the beautiful garden; for adorned with beautiful deeds, thou didst bring forth good fruits, whose fragrance of grace we now perceive; I pray thee therefore, O blessed one: grant them as a gift unto me also from God my Savior!
*Glory:*
From the labor of thine ascetic struggles, O venerable Nestor, having shed many drops of sweat, thou didst water the field of thy soul, and having grown up the sheaves of virtues, thou now reapest sheaves, living unto the ages in the heavenly mansions: pray Christ my Savior that I also may be therein!
*Both now... Theotokion:*
O holy land watered by the morning Spirit, rejoice, for thou didst sprout forth for us a tree of goodly leaf-shade; bring me who am scorched by the heat of passions beneath its shade, O Sovereign Lady, I pray thee, as the mediatress of my salvation!
*Kontakion, Tone 2:*
Being a disciple of the God-bearing Theodosius, and a true imitator of his life, Thou wast counted worthy to be the first eyewitness of his honorable relics; which having translated with the others in God-befitting manner, Thou didst inherit with them the Heavenly Kingdom: pray the Lord that we also who honor Thee may obtain it!
*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
*The Abrahamic children in Babylon of old trampled upon the flame of the furnace, crying aloud with hymns: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!*
*Troparia:*
Following in Abraham's footsteps, O holy one, by the fulfillment of the commandments thou didst offer a pure mind unto the Master, as he did Isaac; therefore departing from the distress of this life, thou didst pass into the land flowing with endless sweetness: where pray that I also may be, O Nestor, companion of our God-bearing fathers!

*Service to the Venerable One.*
Having departed from this passionate world, thou didst enter into the passionless world, where living in peace with the sons of peace, depart not from us who are in this world, praying unto thy Creator that we may be fellow partakers with thee, singing: Blessed art Thou, O God!
Thou didst not incline thy heart unto words of malice in this tumultuous life, O venerable one; therefore, having untroubledly passed through the malice of the aerial spirits, thou didst reach the heavenly gates: which hasten to open also unto us by thy prayers, for thou art blessed.
*Both now... Theotokion:*
I turned aside from the path of righteousness, and walked not with my feet on the right ways, but wandering over the precipices of passions, I was bound fast, captive by mine endurance of sin; yet even limping I run unto thy goodness, and pray thee, O Sovereign Lady: heal me, for thou art the medicine of my soul!
*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
*In Babylon the children, kindled with zeal through strength, manfully trampled upon the tormentor and the threat of the flame, and cast into the midst of the fire, being bedewed, they sang: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!*
*Troparia:*
Strengthened with spiritual power, O God-wise one, Thou didst fly with the wings of thy virtues unto the things on high, and by their flight Thou now livest where years fail not: pray that we also may attain thither, who sing: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!
From the house of the Mother of God thou didst pass, O Nestor, into the house of God, the Jerusalem that contains Him, and didst dwell as a fellow citizen with the angels in the number of heavenly citizens; intercede for us also, that we may be numbered with them, who sing unto the Lord in hymns at Thy memory.
*We bless the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit: the Lord.*
O Trinity, one Nature: Father, Son, and Holy Spirit, one Kingdom, having power and authority: deliver us from the power of the prince of darkness, through the prayers of our God-bearing Caves fathers, with whom we also bring Nestor today in supplication unto Thee, blessing Thee the Lord unto the ages!
*Both now... Theotokion:*
Standing before the throne of the Trinity, O Mother and Virgin, and ever beholding the glory of the Ever-Existing Godhead: pray for us sinners, that we also may not be deprived of heavenly glory, who praise thee in hymns, and the Lord Who was born of thee!
*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
*Thy childbirth was shown to be incorruptible: God came forth from thy womb, as a flesh-bearer He appeared upon earth and lived with men; therefore, O Theotokos, we all magnify thee.*
*Troparia:*
Thou hast departed into the mansions of the righteous, and in spirit standest before the Ever-Existing Light *To Nestor the Chronicler.*
...standest before the Ever-Existing Light: look down therefore upon us who dwell in the villages of this tumultuous world, and fight for those who are in battles, O blessed Nestor; for now with hymns we magnify thee!
With those with whom thou didst acquire love while living in the body, with them also having departed in spirit thou dwellest inseparably, as thy relics show, O venerable one, lying with the saints in the cave; stand with them praying unto God for us who magnify thy memory!
*Glory:*
The Lord works mercy and truth: and do thou, O most blessed one, imitating Him, be a merciful intercessor for our salvation, that for thy sake we also may find grace and not fall away from eternal glory, as we magnify thy holy dormition with hymns.
*Both now... Theotokion:*
The Master of all accepts thine intercession, O most pure Virgin, and receives thy prayers for the human race: pray therefore also for us thy servants, O all-pure one, that we may obtain mercy on the day of judgment; having thee as mediatress, we bow down and magnify thee!
*At the Aposticha, Stichera from the Octoechos.*
*Glory: Tone 2:*
By preparatory monastic actions, O venerable one, thou didst make thyself an honorable vessel of the Holy Spirit, from Whom thou didst receive inexhaustible grace to fight for all who flee unto thee with faith in afflictions. And now unto us who fall down before the shrine of thy relics, be an unceasing helper, invisibly warring against those who war against us, and pray the Creator of all and God to grant us forgiveness of sins and to obtain mercy on the day of judgment, who bless thy memory, O God-wise Nestor!
*Both now... Theotokion or Stavrotheotokion.*

---

# MONTH OF NOVEMBER

[Woodcut Engraving: The Venerable Varlaam, Abbot of the Kyiv Caves, standing in monastic habits surrounded by scenes of his life: his tonsure, his wife entreating him, his father beating him, and his holy repose]

*On the 19th Day: Service to Our Venerable Father Varlaam, Abbot of the Caves.*
*At Small Vespers, Stichera, Tone 5. Podoben: "Rejoice, O Container of Ascetics" (Ра́дꙋйсѧ по́стникѡвъ):*
O venerable father Varlaam, having taken the yoke of Christ upon thy shoulders, thou didst manfully endure the assault of the enemy, and having traversed the narrow way without turning back, thou didst attain the reward of the high calling; but as of old, O praise of monastics, unceasingly pray Christ to grant great mercy unto our souls!
O God-wise father Varlaam, having loved Christ from thy youth, and having enlightened thy heart with the divine rays of the All-Holy Spirit, thou wast truly radiant; therefore fulfill the petitions for profitable salvation of those who run unto thy temple, and pray Christ God to grant great mercy unto us!
O father Varlaam, having taken up thy cross upon thy shoulder, and having affectionately traversed the beautiful journey of Christ thy King, thou hast stood before those who dwell in the holy heavenly city; whence look down now upon those who honor thine ascetic struggles, O father, and pray Christ God that He may guide our steps into the way of peace!
*Glory: Tone 8:*
Having adorned thy life with much abstinence, O venerable one, thou didst show the way leading into life by the acquisition of virtues; having rejected the world, and despised all the beautiful things in the world, by a severe life thou didst wear away thy flesh, and by humility didst trample upon the proud serpent; therefore dwelling in Heaven, pray that our souls may be saved.
*Both now... Theotokion.*
*At the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 2. Podoben: "House of Ephratha" (До́ме є҆ѵфра́ѳовъ):*
Thou didst despise the passing life upon earth, O blessed one, that thou mightest obtain the endless life!
*Verse: Blessed is the man that fears the Lord; he shall greatly delight in His commandments.* (Ps. 111:1 [LXX])[^42]
For the sake of the love of Christ, O venerable Varlaam, Thou didst mortify the tormenting mindset of the flesh.

*To Varlaam, Abbot of the Caves.*
*Verse: Precious in the sight of the Lord is the death of His venerable ones.* (Ps. 115:6 [LXX])[^43]
By thy prayers that clothe us, help in afflictions those who affectionately hymn thy memory, O God-blessed one!
*Glory: Tone 2:*
Having first entered into the cave, O venerable one, from thy labors for the sake of virtues thou didst pass unto the heavenly city, and didst attain the repose of the righteous and the rejoicing of the saints; therefore with them pray diligently for all who honor thy memory with faith, to be delivered from every hostile adversity, O God-wise Varlaam!
*Both now... Theotokion.*
*At Great Vespers.*
*Blessed is the man: the 1st antiphon.*
*On "Lord, I have cried", Stichera, Tone 1. Podoben: "Being Called from on High" (Зва́нный свы́ше):*[^44]
Beholding the ever-flowing fountain of honey-sweet words proceeding from the eyes of the great father, O blessed Varlaam, as a hart thirsty for the flowing streams of living waters thou didst come with insatiable love; whence having watered thy soul with compunction, thou didst despise all the beautiful things of this world, and by words didst follow the Word: before Whom now standing with gladness *Service to the Venerable One.*
...standing before Him, pray that our souls may be saved and enlightened!
When the great luminaries hid beneath the confines of the holy cave, thou didst perceive them with noetic eyes, O venerable one; then, meeting them in youth with the light of virtues in thy heart, and kindled with divine fire to dwell together with them, thou didst leave behind the house of thy parents and thy friends, and becamest a fellow partaker with monastics; and now being numbered with the light-bearing choirs in Heaven, enlighten us earthborn mortals with the rays of grace, and grant gifts, praying Christ that our souls may be saved and enlightened!
When the God-blessed father showed unto thee the divine love of the good things to come, and the ever-flowing glory in the celestial bridal chamber, thou didst immediately count earthly good things as refuse: having despised riches, glory, the greatness of thy lineage, beautiful chambers, and thy betrothed bride, thou didst love Christ alone, and imitating His poverty, thou didst traverse the narrow path in ascetic struggles and labors, and after thy repose didst inherit the desired things: enjoying which now, pray unceasingly to grant peace unto the world, and to save our souls!
*Glory: Tone 8:*
Living upon earth in a corruptible composition, thou didst emulate the life of the bodiless hosts, O venerable father Varlaam: by the casting away of thy glory thou didst astonish men, by poverty and abstinence thou didst put the demons to shame, by purity thou didst gladden the angels, and by thy great ascetic struggles thou wast an example unto monastics; therefore worthily do we bless thee, adorned by the Master on high: Whom ever pray, O blessed one, to grant peace unto our EMPRESS,[^45] and great mercy unto our souls!
*Both now... Theotokion: The King of Heaven... The Entrance; Prokeimenon of the day; and 3 Readings for Venerable Ones (see fol. 1v).*
*At the Litiya, Stichera, Tone 1:*
Today the holy cave joyfully keeps festival, possessing thy holy relics, O blessed Varlaam; for they grant health unto those who run with faith unto thy venerable shrine; whence being enlightened, we say: count us worthy of thy gifts, O blessed one, and preserve thy flock by thy prayers, granting salvation unto our souls!
O venerable father Varlaam, having loved from youth the beauty of Christ the Master, and having an unwavering desire toward Him, thou didst leave behind fatherland and lineage, didst hate riches and glory, and following Him alone, didst live like an angel in the cave *To Varlaam, Abbot of the Caves.*
...therefore standing before Him now in Heaven with the angels, with boldness pray for our souls!
*Tone 1:*
Strengthened by the all-holding right hand, O venerable father Varlaam, thou didst acquire abstinence in food, and through unceasing prayer and nocturnal vigilance wast counted worthy to become a fellow partaker with blessed Anthony; with streams of tears thou didst drown all the demonic armies; therefore Christ also, Whom thou didst desire and for Whose sake thou didst bear the heat of the day, glorified thee with the gift of wonders: Whom pray that our souls may be saved!
*Tone 3:*
Come, all ye assemblies of monastics, with psalms and hymns let us piously praise today the honorable festival of the venerable one: who, even though he shone forth later, was counted worthy of the honor of the ancient fathers, and prays Christ that our souls may be saved!
An honorable receptacle of the All-Holy Spirit wast Thou, O venerable Varlaam; for imitating the bodiless hosts while in the flesh, Thou didst emulate their life; therefore Thou didst preserve the crowns of restraint in the right hand; and though Thou hast departed from earth, yet living unto God Thou standest before Him with the saints, and prayest for our souls!
*Glory: Tone 8:*
Having hated the beauties of this world from youth, O venerable one, from the heart thou didst love the One Who is fairer than the sons of men, and cleaving unto Him with all thy soul, as one bodiless upon earth living in a mortal and corruptible body, thou didst obtain the reward of incorruption; and now clearly beholding the Holy Trinity, pray diligently, O venerable one, to grant peace unto the world and save our souls!
*Both now... Theotokion: O Unwedded Virgin...*
*At the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 2. Podoben: "What shall We Call You, O Saints?" (Что́ ва́съ нарече́мъ ст҃і́и):*[^46]
What shall we name thee, O venerable one? An angel existing in the flesh, for thou didst live angelically upon earth? A luminary unto the venerable, for thou wast astonished at their struggles? Thou didst put the demons to shame, having subjected the spirit unto the bodily passions,[^47] and now standing before Christ, pray that our souls may be saved and enlightened!
*Verse: Precious in the sight of the Lord is the death of His venerable ones.* (Ps. 115:6 [LXX])
What shall we proclaim thee, O glorious one? A living pillar, for thou didst abide unshaken in temptations? A river of living water *Service to the Venerable One.*
...pouring forth streams of grace; an excellent instructor of monks, equal in manner unto the bodiless hosts, and a fervent intercessor for our souls!
*Verse: The righteous man shall flourish like a palm tree, and like a cedar in Lebanon shall he be multiplied.* (Ps. 91:13 [LXX])[^48]
What shall we name thee, O father? Instructor of the uninstructed, fountain unto those in sickness, fragrant sprout of the cave, establishment of monks, ever-flowing wellspring of wonders, good ascetic, and speedy intercessor unto all who come unto thee with faith!
*Glory: Tone 8:*
O venerable father, having loved God from youth, thou didst prefer nothing above Him; having escaped the turmoil of the world and of this life, thou didst reach the tranquil haven of the father of the cave, and having settled in it as into a most beautiful bridal chamber, thou didst betroth thy soul unto the Master alone, having pre-purified it with fasting and prayers; therefore thou becamest a beautiful palace of the Holy Trinity, before Whom standing now with gladness, remember those who honor thy most honorable memory!
*Both now... Theotokion: O Theotokos, thou art the true vine...*
*At Matins, on "God is the Lord", Troparion, Tone 4:*
Having left behind the beautiful things of the world and its glory, thou didst run into the cave, where from strength thou didst advance unto strength, and becamest a leader of Christ's flock, O all-blessed Varlaam; which having shepherded well, shepherd also us from all evils, that we may bless thee!
*Glory... Both now... Theotokion according to the tone.*
*Following the 1st Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 5. Podoben: "The Word Co-unoriginate" (Собезнача́льное Сло́во):*
Come, ye assemblies of monastics, and beholding in the fathers the glory of God, open your lips in praise, blessing your Creator and Master Who is glorified in His saints, and sing in psalmody, saying: Unto us this honorable friend of Thine, O Christ God, their principalities are exceedingly strengthened; strengthen them, O Savior and Redeemer of our souls!
*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
My heart is established to hymn, O all-immaculate Virgin, thy most pure childbirth, whereby thou didst deliver us from the ancestral curse, and didst raise fallen human nature unto the height; therefore, as is our duty, we hymn and magnify thee!
*Following the 2nd Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 1. Podoben: "The Angelic Choir" (Ли́къ а҆́гг҃лскїй):*[^49]
Spiritual wings of knowledge and action thou didst receive from the words of the great father, O God-wise Varlaam, and having lifted up thy mind with them unto the height, thou didst escape *To Varlaam, Abbot of the Caves.*
...thou didst escape like a bird from the snare of the hunters, and didst attain the highest blessedness, where the choirs of the righteous abide; glory to Him Who gave thee this true desire; glory to Him Who gave thee victory over the passions; glory to Him Who brought thee into the eternal dwelling!
*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
O pure Virgin who didst receive the Holy Spirit into thy womb, and didst bear the Word begotten of the Father before the ages: bring us, who have made ourselves like unto irrational deeds, unto understanding, and grant direction unto our life, that we may ever magnify thee!
*Following the Polyeleos, Sessional Hymn, Tone 8. Podoben: "Having Ascended the Wisdom" (Премꙋ́дрости возше́дъ):*[^50]
Having enlightened the heat of divine words with brightness, thou didst leave behind the things that are in the darkness of this life, and having run unto the noetic Light, in the flesh thou didst receive an immaterial life with the bodiless hosts; whence having pre-purified thyself by the grace of the Spirit, thou wast an instructor of monastics, O God-wise Varlaam; therefore praising thee we cry aloud: pray Christ God to grant remission of transgressions unto those who honor thy holy memory with love!
*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
I have fallen into the mire of sinful pursuit, and there is no hope of rising for me, for I am grievously submerged by despair; but as thou didst bear the Hope of salvation, look down upon me thy servant, and deliver me from sin that destroys me, and from soul-corrupting passions, and from the perpetual malice of the deceitful enemy, O unwedded Theotokos; pray Christ God to grant me remission of sins, for I thy servant have thee as my hope!
*Songs of Ascents [Anabathmoi]: The 1st Antiphon of Tone 4.*[^51]
*Prokeimenon, Tone 4:*
*God is wonderful in His saints, the God of Israel.* (Ps. 67:36 [LXX])[^52]
*Verse: In the congregations bless ye God, the Lord from the springs of Israel.* (Ps. 67:27 [LXX])[^53]
*Gospel for a Venerable One. Sticheron following the Gospel, Tone 6:*
The glory and adornment of the venerable, citizen of Zion on high, earthly angel and heavenly man, fellow helper of fasters, and praise of those living in virginity, leader and beauty of monastics: come, ye assemblies of feast-lovers today, let us sing with them, having woven praiseworthy crowns with love, let us form a spiritual choir at the honorable tomb of our God-bearing father, and let us bring worthy hymns, singing in psalmody: Precious is thy death in the sight of the Lord, O venerable Varlaam! For though in spirit thou hast departed unto God, yet in body thou ever abidest with us, and fillest with spiritual gifts those who venerate thy relics; and now as one who hast acquired great boldness before the Lord, pray for those who hymn thine honorable dormition *Service to the Venerable One.*
...thine honorable dormition: to grant us likemindedness, strengthening unto the Orthodox EMPRESS,[^54] and confirmation unto the whole world!
*CANON to the Venerable One.*
*Tone 1. Ode 1. Heirmos:*
*Israel, delivered from bitter bondage, traversed the impassable sea as dry land; and beholding the enemy drowned, they sang a hymn unto God their Benefactor, through wonderworking with a high arm, for He is glorified!*
*Troparia:*
Seeing many captive to the bitter bondage of the cares of this world, O blessed one, thou didst consider that there is no deliverance from it, unless one follows the footsteps of Christ the Deliverer; therefore thou wast an unslothful zealot of Him. By thy vigilant prayer, O father, stir up also me who am despondent to praise thy struggles well, that I may quickly sing a hymn unto God, for He is glorified!
Nourished by the honey-sweet words from the great father, thou didst not desire to look back any longer, but forgetting what was behind, thou didst reach forth like a winged creature toward what was ahead, minding only one thing: to seek God with all thy heart, and ever to sing unto Him a hymn, for He is glorified!
Seeing the warm love of the venerable Anthony toward God, as a flame kindled in thy heart, O blessed one, thou didst return unto thy house rejoicing, as having found a treasure; but the resolve of thy will thou didst leave at the feet of the God-blessed father; thereafter running back unto him, thou didst ever sing unto God: For He is glorified!
*Glory:*
Taking heed unto the light of the God-inspired Anthony, Thou didst burst the hindering nets of worldly love, and running unto him, Thou wast a constant hearer of his teaching, having obtained with him unto God, for He is glorified!
*Both now... Theotokion:*
Drive away by thine intercession the afflictions and enemy assaults that ever vex me, O pure Virgin; and put me not to shame from mine expectation, O Mother of the Lord; for thou alone with thy Son art the hope of the hopeless.
*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
*The stone which the builders rejected, the same is become the head of the corner: this is the stone whereupon Christ established the Church, which He redeemed from the nations.*
*Troparia:*
Thou, O Christ, the unhewn stone, wast the head of the corner; and upon Thee Thy venerable one, desiring to build a spiritual house, laid the walls of the resolve of humility, poverty, and other virtues; therefore having come unto the great father, he prayed him, saying: Establish me on the rock of the Lord's commandments, O venerable one!

*To Varlaam, Abbot of the Caves.*
Coming in glory and riches with many servants unto the holy cave, thou didst cast down under the feet of the elder all thy beautiful ornaments, and thyself, together casting down the pride of the deceitful one.
Knowing, O father, that a noetic treasure was hidden in the holy cave, thou didst sell all the wealth brought by thee and thyself unto Anthony; and like a prudent merchant, thou didst buy that field wherein thou didst enrich thyself with this, through which we also glorify thee after death!
*Glory:*
Having understood, O venerable one, that the wealth, glory, and all honor of this age are like a shadow that quickly passes away, therefore thou didst count all things as refuse, that thou mightest gain Christ alone, and didst love to walk the path of monastics toward Him!
*Both now... Theotokion:*
O pure Dawn, who didst bear the Cultivator of the Highest: brush aside and cleanse the path of my life that is slandered by lawless passions, that the path of virtues may appear unto me, and a right spirit may be renewed in my inward parts!
*Sessional Hymn, Tone 1:*
Seeing thy desire to be unfeigned, the great one among the fathers placed upon thee the helmet of salvation, clothed thee in the breastplate of righteousness, and numbered thee in the monastic regiment; and thou, a soldier appointed unto the Heavenly Kingdom, didst fight well against visible and invisible enemies; having conquered whom, thou didst receive the crown of heavenly honor: which pray that we also may obtain, O venerable Varlaam, who honor thy holy memory with love!
*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
Grant help from on high against the enemies that war against me, O Virgin Bride of God, and teach me, as one who knowest their crafty snares, to conquer them; for none shall vanquish them unless thou assistest those who especially honor thy most immaculate childbirth with love!
*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
*The seer Habakkuk heard Thy marvelous report, O Christ, and cried aloud with fear: God shall come from Teman, and the Holy One from the mountain overshadowed with dense thicket, to save His anointed ones: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!*
*Troparia:*
When the three warring adversaries heard that thou wast stripped of their love, when thou didst clothe thyself in the robe of salvation, they raged against thee, and stirred up fearful things against thee through thy father; but thou didst place thy hope in the power of the Lord.
Thy foolish father ran unto the flock at the cave with many servants, and having dispersed all, he dragged thee away, who wast bound with warmest love unto God, hoping in Whom thou didst cry aloud: *Service to the Venerable One.*
...Glory to Thy power, O Lord!
Dragging thee outside, O blessed one, thy father stripped off the holy mantle and threw it into the ravine, and likewise the helmet of salvation, which was upon thy head, he tore off; then he clothed thee in bright and costly raiment; but thou, despising all these things, didst unswervingly obey the angelic life, calling aloud and crying: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!
*Glory:*
Clothed by force in a most radiant garment, thou didst trample it into the mire, together with it trampling upon the proud enemy, who flits about with various guises unto those who hasten to sing: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!
*Both now... Theotokion:*
Strip me, covered with the shame of grievous iniquities, O Theotokos, and make clean my defiled garment by thy grace, granting me repentance and compunction, that I may thankfully stand before thee, crying: Rejoice, garment of boldness for the naked, O Sovereign Lady!
*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
*Shine forth Thine unwaning light, O Christ, unto all who diligently sing of Thee, granting us peace which surpasses understanding; therefore from the night of ignorance running by day in Thy light, we glorify Thee, O Lover of mankind!*
*Troparia:*
Led by force into the house in bright robes, O blessed one, the unwaning light of God illumined thy heart, therefore thou didst not look upon the deceptive light, but beholding God alone before thee, thou didst zealously glorify Him as the Lover of mankind!
Placing the Master before thee Who calls unto the immortal supper, O venerable one, thou didst count the table of delicacies as nothing, but looking down with thine eyes, thou didst lift up thy mind unto the height, contemplating the banquet of the Heavenly King and His blessedness, which is the true feast in the Heavenly Kingdom!
Preparing a beautiful chamber in thy heart for the Spirit, thou didst not sit in a place of renown in thy palace, but in a single corner thou didst choose to select a narrow living-place to glorify the Lord, the Lover of mankind!
*Glory:*
Thou didst not incline thy heart unto the deceitful words of thy seductress, nor didst thou ascend onto her couch, taking heed unto what was spoken: "The venerable ones shall rejoice in glory upon their beds,"[^55] of which thou art now a partaker and heir!
*Both now... Theotokion:*
Raise me up lying upon the couch of evil deeds, O Theotokos, and by thy guidance direct my life, that I may spend the remaining time in repentance, and glorify thee, O blessed Maiden, pure Mother!
*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
*Held fast in the depth of sins am I, O Christ, and tempest-tossed in the sea of life; but as Jonah from the monster, raise me up also from passions, and save me!*

*To Varlaam, Abbot of the Caves.*
*As Thou didst receive Jonah, grant me also to endure the beast, that in the voice of praise and in the spirit of salvation I may sacrifice unto Thee.*
*Troparia:*
Wholly encompassed by the afflictions of temptation, thou didst call unto the Lover of mankind, O venerable one, that He deliver thee from the snare of the hunters; and thou didst remain for three days fasting, nourishing thy soul with prayer alone, and saying with the prophet: "Raise me up from corruption, O God, and the remaining years I shall sacrifice unto Thee in an incorruptible spirit!"[^56]
Concerning thee, O blessed one, when in so great an affliction, diligent prayer was made by the holy ones in the cave; for the righteous cried, and the Lord heard them,[^57] and delivered thee from sorrow, inclining the heart of thy father unto mercy, who had compassion on thy patience, and having affectionately kissed thee, dismissed thee in peace unto the holy cave!
A mournful voice was heard then in the house of thy father: thy parents wept for thee, their beloved son; thy wife bitterly lamented thee, her betrothed; the manservants and maidservants groaned with weeping for their master, and all escorted thee with tears; but thou, having departed rejoicing as from Egypt into the Promised Land, offered thanksgiving unto God, for He delivered thee from the cruel ruler of the world!
*Glory:*
Glory to Thee, the one God glorified in the Holy Trinity, for Thou hast saved Thy servant from the deceitful adversary: from whom save also us through the prayers of Thine obedient favorite, who honor his memory!
*Both now... Theotokion:*
O thou who art the best helper of those submerged in passions, stretch forth a hand unto the wretched, that having been saved through thee, in a true spirit we may offer praise unto thee with thine Only-Begotten Son and God!
*Kontakion, Tone 2:*
Thou wast not terrified by thy father's threat, thou didst not submit unto his flatteries, neither wast thou cast down by womanly deceit, O most honored Varlaam, but didst remain unconquered by them, hoping in thy Lord: Whom pray for our souls!
*Ikos:*
Beholding thy voluntary suffering, who shall not be astonished, O most blessed Varlaam? For thou didst expose demonic snares, and manfully resisting lustful passions, how greatly didst thou suffer from adversity, that thou mightest present thy pure soul unto God: Whom also pray diligently for us, O father, that we may be delivered from carnal passions *Service to the Venerable Father*

...and the plundering of invisible enemies, having become our helper through thy help and thine unceasing prayers unto God for our souls.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*[^58]
For them that passed through the furnace as a bridal chamber, He quenched the furnace flame; they that were of old the holy youths for the sake of piety were clearly shown forth, and singing in concord, they chanted a hymn: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparia:*
Thou didst pass through the furnace of temptation, O venerable father, and remaining unburned by it, thou didst rather burn up the enemy that invisibly waged war against thee, having been helped against him by the prayers of thy director; for he cried out unceasingly: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

Anthony marveled at thy longsuffering, that warring against bitter Belial, thou didst not fear his multifaceted snares, wherewith though thou wast hindered, yet by endurance and humility didst thou conquer, and didst chant the hymn of the youths: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

When thou didst return with victory unto the fathers in the cave, all were filled with joy, for they beheld thee bearing a crown; and thy elder also rejoiced, receiving thee in health; therefore they also cried out: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Glory:*
Anthony was refreshed, for upon the root of his teachings thou didst grow up in virtues as a fair-flowering tree, O venerable one, and the shoot of thy youth brought forth elder-like grace as fruit; marveling, he cried out thanksgiving: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Both now:*
The tree of my life that rots away with sins, do thou sprinkle with the dew from thy grace, O pure Virgin, that it may at least put forth small branches, and bring forth for this reason the fair fruit of humility and prayer, crying out: Rejoice, acquisition of the immortal fruit!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*[^59]
The dew-bearing furnace of old showed forth the image of a supernatural wonder; for the fire burned not the young children, revealing Christ's seedless birth. Therefore singing, let us chant: Let all creation bless the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparia:*
A wonder! For the gift began to operate while thou wast yet in thy youth; which Anthony beholding, placed thee as leader in his stead over the brethren, and thou wast a good shepherd unto the fathers that sing and supremely exalt the Lord unto ages.

Receiving the beginning of rulership, thou didst receive also a new beginning unto thy life, O father; for thou didst add labors unto labors, and ascetic feats unto watchful vigil; so that all that desire to sing unto the Lord unto ages might have thee as an example.

*To Varlaam, Hegumen of the Caves*

A good director and father wast thou, O blessed one; for as thou thyself didst never alter thy rule, so also didst thou instruct the brethren and beseech them to have diligence for salvation, and to care for the soul, the immortal thing, if they desire to supremely exalt the Lord with the angels unto ages.

*We bless the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit: the Lord.*

Seeking the noetic Jerusalem, thou didst journey as a pilgrim unto the sensible one, sweetly enduring every labor on the path for Christ's sake; and having affectionately kissed His life-giving tomb, thou didst return, singing unto the Lord unto ages.

*Both now:*
All-hymned Mother, Lady of the heavenly ranks, Queen of heaven and earth, turn me away from earthly things, and grant me to seek the things on high; and vouchsafe that in the Jerusalem above I may sing to thee, and supremely exalt the Lord unto ages.

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*[^60]
The ineffable mystery of the Virgin: for she showed herself to be the heaven, and the cherubic throne, and the radiant bridal chamber of Christ God the Almighty. Her do we piously magnify as the Mother of God.

*Troparia:*
Enjoying ineffable joy, O father, now with the angelic ranks thou standest before the never-waning light; and guide us by thy prayers to walk in the light of the Lord's commandments.

From this life thou hast removed unto eternal life, O venerable father, and hast received the place of resting. Keep our life, tempest-tossed by the assaults of adversaries, unharmed; and grant us by thy guidance to attain the dwelling-place of paradise.

Returning from the city of Constantine, thou didst depart with thy soul into the most radiant city of the heavenly King; and thy relics thou didst leave in the monastery of the Holy Mountain, which thou didst command to be brought into the cave, where they now lie incorrupt, pouring forth a fountain of miracles unto them that have recourse thereto.

*Glory:*
O Thou Who art glorified in Thy saints, O only God, glorify all them that faithfully bow down before Thy magnificent glory; for lo, we bring unto Thee in prayer Thine agreeable servant, for whose sake save us all.

*Both now:*
We hymn thee, O Virgin, with unceasing mouths, and as is meet we call thee blessed; for thou alone art blessed among women, having given birth unto the Father's Only-begotten Word: with Whom we, the faithful, together magnify thee.

*Exapostilarion:*
Wounded in thy soul, O venerable father, with the sweet longing for the love of Christ, and desiring to be filled with it, from the corrupt world *Service to the Venerable Father*

...with its lusts thou didst turn away; leaving home and parents, thou didst neglect thy betrothed; therefore thou hast received a hundredfold, and dost inherit life eternal: whereof, that we also be not deprived, grant help by thy prayers.

*Theotokion:*
O fair bridal chamber, Mary, Bride of God, longing for whose beauty the Most High, by the operation of the All-Holy Spirit, dwelt within thy most consecrated womb and took flesh for our sake: make my soul a bridal chamber for the indwelling of the heavenly King, that I, thine unprofitable servant, may receiving, magnify thee.

*On the Praises, Stichera, Tone 4. Podoben: "Thou hast given a sign" (Да́лъ є҆сѝ зна́менїе):*[^61]
A life unspotted, endurance, meekness, and unfeigned love toward God, great abstinence, divine compunction having acquired, O father, like an angel didst thou live in the flesh, O blessed Varlaam, intercessor for our souls.

A house for thy soul, a house prepared beforehand for the Holy Spirit, thou didst despise the beauty of this world; and having laid an immovable foundation upon the rock of the Lord's commandments, thou didst complete it by the height of humility, and becamest a dwelling of the Most Holy Trinity: Whom do thou entreat for us, to give beauty unto our souls, and to make us receivers of spiritual grace.

By the renunciation of the world and carnal things, thou didst bring the fruits of virtues unto God from the noetic tree of thy life, O most blessed father; receiving from Him the reward of thy labors, thou dost now delight in the tree of life in paradise: whereof to be vouchsafed its sweetness, do thou entreat Jesus the Lover of mankind and Savior of our souls.

*Glory, Tone 8:*
The great director of monastics among the fathers, seeing thy warmth toward God, O father, directed by many virtues, entrusted unto thee the God-gathered flock, which having well pastured upon the pastures of thine instruction, thou didst lead whole unto Christ God, the Chief Shepherd: Whom do thou now entreat, to preserve His spiritual flock in peace, and to protect it from destroying wolves.

*Both now. Theotokion:*
O Sovereign Lady, accept the supplication of thy servants, and deliver us from every distress and sorrow.

*Great Doxology, and Dismissal. At the Liturgy, the service to the venerable one, Common.*

*(Woodcut Engraving: Within an architectural frame, Saint Athanasius the Recluse of the Caves in monastic habit, seated in prayer before a crucifix inside his rock-hewn cave cell; upon the stone desk rest a book and a human skull, with an oil lamp suspended from the vaulted ceiling. Inscribed banner above reads: "Прпⷣ: А҆ѳана́сїй Затво́рникъ, и҆́же ᲂу҆мрѐ, и҆ па́ки воскре́се.")*[^62]

---

# MONTH OF DECEMBER

*On the 2nd Day: Service to our Venerable Father Athanasius the Recluse of the Caves.*[^63]

*At Vespers, on "Lord, I have cried," Stichera on 6, Tone 4. Podoben: "As one valiant" (Ꙗ҆́кѡ до́блѧ):*[^64]
Desiring to mortify thy flesh, thou didst enter into narrow seclusion, O father, and didst bury thy body by unslackening ascetic labors; but before the general resurrection, showing forth a certain resurrection of the dead, after two days thou didst rise again; and now living in immortality *Service to the Venerable Father*

...living in immortality, pray thou that we also may escape eternal death, and obtain great mercy from Christ God.

He that believes in Me, as the Scripture hath said, though he die, yet shall he live unto ages; in thee the word was fulfilled in deed, O venerable Athanasius: for thou didst die unto the world and the flesh, but knowing thee hereafter by good deeds, He raised thee up unto a better life, and thou wast an awe-inspiring wonder unto all, and rather unto profitable correction: which grant also unto us by thy prayers, as we prayerfully celebrate thy memory.

He that showed the paths of eternal life, did not suffer His venerable one to see corruption, raising him up after two days, like four-days-dead Lazarus, having loosed the bonds of death by the call of the divine voice: by this raise us up also unto God, and raise us up from dead works, O intercessor, Giver of life, and Savior of our souls.

At Thy resurrection, O Christ Savior, the things of death were shaken, and the faithful people are not held in dominion by it, as Athanasius, marvelous among the venerable, showed unto us, having learned by his own resurrection, and providing profitable correction unto monastics by three virtues: confirm this also in our hearts, O Savior, by his prayers, as we honor the yearly memory of Thine ever-memorable servant.

*Both now: Theotokion, or Stavrotheotokion. At the Aposticha, in the Oktoechos.*

*Glory, Tone 3:*
For thou didst drown the death that relaxes the limbs, and wast likened unto the Life of all that spent three days in the tomb, rising from the dead after two days, thou didst choose the narrowest path unto eternal life: having attained which in the immortal land, look down upon us from thence without delay, that thou mayest drive away the passions that mortify us, and make us partakers of thine immortality, on this day as we celebrate thy memory, O father and most blessed Athanasius.

*Both now: Theotokion, or Stavrotheotokion. Troparion, Tone 3:*
Desiring to obtain the beatitude of them that weep, day and night thou didst weep unceasingly, having in mind the hour of judgment; therefore after thy falling asleep thou didst find consolation in heaven, O venerable Athanasius. Grant also unto us to weep here for our sins, so that having escaped endless weeping, we may receive eternal consolation there.

*Canon*

*Tone 4. Ode 1. Heirmos:*[^65]
Let us all sing a song of victory unto God, Who wrought marvelous wonders with a high arm, and saved Israel, for He hath been glorified.

*To Athanasius the Recluse of the Caves*

*Troparia:*
Unto them that now desire to sing thy fitting falling asleep with blessed mouths, O father, not a song, but compunction do thou grant, as they pray and cry out now in the day of thy solemnity, wherein thy memory is glorified.

Understanding that human life in this world is cut down by the axe of death, O venerable one, that thou mightest crush the passions by thyself, thou didst run unto the firm rock; and by most marvelous virtues escaping the assault of death, thou singest unto God, for He hath been glorified.

*Glory:*
Having first died unto the world by the renunciation of its many-passioned snares, thou didst direct thy goings from thence, O father, unto the unapproachable life; therefore being now a son of life, look down upon us that sing unto God in thy memory: for He hath been glorified.

*Both now:*
My soul, mortified by voluntary passions, hasten to quicken, O pure Virgin; for thou art the Mother of life, and givest resurrection unto the dead.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*[^66]
The stone which the builders rejected, the same is become the head of the corner: this is the stone whereon Christ established the Church, which He redeemed from the nations.

*Troparia:*
Bound by the faith of a God-pleasing life, O most blessed one, death was taken captive, having no dominion over thee; for though it snatched thee away, yet He commanded to return thee again for the profit of many, Who by His own death delivered all from death.

Lo, thou didst lie dead upon the bed, and the providence of the Most High was diligent to work wonders through thee; for after two days He returned thy spirit, and thou wast found alive, unto the wonder of all that beheld, who had seen thy falling asleep.

*Glory:*
Thy sickness, O venerable one, was not unto death, but unto the glory of God; for having died, thou didst receive death as sleep in the departure of thy sickness, and after two days, as if awaking from sleep, thou didst wash thy face with streams of tears.

*Both now:*
Having cut off the sickness of death by thy holy childbirth, and given life unto all the world, I alone have remained mortified by defiled deeds: O Mother of God, lay thy hand upon me, and I shall turn unto a better life.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 8:*
Looking upon immortality, that thou mightest escape eternal death, thou didst choose a narrow life, O most blessed Athanasius, in thy mortified body; therefore marveling at the daily providence over thee, we glorify the only Lover of mankind, Who gave life and incorruption unto all.

*Service to the Venerable Father*

*Glory, both now. Theotokion:*
Them that were subject unto deathly bondage hast thou delivered by thy childbirth, O Virgin Bride of God; therefore the race of Christians glorifies thee with songs, for thou hast given unto them life and incorruption.

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*[^67]
The sun was taken up, and the moon stood in its order; Thou wast lifted up upon the Tree, O longsuffering One, and didst establish Thy Church upon it.

*Troparia:*
Lying breathless, thou didst rise up, and having received breath, thou camest again into being, O father; which they that stood by beholding, glorified the divine power with fear.

Seeing how fearful is the hour of death, O blessed one, thou didst bitterly weep, and didst deliver thyself by silence and sighing; and thy tears became for many an inscription of the fearful judgment.

*Glory:*
Ever mindful of thine exodus, O father, thou didst weep in this sorrowful life; and past mournings were added unto thee, having sensibly beheld what manner of fear and trembling they have that depart: which, that we also may escape, pray thou, O venerable father.

*Both now:*
I fear not the fearful judgment, nor dread Gehenna, for my heart is hardened by many passions: soften it by thy gentle breeze, O pure Virgin Mother, and pierce it, that it may be made whole by the remembrance of fearful torments.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*[^68]
Grant us Thy peace, O Son of God, for beside Thee we know no other God; we name Thy name, for Thou art the God of the living and of the dead.

*Troparia:*
Beholding thy weeping and inconsolable lamentation, they that stood by, terrified, asked that thou wouldest tell them a word for profit: and thou didst tell them three profitable things: that they maintain obedience, repentance, and prayer, as higher than other virtues: in which that we also may be established, pray thou, O father Athanasius.

Being thyself obedient unto the commandments of Christ, thou didst tell the brethren that obedience is higher than all virtues, O venerable one; for it dies not even after death, but preserves life with God, Who is the fearful Judge of the living and the dead.

*Glory:*
Repentance and prayer are two fiery wings, wherewith thou didst learn that passage through the aerial toll-houses is unrestrained, O blessed one; thou didst find the paths of silence, whereon thou didst hasten unto higher things, leaving instruction unto them that asked:

*To Athanasius the Recluse of the Caves*

...and grant us correction, as we celebrate the day of thy departure.

*Both now:*
Speedy intercessor for the Christian race, O Mary Virgin, having received the prayers of thy singers into thy most pure hands, bring them unto thy Son, and ask for us forgiveness of sins, and grant stillness unto them that are in passions.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*[^69]
As Thou didst swallow me into the deep of the heart of the sea, and didst save me, O Savior, from the bondage of death, and didst loose the bonds of mine iniquities.

*Troparia:*
Having utterly renounced life and the seeing of sensible light, thou didst enter into the dark seclusion, exchanging sensible sight for the vision of the noetic light: which that we also may obtain by thy prayers, O father, having cast off our iniquities.

That thou mightest quicken the flesh mortified by ascetic labors, O father, thou didst continually water it with the streams of thy tears, remaining twelve years buried alive in the cave, quickened by the hope of immortal life.

*Glory:*
Thy flesh, O venerable Athanasius, a living tree, brought forth the fruits of repentance unto all; whereof grant me also to taste by thy love, that I may be strengthened to loose the bonds of mine iniquities.

*Both now:*
Thou art an ever-flowering tree, O All-Pure Virgin, covering with thy shadow them that are burned by the swelter of iniquities: with this cover also us that are scorched by passions, and we will sing: Rejoice, well-shaded leafy tree, beneath which many are sheltered.

*Kontakion, Tone 5:*
As namesake of immortality, after thy mortification thou didst rise alive; and showing that death is exceedingly bitter, thou didst weep bitterly: now having found unending joy, pray for us, that we may escape eternal weeping.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*[^70]
Sevenfold in the furnace unto Thy youths, O Savior, the fire was untouched, neither did it scorch them; then the three as with one mouth sang and blessed, saying: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparia:*
Thou didst spend thy life in weeping and sighing, and having whitened the robe of the angelic habit with the streams of thy tears, thou didst depart unto the regions free of tears, rejoicing and singing: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

The angelic ranks having come on the day of thy falling asleep, and peacefully receiving thy spirit, guided it into the dwellings of paradise, where living, remember us, O venerable father, that sing: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Service to the Venerable Father*

...O venerable father, that sing: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Glory:*
Twice wast thou a dead man in this life, O father, and first indeed all saw thee returning again into bodily members, from which having been loosed a second time, thou now livest with the angelic ranks: where also forget not us that sing: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Both now:*
Hasten to quicken me who am dead through evil deeds, O Sovereign Lady; for without doubting have I placed my hope in thee, as having given birth unto the immortal King and Giver of life unto all the world.

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*[^71]
All ye works, and all creation as much as is, bless, sing unto the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparia:*
The heavens rejoice, having received thy spirit into their bosom, and we make merry as we bow down before thy relics, O venerable one, from which grant grace unto them that are in need, and vouchsafe us to sing unto the Lord unto ages.

The angels celebrate having thee as fellow-citizen, and the Caves Mountain makes merry, possessing the shrine of thy relics as a most radiant pearl: by this enlighten us that are held by the darkness of passions, and grant us to sing unto the Lord unto ages.

*We bless the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit: the Lord.*

Thou didst well please thy Master in this life, O father, and didst receive the gift to heal infirmities: visit us that are held by passions, as once thou didst Babylas, and vouchsafe us to sing unto the Lord unto ages.

*Both now:*
I am in sorrow and grief, reflecting upon my defiled life, and I have no hope: unto thee alone, the worldwide joy, do I flee, O pure Virgin, and I pray that joy may shine upon my soul unto ages.

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
He hath wrought strength with His arm; for He hath put down the mighty from their thrones, and exalted the humble, the God of Israel. Wherein the Dayspring from on high hath visited us, and hath guided us into the way of peace.

*Troparia:*
Show love unto us that pray unto thee, O father, and by thy prayer grant healing unto them that suffer soul-infirmities; and especially guide them that are assailed by enemy snares into the way of peace.

The Great Lavra sings thy labors and ascetic feats, O venerable one, the cave glorifies thee, preserving thy wonder-working relics; whereunto we also bowing down, beseech thee: guide our steps into the way of peace.

*To Athanasius the Recluse of the Caves*

Arise quickly and come, healing them that entreat thee, O most blessed servant Athanasius; for lo, from many a soul-infirmity I cry unto thee, having no relief; rather I pray: guide me into the peaceful path of salvation.

*Glory:*
I glorify Thee, O Most Holy Trinity, unoriginate Father, with the co-eternal Son, and I hymn Thy Holy Spirit; Whom grant unto my heart through the intercession of Thine agreeable servant, that I may find a peaceful path.

*Both now:*
If any glorify not thy pure conception, and sing not thine ineffable childbirth, O Mary, Bride of God, let him fall away from eternal glory, and let him not be a partaker of the light, nor a son of the everlasting peace.

*Exapostilarion:*
Let us the people crown with hymns of God Athanasius, marvelous among the venerable; for he while living died, and after two days rose again, and again departed; he lives no longer held in dominion by death, but began an everlasting life, being a son of life and heir of the resurrection of the righteous.

*Glory, both now. Theotokion:*
Today who shall recount thy mysteries, O pure one? Or who shall proclaim the coming to pass of ineffable things in thee? For in time thou gavest birth unto the Son Who before ages was begotten of the Father, the Firstborn of life and resurrection; entreat Him therefore, that I also may be an heir of the resurrection and of life.

*On the Praises, Stichera, Tone 2. Podoben: "When from the Tree" (Є҆гда̀ ѿ дре́ва):*
Come, ye people, let us sing of marvelous Athanasius, who is sung in the heavens by angels, the beauty of fasters, and radiant luster of singers; who having zeal for the monastic life, traversed the narrow path without slackness, having overcome all passions by abstinence, having an unrestrained desire toward God, before Whom he now stands with the angels, and prays for our souls.

Come, ye brethren and assemblies of feast-lovers, every choir of God-bearing fathers, let us bring crowns of songful flowers unto the tomb of the most blessed father, and let us fall down, saying: O venerable, most blessed fellow-citizen of angels, forget not thine intercession unto God the Master of all, that we may obtain eternal life and mercy in the day of judgment.

Come, ye choirs of monastics, unto the shrine of the many-miraculous relics of ever-memorable Athanasius, and let us honor the God-speaking father, who instructed us unto obedience, and unto continual repentance and prayer, and ascending unto God by other virtues as by fiery wings, let us find grace and great mercy.

*Service to the Venerable Father*

*Glory, Tone 8:*
Ever most blessed, father of the holy, Athanasius! For thine instruction became for many a correction and a rising up from falling through the remembrance of the future judgment; expecting which thyself also after thy resurrection, thou didst pour forth inconsolable tears from thine eyes; now standing before Christ with rejoicing, pray thou for them that sing thy honorable falling asleep, that they may receive forgiveness of sins and great mercy in the day of judgment.

*Both now. Theotokion:*
O Sovereign Lady, accept the supplications of thy servants, and deliver us from every distress and sorrow.

*(Vacat — Fol. 31v tail: Woodcut vignette depicting a baroque foliate cartouche with acanthus scrolls and floral festoons) (Woodcut Engraving: Within a decorated frame, Saint Nikon the Dry (*Прпⷣ: Ни́конъ сꙋхі́й*) standing in prayer with uplifted hands, his ankles bound with iron fetters; around him, scenes of his endurance under Polovtsian captivity. The lower register illustrates his miraculous translation through the air, inscribed: "Принесенїя Плѣнни О҆́нꙋ невидимо во Цр҃ковь Пече́рскꙋю Пр: Бцы".)*[^72]

*On the 30th Day of the Month of December: Service to our Venerable Father Nikon the Dry.*[^73]

*At Vespers, on "Lord, I have cried," Stichera, Tone 4. Podoben: "Thou hast given a sign" (Да́лъ є҆сѝ зна́менїе):*[^74]
Pillar of endurance, glorious and God-bearing Nikon, established in the love of God, wherein thou didst abide immovable through many temptations; therefore do we glorify thee, O father, as we celebrate thy memory on this day, as one glorified in the heavens by Jesus the Lover of mankind and Savior of our souls.

Unharmed by fire, and mistreated in every way *Service to the Venerable Father*

...thanksgiving didst thou send up, O blessed one, unto the Creator and God; therefore, delivered from the hand of the ungodly, thou didst stand in the holy church, and now standing before Christ in the church of them that triumph, grant that we also may receive repentance from the Lover of mankind and Savior of our souls.

The fire of thy love toward God quenched the sensible fire, and the streams of thy blood drowned the hosts of the noetic enemy, and thou didst conquer them that tormented thee, O venerable father; therefore Jesus the Lover of mankind and Savior of our souls gave thee the reward of the highest ranks.

*Glory, Tone 6:*
The multitudes of monastics marvel at thy patience, how being mistreated by the unfaithful, baked with fire, and cut with knives, enduring heat and swelter, hunger and thirst, and having hope in God for thy salvation, thou didst easily traverse the narrow path and attain the highest beatitude: blessed art thou having so worked for Christ, and thou hast received as the reward of thy labor a dwelling with the angels and participation in the divine light: therefore we beseech thee, O most blessed Nikon, as having boldness before Christ God, ask peace for the world and great mercy for our souls.

*Both now: Theotokion, or Stavrotheotokion. At the Aposticha, in the Oktoechos.*

*Glory, Tone 6:*
Come, ye Christ-loving assemblies, clapping your hands, and gatherings of the choirs of monastics: come piously unto the holy cave, let us wisely bring praiseworthy hymns as a gift, and bow down before the relics of glorious Nikon, that by his holy prayers we may receive the crown of unfading glory from Christ God and the Savior of our souls.

*Troparion, Tone 8:*
Having loved the good God, O stout-hearted father Nikon, thou didst receive the monastic life, and having fought well in it, thou wast taken captive by the Polovtsi: by whom being much tormented, by the grace of Christ thou wast freed from them invisibly; and now abiding in unapproachable light, pray thou that we who honor thy memory may be saved.

*Canon to the Saint*

*Tone 4. Ode 1. Heirmos:*[^75]
He was my helper and protector unto salvation; this is my God, and I will glorify Him, the God of my father, and I will exalt Him, for gloriously hath He been glorified.

*Troparia:*
Help me, O Lord my God, and to honor glorious Nikon in song, send down the All-Holy Paraclete, that girded with vigil, I may bring a song unto Thee, Who givest strength unto all.

Who shall worthily bless thee, O most blessed father, who from youth didst love thy Lord, and didst follow Him with voluntary poverty even unto death? Therefore now living with the angels, pray that strength unto endurance be sent unto us also.

*Glory:*
Having traversed without stumbling the darkness and gloom of the lustful world by burning love toward Christ, and being reckoned unto the brightness of the angelic habit, thou wast as it were outside the flesh; therefore thou singest unto Him that gave thee strength.

*Both now:*
Drive away, I pray thee, O pure Virgin, the darkness that hath blinded the mental eyes of my soul with passions; for thou art the divine light-receiving candle, kindled with immaterial fire, and thou dost guide unto divine understanding them that sing: Rejoice, luminary of the never-waning light!

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*[^76]
As the barren church from the nations hath brought forth, and the synagogue great in children hath grown weak, unto our marvelous God let us cry out: Holy art Thou, O Lord!

*Troparia:*
As the barren woman rejoices having received in the womb, so *To Nikon the Dry*

...so makes merry the holy Caves Mountain, having revealed thee as a luminary unto them that receive thee, and among monastic ranks it sings: Holy art Thou, O Lord!

Imitating the first fathers, having laid the cross upon thy shoulder with joy, O glorious Nikon, thou didst bear it with great courage even unto death, unto Whom thou now singest: Holy art Thou, O Lord!

*Glory:*
Armed with the shield of firm faith, thou didst go into captivity, O venerable one, and desiring to take captive the enemy there, in fortitude and magnanimity thou didst quench him, treading upon him with thy foot; and unto God Who strengthened thee, thou didst cry out: Holy art Thou, O Lord!

*Both now:*
Unable to overcome the enemy that rises up against me by the strength of passions, unto thee do I flee, O Mother of God, help me: for thou art a strong pillar, a weapon in battle, and an unconquerable leader.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 8:*
Desiring to cultivate the field of thy soul, thou didst lay upon thyself the cross as the yoke of voluntary patience, and with this as with a plow thou didst cultivate the rot-bearing ground of passions, sowing with tears, casting the seed of fasting labors, from which thou now reapest joyful sheaves with thy hands *Service to the Venerable Father*

...reapest joyful sheaves with thy hands, standing before Christ God: Whom do thou entreat, O glorious Nikon, as being an ever-present intercessor, for them that bless thy holy memory with love.

*Glory, both now. Theotokion:*
Rejoice, unplowed field that hast sprouted the heavenly ear of grain, whereof the nourishing Bread for the world was made: with this nourish the souls of them that honor thy blameless conception with love, O pure Virgin.

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*[^77]
For the sake of the love of Thine image, O compassionate One, Thou didst stand upon Thy cross, and the nations melted away; for Thou, O Lover of mankind, art my strength and praise.

*Troparia:*
Thy love toward God was strong, O father, for Whose sake desiring to suffer from the unfaithful, thou wouldest by no means be ransomed: for having loved the Lord Who loved thee, and hoping in Him, thou didst cry: Holy art Thou, O Lord!

Having received the instruction of the Lord affectionately, sweetly didst thou enter into the trial of sicknesses, O blessed one, saying with David: Blessed is the man whom Thou chastenest, O Lord; therefore I cry unto Thee: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!

*Glory:*
Looking upon future things, thou didst account present daily afflictions as nothing, O marvelous Nikon, though fire, and frost, and iron assailed thee, yet thou didst endure all these, remaining like a firm adamant, crying: Holy art Thou, O Lord!

*Both now:*
Looking not upon future things, my soul hath cleaved unto the earth, and I was likened unto senseless beasts: but do thou, O pure Virgin, open my mental eyes, and make marvelous thy mercy upon me, that understanding I may cry: Holy art thou and marvelous in truth!

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*[^78]
Thy light, O my Lord, came into the world, a holy light, turning back from the gloom of ignorance them that praise Thee with faith.

*Troparia:*
Being mistreated in every way by the unfaithful, O father, thou didst not despair thereof, but sending up thanksgiving unto God, thou didst say: Quench me, O God, for the waters are come in even unto my soul; for Thou alone art He that frees the shackled with courage, who praise Thee with faith.

Thy patience, O most blessed one, confounded the Polovtsian who took thee captive; therefore three years he spent tormenting thee every day, afflicting thee with hunger, thirst, cold, and swelter: but thou didst have hope in God, that He delivers them that praise Him with faith.

*Glory:*
The streams of thy blood, cutting off the leaves of passions, and seeing other torments, the mental enemy assailed the Polovtsian, who hearing that thy deliverance from on high was to be, laid heavy chains upon thee;

*To Nikon the Dry*

...laid heavy chains upon thee; but thou, O father, didst cry out in the words of the Psalm: O God, give ear unto my help: for Thou alone art He that saves them that praise Thee with faith.

*Both now:*
By the streams of thy help, O Virgin Bride of God, drown the invisible enemies that drag my soul into the abyss of Hades with the chains of passions, that being freed from them I may cry unto thee: Rejoice, sea that didst drown the mental Pharaoh!

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*[^79]
Prefiguring the three-day burial, the prophet Jonah cried out, praying in the sea-monster: Deliver me from corruption, O Jesus, King of hosts!

*Troparia:*
The iron of sorrows pierced thy soul, O glorious Nikon, until the martyr gave assurance in faith of thy deliverance sent unto thee quickly from God, unto Whom thou didst cry: In Thee, O Lord, have I put my trust; by Thy righteousness deliver me, O Jesus, King of hosts!

*Glory:*
As Habakkuk invisibly, the right hand of the Most High snatched thee away; and they that beheld marveled, hearing a voice from on high, and were put to shame, not knowing God, that He alone is the King of hosts.

*Both now:*
O mighty Deliverer, deliver me from the hands of adversaries, for lo, my head sinks down into the abyss, and I shall not escape the eternal bars of Hades, unless thou grant me help, who art the Mother of the King of hosts.

*Kontakion, Tone 6:*
Imitating the Protomartyr, thou didst pray for thy tormentor, and having delivered him from death, thou didst lead him unto Christ, O glorious venerable martyr Nikon; therefore, beholding Christ at the right hand of the Father with the first-crowned, pray for us, that we remain not outside the bridal chamber of Christ.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*[^80]
The Abrahamic youths of old in Babylon trampled the furnace flame, crying out with songs: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparia:*
While the awesome service of the mystery of ordination was being celebrated in the church, snatched away from the shroud by the power of God, thou didst stand in the midst of the brethren, O glorious Nikon; and all beholding thy fetters, and the blood dripping from thy cruel wounds, lifted up their hands for thy deliverance, saying: Blessed art Thou, O God!

He that first held thee in captivity, O father, lo, afterwards seeing thee free by the right hand of the Most High in the midst of the brethren, was moved to compunction and baptized, taking himself captive into the obedience of Christ, crying: Blessed art Thou, O God!

*Glory:*
Blessed are the captives that had thee as captive, O wise one; for by thee delivered from sickness, they escaped from the hands of the ungodly, and that which was spoken in the Scripture was fulfilled *Service to the Venerable Father*

...was fulfilled that which was spoken in the Scripture: The prayer of a righteous man avails much in its working.

*Both now:*
Held captive by mental enemies, and bound with the chains of passions by bitter Belial, unto thee do I cry, O unwedded Mother: snatch me away from them, that being free I may cry: Rejoice, thou who gavest birth unto the Deliverer of captives!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*[^81]
O almighty Deliverer of all, descending in the midst of the flame unto them that practiced piety, Thou didst bedew them, and taught them to sing: Bless the Lord, all ye works, and sing unto Him!

*Troparia:*
Bearing in thy heart the Deliverer of all and thy Savior, thou didst not fear the tormentor who desired to crucify thee, O father; but rendering good for evil, thou didst heal him lying in sickness, that he also turning, might bless the Lord and supremely exalt Him unto ages.

In the earthen vessel of thy body, how great a treasure of wonderworking do we honor, O blessed one, and we glorify the heavenly Father Who gave thee healing for our profit; by this heal us also, we beseech thee, that through thee we may sing and supremely exalt Him Who works glorious things unto ages.

*We bless the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit: the Lord.*
