Thou didst stand against the world and the flesh, and having bound both, thou didst subdue them unto the spirit; therefore unto the higher world thou didst ascend rejoicing, and with the angels thou singest and supremely exaltest the Lord unto ages.

*Both now:*
Stand by to help us who speedily invoke thee, O All-Pure Virgin, and calm the passions that lust against the spirit contrary to the world and the flesh; for lo, we bring glorious Nikon unto thee in prayer, for whose sake grant us to supremely exalt thee in the light of the saints unto ages.

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*[^82]
Eve by the infirmity of disobedience introduced the curse; but thou, O Virgin Mother of God, by the blossoming of thy womb-bearing didst make blessing to bud forth for the world: therefore we all magnify thee.

*Troparia:*
Desiring to heal the infirmity of despondency in their souls, the host of valiant-minded fathers gathered on the Caves Mountain, among whom glorious Nikon shone forth like a star, and with the beams of his prayers enlightens us; therefore do we magnify him.

Thou wast illumined with mental light, who first by the renunciation of passions didst lighten thine eyes not to behold vanity; therefore we magnify Christ, Who established His word in His servant in His fear.

*Glory:*
Marvelous are Thy works, O uncreated Trinity, and Thou art glorified in Thy saints, O God of Israel: by the supplications of Thine agreeable servant Nikon, make marvelous Thy grace upon us also, by turning us away from unfitting deeds, that we may ever magnify Thee.

*To Nikon the Dry*

...make marvelous Thy grace upon us, by turning us away from unfitting deeds, that we may ever magnify Thee.

*Both now:*
The Most High wrought marvelous works of His dispensation upon thee, O Virgin Bride of God, and all the generations of the earth-born magnify thee, calling thee blessed from generation to generation; by the supplications of the ever-memorable fathers of the Caves, grant that we also may magnify thee unto ages.

*Exapostilarion:*
By firm abstinence and suffering from the unfaithful, thou didst slay the serpent that watches thy heel, O father; after thy departure thou didst receive a twofold crown of thy virtues; and now living in heaven, as the glory of the venerable and the praise of martyrs, pray for us, that we also may be partakers of thy glory, O wise Nikon.

*Glory, both now. Theotokion:*
For our race having entered into darkness through intemperance, thou that by thy childbirth didst bring us unto the light, deliver us from intemperance, and guide us unto the light of the commandments of thy Son, that we may sing unto thee, O Mother of God: Rejoice, unfading crown of abstinence!

*At the Aposticha, Glory, Tone 5:*
Earthly angel and heavenly citizen, equal standing-companion of captives, glorious Nikon, come, ye people, let us bless: for he fought with twofold sufferings, and by his ascetic contest receiving twofold crowns, stands before Christ God, unceasingly praying for this city and monastery, that it may obtain great mercy.

*Both now: Theotokion, or Stavrotheotokion according to the Tone.*

*(Vacat — Fol. 35r tail: Woodcut vignette depicting a baroque foliate cartouche with acanthus leaves and scrolls) (Woodcut Engraving: Above, Saint Mark the Grave-Digger (*Прпⷣ: Ма́рко Пече́рникъ*) within a rock-hewn cave holding a spade and lantern amidst bones and skulls. Below, two narrative panels illustrate his miracles: left panel inscribed "Повелѣ ма́рко ме́ртвꙋ ма́сло возлїи на ся̀" [Mark commanded the dead man to pour oil upon himself]; right panel inscribed "Вторый ме́ртвый воста ждаше до ᲂу҆́тра" [The second dead man arose and waited until morning].)*[^83]

*On the 29th Day of the Month of December: Service to the Venerable Fathers of the Caves, Mark and Theophilus.*[^84]

*At Vespers, "Blessed is the man," 1st Antiphon. On "Lord, I have cried," Stichera, Tone 5. Podoben: "Rejoice" (Ра́дꙋйся):*[^85]
Desiring to build a house of virtues, thou didst lay the foundation of patience, O most blessed father Mark; and the tempestuous sea of passions thou didst calm by the gentle breezes of humility, and the enemy burning with youthful lusts and pleasures thou didst drown in the abyss of fasting, and by the guidance of divine grace, having without turmoil crossed the deep of this life, thou didst reach the desired haven: where now standing before Christ, pray thou to grant great mercy unto our souls.

When many that were wandering on the path of life, and in hardness of heart rejecting the heavenly calling, thou, O venerable father, didst hear the voice of the Lord calling unto the heavenly banquet, thou didst leave the turmoil of this world, and swiftly didst run, having thy lamp pre-adorned with love, and entered into the heavenly bridal chamber; into which that we also may be vouchsafed to enter, we beseech thee, pray diligently unto the Sovereign Lady, and ask for our souls a multitude of compassions and great mercy.

Having despised every passing love of this world, and the flesh sporting in pleasures, O most blessed father, by ever-abiding love thou didst give wings to thy mind toward God; for Whose sake taking up thy cross, thou didst crucify all thy members, and having mortified the ruler of the world, thou wast an adornment of the bodies of fasters and an ornament of the venerable: with whom standing before the Sovereign Lady, tarry not to entreat Her to grant peace and great mercy unto our souls.

*Other Stichera, Tone 4. Podoben: "O all-praised martyrs" (Прехвали́нїи мч҃нцы):*[^86]
Most blessed Mark, for the sake of the love of Christ *To Mark and Theophilus*

...having left the spacious life, thou didst take up thy dwelling in a narrow cave, wherein, that thou mightest ever have the memory and hour of death before thine eyes, with thine own hands thou didst prepare graves for the departed brethren, and didst foresee thine own departure unto God: before Whom now standing, pray thou to grant peace and great mercy unto our souls.

O ever-memorable father, hearing thy voice, the dead submitted unto thy command, and having received breath again, were quickened, until thou didst command them to depart unto the desired Master: before Whom thou also standing after thy falling asleep, pray diligently to grant endless life and great mercy unto our souls.

O father worthy of wonder, having encompassed thyself with many labors, thou didst humble thy fierce domestic enemy, and having bound him with chains, thou didst subdue him unto the spirit; therefore wast thou the praise of monastics, with whom pray thou to grant peace and great mercy unto our souls.

*Glory, Tone 6:*
Who shall recount as is meet thy labors and ascetic feats, O ever-memorable father? For since thou didst receive the angelic habit upon thyself, thou gavest no rest unto thy body, but didst lift up thine eyes unto the Lord, and didst ever dispose thy lips unto prayer *Service to the Venerable Fathers*

...thy hearing thou didst extend unto the hearing of the word of God, thy hands thou didst strengthen for the digging of graves: blessed art thou, O father, having in all things worked for Christ, before Whom now standing, pray thou to give peace unto the world and to save our souls.

*Both now: Of the Feast, Tone 4:*
Glory to God in the highest in Bethlehem hear I today from the bodiless ones, unto Him that was well-pleased that peace should be upon earth. Now is the Virgin wider than the heavens: for light has shone forth unto them that were in darkness, and has exalted the humble, who chant angelically: Glory to God in the highest!

*Entrance: Prokeimenon of the day, and three readings: (Look: August 1, on the reverse).*[^87]

*At the Aposticha, Stichera of the Feast, Tone 6. Podoben: "On the third day" (Трїдне́венъ):*
I behold an awesome mystery. (See August 12).

*Glory: To the Venerable, Tone 8:*
Come, ye people, today, having assembled for the virgin memory of the cross-bearing father, see how, being in this mortal body, having dominion over death, by a word he called forth the dead, and they listened to his voice: one himself poured oil upon himself; another by command yielded his place to another; and another, having revived after his falling asleep, remained until a place was prepared for him. Oh, the wonders! How, having received so great grace from God, blessing him with hymns of praise, we pray that he may pray for us unto Christ, that He may raise us up from our transgressions and deliver our souls from afflictions.

*Both now: Of the Feast, the same Tone:*
O Lord, having come to Bethlehem, Thou didst dwell in a cave; having heaven as Thy throne, Thou didst recline in a manger; Whom the hosts of angels surround, Thou didst descend unto the shepherds, that as Thou art compassionate Thou mightest save our race: glory to Thee!

*Troparion, Tone 4:*
Having mortified carnal desires by much abstinence, and by digging graves for the saints, ever living in a cave as dead, by thy command thou didst raise up the dead, O all-praised Mark: mortify also our carnal mindsets, and direct us unto the course of virtues, praying for us unto the only Lover of mankind.

*Glory, both now: Of the Feast.*

*At Matins, on "God is the Lord": Troparion of the Feast. Glory: To the Venerable. Both now: Of the Feast.*
*After the 1st Sticology, Sessional Hymn, Tone 4. Podoben: "Thou hast appeared today" (Ꙗ҆ви́лсѧ є҆сѝ дне́сь):*
Having received the divine voice into thy heart, thou didst leave the world, and didst follow the calling of thy Lord: therefore taking the Cross as a staff into thy hand *To Mark and Theophilus*

...thou didst quietly cross therewith the sea of this life, and didst drown the noetic Pharaoh in the abyss of thy labors, and wast an adornment of the venerable, O Mark worthy of wonder.

*Glory, both now: Of the Feast. Or this Theotokion:*
We bring unto thee Gabriel's greeting, O pure Virgin: Rejoice, thou that didst conceive God, the Creator of all, and didst renew decayed human nature; rejoice, for thou wast a virgin in childbirth, and after childbirth thou dost remain a virgin still.

*After the 2nd Sticology, Sessional Hymn, Tone 5. Podoben: "Co-beginningless" (Собезнача́льное):*
Who can recount as is meet Thy love for mankind toward the earthly, O Master, that Thou hast given them Thy Cross as a help, which Thy servant also taking into his hand, conquered all the demonic regiments? For this was unto him a breastplate and helmet of salvation; this was sweet food and drink; this was unto him glory and an adornment before the venerable.

*Glory, both now: Of the Feast. Or this Theotokion:*
Who shall worthily praise thy childbirth, O all-pure one! For seedlessly didst thou conceive of the Holy Spirit, and in time didst give birth unto the Word begotten of the Father before the ages: therefore, honoring thee in hymns as the Mother of our true God, we chant and magnify thee.

*After the Polyeleos, Sessional Hymn, Tone 8. Podoben: "Wisdom" (Премꙋ́дрости):*
Who can recount thy labors and ascetic feats, O most blessed one! For having been a faithful servant unto thy Lord, and faithfully serving Him, thou didst multiply the talent entrusted unto thee, and with the good laborers didst receive the denarius of eternal life: therefore, as thou hast boldness, pray thou unto Christ God to grant remission of transgressions unto them that honor with love thy holy memory.

*Glory, both now: Theotokion:*
Spending the time of my life in slothfulness, I lost the talent entrusted unto me by my Lord, and now deprived of divine grace, I cry unto thee, O pure one: by thy maternal prayers be thou a good intercessor for me unto God the Lover of mankind; remember thy righteous dispensation concerning thy servants, and ask remission of transgressions for them that honor thee with love and piously bow down before the cave of thy childbirth.

*Stepénna, the 1st Antiphon of the 4th Tone.*
*Prokeimenon, Tone 4:*
Precious in the sight of the Lord is the death of His venerable ones.
*Verse:* What shall I render unto the Lord for all [that He has rendered unto me]?
*Let every breath praise the Lord.*
*Gospel according to Luke, Composite 44 [Luke 9:28–36].*
*After the 50th Psalm, Sticheron, Tone 6: (Look at Vespers):*
Who can recount as is meet thy labors and ascetic feats *Service to the Venerable Fathers*

*Canon of the Feast, on 6; and of the Venerable, on 6.*
*Tone 4. Ode 1. Heirmos:*[^88]
Chant, all ye peoples, unto Him that delivered Israel from the bitter servitude of Pharaoh, and guided them with unwetted feet through the deep of the sea, a song of victory: for He is glorified!

*Troparia:*
Let us all chant, having come together into this most honorable temple, to praise the memory of God's servant: for gloriously has he been glorified in the name of Christ his Lord.

Taking the angelic habit upon thyself, angelically wast thou recognized in the cave, O father; and digging with thine own hands many places for the departed brethren, thou didst pass from them unto the place of the marvelous tabernacle, to sing unto the Lord: He is gloriously glorified!

That thou mightest have death before thine eyes, O father, thou didst dig graves for the brethren that departed unto Christ, carrying out the dust upon thy shoulders: therefore after thy falling asleep, precious in the sight of the Lord was thy death, O blessed one, and with the angels thou singest unto God Who is glorified in His saints: For He is glorified!

*Glory:*
For the sake of love toward all didst thou labor, meticulously preparing graves, receiving nothing from anyone, but only repayment from God: therefore now is Thy reward great in heaven, O venerable one, where thou singest unto God: For He is glorified!

*Both now:*
Who can worthily praise thee, the most glorious Mother of God, in all generations! Therefore neither do I dare beyond my mind, but bring only the angelic greeting: Rejoice!

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*[^89]
Establish me, O Christ, upon the immovable rock of Thy commandments, and enlighten me with the light of Thy countenance: for there is none holy above Thee, O Lover of mankind.

*Troparia:*
Established upon the rock of the daily commandments, desiring to be a house of the Holy Spirit, thou didst bind thy loins with irons, O blessed one, which on every day, as thy soul traversed, inflamed thee to sing: There is none holy above Thee, O Lord!

Having set up pure prayer as gates, and as a firm wall severe fasting: (*for no fermented mead, but only water didst thou drink*) an unapproachable and walled temple against enemies wast thou shown to be, O father, whom thou didst conquer unto the end: with gladness thou didst sing: There is none holy above Thee, O Lord!

Vigil, fasting, and girding the loins with irons taking upon thyself as armor, thou didst go forth to battle against death by carnal passions; therefore the Lord granted thee to desire by them, as thou didst cry out: There is none holy above Thee, O Lord!

*To Mark and Theophilus*

...to grant it, as thou didst cry out: There is none holy above Thee, O Lord.

*Glory:*
As a captive, death was subject unto thee, O father: for without harm it returned the dead that submitted to thee, and they remained alive until thou didst command them to sleep again, and to abide in the graves until the general resurrection.

*Both now:*
Me who am taken captive by invisible enemies, deliver, O Sovereign Lady, by thy swift intercession, and by power driving them away as with fire: that being saved through thee, I may ever sing to thee: There is no other protectress; rejoice, all-pure Sovereign Lady of the world!

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 8:*
Living by abstinence, thou didst subdue natural passions unto the spirit, being an angel in the flesh, and fulfilling in deed the things commanded in the Law: therefore didst thou receive the gift of wonderworking from God to raise the dead unto life by a word; and having thyself departed unto eternal life, pray for us, O father, that we who honor with love thy holy memory may be delivered from eternal death, O Mark worthy of wonder.

*Glory, both now: Theotokion:*
Having conceived beyond nature, and beyond nature given birth unto thy Creator and God, pray for us who constantly sin, that being liberated, we may sing to thee with love, O pure Virgin Mother.

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*[^90]
Foreseeing from on high the radiance of the mystery of Thy dispensation, O Christ, Habakkuk divinely beholding it cried out clearly: Thou hast gone forth for the salvation of Thy people, O Lover of mankind!

*Troparia:*
Great are the mysteries of Thy word, O Christ, for Thou didst say: He that believes on Me, the works that I do shall he do also; as Thou Thyself didst raise up the dead Lazarus by a word, so also unto Thy servant Thou gavest power to raise the dead by an utterance, and to pour oil upon himself, to the astonishment of all that stood by, O Lover of mankind!

Our God is peace, and the unworthiness of enmity has He dissolved, that there should be no enmity against His servant on account of the grave prepared for the dead man, by the wonderworking of his holy words; for he moved the spirit of his servant to say unto the dead man: Brother, the place is narrow, move thyself, and pour oil upon thyself; and immediately the word was confirmed by the deed, and all cried out: Glory to Thee, O Lover of mankind!

With simplicity of heart and in deep humility the venerable one cried out, and the Lord heard him, and commanded the dead man to receive his spirit and to do what was commanded; for he poured oil upon himself, and falling asleep *Service to the Venerable Fathers*

...again returned the vessel; which all beholding with fear and dread, cried out: Glory to Thy dispensation, O Lover of mankind!

*Glory:*
The prayer of Thy humble cave-dweller, O Lord, passed through the clouds: whom we also in compunction bring unto Thee, and ask: Be merciful unto us, Thy servants; take away enmity from Thy people; give Thy peace for our salvation, O Lover of mankind.

*Both now:*
Thou that didst give birth unto the eternal Life, and through Him that was condemned didst quicken all: quicken us that are deadened by the venom of sin, and have compassion through the oil of thy mercy, that we may cry unto thee, O Sovereign Lady: Thou art the cause and deliverance of our life.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*[^91]
Rising early from the night we hymn Thee, O Christ, Co-beginningless with the Father and Savior of our souls: grant peace unto the world, O Lover of mankind.

*Troparia:*
Rising early from the night in prayer unto God, O blessed one, having been exhausted, thou didst not prepare a grave for the dead man: therefore thou didst say: Go, say unto the dead man to wait until morning, until I dig a place, and we bury him, glorifying the Lover of mankind.

According to thy word, O venerable one, when he that was sent said: Brother, Mark says unto thee, rest yet a little, immediately the dead man opened his eyes, while all looked on, and remained until morning: Oh, the wonders! Even as Thou Thyself, O God, hast dominion over life and death, so also dost Thou give Thy servants to have dominion, O Lover of mankind!

Laboring night and day, thou didst prepare a place for burial, and saidst, O father: Say unto the dead man, yield thy spirit unto God: and he, having obeyed thee, closed his eyes and departed, and all were astonished, and seeing Thy power, glorified God the Lover of mankind.

*Glory:*
A strange wonder, that the dead should raise the dead: but for them that love God, all things work together for good, which all happen for our edification, that with warmer love we may run unto the Good One, and with the venerable glorify Him, as the compassionate Lover of mankind.

*Both now:*
A strange wonder, how within thy flesh-bearing womb thou didst receive the divine fire, O Virgin, and didst remain unconsumed, even as of old the unburnt bush; which came to pass, that upon thee, O pure one, the mystery of God's dispensation might be fulfilled.

*To Mark and Theophilus*

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*[^92]
Thou didst save the Prophet from the whale, O Lover of mankind: lead me up also from the depth of transgressions, I pray Thee.

*Troparia:*
Hearing of the defilement of love of glory, thou didst command the departed John, O blessed one, to give up his place to Theophilus: Oh, the wonders! Having obeyed, the dead man moved unto a lower place, and all were filled with fear; but thou, O father, with humility didst bow down, saying: From tormenting enmity lead me up, O God, I pray Thee!

When Theophilus fell down at thy feet and asked for forgiveness, thou didst say, O venerable one: The Lord Who takes away enmity commanded the dead man for the sake of love to yield seniority unto thee; but that thou mayest be brought forth from the noetic whale that devours thee with the love of glory, pray thou unto the Lover of mankind!

Thy word was as thunder, O father, whose terror fearing, Theophilus thought that falling down there he would die; but coming a little to himself, and entering into his cell, he remained the rest of his years weeping, saying: From corruption lead me up, O God!

*Glory:*
Thy instruction, O most blessed father, brought Theophilus into so great humility, that no one was able to raise him up from weeping; but tears became food and drink unto him, for day and night he cried out: From falling into sin lead me up, O God, I pray Thee!

*Both now:*
Me who have slipped into the corruption of voluptuousness and have no compunction, grant, O Sovereign Lady, streams of tears, that having washed away the defilement of my soul, I may appear pure before the face of God, when His glory shall appear unto me.

*Kontakion, Tone 8:*
An excellent physician and wonderworker let us the faithful bless with love, asking him that by his prayers he may heal our passions, both of soul and body; for he has grace from God for this, and drives away evil spirits from all who faithfully have recourse to his tomb and cry: Rejoice, O Mark, healer of our infirmities!

*Ikos:*
Crying unto the Creator of angels and of all creation, and having ever served Him upon earth by an angel-like life, and now with the angels unceasingly chanting the thrice-holy hymn, we praise thee, O blessed one, thus: Rejoice, thou that didst renounce all earthly things; rejoice, thou that didst wing all thy mind on high! Rejoice, for as one dead livingly thou didst bury thyself in a cave; rejoice, for therein thou didst pour out many labors and sweats! Rejoice, thou that didst dig graves therein for many saints; rejoice, thou that didst raise the dead by thy command *Service to the Venerable Fathers*

...rejoice, thou that didst make the dead John move from his place; rejoice, thou that didst guide Theophilus unto salvation! Rejoice, thou that didst acquire nothing upon earth; rejoice, thou that hast imperishable riches in heaven! Rejoice, thou that didst satisfy thy humble body with a single prosphora and moderate water with a cup; rejoice, thou that by thy cross didst heal many passions! Rejoice, thou that grantest help in necessities unto all who have recourse to thy tomb; rejoice, O Mark, healer of our infirmities!

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*[^93]
When the holy servers heard the concord of the tormentor's people agreeing together, hearing the youths of David singing songs, they destroyed the tormentor's impious command, and changed the flame into dew, singing the hymn: Supremely exalted God of our fathers and our God, blessed art Thou!

*Troparia:*
When the time of prayer unto God came upon thee, O father, thou didst call the blessed Theophilus, saying: Forgive me, for I have grieved thee; for lo, I now depart, but do thou pray for me, that we both may be vouchsafed to sing with the saints: O God, blessed art Thou!

Thy words added weeping unto him that wept, O father: for not enduring to be left by thee, he said: I know that the Lord will entreat Thee, if Thou wilt desire to have me sing with thee: O God, blessed art Thou!

Comforting him that wept, thou didst say, O venerable one, that he should not sorrow, nor seek to see sensible light: for weeping with noetic eyes over sins, he shall see intelligible light, and be vouchsafed the blessedness of them that weep; for they that weep shall be comforted, singing: O God, blessed art Thou!

*Glory:*
For the comfort of him that sorrowed, thou didst announce unto him, O father, this sign of his departure unto God: that three days before his end he should recover his sight, and so pass unto the places devoid of weeping, to sing with the saints in unapproachable light: O God, blessed art Thou!

*Both now:*
Being in the sorrow of my despair, I have no comfort; therefore unto thee, O Sovereign Lady of the world, do I flee, saying: O my joy, deliver me from them that have compassed me about, for many dogs have surrounded me, and there is none that can deliver me; rejoice, O blessed Virgin!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*[^94]
Unto Him that preserved the children from the flame of the burning fiery furnace, and descended unto them in the appearance of an angel, chant unto the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparia:*
The Holy Spirit Who grants the gift of prophecy brought thy words to pass *To Mark and Theophilus*

...thy words, O venerable one: after thy falling asleep, thou didst commit thy spirit into the hands of God: before Whom now standing, thou singest with the Prophets: Chant unto the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

Thy words were ever unto the blessed Theophilus more refined than silver, which remembering, though he wept greatly, yet he was not able to extinguish them, as they burned in his heart; for day by day he looked for his departure, desiring to depart unto the Lord, with praises to supremely exalt Him unto all ages.

A whole gift of compunction, and an inexhaustible fountain, by thy words, O blessed one, didst thou dig in the heart of Theophilus: for as much as he poured forth in tears, so much did the streams of tears multiply, wherewith he quenched the passionate flame that hindered him from singing unto the Lord unto all ages.

*We bless the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit: the Lord.*

O hospitable Light, Holy Trinity, Thou didst fulfill the promise of Thy servant Mark: Thou didst enlighten the pupils of him that wept, and rightly, O father, the light with sensible eyes, but with noetic eyes his departure unto Thee, his God, Whom with all his heart he desired to sing with the saints unto all ages.

*Both now:*
Thou that didst give birth unto the true Light, O all-pure one, me who wander in sinful darkness and have no vision of repentance, enlighten by thy grace: for thou art radiant, swiftly showing forth light unto them that are in darkness, O Virgin Mother of God.

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*[^95]
Thee, the luminary of the Light and Mother of God, the most marvelous glory and higher than all creatures, in hymns do we magnify.

*Troparia:*
According to thy word, O father, Theophilus, knowing his departure three days beforehand, uttered words of repentance; and immediately, for the soothing of his weeping, an angel stood before him, showing a vessel filled with tears, blessing his repentance as acceptable unto God.

By thy instruction, O father, God-wise Theophilus in his life was radiant with tears; and having departed unto the heavenly mansions, joyfully he reaps the sheaves, and receives now the recompense of his labors, blessed by all unto all ages.

In the vale of weeping ye chose, O right blessed fathers, all the years of your life to labor together with tears, having acquired the pearl of great price, Jesus, by Whom having redeemed yourselves from eternal death, now in a radiant place ye rejoice, being blessed.

*Glory:*
The pair of fathers, Mark and Theophilus, do we bring unto Thee in prayer, [Christ God] *Service to the Venerable Fathers*

...O Christ God, Who willest and workest in duality: receiving their supplication, and preserving us from the enemy's craftiness by Thy Holy Spirit, direct us to magnify Thee unto all ages.

*Both now:*
How shall we bless thee, O pure Virgin, whom the heavenly hosts are not able worthily to bless? How shall we glorify her that is more glorious than all generations, and the blessed Mother of all Christians?

*Exapostilarion:*
Thy tears were as fragrant myrrh, wherewith thou didst cleanse the defilement of sensual pleasure; and now in the mansions on high, shining like a hospitable star, illumine us that dwell in the darkness of the love of sin, and pray, O Theophilus our father, with blessed Mark, unto Christ the Lover of mankind, that walking here in the light of His commandments, we may be vouchsafed to behold the light of heavenly glory.

*Glory, both now: Of the Feast.*
Our Savior, the Dayspring of daysprings, has visited us from on high, and we that were in darkness and shadow have found the truth: for the Lord was born of the Virgin.

*At the Praises, Stichera on 6: Of the Feast, on 3; and of the Venerable, 3, Tone 8. Automela:*
Every sweetness of this life for the sake of the sweetness on high thou didst spit upon, having emulated the hard path of the right blessed fathers, and the furnace of passions by thy many labors thou didst strive to quench, having become an honorable adornment of them that pray: therefore rejoicing now with the angels on high, pray thou for us, O venerable Mark.

By the narrow and tight way from earth unto heavenly glory didst thou journey; unto many ascetic feats and afflictions for the sake of divine love didst thou give thyself: therefore now standing before the throne of the Blessed One, thou enjoyest His splendors; and unto us that brightly honor thy memory, grant by thy prayers communion in divine things and the reception of eternal joy, O venerable Mark.

Ascending noetically unto the unsetting light, O glorious one, and being delivered from the darkness of life, before the omnipresent thrice-sunlit Light with the ranks on high thou hast stood: whence, that we also may be illumined by the beam of the splendor sent down, unto us that bow down before the shrine of thy relics and glorify thine honorable falling asleep, ask thou by thy prayers, O venerable Mark.

*To Mark and Theophilus*

*Glory, Tone 1:*
The good vines of Christ's vineyard, that have budded forth profitable clusters for us, from which flows forth fragrant wine making glad the hearts of them that have recourse thereto, let us hasten to praise with love: beholding the cross of the one and his iron chains, we kiss the relics that bore them, and receive healing of passions; and encompassing the other, we remember his tears, and receive compunction. O good assembly of right blessed fathers! Therefore we beseech you, O God-bearing Mark with Theophilus, as ye stand before Christ God, ask for your servants to receive remission of sins and great mercy.

*Both now: Of the Feast, the same Tone:*
Heaven and earth today are united, Christ being born; today God has come upon earth, and man has ascended into the heavens; today He is seen in the flesh Who is invisible by nature for the sake of man. Therefore we also, singing praise, cry out unto Him: Glory to God in the highest, and on earth peace; for Thy coming has granted it, O our Savior: glory to Thee!

*Great Doxology, and Dismissal. At the Liturgy, the service to the venerable, Common.*

*(Vacat — Fol. 41r tail: Woodcut vignette depicting a baroque foliate cartouche with acanthus leaves, flower basket, and scrolls) (Woodcut Engraving: Saint Gregory the Wonderworker of the Caves (*Прⷣ: Григо́рїй Чꙋдотво́рецъ Пече́рскїй*) standing in monastic habit with hands lifted in prayer. Below, two narrative panels illustrate his life and martyrdom, inscribed: "Григо́рїй ѿ кнѧ́зѧ Ростисла́ва въ во́дꙋ ᲂу҆топле́нъ, привяза́ше ка́мень" [Gregory drowned in the water by Prince Rostislav, who tied a stone to him].)*[^96]

---

# MONTH OF JANUARY

*On the 8th Day: Service to our Venerable Father Gregory the Wonderworker of the Caves.*[^97]

*At Vespers, "Blessed is the man," 1st Antiphon. On "Lord, I have cried," Stichera on 6, Tone 4. Podoben: "O all-praised martyrs" (Прехвали́нїи мч҃нцы):*[^98]
O father Gregory, inflamed with the fire of love toward God, thou didst burn up the passions that extinguish the spirit; whence, living in infirm flesh, thou didst attain unto the angelic life, with whom now dwelling, pray thou unto Christ God to grant peace and great mercy unto our souls *To Gregory the Wonderworker of the Caves*

...to grant peace and great mercy unto our souls.

O father Gregory, attending unto the words of David, thou gavest no rest unto thy body, nor slumber unto thine eyelids, until thou wast cleansed from passions, and receiving the ladder of divine vision, didst ascend unto the purest light, where now living, pray thou to grant peace and great mercy unto our souls.

O father Gregory, the working of thy wonders traversed the whole land of Rus, like a loud-voiced trumpet crying out and calling together the feast-loving people to praise thine honorable memory: with whom we also bring praise, and bow down before the shrine of thy relics, praying to obtain through thee peace and great mercy.

*Glory, Tone 8:*
Honoring thy guided life, O host of monastics, we marvel, and glorify God Who has glorified thee: for He granted thee manifold wonderworking, wherewith thou didst drive away demons from men, and didst lead sinners unto correction. Whom also entreat for us that honor the day of thine honorable falling asleep, to grant mercy in the day of judgment.

*Both now: Of the Feast, Tone 1:*
The hand of the Baptist trembled when it touched Thy most pure head; the Jordan River turned back, not daring to minister unto Thee: for if it was awed by Joshua the son of Nun, how should it not be fearful of its Creator? But Thou didst fulfill all the dispensation, O our Savior, that Thou mightest save the world by Thy manifestation, O only Lover of mankind!

*Entrance: Prokeimenon of the day, and readings: (see fol. 30v).*[^99]

*At the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 6. Podoben: "On the third day" (Трїдне́венъ):*
Our nature, decayed by passions, the Master renews, having descended into the waters: and thee, O father, who didst come unto the streams of water, the Lover of mankind renewed, having translated thee unto eternal life.
*Verse:* The righteous man shall be glad in the Lord, and shall hope in Him.
Emulating Christ Who renewed human nature by water and the Spirit, O venerable one, therefore for the sake of the truth thou wast drowned in the water; having finished which course, now thou rejoicest with Christ beside the waters of rest.
*Verse:* The righteous man shall be glad in the Lord, and shall hope in Him.
Standing again in the streams of Jordan, the Master looses the human race from bonds *Service to the Venerable Father*

...from the bonds of hades: Who also loosed thee with water, O blessed one, when He led forth thy spirit from the prison of the flesh.

*Glory, Tone 5:*
Thy life, adorned with the splendor of divine virtues, causes the land of Rus to marvel; and the shrine of thy holy relics emits beams of wonders unto all who approach with faith. Grant therefore unto us also a sign of thy love; for though thou hast departed, yet spiritually thou ever abidest, and beholding the warm supplication of all, by thy prayers thou grantest grace and great mercy from Christ God.

*Both now: Of the Feast, Tone 1:*
Beholding Thee coming unto him to cleanse the sin of the world in the river and with fire, the Baptist was struck with awe and trembled, crying aloud and saying: I dare not lay hold upon Thy most pure head: sanctify Thou me, O Master, by Thy divine manifestation, O only Lover of mankind!

*Troparion, Tone 3:*
O receiver of great gifts, and by the power of God worker of great wonders, O most marvelous father Gregory: as thou hast great boldness toward Christ God, unceasingly pray unto Him for us, that He may grant remission of sins unto our souls.
*Glory, both now: Of the Feast.*

*At Matins, on "God is the Lord": Troparion of the Feast, twice. Glory: Of the Saint. Both now: Of the Feast.*
*After the 1st Sticology, Sessional Hymn, Tone 1. Podoben: "Quickly anticipate" (Ско́ро предварѝ):*
Desiring to inherit the blessedness of them that fear the Lord, in deed Thou didst fulfill the commandments of God: all thy substance that Thou hadst, Thou didst disperse unto the glory of God, even unto them that deceitfully demanded it, O God-wise Gregory, most rich in virtues: therefore thy memory and thy righteousness abide unto ages.
*Glory, both now: Of the Feast.*

*After the 2nd Sticology, Sessional Hymn, Tone 3. Podoben: "At the beauty of virginity" (Красотѣ̀ дѣ́вства):*
When Thou, the Good One, camest unto Jordan, O Christ, the servant who faithfully fled unto Thee and served in righteousness, drowned by command of the prince, entered into unquenchable life: having whom as a swift intercessor before Thy majesty, Thee our God do we glorify in all things.
*Glory, both now: Of the Feast.*

*After the Polyeleos, Sessional Hymn, Tone 8. Podoben: "Wisdom" (Премꙋ́дрости):*
Desiring the blessedness of the righteous and choir-standing with the angelic ranks, despising all things in the world, thou didst take no thought even for life itself: therefore thou didst not feel ashamed before the soul of the prince, but *To Gregory the Wonderworker of the Caves*

...but didst admonish him as a father, leading him unto repentance: by whom innocently drowned in the water, through fire and water thou didst depart unto Him that clothes Himself with waters, and Thou, O God, didst lead him out into Thy rest: where now living, pray that we also who honor with love thy holy memory may not be deprived thereof, O Gregory worthy of wonder.

*Glory, both now: Of the Feast.*
*Stepénna, the 1st Antiphon of the 4th Tone.*
*Prokeimenon, Tone 3:*
Precious in the sight of the Lord is the death of His venerable ones.
*Verse:* What shall I render unto the Lord for all that He has rendered unto me?
*Let every breath praise the Lord.*
*Gospel according to Luke, Composite 24 [Luke 6:17–23].*
*After the 50th Psalm, the Evening Sticheron, Tone 8:*
The host of monastics...

*Canon of the Feast, with the heirmos, on 6; and of the Venerable, on 6, Tone 2.*
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*[^100]
He uncovered the bottom of the deep, and on dry land leads His own, having covered in it the adversaries, the Lord strong in battles: for He is glorified!

*Troparia:*
With the light of Thy grace, O Christ, Who didst illumine Thy righteous ones and with the beams of their deeds didst irradiate the ends of the world: illumine my mind darkened by ignorance, that honoring the memory of Thy servant, I may sing a hymn unto Thee: For He is glorified!

With the fire of love Thou didst inflame Thine ever-memorable father to run after Thee, and having found Him Whom he desired, now joyfully he sings: For He is glorified!

Desiring to emulate the life of the great fathers, thou didst come, O Gregory, into the monastery of the Mother of God, where taking upon thyself the angelic habit, thou didst accomplish in thyself the forms of virtue; and thy soul became a fair bride unto the immortal Bridegroom, Who has now appeared at the Jordan.

*Glory:*
For the sake of the many ascetic feats and labors wrought by thee, which through the count of time are innumerable, thou didst receive power from God over demons, O venerable one, even as they cried out: Unprepared art thou for us, Gregory, walled about by prayer and the terror of the Cross!

*Both now:*
For the sake of the prayers of thy servant, wall me about by thy power, O pure Virgin, and show me unapproachable unto enemies: for thou didst give birth unto the unapproachable God, Who in human nature for my sake now stands naked at the Jordan.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*[^101]
The Lord Who gives strength unto our kings, and exalts the horn of His anointed, is born of the Virgin, and comes unto baptism: unto Him let us, the faithful, cry out: There is none holy as our God!

*Service to the Venerable Father*

*Troparia:*
Beholding the power of thy prayer, the spirits of wickedness under heaven bitterly cried out against thee, and pondered how they might do thee harm: but thou, O father, didst not fear their terror, but criedst out unto Him that strengthens thee: The Lord is my strength unto salvation!

The rulers of darkness and sons of Gehenna gnashed their teeth at thee who wast illumined with divine light, and set manifold snares for thy feet, fearing lest they might grieve thee even a little, but they could not: for unceasingly thou didst cry out: There is none, O father, as our God!

In the manner of thieves the fierce demons that assault the servants of God led evil men to rob thee, O blessed one; but what could they take from thee who hadst nothing, except books preserved for the praise of God?

*Glory:*
Darkened with the darkness of evil-doing, thieves coming in the night unto thy cell, O father, waited until thou shouldest go forth unto morning singing in the church, that then they might snatch thy goods: whose coming having perceived, thou didst pray, and they were weighed down with sleep for five days and five nights.

*Both now:*
The gloom of my soul, wherewith the demons darken me, drive away, O Mother of God: for thou didst give birth unto the Light-giver, Who by the radiance of His countenance enlightens every man that comes into the world.

*Kontakion and Ikos of the Feast.*
*Then, Sessional Hymn to the Venerable, Tone 4:*
The divine light shining around thee drove away its beams, and pursued them that walk in darkness, not permitting them to approach thee: for everyone that does evil deeds comes not to the light, lest he be reproved: but the enemy of God's yoke fell, and was put underfoot forever: from whom that we may be preserved, pray thou unto Christ God, O venerable father.

*Glory, both now: Of the Feast.*
Jesus born of the Virgin Mary, and baptized in Jordan by John: the Holy Spirit descended upon Him in the form of a dove: therefore the Prophet with the angels cried out: Glory to Thy coming, O Christ; glory to Thy kingdom; glory to Thy dispensation, O only Lover of mankind!

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*[^102]
I heard, O Lord, Thy voice which Thou didst speak, the voice of one crying in the wilderness, when Thou didst thunder over many waters, bearing witness unto Thy Son, wholly being filled with the Spirit that descended, he cried out: Thou art Christ, the wisdom and power of God!

*To Gregory the Wonderworker of the Caves*

*Troparia:*
Thou wast heard by God, O father, for the prayer of thy humility passed through the clouds: therefore the Most High, receiving thy prayers, preserved thee from every evil: for thou didst confess the power and wisdom of God to be one.

Having fallen asleep with their own sleep, and being unable to snatch anything, the first thieves, by thy prohibition, O blessed one, whom thou after five days and five nights didst awaken, saying: How long will ye suffer? sleep! but they could not, being held by an invisible divine power.

Beholding the thieves perish by spiritual and bodily famine, O father, thou didst not render evil for evil, but fulfilling that which is written, thou didst feed the hungry enemies with bread, and didst dismiss them unto their homes, that at least thus they might be brought to wisdom, and confess how in truth the wisdom and power of God work in thee.

*Glory:*
Unto God didst thou commit the judgment of them that grieved thee, Whose vengeance did not spare the thieves, but brought report unto the prince, who, seizing them, was about to deliver them unto torments: but thou, knowing human frailty, gavest thy books unto the judge for a ransom, and deliveredst them from bitter torments.

*Both now:*
A fierce adversary, conscience contending with me concerning sins, with which I did not come to agreement by correction when I was on the path of life, now has dragged me unto the Judge, that I may receive judgment according to deeds, and not go forth thence until I have reckoned unto the last farthing. Woe is me! Who shall deliver me from this evil, if thou, O pure one, grant me not help?

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*[^103]
Jesus the Author of life comes to loose the condemnation of Adam the first-formed; needing no purification as God, He cleanses the fallen in Jordan: wherein having lulled to sleep enmity, He grants the peace that surpasses all understanding.

*Troparia:*
Jesus the Author of life, guiding sinners unto the right path, gave the thieves an upright spirit, wherewith being softened they came unto repentance, and giving themselves unto the labor of the monastery of the Mother of God, were reconciled unto God, Who grants peace unto them that love His law.

Having loved Thee, the only Author of life, His word didst thou fulfill in deed, O father: for all that thou hadst thou didst sell, that no one might fall into distress again on their account, and the price thereof thou didst distribute unto the poor *Service to the Venerable Father*

...and by their hands thou didst carry it into the treasury where a thief does not approach, but it remains intact.

Again the enemy worked mischief against thee, O venerable one, through other thieves, who, having entered into thy garden, filled their vessels with fruit, but wishing to depart they were unable, being held by an invisible cord; and weighed down by their burdens, they remained standing for two days and two nights.

*Glory:*
They that expected to hide their evil-doing, having this plunder and the conviction of unrighteousness upon their shoulders, cried out, asking forgiveness from thee, O father, promising to spend the rest of their years in repentance.

*Both now:*
I desired to hide myself from the all-seeing God by mine evil deeds, but the conviction that exists in my inward parts, like a great burden, uncovers my shame: knowing not what else to do, I cry unto thee: Have mercy on me, O compassionate Mother, even though shame has covered my face.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*[^104]
The voice of the Word, the candlestick of the Light, the dayspring of the Sun, the Forerunner in the wilderness cries out repentance unto all peoples, and says: Cleanse yourselves beforehand, for lo, Christ stands by, delivering the world from corruption!

*Troparia:*
The voice of thy words, O father, troubled the bones of them that stole, and their soul fainted from fear: for they were as though struck down by frost, motionless in one place, and there was none that could deliver them from so great an evil.

When the thieves heard the preaching voice calling unto repentance, they fell down before thee, O blessed one, with tears, asking for compunction: and thou, O father, having been moved with compunction, saidst with a gentle voice: Blessed is God Who strengthens you unto a good work, and may He loose you, Who delivers all from corruption!

When Christ appeared at the Jordan, publicans and all sinners came unto Him, and received the renewal of their life: so also the thieves, having labored under thine instruction, O father, began repentance in novelty of life, and completed it by labor.

*Glory:*
A good example didst thou give by thyself unto thy people, not to avenge oneself by Satan: therefore thy servant, being forced a third time by evil men, avenged not himself, but being a guileless son, gave what they demanded, committing unto perdition the one whom heaven delivered not.

*Both now:*
Drawn by mine own will, I fell into the pit of destruction, and I have no hope of being raised up: unto thee do I stretch forth my voice, O Sovereign Lady: pray for me unto thy Son, Who today appeared at the Jordan, delivering not only me, but the whole world from corruption.

*To Gregory the Wonderworker of the Caves*

*Kontakion, Tone 7:*
Forbidding the lawless to do lawlessness, thou wast cast by them into the streams of the river, O Gregory worthy of wonder; where receiving a blessed end, now with the angels thou standest before the Master's throne: pray thou for our souls.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*[^105]
The godly youths that had communion with the burning fiery furnace, the whistling dew-bearing spirit preserved unharmed, and the descent of God's angel: therefore bedewed in the flame, thankfully they chanted: O supremely praised Lord and God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!

*Troparia:*
Unto the Hebrew youths of old in the furnace, who manfully trampled the flame and strongly opposed the tormentor, but not to the enemy's counselor: yielding themselves unto the passionate flame, they thought before thee, O father, to hide their guilt, which the blessed God revealed unto thee.

Beholding prophetically by the Spirit what was inside the hearts of the thieves, that they had an intention against righteousness, thou didst weep, O blessed one, foreseeing the wretchedness of him whose day of destruction had arrived, and unto the liar the blessed God gave no will to escape.

The Holy Spirit is able to open even unclean mouths, that they may speak the truth for the correction of others: therefore when thou didst ask, O venerable one, by what death the son of perdition should die, even without knowing it the thieves prophesied that he would be hanged upon a tree, which also came to pass in its own time.

*Glory:*
The sting of death is sin, which kills a man, and there is no healing for this until life is preserved by repentance: for though thou gavest thy books as a ransom for the thieves, O father, yet the guilty one escaped not destruction, but rather was his memory blotted out from the books of the living.

*Both now:*
Being the intelligible book, O Mary Bride of God, in which by the finger of the all-holy Spirit the Father's Word was written, vouchsafe all of us, O blessed one, to be written in the books of the living.

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*[^106]
A most glorious mystery did the Babylonian furnace show forth, pouring forth dew; even as Jordan was to receive with its streams the immaterial fire, and to embrace the Creator baptized in the flesh: Whom the peoples bless, and supremely exalt unto all ages!

*Troparia:*
Foreseeing thee, O father, discerning the mystery of their deceitful heart, the thieves shut thee in the cave, and opened their thought unto evil-doing *Service to the Venerable Father*

...unto evil-doing; but when they began to gather the fruits of others' labors, the judgment of God overtook Him concerning whom Thou didst prophesy: that he should not bless the Lord with the living unto all ages.

Desiring to devour thy labors, O father, like a wild boar, the thief stretched forth his hand to steal from the tree, but was not sustained: for the branch broke, and immediately by hanging the tree of his life withered, so that he could no longer yield the fruit of repentance unto all ages.

Let no one judge an unrighteous judgment, nor speak a lie: for God is not mocked. The mocking thieves, seeing one worthy of hanging upon a tree, and that what was spoken had come to pass: but thou, O most blessed one, supremely exaltest the Lord unto all ages.

*We bless the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit: the Lord.*

Seeing that nothing was hidden from thee, O father, falling down they asked forgiveness, that a similar destruction might not come upon them also: but thou, O father, didst admonish the thieves no longer to steal, but rather to labor for the brethren, and to eat blessed fruit, if they desire to supremely exalt the Lord unto all ages.

*Both now:*
Having eaten of the unblessed fruit, I faint with spiritual death: beholding thee therefore, the Mother of life, I stretch forth my voice: add unto me the life-giving weeping of repentance, that coming a little to myself, I may find life, and supremely exalt thee, O Mother of God, unto all ages.

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*[^107]
Every tongue is at a loss to praise thee as is meet, and an intellect transcendent is dazed to sing of thee, O Mother of God: but being good, accept our faith; for thou knowest our divine love: for thou art the protectress of Christians: thee do we magnify.

*Troparia:*
The enemy was at a loss how to ensnare thee, O father: he brought the foolish servants of the prince, who, when thou wast drawing water in the river, mocked thee, speaking wretched words: but thou, O venerable one, foreseeing by the Spirit their destruction, didst admonish them not to speak foul things, but rather with their tongue to magnify God.

The boastful men were not corrected by thine instruction, O father, but rather mocked thee, insulting thee: therefore again with compassion didst thou admonish them, that they had rather need of prayer, for soon they should be drowned with their prince Rostislav.

Reprove not a fool lest he hate thee, says the Scripture, which also was fulfilled upon thee, O most blessed one: for the prince, hearing the death prophesied concerning himself by thee, taking it as a reproach, was wroth against *To Gregory the Wonderworker of the Caves*

...was wroth against thee, and held by fearlessness commanded thee to be drowned in the water.

*Glory:*
Unto Christ Who is now glorified by waters, by water thou didst depart, O father, and death cut down the tree of thy temporal life, which now in diverse ways blossoms beside the outgoings of living waters, and the foliage of thy prayers gladdens the Church: therefore thee, O father, do we magnify.

*Both now:*
In the temple of thy glory, preserve in peace them that bring Gabriel's greeting unto thee, O pure Virgin, and establish the scepters of the kingdom, granting them the power of thy grace unto the driving away of all corrupting passions, that we all may magnify thee.

*Exapostilarion:*
Thou that didst assimilate thyself unto the angels in life, O blessed one, even after thy falling asleep wast not separated from them: for the King on high commanded His angels after three days to bring thy much-suffering body out of the water into thy cell, and the working thereof to be seen in the holy tabernacles: where now living, remember us that honor thy memory, O most sacred father.

*Glory, both now: Of the Feast.*
The Savior, grace and truth, has appeared in the streams of Jordan, and has enlightened them that slept in darkness and shadow: for the unapproachable Light has come and appeared.

*At the Praises, Stichera on 4, Tone 1. Podoben: "Joy of the heavenly ranks" (Нб҃ныхъ чинѡ́въ):*[^108]
Having ended thy life with water and a stone tied unto thy neck, O blessed one, thou didst depart unto Christ Who stood upon the waters, that solid Cornerstone upon Whom Jerusalem was founded: having now thy dwelling there with the angels, remember them that honor with love thy most honorable memory. *(Twice)*

Obeying the law of God in this life, O venerable one, lawfully, as was meet, didst thou live, and having ended the life of the present age as a martyr, thou didst pass unto Christ the Lawgiver and Crowngiver, from Whom receiving an imperishable crown, now thou rejoicest with the angelic choirs: vouchsafe us also by thy prayers to be partakers with them of the mansions and of gladness.

Being bound hand and foot, and cast into the watery stream, thou wast loosed from carnal bonds; the Lord indeed led forth thy soul from this prison into the unwaning light, where the spirits of the righteous enjoy the vision of God *Service to the Venerable Father*

...having now given them rest: which that we also may obtain, we pray through thine intercession, O most blessed father.

*Glory: Tone 8:*
Having the unerring gift of prophecy, O venerable one, thou didst foresee the destruction of thy murderers, who, being instructed by thy words, were not corrected, nor were they terrified by the lack of thy blessing and prayer which assist in battles. Therefore as Pharaoh he sank into the water with his warriors, and was deprived of both lives, receiving reproach sevenfold into his bosom, and his memory perished. But do thou, O father, standing before the throne of God, grant a blessing unto thy servants, and prayer, unto them that bow down before thy wonderworking relics, and honor thy memory with praises.

*Both now: Of the Feast, the same Tone:*
O Lord, desiring to fulfill what Thou didst ordain from eternity, from all creation Thou didst receive ministers of Thy mystery: from the angels, Gabriel; from mankind, the Virgin; from the heavens, the star; and from the waters, the Jordan, wherein Thou didst destroy the iniquity of the world: O our Savior, glory to Thee!

*Great Doxology, and dismissal.*
*At the Liturgy, the common service to a Venerable.*

*(Woodcut Engraving: Saint Ephraim, Bishop of Pereyaslavl (*Ѡ С͠тъмъ Е҆фремѣ Е҆пⷭ҇копѣ Переꙗславскомъ*), standing in episcopal vestments holding a crosier and a model of a church. In the background, narrative panels show him journeying to Constantinople (*"Многа́жды с͠ты́й Е҆фре́мъ хожда́ше впꙋть в Царигра́дъ и па́ки возвраща́шеся в Печер: м:"* [Many times holy Ephraim went on the way to Constantinople and returned again to the Caves Monastery]) and kneeling in prayer outside the city of Pereyaslavl.)*[^109]

*In the Month of January, on the 28th Day:*
*Service to our Venerable Father Ephraim of the Caves, formerly Eunuch of Prince Izyaslav of Kyiv, and afterward Bishop of Pereyaslavl.*[^110]

*At Vespers, "Blessed is the man," 1st Antiphon. On "Lord, I have cried," Stichera, Tone 4. Podoben: "Called from on high" (Зва́нный свы́ше):*
When by the good pleasure of Jesus, the Chief of pastors on high, Thou wast called, O venerable one, then reckoning the corruptible things of the world as nothing, and leaving all fair dominion, Thou didst go by an irreversible course into the life devoid of worldly care *Service to the Venerable Father*

...into the life devoid of worldly care, by whose assistance, having departed as a good soldier, thou rejoicest now in the heavenly courts, dwelling with the ranks of the venerable, where make haste to offer supplication unto Christ God, that He may give peace unto the world and save our souls.

When by the anointing of the Holy Spirit thou wast chosen, O venerable Ephraim, to shepherd the noetic flock of the heavenly King, then as a good shepherd and teacher, continually guiding with good instruction the sheep entrusted unto thee, warding off enemies and nourishing them with the word of comfort, thou didst confirm them, showing thyself as a pattern unto them in word and deed; and having well pastured them upon spiritual pastures, thou didst lead them whole unto the Chief of pastors, Christ God, Who has passed through the heavens, Whom entreat to preserve and save us also from invisible enemies.

When the good pleasure of God kindled the hearts of all into love toward thee, O most blessed father, then by the desire of him that wore the diadem, by the common counsel of all, thou wast appointed hierarch unto the glorious city, wherein, that faithfully as a faithful servant thou mightest serve with thy keys, thou hadst great diligence concerning the erection of temples in thy bishopric; and this before all things thou didst account unto thyself the most honorable and lovely work, to multiply praise unto the glory of Christ our God, Whom entreat to save and enlighten our souls.

*Glory: Tone 6:*
Adornment of hierarchs, most blessed and most sacred father, excellent disciple of Christ, good shepherd who didst pasture thy flock upon spiritual pastures, O Ephraim worthy of wonder: now standing with the hierarchs before the throne of Christ, cease not to pray for us that chant thy venerable falling asleep.

*Both now: Theotokion:*
Who will not bless thee...
*Entrance. Prokeimenon of the day, and the readings:*

*The Reading from Proverbs. (Chapter 10):*[^111]
The memory of the just is with praises, and the blessing of the Lord is upon his head. Blessed is the man that has found wisdom, and the mortal that knows understanding. For it is better to purchase her than treasures of gold and silver. And she is more honorable than precious stones: and nothing honorable is worthy of her. For length of days and years of life are in her right hand: and in her left hand are riches and glory. Out of her mouth *To Ephraim, Bishop of Pereyaslavl*

...proceeds righteousness: and she bears law and mercy upon her tongue. Give ear therefore unto me, O children, for I speak honorable things: and blessed is the man that shall keep my ways: for my outgoings are the outgoings of life, and the will is prepared from the Lord. Therefore do I entreat you, and put forth my voice unto the sons of men: for I, wisdom, have set up; counsel, understanding, and knowledge have I called. Counsel is mine, and confirmation; understanding is mine, and strength is mine. I love them that love me, and they that seek me shall find grace. Understand craftiness, therefore, ye simple ones, and apply your hearts, ye uninstructed. Give ear unto me yet again, for I speak honorable things: and I will open right things from my lips; for my throat shall meditate truth, and lying lips are an abomination before me. All the words of my mouth are with righteousness: there is nothing crooked or perverse in them. They are all right to them that understand, and straight to them that find knowledge. For I teach you truth, that your hope may be in the Lord, and ye may be filled with the Spirit.

*The Reading from the Wisdom of Solomon. (Chapter 10):*[^112]
The mouth of the righteous drops wisdom, and the lips of wise men know grace. The mouth of the wise meditates wisdom: and righteousness delivers from death. When a righteous man dies, his hope perishes not. For a righteous son is born unto life, and in his good things he shall gather the fruit of righteousness. Light is ever unto the righteous, and from the Lord they shall find grace and glory. The tongue of the wise knows good things, and in their heart wisdom rests. The Lord loves venerable hearts, and all that are blameless in the way are acceptable unto Him. The wisdom of the Lord enlightens the face of the prudent. For she anticipates them that desire her, before they know her: and she is easily seen of them that love her. Whoso rises early unto her shall have no travail: and he that watches for her sake shall quickly be without care. For she goes about seeking such as are worthy of her, and shows herself favorably unto them in the ways. Malice shall never prevail against wisdom: for these things' sake I was an admirer of her beauty, and I loved her, and sought her out from my youth. And I sought to bring her to myself as a bride, for the Master of all loved her. For she is privy to the mysteries of the knowledge of God, and a chooser of His works. Her labors are virtues: for she teaches temperance and prudence, justice and fortitude, than which men can have nothing more profitable in life. And if *Service to the Venerable Father*

...And if a man desire much experience, she knows things of old, and considers things to come: she knows the subtleties of speeches, and the solutions of riddles: she foresees signs and wonders, and the events of seasons and years: and she is a good counselor unto all, for immortality is in her, and good report in communion with her words. Therefore I spoke unto the Lord, and prayed unto Him, and said from my whole heart: O God of the fathers and Lord of mercy, Who hast made all things by Thy word, and by Thy wisdom hast fashioned man, that he should have dominion over the creatures made by Thee, and order the world in holiness and righteousness: give me wisdom that sits by Thy thrones, and reject me not from among Thy servants: for I am Thy servant and son of Thine handmaid. Send her out of Thy holy heavens, and from the throne of Thy glory, that being present with me she may teach me what is well-pleasing before Thee: and she may guide me in understanding, and preserve me in her glory. For the thoughts of mortal men are all fearful, and their devices are fallible.

*The Reading from the Wisdom of Solomon. (Chapter 4):*[^113]
When the righteous is praised, the people shall rejoice: for the memory of him is immortal, because it is known with the Lord and with men: and his soul is pleasing unto the Lord. Love wisdom therefore, O ye men, and ye shall live: desire her, and ye shall be instructed: for the beginning of her is love, and the keeping of laws. Honor wisdom, that ye may reign unto the ages. I will tell you, and will not hide from you the mysteries of God: for He is the guide of wisdom, and the directer of the wise: and in every sense and work an artificer. And wisdom teaches all understanding, for in her is a spirit of understanding and holy, and the brightness of the everlasting Light, and the image of the goodness of God. She makes men friends of God and prophets. She is more beautiful than the sun, and above all the order of stars; being compared with the light, she is found before it. She delivered them that pleased her from troubles, and guided them in right paths. She gave them to have holy understanding, and preserved them from them that lay in wait, and gave them a mighty conflict. That all may know that godliness is stronger than all: and malice shall never overcome wisdom, neither shall judgment pass by, convincing the wicked. For they said within themselves, reasoning unrighteously: Let us oppress the righteous man, and let us not spare his holiness, nor reverence the ancient gray hairs of the aged. For let our strength be the law: and let us lie in wait *To Ephraim, Bishop of Pereyaslavl*

...and let us lie in wait for the righteous, because he is not for our turn, and he is contrary to our doings, and reproaches us with our offending the law, and objects to our infamy the transgressions of our education. He professes to have the knowledge of God, and he calls himself the child of the Lord. He was made to reprove our thoughts, and he is grievous unto us even to behold, for his life is not like other men's, his ways are of another fashion; we are esteemed of him as counterfeits: and he abstains from our ways as from filthiness, and pronounces the end of the just to be blessed. Let us see if his words be true, and let us test what shall happen in the end of him. Let us examine him with despitefulness and torture, that we may know his meekness and prove his patience; let us condemn him with a shameful death: for there shall be a visitation from his words. Such things did they imagine, and were deceived: for their own malice blinded them, and they knew not the mysteries of God, nor did they discern that Thou art the only God, having the power of life and death, and saving in the time of tribulation, and delivering from every evil; Who art compassionate and merciful, and givest grace unto Thy venerable ones, and with Thine arm resistest the proud.

*At the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 6. Podoben: "Having set aside all" (Всю̀ ѿло́жше):*[^114]
By the purity of thy life and the radiance of thy many-streamed ascetic feats, having illumined them that beheld the comeliness of thy face, thou didst draw all into love for thee; for thou wast like a radiant sun, another to them that are of the unwaning Light, and thou didst enter where the unapproachable Light shines: therefore, as thou hast boldness unto Christ God, pray thou for them that honor thy venerable memory, O most sacred father Ephraim.
*Verse:* Thy priests shall be clothed with righteousness, and Thy venerable ones shall rejoice.
By the gentleness and understanding of thy lowliness of mind, having ascended unto the transcendent Mind, thou didst enter into the Holy of Holies, as a venerable hierarch and as a blessed mystery-initiate; thou didst partake of the Holy Mysteries, whence by the light of the vision of God thou wast illuminated: therefore with boldness pray thou for us that sing thy memory, O Ephraim worthy of wonder.
*Verse:* The venerable ones shall boast in glory, and they shall rejoice upon their beds.
All thine understanding and diligence unto the comeliness of the Church of Christ Thou didst send forth, and this, as *Service to the Venerable Father*

...the heavenly, as with a golden chain thou didst adorn her with the teaching of thy words, and having girded her with thy God-inspired words, thou didst present her all-fair unto thy Master: Whom now diligently entreat to be delivered from corruption and afflictions, unto them that celebrate with faith thy most honorable memory, O most blessed hierarch Ephraim.

*Glory: Tone, the same [Tone 6]:*
On the auspicious day of thy feast, O venerable father, the sacred assemblies harmoniously rejoice and leap for joy, and the Church of Christ mystically dances, offering praises unto thee, and glorifying Christ God in thy memory.

*Both now: Theotokion:*
O Mother of God, thou art the true vine...

*Troparion, Tone 8:*
Burning in spirit with a journey unto the holy places, thou didst travel unto the Greek land, O hierarch Ephraim, whence returning with much profit, having transcribed the rule of the holy Studite Monastery, thou didst bring it unto this monastery as a precious gift, whereby even today the Russian Church is guided, and glorifies thee, her true provider.

*Glory, both now: Theotokion:*
Thou Who for our sake...

*At Matins, on "God is the Lord": Troparion of the Saint, twice. Glory, both now: Theotokion.*
*After the 1st Sticology, Sessional Hymn, Tone 1. Podoben: "Thy tomb" (Гро́бъ тво́й):*[^115]
Standing now in the highest before the unapproachable Light, unto us that praise thy memory, O most sacred father Ephraim, pray that we may obtain grace, and cleansing of transgressions, and in the day of the dread examination great mercy, that with thee we may be vouchsafed to glorify the only Lover of mankind.
*Glory, both now: Theotokion:*
The Creator and Savior of the world, born before the ages from the bosom of the Father, having in time come forth from thee, O Virgin Bride of God, renewed decayed human nature, and showed thee unto all as the mediatress of our salvation.

*After the 2nd Sticology, Sessional Hymn, Tone 1. Podoben: "The stone" (Ка́мени):*[^116]
The hard path in this life thou didst tread, O father, and the longed-for haven by thy many labors thou didst attain, and having entered into the transcendent heavenly expanse, thou didst leave unto us the ladder of thy virtuous ascent, O most blessed pastor Ephraim.
*Glory To Ephraim, Bishop of Pereyaslavl*

...*Glory, both now: Theotokion:*
Unto me who walk in the broad way of diverse temptations, and am not able to reach the haven of salvation, appear quickly, O all-pure Virgin: for thou art the good guide unto all that call upon thee.

*After the Polyeleos, Sessional Hymn, Tone 5. Podoben: "Co-beginningless Word" (Собеꙁнача́лное сло́во):*[^117]
By the sanctification of the passions of soul and body, in righteousness thou wast called a prophet, O venerable one; therefore, as one that escaped the drowning of the passionate sea, make haste for us who honor thy memory with love to make supplication unto Christ God, that we may receive grace and mercy, and the comfort of the liberation of all.
*Glory, both now: Theotokion:*
We diligently pray unto thee, O Virgin Bride of God: as thou hast boldness toward thy Son and God, ever pray for us, thine unprofitable servants, that we may find grace and mercy in the day of judgment.

*Then the Graduals, the 1st Antiphon of Tone 4. Prokeimenon, the same Tone:*
Thy priests shall be clothed with righteousness, and Thy venerable ones shall rejoice.
*Verse:* Give ear unto these words, all ye nations; give ear, all ye that inhabit the world.
Let every breath praise the Lord.
*The Gospel from Matthew. After Psalm 50, Sticheron, Tone 6: "Adornment of hierarchs" (look on "Lord, I have cried").*

*Canon to the Mother of God with the Heirmos on 6, and to the Saint on 6. Tone 6. Ode 1. Heirmos:*[^118]
Come, ye people, let us chant a song unto Christ God, Who divided the sea, and guided the people whom He led forth from the bondage of Egypt: for He is glorified.

*Troparia:*
Come, Church of Christ assembled by God, and with hymnographic crowns crown him who was crowned from on high with grace, the adornment of hierarchs, marvelous Ephraim, bringing a victorious hymn of praise unto God: for He is glorified.

Thou, O most sacred father, bearing the pattern of meekness and humility unto teachers, wast a father unto the poor, orphans, and widows, and a speedy defender unto the wronged.

Worthily wast thou called by the great High Priest Who is at the right hand of the Father in the highest, to pasture His chosen flock which He purchased with His own blood; and having well pastured it, thou didst enter into the heavenly realms to stand with the hierarchs before the Chief of pastors, Christ.

*Glory:*
Beholding thy head adorned with a pastoral crown, O holy *Service to the Venerable Father*

...O holy one, the cherubim opened unto thee the gates of Eden, and thou didst enter where the tree of life and the stream of sweetness satisfy the chosen ones endlessly.

*Both now: Theotokion:*
Thou that didst open the heavenly gates unto all by thy childbirth, O Virgin Mother, open unto us the doors of thy mercy, and lead us into the heavenly Fatherland.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*[^119]
There is none holy as Thou, O Lord my God, Who hast exalted the horn of Thy faithful, O Good One, and established us upon the rock of Thy confession.

*Troparia:*
A fervent mind, thine intellect thou hadst from thy youth, O father, to emulate the life of the angels; therefore leaving the service of Prince Izyaslav, thou didst come unto Anthony in the cave, and from him wast instructed how to gird thyself with power against the enemy.

Inflamed with love toward Christ, and having hated the world, thou didst clothe thyself in the garments of salvation; and having bowed thy head, when the hands of blessed Nikon were laid upon thee, thou wast recognized as an angel upon earth, being outside of the world and the flesh.

Seeing thee, O pastor, fierce Belial, that thou wast numbered with the most radiant flock of the holy God-bearing fathers, purposed to scatter it: but taking up the weapons of prayer, fasting, and vigilance, thou didst gird thyself with spiritual power against him.

*Glory:*
Obeying in all things the instruction of thy father, the venerable Anthony, as a true emulator of Jesus, thou wast obedient even unto death: therefore thou wast exalted by Him unto virtues, being girded with power.

*Both now: Theotokion:*
Fainting in soul and body, unto thee, as unto a strong and all-powerful protectress, O Mother of God, with my whole heart do I flee, praying unto thee: be pleased by power from on high to gird me unto virtues, that thankfully I may sing of thy goodness, O all-hymned one.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 5:*
A fervent intercessor, and a lover of the comeliness of the church, the adornment of hierarchs, the hierarch of God Ephraim, on this present day let us honor with praises: for upon earth in the flesh he assimilated himself unto the bodiless ones, by the word of fervent prayer he cut down all carnal passions, and received boldness to assist all in necessities.

*Glory, both now: Theotokion:*
Unto thee, a fervent intercessor and unbreakable wall, with our whole heart do we flee, O blessed Virgin Mother of God, asking help: deliver us from troubles and measureless sorrows; for thou hast grace and power to assist all in necessities.

*To Ephraim, Bishop of Pereyaslavl*

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*[^120]
I heard, O Lord, of Thy glorious dispensation, and I glorified, O Lover of mankind, Thine unsearchable power.

*Troparia:*
Hearing, O father, of the angelic life of the God-bearing fathers, thou wast inflamed in spirit toward the holy places, desiring to be an eyewitness of their life, and an emulator of their virtues.

Desiring to journey unto the ruling city of the Greek land, thou didst wish to receive the blessing and prayer of thy guide, knowing that with blessing, everyone reaps ears of grain as one blessed.

Having received exceedingly strong blessing and prayer of his father, like a dove from the ark thou didst fly forth unto the Greeks, seeking the spiritual olive branches of virtues and the profit of the God-bearing fathers.

Having found what thou didst desire, O most wise one, seeing the venerable fathers, like cedars in Lebanon or palms blossoming in the midst of the paradise of God, from their flowers thou didst bring unto us the fruits: the rule of the Studite Monastery.

*Glory:*
Thou didst fly like an eagle over the circle of thy bishopric, driving away the noetic crows, the spirits of malice under heaven, and protecting all in unanimity from destruction, from all the snares of the enemy.

*Both now: Theotokion:*
The tree of my soul, barren through slothfulness, the all-evil enemy has made: but lest it be cut down like that fig tree, water it from the fountain of thy goodness, O Virgin Mother, that it may bring forth fruit in its season: a corrected life.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*[^121]
O Giver of light and Creator of the ages, O Lord, in the light of Thy commandments direct us: for beside Thee we know no other God.

*Troparia:*
Jesus, the Giver of light, seeing thy soul radiant with purity, suffered thee not to be under a bushel, but set thee upon the candlestick of the high-priesthood, that thou mightest shine in His Church, which He purchased with His precious blood.

The city of Pereyaslavl rejoiced, having received thee as a luminary unto its citizens, and wove for thee a crown of praise, because thou didst enlighten them with God-inspired words, and even more by the pattern of thy pure life.

With the pastoral staff thou didst drive the noetic wolf far from thy flock, O father, which having led forth untroubled, thou didst confirm by thine active and God-inspired labors *Service to the Venerable Father*

...The noetic enemy often attacks us with evil craftiness, striving to snatch us into his bosom; but thou, O child-loving Mother, deliver us not unto his hunt: but like a bird thou didst build this habitation here for thyself, wherein thou didst place us like nestlings, thy singers.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*[^122]
Unto the Lord from the sea-monster Jonah cried aloud: Lead me up from the depths of hades, I pray, that unto Thee as Deliverer in the voice of praise and the spirit of truth I may sacrifice.

*Troparia:*
Inflamed with love and prayer toward the Lord, thou didst erect many churches, in which for all thy rational sheep thou didst offer bloodless sacrifices: pray now for us also, O intercessor, that in a contrite spirit here we may offer praise unto our Master.

Having adorned the Church of Christ like a fair bride with comeliness and diverse paintings, thou wast in her like a gold-gleaming luminary, shining with virtues, O God-wise Ephraim: therefore by the beams of prayers, in thy long-suffering praise, enlighten the mind of them that praise thee.

Sitting upon thy throne, O most sacred father, many who sat in the darkness of sin by thy teaching thou didst raise up unto repentance: raise up us that are fallen in transgressions, that in the voice of praise with a true spirit we may offer amendment of life unto our Master.

*Glory:*
Many labors and ascetic feats for thy flock, O blessed one, thou didst endure, until thou didst cleanse it from the snares of the noetic enemy: guide us who are darkened with the darkness of sin unto the light of repentance by thy prayers, O Ephraim worthy of wonder.

*Both now: Theotokion:*
Held by grievous and countless passions, we cry unto thee among all generations, O most blessed Mother of our God: lead us forth unto the light of dispassion, that in the voice of praise we may offer unto thee our humble prayers.

*Kontakion, Tone 8:*
As one that brought the Studite rule unto the Orthodox Russian land according to the desire of God-bearing Theodosius, and shone brightly among hierarchs, and well pastured his flock, we praise thee, O Ephraim, as thou prayest unto the Lord for us all.
