*To Ephraim, Bishop of Pereyaslavl*

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*[^123]
Unto the golden image worshipped in the plain of Dura, Thy three children paid no heed to the godless command; cast into the midst of the flame, being bedewed they sang: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparia:*
Having adorned thy soul with sweet-smelling virtues, by fasting, prayer, and humility of mind, thou didst present her like a pure bride unto thy Master, before Whom standing thou singest: Blessed is the God of our fathers!

Even as in this life by love thou didst cleave unto thy guide, blessed Theodosius, so also after his end remaining inseparable, unto the translation of his relics thou didst hasten with the other bishops, and seeing them incorrupt, thou didst sing: Blessed is the God of our fathers!

From thy youth having followed the calling of thy Master, after thy many labors which on the throne of the bishopric of Pereyaslavl thou didst offer, thou wast seated upon the heavenly throne in the habitation of paradise, where with the hierarchs and the ranks of the venerable thou singest: Blessed is the God of our fathers!

*Glory:*
The evil aerial nets gnashed their teeth, seeing thee guided by angels from earthly things unto eternal life, but they breathed fire: for thou wast unapproachable unto them, surrounded by heavenly light, where also pray thou for us, O most sacred father Ephraim.

*Both now: Theotokion:*
With loud voices the enemies attack me, O Virgin Bride of God: break their teeth, and crush the jaw-teeth of the noetic lions, as I pray unto thee, that without fear and their snare I may sing: O Lady, blessed art thou!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*[^124]
The thrice-blessed youths, paying no heed to the likeness of gold, and beholding the unchangeable and living image of God, sang in the midst of the fire: Let all creation bless the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparia:*
Thou who didst adorn the Church of Christ with comeliness, O father, for this cause Christ also after thy falling asleep adorned the temple of thy soul, honoring thy body with incorruption: bowing down before the shrine thereof, we sing with them that chant: Bless the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

Unto the hierarch commander of the heavenly hosts, in whose name thou didst erect a fair temple, thou wast introduced, O Ephraim, into the temple of them that celebrate, to sing unto the Lord unto the ages.

The First-Called of the Apostles, unto whom with the brethren thou didst erect a house of prayer, with the Great Martyr Theodore called thee, O father, and the heavenly ones received thee into the house of God, to sing with them unto the Lord unto the ages.

*We bless the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit: the Lord.*
For us, O hierarch Ephraim, as thou hast boldness toward the Life- *Service to the Venerable Father*

...unto the Life-Originating Trinity, cease not praying, that we may be vouchsafed to attain unto the place of the marvelous tabernacle, even unto the house of God, where is the voice of rejoicing, and with sweetest sound they sing unto the Lord unto the ages.

*Both now: Theotokion:*
O Holy Trinity, one Godhead, by the supplication of the all-pure Mother of God and of Thine agreeable servant Ephraim, grant us in the heavenly habitations to sing of Thee unto the ages, O blessed one.

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
The luminary, God Who shone forth before the sun, Who bodily came unto us, having incomprehensibly become incarnate from the virginal loins, O blessed and all-pure one: thee, O Mother of God, do we magnify.

*Troparia:*
Having left earthly borders, thou didst enter into the heavenly habitations, where enjoying the tree of life, pray thou that we also may not be deprived of partaking thereof, O most blessed Ephraim.

The city of Pereyaslavl rejoices, remembering thee, its pastor; and we celebrate, making merry, and bow down before thy relics, seeing their incorruption, O most sacred father.

The cave is not able to hide thy relics, O venerable one; the candle burning by thee therein shall not be extinguished: for thou art its praise and adornment.

*Glory:*
Seeing the incorruption of thy relics, O father, we remember the light of thy virtues, and we glorify the heavenly Father, that He granted thee to pass before angels and men.

*Both now: Theotokion:*
By thy incorrupt childbirth, O all-pure one, having been delivered from corruption, we sing with unceasing voices, and bowing down before thy childbirth, thee, O Mother of God, do we magnify.

*Exapostilarion:*
Thy purity, O most sacred father, drove away the darkness of carnal passion, and thou wast shown forth like a radiant star, illuminating our thoughts and hearts, O wise Ephraim, glory of fathers and adornment of the venerable.

*Glory, both now: Theotokion:*
Being all-fair and without blemish, more honorable than the angels, O Virgin, transform the deformity of my soul by thy grace into goodliness: for thou art the adornment and praise of all.

*At the Praises, Stichera, Tone 4. Podoben: "Joy of the heavenly ranks" (Нб҃ныхъ чинѡ́въ):*[^125]
With myrrh from on high, O God-wise one, having received the sacred anointing, thou wast a pastor unto Christ's flock, which having pastured upon the pastures of the Gospel teaching, and led whole unto the Chief of pastors, thou didst receive the recompense of good laborers: with whom now dancing, remember them that honor with love thy memory, O hierarch Ephraim. *(Twice) To Ephraim, Bishop of Pereyaslavl*

A temple of the All-Holy Spirit wast thou, which first having pre-cleansed with the water of humility, and anointed with the myrrh of grace and spiritual purity, thou didst enlighten with all virtues; and the Father with the Son having come, made an abode in thee: therefore we pray thee, O most sacred father: pray thou unto Christ God that we also may receive grace through thee, and great mercy.

As a teacher of piety, and a faithful laborer in the vineyard of Christ, and a most honorable hierarch, assembling today on thine annual memory, ye people, let us bless him with odes and hymns, saying: O venerable father Ephraim, forget not us that honor with love thy falling asleep, but pray thou unto Christ God to grant us great mercy.

*Glory: Tone 2:*
The God-bearing hierarch of Christ, our father Ephraim, come today, ye assemblies of feast-lovers, let us sing with hymnographic praises, saying: Rejoice, pastor and most excellent guide of Christ's rational sheep; rejoice, honorable adornment of hierarchs; rejoice, all-radiant luminary and guardian of the church; rejoice, fervent intercessor for all that faithfully flee unto thee: deliver us from afflictions by thy prayers, who now celebrate thy memory with love and fall down with joyful soul before the shrine of thy relics, and pray thou unto Christ God to give peace unto the world and save our souls.

*Both now: Theotokion:*
Save from perils thy servants...
*Great Doxology, and dismissal.*

*(Woodcut Engraving: Saint Prokhor the Weed-Eater of the Caves (*Прпⷣ: Про́хоръ Ло́бодникъ*), standing holding wild orache and a cross. Below, two narrative panels depict him gathering and baking orache bread for the people, and Prince Svyatopolk carrying his holy relics on his shoulders: "Кнѧ́зь Свѧтопо́лкъ свои́ма рꙋка́ма несе́ Про́хора и҆ въ пеще́рꙋ положѝ" [Prince Svyatopolk carried Prokhor with his own hands and laid him in the cave].)*[^126]

---

# MONTH OF FEBRUARY

*On the 12th Day: Service to our Venerable Father Prokhor the Wonderworker of the Caves.*[^127]

*At Vespers, on "Lord, I have cried," Stichera, Tone 4. Podoben: "Thou hast given a sign" (Да́лъ є҆сѝ ꙁна́менїе):*[^128]
O marvelous Prokhor, passing through the angelic life, thou didst flee all worldly pleasures, being nourished rather by the word of God than by meats. Therefore thou wast truly an edifice with them, with whom Jesus the Lover of mankind and Savior of our souls granted thee an habitation in the heavenly mansions.

*To Prokhor the Wonderworker of the Caves*

O glorious Prokhor, fleeing human glory, like a soaring bird thou didst hasten unto the hill of the Caves, and didst fly across, being enriched with vision and the doing of the law of the Lord, whence having ascended unto the summit of virtues, thou didst receive the gift of wonderworking from Jesus the Lover of mankind and Savior of our souls.

O most blessed Prokhor, following the footsteps of the blessed fathers, thou didst acquire a narrow way of life in this world, so that thou didst not even taste of ordinary bread, but wast satisfied with wild-growing herbs; therefore now dwelling in the blessed life, thou art nourished with the food of heavenly joy, which Jesus the Lover of mankind and Savior of our souls gave thee from His goodness.

*Glory: Tone 6:*
Who will not marvel at thy life, or who will not recount thy patience, O most blessed father Prokhor? For passing through life in great abstinence, thou didst neglect the body, and didst crush the pleasure-loving serpent unto the end, having established thy feet upon the steps of fasting virtues. Therefore on this present day, honoring thy memory, we confess thy labors, and we pray, falling down before the shrine of thy relics: as thou hast boldness toward Christ God, ask peace for the world, and great mercy for our souls.

*Both now: Theotokion, or Stavrotheotokion.*
*At the Aposticha, all from the Octoechos.*
*Glory: To the Venerable, Tone 6:*
The multitudes of monastics in thy memory, O venerable one, having formed a choir of praises, we honor thy labors and ascetic feats, and marvel how, thyself leading a narrow and poor life, thou didst preserve others living in abundance in the time of necessity: for them that were fainting with hunger thou didst feed with bread made from weeds, and unto them that needed salt thou didst give from ashes: and now strengthen us that faint with spiritual hunger by thy prayers, and ask great mercy from Christ God.
*Both now: Theotokion, or Stavrotheotokion.*

*Troparion, Tone 1:*
O venerable and wonderworking father Prokhor, who in famine didst feed the hungry with bread from weeds, and with salt from ashes: feed us also with spiritual food, praying unto Christ God for our souls.

*Canon, Tone 8. Ode 1. Heirmos:*[^129]
He who threw down the chariot-driving Pharaoh, the wonderworking staff of Moses of old, striking crosswise and dividing the sea, saved Israel a fugitive, walking on foot, chanting a song unto God.

*Service to the Venerable Father*

...Gird us, O Master Christ, with the spiritual weapon against invisible enemies, and teach us how to contend and overcome their power, that we may offer unto Thee our God a festive song in the memory of Thy servant, sending it forth with love.

Having their loins girded with vigilance, the ranks of monastics remember thee, O father, and celebrate thine annual memory with praises, sending forth a song unto God.

Remembering eternal blessedness, O blessed one, thou didst leave the corruptible things of this world, and that thou mightest be a co-dweller with angels, thou didst clothe thyself in the angelic habit; therefore we honor thy fasting life, sending forth a song unto God.

*Glory:*
Taking upon thyself the good yoke of abstinence, thou didst acquire the hard life of fasting deeds, O God-wise one, wherein having wrestled with the invisible enemy, thou didst conquer him: therefore we glorify thee, sending forth a song unto God.

*Both now: Theotokion:*
Unto us that walk in the broad way and flow by pleasure into the abyss of hades, guide us, O Mother of God, unto the narrow way leading unto the blessed life: that with thy chosen servants on this present day we may fall down before thee, sending forth supplications.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*[^130]
Thou Who in the beginning didst establish the heavens with understanding, and founded the earth upon the waters, establish me, O Christ, upon the rock of Thy commandments: for there is none holy beside Thee, O only Lover of mankind.

*Troparia:*
Having established thy feet upon the rock of Christ's commandments, O father, thou didst remain unshaken by the snares of demons, ever offering praise unto God: There is none holy beside Thee, O Lover of mankind.

The kindling of carnal passions thou didst extinguish by the dew of abstinence: for not even eating bread, but wild orache, thou didst continue, having the word of God rather as food, and this strengthened thee, O venerable one.

Famine thou didst account as nothing, O blessed one, hoping upon Him that feeds the birds; and He gave thee to eat bread not from sown grain, which was sweeter unto thee than honey and the honeycomb.

*Glory:*
We marvel at thy marvelous possession, O father, that even for orache bread thou didst condemn thyself, making thyself unworthy even of such food, wherewith lightly thou didst traverse the sorrowful fasting way.

*Both now: Theotokion:*
Our enemies who exalt themselves with pride bend down unto humility, O pure Virgin: for thou alone didst say: Behold the handmaid of the Lord, and thou reignest unto the ages over all generations that bless thee.

*To Prokhor the Wonderworker of the Caves*

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 1:*
Emulating the great fathers, extreme fasting thou didst earnestly desire: for being nourished with weed alone and water, thou didst not faint from fasting, but didst remain stronger than adamant; and now receiving the fruits of thine abstinence, recompense from God, pray thou ever for us, that through thee we also may receive a reward, as we bless thy memory with love, O one worthy of wonder.
*Glory, both now: Theotokion:*
With choirs of them that bless thee, the human race, and having surpassed every creature with glory, in hymns we honor thy seedless conception and thine ineffable childbirth, O Mary, pure Virgin Mother.

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*[^131]
Thou art my strength, O Lord, Thou art my power, Thou art my God, Thou art my rejoicing, Who didst not leave the bosom of the Father, and didst visit our poverty: therefore with the Prophet Habakkuk I cry unto Thee: Glory to Thy power, O Lover of mankind!

*Troparia:*
Having from God the gift of wonderworking, O father, thou didst feed many with orache bread in the time of famine, and making salt from ashes, thou didst give it freely unto them that came: and glory was unto the Lover of mankind Who became a defender there.

By thy prayer, O venerable one, thou didst give sweetness unto the orache bread: for it was sweet for food, and radiant and pure like wheat bread unto them that received it with a blessing. From the blessing of thine herbs grant unto us also, O most sacred father, as we have thee as an intercessor toward Christ the Lover of mankind.

Thy weed-bread, O most blessed father, was sweet and radiant unto them that received it with a blessing; but unto them that stole it secretly, it appeared black like soot and bitter. With thy blessing bless us, and grant of thy sweetness, as thou sittest at the heavenly table, O glorious Prokhor.

*Glory:*
Not sated, but leading a choir of good praises, fainting with spiritual hunger, we come unto thee, O father, on this present day of thy memory, and we pray: grant us of thy grace, that in thee we may glorify Christ the Lover of mankind.

*Both now: Theotokion:*
Who that flees unto thee departs from thee confounded, O pure Virgin! Or who that asks receives not of thy grace, O Lady! Which we also believing and confessing, offer supplication unto thee, O pure one: grant us thy bounties, as the good Mother of the Good Son.

*Service to the Venerable Father*

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*[^132]
Why hast Thou cast me off from Thy countenance, O unwaning Light, and a strange darkness has covered me, the wretched one? But convert me, and direct my ways unto the light of Thy commandments, I pray.

*Troparia:*
O glorious Prokhor, delay not to come, and vouchsafe us thy blessing, even as of old in time of need thou didst give it unhindered; and direct the ways of them that praise thee with love unto the commandments of the Lord, as our fervent intercessor.

He who troubles the earth and shakes the people brought war upon us, the adversary enemy, and brought spiritual famine upon all our entrance; but do thou, O most blessed one, appear quickly, and by thy prayer feed us with noetic food, and direct our ways, as our intercessor.

Seeing thy compassions, O father, many came unto thee in the time of famine, unto whom the bread made from herbs and the salt made from ashes by thy hands thou didst give without fail; look down therefore now upon us, and by thy prayer grant every abundance unto our monastery, we pray thee, O venerable father.

*Glory:*
Many that hate us, enemies have risen up against us, striving to deprive us of spiritual and bodily food; but come thou unto us and rebuke them, and by thy prayer grant unto our monastery all that is unto profit, we pray thee, O venerable Prokhor.

*Both now: Theotokion:*
The adversary enemy, envying thy flock, continually stirs up war against us; but do thou, O all-pure one, as the Mother of the mighty King, turn him back, and direct our ways unto the commandments of the Lord, as our intercessor.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*[^133]
Cleanse me, O Savior, for many are mine iniquities, and lead me up from the depths of evils, I pray: for unto Thee have I cried, and give ear unto me, O God of my salvation.

*Troparia:*
Cleanse the defilement of my soul by thy prayer, O glorious Prokhor, and heal the fiery darts of my heart with the plaster of thy grace: for unto thee on this present day do I cry aloud, as unto an intercessor for my salvation.

Overcome by the thought of extortion, the prince commanded all the salt made from ashes by thy prayer to be plundered, which being turned into ashes again, was found bitter in their mouths; from such malice and passion deliver me, O father, as a helper of my salvation.

Shame covered the faces of them that slandered the food of the hungry: for seeing the transformation of thy salt into ashes, they cast it out from the house; but thou didst command the cast-out ashes to be gathered up, and it became pure salt again by thy prayer, even as before. Grant unto me also now thy prayer, O prayerful helper of my salvation.

*Glory:*
Thinking to hide thy wonder, they commanded the salt that had turned into ashes to be poured out by night outside; but the night witnessed all the more unto thy wonders: for the ashes having again been changed into salt, by thy command the needy gathered it, blessing thee, O venerable one, as their defender.

*Both now: Theotokion:*
Night holds me fast in slothfulness, an intercessor of sleepiness, not drawing near unto the light of spiritual vigilance; O Mother of God, enlighten my mind, guiding me unto doxology, that I may ever sing unto thee: Rejoice, candle kindled with the divine flame!

*Kontakion, Tone 2:*
Having mortified thy flesh with extreme abstinence, O father, being deprived of human food, herbs and grass were in place of bread, and with ashes like salt thou wast nourished, O wonderworking Prokhor; which seeing, the Lord changed the herbs into bread, and the ashes into salt, wherewith thou didst feed the multitude of the hungry: therefore we honor and bless thee.

*To Prokhor the Wonderworker of the Caves*

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*[^134]
The fire was ashamed before the condescension of God in Babylon of old; therefore the children, dancing in the furnace with joyful step, as in a meadow, sang: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparia:*
Remembering the condescension of God and the dread judgment, the prince was afraid, seeing how he had surrounded thee, O venerable Prokhor, not knowing the grace that was wrought in thee; but coming to himself, he cried aloud: Blessed is the God of our fathers!

Recognizing thee, O father, as a servant of God, the ruler afterward blessed thy benefactions, and having received warmer love toward the most marvelous monastery, he repented of his extortion, and asking forgiveness for the evil done, sang: Blessed is the God of our fathers!

As a true minister and servant of the innocent Lamb, the Son of God, having shown thy guiltlessness, thou gavest forgiveness unto him that wronged thee, O blessed one, and confirmed a covenant with him, that the one should lay the other in the grave, when he should depart from the body, as the God of our fathers should command one of you.

*Glory:*
Having lived in holiness and directed thy life, thou didst attain unto a blessed end, O father; therefore rejoicing unto the angelic choirs, crying aloud, thou didst sing: Blessed is the God of our fathers!

*Both now: Theotokion:*
I have not emulated them that lived in holiness, but in slothfulness have spent my life, unto the end approaching the end, unto whom dost thou flee, O my soul? Having only the Mother of God as hope, cry aloud: O Full of grace, help, and deliver me from eternal condemnation.

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*[^135]
The Chaldean tyrant furiously kindled sevenfold the furnace for them that honored God; but seeing them saved by a higher power, he cried aloud unto the Creator and Deliverer: Ye youths, bless; ye priests, sing; ye people, supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparia:*
Having slain the seven-headed serpent with the spear of ascetic feats, and preparing thyself to receive the crown, with joy didst thou call aloud, O Prokhor: Ye people, sing unto the Lord Creator and Deliverer, and supremely exalt Him unto ages!

Having received word of thy blessed departure, hearing of it from thy sending, the prince left his warriors, and hastily ran unto thee, that he might be vouchsafed thy blessing; and he was not deprived of that which he desired: having received thy blessing, he sang: Ye people, supremely exalt Christ unto ages!

Even as aforetime in prayers thou didst stretch forth thy hands, O venerable one, so at thy departure, having given the kiss unto all, and having lifted up thy hands on high, thou didst yield up thy spirit; and all cried aloud: Ye people, glorify your protector unto ages!

*We bless the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit: the Lord.*
Thy sorrowless departure unto the heavenly habitations filled all with joyful weeping: for all lamented, the poor for their nourisher, the orphans for a father; but we on this present day glorifying thy falling asleep, O father, call aloud: Ye people, supremely exalt Christ unto ages!

*Both now: Theotokion:*
Thou that didst bear the Joy of all the venerable, eternal joy, by thine intercession vouchsafe me, O Mother of God, and vouchsafe me to glorify thy loving-kindness unto ages.

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*[^136]
Heaven was struck with awe at this, and the ends of the earth were amazed, that God appeared unto men in the flesh, and thy womb became more spacious than the heavens; therefore the ranks of angels and men magnify thee, O Mother of God.

*Troparia:*
Everyone who remembers thy life is amazed, O father, that being in the flesh, thou wast assimilated unto the angels by ascetic deeds; therefore all magnify thee, O Prokhor.

The wearer of the diadem, not loathing thee who wast poor in spirit, O holy and venerable one, laid thee into the grave with his own hands; and we now remembering thy falling asleep, call thee blessed, whom also the angelic choirs bless in the heavenly mansions.

The prince, not loathing thee who in this life didst bear the cross upon thy shoulders, carried thee upon his shoulders into the cave, and washed thy grave with warm tears, repenting that he had formerly vexed thee; but both things he set right by thy blessing, of which vouchsafe us also, as out of love we magnify thee with hymns.

*To Prokhor the Wonderworker of the Caves*

*Glory:*
Glory to Thee, O Most-Holy Trinity, Father, Son, and Holy Spirit, one God: for by Thy grace Thou givest eternal glory unto them that love Thee from the heart, of which vouchsafe us also, through the supplications of venerable Prokhor and of all the venerable saints, where their choirs continually magnify Thee.

*Both now: Theotokion:*
Glory to thee, O Mother of God, sole refuge of the faithful, sole reliance, sole hope and salvation of the Christian race; therefore as is due, all of us magnify thee.

*Exapostilarion:*
Having left the corruptible things of the world, thou didst attain unto the heavenly, honorable paths; and standing before the unoriginate Light with the ranks of the venerable, pray thou, as thou hast boldness to assist in necessities, for the pacification of the world, and for the empire of Christian sovereigns, who glorify thy memory.

*Glory, both now: Theotokion:*
O intercessor before the Holy Trinity for them that are tempest-tossed in the deep of worldly passions, stand forth quickly unto help, and by the calm waters of thy grace lead us into the blessed haven, as thou art a speedy helper unto them that glorify thy name.

*At the Aposticha, all from the Octoechos.*
*Then, to the Venerable:*
*Sticheron: Tone 4:*
*Verse:* The venerable ones shall exult in glory, and shall rejoice upon their beds.[^137]
Celebrating like angels, O ye people, let us bring a hymn unto God on the annual memory of the God-bearing father, exalting His honorable name, and calling aloud like the seraphim: Holy art Thou, O unoriginate Father; holy art Thou, O timeless Son; holy art Thou, O co-everlasting Spirit!

*Glory: Tone 4:*
Having formed an honorable choir, assimilating ourselves unto the choirs of the bodiless ones, come, ye people, lovingly now let us bring spiritual gifts unto the holy cave, and looking into the grave of the most blessed father, let us praise him, calling aloud: Rejoice, O Prokhor of glorious name, and intercessor for our souls!

*Both now: Theotokion, or Stavrotheotokion.*

*(Woodcut Engraving: Saint Isaac the Recluse of the Caves (*Прпⷣ: І҆са́кїй Пече́рскїй*), standing in monastic habit in the center, flanked by six narrative vignettes depicting scenes from his Life in the Caves: the demons appearing as radiant angels; the deceiver claiming to be Christ; Isaac bowing down in deception; the demons playing pipes and dancing with him; Isaac struck down and left half-dead; and St. Theodosius and the brethren nursing and restoring him.)*[^138]

*In the Month of February, on the 14th Day: Service to our Venerable Father Isaac the Recluse of the Caves.*[^139]

*At Vespers, on "Lord, I have cried," Stichera, Tone 8. Podoben: "O most marvelous wonder" (Ѡ҆ пресла́внагѡ чꙋдесѐ):*[^140]
O venerable father Isaac, though at first the dark enemy appeared in the form of light, and through ignorance deceived thee, and mocked thee with demonic delusions; yet afterward thou didst correct thyself, and wast shown forth terrible unto him: for thou didst receive from Christ God victory over all the power of the enemy, Whom *To Isaac the Recluse of the Caves*

...Whom cease not to pray that we also may be delivered from all the wiles of the enemy, and from grievous afflictions.

O God-wise father Isaac, though the enemy who appeared as light deceived thee, yet afterward through thy correction he that deceived thee fell: for having caught him by his own murderous scheme, thou didst cast him down, overturning him utterly; and having trampled him under thy feet, thou didst cry aloud: I will not fear your terror, for God is with me, and His strength is my confirmation against all your frightful devices.

O most blessed father Isaac, though in ignorance thou didst bow down unto a demon as unto the person of Christ, yet after this fall having corrected thyself again, thou didst spit upon the demonic legions, and didst fill their faces with dishonor: who, being put to shame, vanish like smoke and are driven away as by fire; for the fire of thy love toward God drives away their assaults, and helps all that call upon thy name.

*Glory: Tone 2:*
O father Isaac, by thyself thou hast given an example unto them that struggle, not to believe appearances that show themselves: for satan knows how to transform himself into an angel of light; but let them seal themselves with the sign of the mighty weapon, upon which the spirits of malice are unable to gaze, but even as dust is driven by the wind from the earth, so being scorched by its beams they vanish; therefore we also, holding fast unto this, will not fear the wiles of the enemy, but rather quenching their cunning designs, let us receive from Christ God peace in our bones, and great mercy.

*Both now: Theotokion, or Stavrotheotokion.*

*Troparion, Tone 2:*
Desiring to enjoy the radiance of the saints, thou didst settle in a dark cave, O all-praised one, and didst struggle well, O Isaac; and though deceived by the enemy, thou didst again firmly trample underfoot all his wiles; and now as victor standing in joy before Christ God, ask for us peace and great mercy.

*Glory, both now: Theotokion.*

*Canon to the Venerable, Tone 4. Ode 1. Heirmos:*[^141]
O Thou Who wast born of the Virgin, drown, I pray Thee, the three parts of my soul in the depth of dispassion, even as Thou didst drown the mighty captains; that unto Thee, as upon a timbrel, in the mortification of the body I may sing a song of victory.

*Troparia:*
As Thou art almighty, O Christ, in the depth of Thy compassions *Service to the Venerable Father*

...drown my sins like Pharaoh, I pray Thee, that with clay lips in the memory of Thy servant, glorifying Thee, I may worthily bring a victorious hymn.

The mighty ruler of the world stood by, seeking to cover all with the depth of passions; but he was not able to submerge thee, O father: for having recognized his manifold snares, thou didst flee the world like a bird from a snare, and didst bring a victorious hymn unto God.

A subtle merchant wast thou, O venerable one: for by corruptible things thou didst acquire the incorruptible, and for perishable things thou didst purchase the unfading pearl, wherewith thou didst adorn the crown of thy patience; and now rejoicing thou standest before Christ, bringing a victorious hymn.

*Glory:*
Thou didst distribute thy possessions unto the poor, O blessed one, and didst not spare even thine own will, coming unto that great Anthony, from whom having received the angelic habit in exchange, thou wast enriched with the heavenly treasure, and singest unceasing praise unto Christ.

*Both now: Theotokion:*
Burdened with the yoke of sin, unto thee I come, O Mother of God, praying: take away my passionate torment, that at least from now on I may correct myself through repentance, and bring unto thee, O Lady, my fervent prayers, a contrite hymn.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*[^142]
From on high Thou didst come down willingly upon the earth, O Thou Who art above every principality, and hast exalted the humble human nature, which was from the lowest hell: for there is none holy save Thee, O Lover of mankind.

*Troparia:*
Having received the calling from on high, by the exchange of the angelic habit thou didst set fire unto the world, which quenched all its lustful darts, having taught us by an honorable and austere life, O venerable one, having as helper God the only Lover of mankind.

That thou mightest not inherit a portion with the goats in the age to come, O father, in this life mortifying thy flesh, thou didst clothe thyself in a single rough hairshirt and in a goat-skin, wherein thou didst traverse the narrow monastic way, calling upon God for help, the only Lover of mankind.

Thou didst settle upon the hill, thou didst shut thyself in a narrow cave; and by the ladder of virtues wisely reaching forth unto the heavenly things, thou didst set ascents in thy heart, ever to hymn God the Lover of mankind.

*Glory:*
Thou didst traverse the narrow path of humility and poverty, O wise one, having dashed the crafty enemy like an infant against the rock of the love of God, which seeking, thou didst cleanse thy soul with fasting, and by all-night vigilance didst humble the body, that it might unslothfully stand before God the Lover of mankind.

*Both now: Theotokion:*
Overcome by fierceness, I am unable to dash the passions that are born in me, like infants, against the rock of the commandments; thee I call upon, O Mother of God, for help: help me, as thou art the Mother of God Who loves mankind, and they shall fall under my feet, conquered by thy power.

*To Isaac the Recluse of the Caves*

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 8:*[^143]
Mortifying thy flesh by abstinence, thou didst neglect corruptible food, but satisfying thyself with a single prosphora every other day, thou didst abide in abstinence, and didst not rest upon thy ribs; thus contending against the invisible enemy, thou didst cry aloud: O Christ God, grant help unto them that are in battle!

*Glory, both now: Theotokion:*
Darkened by the sleep of slothfulness, my soul I stretch out unto thee, O pure one: by thy vigilant prayer and measureless goodness raise me up unto hymnody, and grant me help as I am in battle.

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*[^144]
For the sake of the love of Thine image, O Compassionate One, Thou didst stand upon Thy Cross, and the nations melted away: for Thou, O Lover of mankind, art my strength and my praise.

*Troparia:*
For the sake of the love of God, in many labors and fastings Thou didst continually abide until the seventh year, desiring her and having no departure from anywhere; and remembering thy dreadful departure, Thou didst call aloud unto Christ Who strengthens Thee: Thou art my strength, O God, and my praise!

Behold, how good and how pleasant it is for brethren to dwell together: a brother helped by a brother is like a strong city, but he that contends alone against the enemy falls; which also happened unto thee, O father, that thou shouldest not rely upon thyself, but upon the living God, for He alone is strength and praise unto all.

*Glory:*
The devil, desiring to darken thy mind, O father, came with a multitude of bright demons, and himself shone like the sun, but covered thee with deceitful darkness, from which being delivered, thou didst cry aloud unto God: Thou art my strength, O God, and my praise!

*Both now: Theotokion:*
Having my mind darkened with passions, I am held fast by many sins; but unto thee do I pray, who beyond mind didst bear the Light of the whole world: receive, O Mother of God, the weapon of thy power, and stand forth unto me for help, that I may behold the light of true divine understanding.

*Service to the Venerable Father*

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*[^145]
Shine forth upon me, O Lord, the light of Thy commandments, for unto Thee my spirit wakes early, and sings of Thee: for Thou art our God, and unto Thee do I flee, O King of peace.

*Troparia:*
They that shone forth in the likeness of light, the sons of eternal darkness, made a great tumult, calling thee their own, O father Isaac, and they surrounded thee like many dogs with their wiles, from which being delivered, thou didst flee unto God, the King of peace.

He who formerly commanded that at the tyrant's voice sound should be made before the golden-forged image, unto the deception of them that know not God: this same one commands pipes and timbrels to sound for thy deception; and though deceived by him, thou wast delivered by the help of God, the King of peace.

The devil commanded that thou who wast overwearied by ascetic labors shouldest be worn out by dancing; fulfilling whose command, the crafty spirits leaped with thee for a long time, and having mocked thee, left thee scarcely alive; but thou afterward having corrected thyself didst mock him, and trampled him underfoot unto the end, by the power of God, the King of peace.

*Glory:*
Seeing thee wounded by the enemy, speechless, only barely breathing, the venerable fathers carried thee out before the cave, and recognizing that this had happened from demonic working, they persevered in prayer, assisting thee; and the Lord, the King of peace, heard their prayer, and had mercy upon thee.

*Both now: Theotokion:*
Having my heart wounded by the darkness of demonic assaults, unto thee I pray, O Lady: apply to it the good plaster of mercy, and save my darkened soul from falling; for thee alone do I have as a speedy visitation, O Queen of light.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*[^146]
Submerged by the tempest of sin, as though held fast in the whale's belly, in holiness I call aloud unto Thee: Lead up my life from corruption, O Lord, and save me.

*Troparia:*
Distressed by the tempest of demonic temptations, O blessed one, thou wast weakened in mind and body, and knowing nothing about thyself, thou didst lie upon a bed for two years, neither eating bread nor drinking water; but being entreated through the prayer of venerable Theodosius, the Lord brought up thy life from corruption, and saved thee.

A strange wonder it is for human nature to be without food; yet this was accomplished in thee, O blessed one: for God, wheresoever He wills, overcomes the order of nature, for in His hand is the breath of every creature, and He does whatever He pleases.

Thy Cross, O Christ, is a mighty weapon against enemies, wherewith if we are not protected, we shall be tempted *To Isaac the Recluse of the Caves*

...we shall be tempted even as was Isaac; therefore being protected therewith, we pray Thee: even as Thou didst not permit Isaac to be tempted unto the end, so also permit not temptation beyond our strength to come upon us, and save us.

*Glory:*
Glory to Thee, O unoriginate Trinity, the Cause of goodness, Who by Thy love for mankind liftest up the fallen from all things: have compassion upon us Thy servants, and raise up the fallen, even as Thou didst save Thy servant Isaac, granting him greater strength against demons.

*Both now: Theotokion:*
Glory to thee, O Mother of God, refuge of the faithful: for through thee the fallen are enriched, the dead are quickened, they receive strength against the devil, and are vouchsafed eternal life, in the glory of thy Son and God.

*Kontakion, Tone 5:*
Having overthrown the enemy's deception, and shone brightly in fasting, come, ye faithful, let us praise victorious Isaac, who passed through temptations like gold, and enlightens the hearts of them that faithfully run unto him, praying unceasingly unto the Lord for our souls.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*[^147]
Deliver us not up unto the end for Thy name's sake, and break not Thy covenant, and take not Thy mercy away from us, O Lord, God of our fathers, supremely praised unto the ages.

*Troparia:*
Having passed through many temptations, O venerable one, thou didst abound all the more unto the glory of God, and standing firmly against the enemy, thou didst sing in psalmic words: Setting my feet in distress, Thou hast placed them in a broad place, O Christ God, all-praised unto the ages.

Closely didst thou bind thyself with haircloth garments, and having firmly bound the enemy also who had set thee in the kitchen, thou didst mock him who had mocked thee, driving him away by prayer, and anticipating all by obedience and ascetic labors.

Having raised up the fallen temple of thy soul through temptation, thou didst run first of all unto the temple of God's Mother, and therein, with thy feet freezing unto the stones from cold, thou didst stand until the completion of the rule, the fire of thy heart secretly kindling thee into love of God.

*Glory:*
Double grace before natural deception thou didst again attain, O father, which the bird above thee signified; which they that saw recognized the grace of God that was with thee, and all being amazed glorified Christ Who glorified thee, and honored thee as a servant of Christ.

*Both now: Theotokion:*

*Service to the Venerable Father*

...Darkened by holy folly through demonic delusions, and not having kept the commandments of my Lord, I transgress; but do thou, O pure Mother, grant me understanding, that I may perceive the right path, and having escaped the hand of Belial who hunts me, I may praise thee unto ages.

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*[^148]
Unto Him Who was nailed in the flesh upon the Cross, and showed us a weapon unto salvation, ye youths, supremely exalt Christ our God unto all ages.

*Troparia:*
Having ascended the cross of endurance, thou wast crucified unto the world, O most blessed Isaac: for thou didst nail thy members by the cutting off of passions; and with the spear of mockery, not knowing thy state for God's sake, they pierced thee, but thou didst endure all things joyfully, praising Christ unto ages.

Having stepped with bare feet upon the flame of a kindled furnace, thou didst remain standing unmoved until it was quenched, and this signified the quenching of demonic assaults, which thou didst quickly cool by the power of Christ's Cross, and therein didst praise Him unto ages.

*Glory:*
Many times the demons assaulted thee, O father, calling thee their own, because thou hadst bowed down unto their prince; but thou by the power of the Cross didst drive them away like flies, and in shame they fled from thee, unable to bear their humiliation; and thy latter victory over them became greater than the first.

*Both now: Theotokion:*
Tried by the onslaught of grievous passions, unto thy protection do I flee, O all-immaculate Virgin: for the sake of thy venerable Isaac, cover me, and save me from these assaults, that in orthodox manner I may glorify thee with Christ unto ages.

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*[^149]
Thy childbirth was shown forth incorrupt: God came forth from thy side, and appeared upon earth as a bearer of flesh, and dwelt with men; therefore, O Mother of God, we all magnify thee.

*Troparia:*
We all magnify God the Lover of mankind, Who so strengthened His servant, that he turned all demonic terrors into flight; and deceit being conquered by no other weapon, he was like a diamond, firm, whole, and unbreakable.

Thine adversary was heard, O venerable one, though many times by night he brought many demons unto his help, with mattocks and spades, crying aloud: Let us dig up this cave, and bury him here, for already he overcomes us, calling upon the unconquerable Name of the Most High, and upon her that bore Him for help.

Recognizing the night-time darkness, the blest one drove away with the light-bearing sign of the Cross them that came by night, wherewith *To Isaac the Recluse of the Caves*

...wherewith being scorched, unable to endure, they turned unto flight, leaving unto thee the crown of victory, as unto a good soldier and invincible ascetic.

*Glory:*
The serpent and fierce ones contending with thee during the summer, O father, were transformed into four-footed beasts, wild animals, reptiles, and mice; but not overcoming thee by their phantoms, by constraint he confessed thee as master: for thou didst overthrow him by the Name of Christ, Whom with her that bore Him we magnify.

*Both now: Theotokion:*
In the time of my life, the enemy, transformed into diverse forms, overcomes me; unable to stand against him, thee do I call upon, O Mother of God: send me strength, as thou didst bear the Mighty One, Whom with thee I magnify.

*Exapostilarion:*
Having undertaken many labors in life, contending against enemies, thou didst attain unto a blessed end; thou wast translated unto the highest, unto heavenly rest, but thy memory abides among us, and the Church confesses thy labors: which remembering, we pray thee: visit us, and grant a blessing on this present day unto them that honor thy translation, O our father Isaac.

*Glory, both now: Theotokion:*
O speedy defense of Christians, make haste to stand forth unto them that are tormented by many afflictions, that hoping upon thee we may not fall away from hope; but delivered through thee from perils, we may glorify thy honorable childbirth, O all-immaculate one.

*At the Praises, Stichera, Tone 3:*
*Verse:* God is marvelous in His saints, the God of Israel.[^150]
Having ever tilled the field of thy soul in this corruptible life, thou didst water it with a stream of tears; and having sown labors like pure wheat, and grown the ears of monastic virtues, thou didst reap sheaves of unceasing prayers, which thou didst bring into the heavenly barn; and now receiving praise with the good servants, remember us, O blessed father, who lovingly honor thy memory.

Having walked by the narrow path in this temporal age, with sorrows and tears, and unceasing vigilance, with sighing and affliction, O long-praised one, thou didst turn away from the broad and ruinous way, and didst attain unto heavenly breadth, where having painless rest *Service to the Venerable Father*

...having painless rest, forget not us also in thy goodness, as an intercessor for our souls.

Having become poor for God's sake, despising all earthly possessions, thou didst follow Christ Who became poor for our sake, from Whom having received the perfection of virtues, thou wast enriched with an incorruptible treasure: forget not us who are impoverished in good deeds, but from thy fullness grant unto us, O venerable father, who on this present day honor thy precious falling asleep.

*Glory: Tone 2:*
Having fled from the world and all voluptuousness, O venerable one, taking thy cross upon thy shoulder, thou didst follow Christ: having lived in a narrow cave, by the extreme sharpness of monastic labors, thou didst conquer him who formerly conquered thee; and after thy demise thou wast shown forth so terrible unto him, that he trembles even at thy name, unable to bear thy rebuke: for living upon it, thou drivest away the spirits of malice under heaven; drive them away also from us, O blessed Isaac, and ask for us great mercy from Christ God.

*Both now: Theotokion:*
Save from perils thy servants, O all-pure Mother of God.
*Great Doxology, and dismissal.*

*(Woodcut Engraving: Saint Eustratius the Venerable-Martyr of the Caves (*Пр: Єѵстратїй Печерскій*), depicted crucified upon a wooden cross above a body of water; on the right bank, Jewish tormentors mocking and pointing spears, while above, the clouds part and celestial rays illuminate the martyr; a dog lies at the base of the cross.)*[^151]

---

# MONTH OF MARCH

*On the 28th Day: Service to the Holy Venerable-Martyr Eustratius of the Caves.*[^152]

*At Vespers, on "Lord, I have cried," Stichera, Tone 1. Podoben: "Joy of the heavenly ranks" (Нб҃ныхъ чинѡ́въ):*[^153]
O thou who formerly didst crucify thyself willingly unto the world by the cutting off of all passions, thou now afterward having become a partaker of eternal suffering, sufferest the passion of ultimate mortification, being nailed upon a cross by the Jews, like a lamb *Service to the Holy Venerable-Martyr*

...like a ladder ascending unto heaven: whence look down, O blessed Eustratius; lift up our minds unto heavenly things, and pray unto Christ God to grant us the cutting off of all passions.

A second Elijah on a chariot wast thou shown forth through zeal for Christ, O marvelous one: for even as he rebuked the madness of the king, so thou also didst reprove the God-slaying deed of the foolish Jew, and wast crucified by him in the body upon a cross; but thy soul ran on a chariot of fire unto heavenly realms, where thou rejoicest, illuminated by the unwaning Light: of which let us not be deprived, through thy prayers.

Depicting the passion of thy Master, thou wast crucified upon a cross by a Jew, by whom being pierced in the ribs with a spear, having mounted a chariot of fire, thou didst signify thy flaming love toward God, before Whom now standing in joy, pray thou for them that hymn thee, O blessed father Eustratius.

*Glory: Tone 2:*
In heaven unto eternal glory, O venerable one, having formerly inflamed thy body with abstinence, and afterward having thy hands and feet nailed by the Jews, thou didst find the ladder of desire, whereby having ascended unto the heavenly hosts, the majestic voice called unto thee: This is a good citizen of heavenly Zion: wherein now rejoicing with the saints, remember us that honor thy holy memory, that we also may become heirs thereof with thee.

*Both now: Theotokion: The shadow of the law has passed:*

*Entrance. Prokeimenon of the day. And the readings:*
*Three from the Common of the Venerable (see fol. 30v).*[^154]

*At the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 4. Podoben: "Called from on high" (Вꙁва́нный):*[^155]
O all-praised venerable-martyr Eustratius, thou didst set forth thy life in care for virtues; for with hymnody the faithful praise Christ in faith, obeying thy teachings: standing before Him, entreat the Holy Spirit that they may be established in peace.

*Verse:* Precious in the sight of the Lord is the death of His venerable ones.[^156]
O God-inspired martyr Eustratius, thy life, equal unto the angels, was sanctified on earth, and thine honorable confession was brought unto the heavenly ones like a sweet-smelling sacrifice and good incense; and now pray thou to grant our souls peace and great mercy.

*Verse:* Blessed is the man that fears the Lord; in His commandments he will desire exceedingly.[^157]

*To Eustratius of the Caves*

Never, O venerable-martyr Eustratius, having become conqueror of passions, didst thou yield to the tormentor: dancing together with the crown-bearers, O blessed one, remember all them that celebrate thy memory; and now pray thou unto Christ to grant our souls peace and great mercy.

*Glory: Tone 3:*
The Jews understood not the words of the prophets, going astray from the womb, speaking a lie against our Son, and by the reproach of the curse condemned Him to the death of the cross; but thou, O blessed one, having been clothed with divine grace, didst show forth the blessing given unto the whole world by the crucifixion: therefore the lawless ones nailed thee to the cross, and pierced thy ribs with a spear, not knowing that for thy cross thou receivest recompense from God, while the ungodly shall quickly perish: they inherited the curse by the tree, but thou the enjoyment of the tree of life: of which let us also be partakers with thee, as we glorify the Son and Lord in thy memory, O father Eustratius.

*Both now: Theotokion: Look down upon the supplications of thy servants:*

*Troparion, Tone 7:*
Thou wast shown forth as the praise of ascetics and martyrs, O venerable Eustratius: for having first mortified the flesh by abstinence, thou didst follow Christ, bearing thy cross; and afterward being thyself lifted up upon a cross, and pierced in the ribs with a spear, thou didst commit thy soul into the hands of God; being borne up unto heaven on a chariot of fire, thou wast doubly crowned by Christ God and the Savior of our souls.
*Glory, both now: Theotokion.*

*At Matins, on "God is the Lord," the same Troparion.*
*After Kathisma 1, Sessional Hymn, Tone 4. Podoben: "The angelic choir" (Ли́къ а҆́гг҃лскїй):*[^158]
Assimilating thyself unto thy Master by passion, thou didst magnificently take up the wood of the cross, and by thy God-inspired lips didst put to shame the blaspheming Jew: therefore the ungodly one pierces thy ribs with a spear, while thou rejoicest, ascending unto thy Lord; pray unceasingly unto Him for us, who out of love honor thy sufferings, O noble and wise Eustratius.
*Glory, both now: Theotokion:*
An angelic hymn the unwedded Virgin brings unto thee: Rejoice, thou who art full of grace and blessed among women, who didst receive in thy womb *Service to the Holy Venerable-Martyr*

...who didst receive in thy womb the Word of God seedlessly, transforming men from seraphic works.

*After Kathisma 2, Sessional Hymn, Tone 5. Podoben: "Co-unoriginate Word" (Собеꙁнача́лное):*[^159]
The citizen of the heavenly Jerusalem, the true witness and good soldier of Christ, noble and wise Eustratius, companion of the venerable and fellow-dancer of the martyrs, let us honor with hymns, O ye people, singing his memory: for standing before Christ God, he unceasingly prays for our souls.

*Glory, both now: Theotokion:*
O Queen of the heavenly Jerusalem, sole immaculate Mother among women, crown of virgins and consolation of the saints, unceasing intercessor before the Holy Trinity: remember us who have wandered far, O Mother of God, and deliver from perils them that hymn thee.

*After the Polyeleos, Sessional Hymn, Tone 1. Podoben: "Make haste to anticipate" (Ско́рѡ предварѝ):*[^160]
Kindled by the flame of burning love toward the crucified Christ, Thou didst not fear the God-slaying Hebrew breathing fury, but didst reprove him manfully, and didst proclaim from the Scriptures the fulfillment of the legal passover, grace having arrived: for this cause Thou wast nailed upon a cross by the Jews, and having received the crown of confession, didst enter into the heavenly courts, where forget not us also, O father Eustratius.

*Glory, both now: Theotokion:*
Unto us who are burned by the flame of passions from destructive assaults, make haste quickly to send down the water of grace, O all-pure Virgin, and quench the fire that burns in our members, that leading this life in quietness, through thee we may radiantly enjoy the illumination of Christ.

*Gradual Hymns, the 1st Antiphon of Tone 4.*
*Prokeimenon:* Precious in the sight of the Lord is the death of His venerable ones.
*Verse:* What shall I render unto the Lord for all that He has rendered unto me?[^161]
*Let every breath praise the Lord.*
*The Gospel from Mark, pericope 37.*
*After Psalm 50, Sticheron, Tone 2:*
Today the hill of the Caves rejoices, possessing thy relics like a priceless treasure, and is illuminated by the beams of thy sufferings, bringing flowers of spiritual songs unto thee and saying: O servant of Christ and our father Eustratius, standing before the Master of all, unceasingly pray unto the Lord for us, to send down great mercy upon our souls.

*To Eustratius of the Caves*

*Canon to the Venerable-Martyr, Tone 1. Ode 1. Heirmos:*[^162]
Thy victorious right hand has been divinely glorified in power, O Immortal One: for being almighty, it shattered adversaries, fashioning a new path through the deep for the Israelites.

*Troparia:*
Desiring today, and seeking to be a partaker of light forever, in the day of the passions, O blessed one, thou wast lifted up upon a cross, and wast perfected in the passion of Christ, having conquered the world; and now being a partaker of ineffable glory, rejoicing, thou singest a victorious song.

Nothing in this tumultuous world didst thou ever love as the Cross of Christ, and having it as a boast, with the Apostle thou didst cry aloud: God forbid that I should boast, save only in the Cross of the Lord, wherein rejoicing, thou singest a victorious song.

Nailed unto the cross by the Jews, O all-blessed father, thou didst nail all corruptible passions, so that with the Apostle thou didst say: The world is crucified unto me, and I unto the world; therefore worthily now rejoicing with the heavenly hosts, thou singest a victorious song.

*Glory:*
Departing from the voluptuous Egypt of this world, upon the all-honorable Cross thou didst traverse its sea untroubled; and writing against it with thy nailed palms, thou didst drown the noetic Pharaoh with his aerial warriors, and now rejoicing, singest a victorious song.

*Both now: Theotokion:*
Departing from this voluptuous world as from Egypt, I return unto former things, pleasing myself by feeding on husks: grant me, O Virgin Bride of God, to turn away at least a little from these, that being free I may bring praise unto thee, singing a victorious song.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*[^163]
O Thou Who alone knowest the weakness of human nature, and didst mercifully take form therein, gird me from on high with power, that I may cry aloud unto Thee: Holy art Thou, O animate temple of Thine ineffable glory, O Lover of mankind!

*Troparia:*
Thou didst follow Him Who said: Come unto Me, all ye that labor, O father, and by His Cross didst disperse the noetic enemies, who being conquered unto the end were confounded with shame: pray thou also for us, that we may stand against their warfare, for upon thee are we established.

Laying the cross upon Thy shoulder, O holy one, by unchangeable faith with love thou didst cleave unto the One, that thou mightest gain Christ, and according to Thy desire didst find Him by the narrow path *Service to the Holy Venerable-Martyr*

...which thou didst traverse without stumbling, having the Cross as dominion and confirmation.

Clothed in the monastic habit, thou wast stripped of the world, O blessed one, and having lightly traversed the path into the promised land, into the Monastery of the Caves, which flows like milk and honey with the teachings of the God-bearing fathers, wherewith being nourished, thou wast strong: by their teachings grant that we also may be confirmed.

*Glory:*
By the word of prayer and fasting having subjected the murderous enemy unto the spirit, and by humility and obedience having conquered the noetic Amalek, thou wast pleasing unto thy Master, O father, and from Him didst receive the Cross, an invincible weapon, wherein we also are confirmed.

*Both now: Theotokion:*
Tossed by the enemy's assaults, having no confirmation, I, the passionate one, flee unto thee, O Mother of God: for thou art an unbreakable wall unto the faithful, wherein we are confirmed.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 5:*[^164]
Come, ye people, let us hymn the memory of the victorious sufferer, passing the day of his rejoicing with odes and songs: for from this valley of weeping he passed unto unending joy, and standing visibly before the throne, he cries aloud unto us: Become emulators of me, even as I am of Christ, Who grants His abundant mercy unto all.

*Glory, both now: Theotokion:*
Having a passionate life in the world, I find myself tossed by deadly winds; O thou that didst bear Christ the Helmsman, direct my life unto the calm haven, I pray, and grant me thy rich mercy.

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*[^165]
Beholding with foresighted eyes thee as a mountain overshadowed by the grace of God, Habakkuk proclaimed that the Holy One of Israel should come forth from thee, for our salvation and renewal.

*Troparia:*
Sole venerable one, for the sake of the incontinence of the first-fashioned man who introduced hell, thou didst betake thyself unto abstinence and severe fasting, and therefrom didst acquire the name of faster: for by the word of God rather than by meats wast thou nourished, singing unto Christ: For Thou art almighty.

Having been taken captive by the Polovtsians by the permission of the Most High, with apostolic words Thou didst cry aloud: He is free who has captivated his mind unto the obedience of Christ; therefore in affliction Thou didst not faint, but rather marveling at the judgments of God, didst glorify Christ: For Thou art almighty.

*To Eustratius of the Caves*

Always bearing upon thy body the image of Jesus Who was betrayed, thou wast sold into the Greek land in the city of Korsun unto a certain God-slaying Jew, and he strove to turn thee away from the love of God, for which thou wast ready even unto death, singing: Christ is risen, for He is almighty.

*Glory:*
Shut up in prison with other Christians, and tormented with hunger, O holy one, enduring sweetly, thou didst say: Who shall separate us from the love of Christ? Neither hunger, nor death, nor distress: for all these things are small compared to the glory that shall be revealed in us, who sing: Christ is risen, for He is almighty.

*Both now: Theotokion:*
Having been taken captive by noetic enemies, and being already near the prison of hades, unto thee, O Compassionate One, do I cry aloud: O Mother of God, when thou shalt stir up the captivity of my soul, then shall my tongue be filled with joy, and shall sing: Thy Son is risen, for He is almighty.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*[^166]
O Christ Who didst enlighten the ends of the world with the radiance of Thy coming, and illuminated them with Thy Cross: enlighten with the light of Thy divine knowledge the hearts of them that sing of Thee in orthodox manner.

*Troparia:*
Oppressed by love for the Cross, O noble Eustratius, resisting them that compelled thee alone to apostatize from thy Christ, Who is the Sun of righteousness, bringing life unto all that cry aloud.

By thy teaching, O father, all the captives who were in prison spat upon the murderous intent of the God-slaying Hebrew, and chose rather to die of hunger and thirst than to deny Christ, Who gives life unto all.

A regiment of fifty martyrs slain by hunger and thirst by the Jew, thou didst send before thee unto thy Christ, O blessed one, thyself intending afterward to suffer the same for the love of Him Who gives life unto all.

*Glory:*
The wretched one, seeing that by the death of all the prisoners in the dungeon he had buried his gold and his hope of drawing them into his impiety, purposed to crucify thee upon a cross, unto which thou didst run hastily, ever calling aloud: O my Christ, lead forth my soul from the prison of the flesh!

*Both now: Theotokion:*
O all-blessed Virgin, have compassion by thy grace upon my soul that perishes in sin, and satisfy it with angelic bread, and water it from the stream of the Church's sweetness, that I may find peace, a comfort for my soul.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*[^167]
The lowest abyss has surrounded us, and there is none to deliver; we are accounted as sheep for the slaughter *Service to the Holy Venerable-Martyr*

...we are accounted as sheep for the slaughter: save Thy people, O our God, for Thou art the strength of the weak and their restoration.

*Troparia:*
Like a water-beast's belly was the womb of the lawless Jew, which sought to swallow thee up and bring thee into the number of the darkened Jewish assembly; but thou, O father, didst boldly stand against his gaping jaws, and didst overcome him, being strengthened by the help of the Most High.

On the day when the Jews raged and hung the Giver of life upon the cross, then the wretched Hebrew crucified thee also: that with Him with Whom thou didst suffer, with Him thou mightest be glorified, O blessed one, in unwaning light, having received recompense from His Son.

Remembering the curse with mockery, the foul God-slayers, even as their fathers did unto Christ, so mockingly nailed thee to the wood of the cross, O father; but the wood of the cross was foolishness unto them, but unto thee salvation and the power of God.

*Glory:*
Thou didst reprove the foul feasts of the Jews, hanging upon the cross, O holy one, not fearing their curse, as thou didst possess Christ God Who was nailed in the flesh and enlightened the world by His three-day resurrection.

*Both now: Theotokion:*
Thou that didst destroy the ancestral curse by thy blessed childbirth, O pure Mother: I pray thee to vouchsafe me the blessing of God's chosen ones, O blessed and glorified one.

*Kontakion, Tone 8:*[^168]
As a zealot of the Master's passions and an agreeable follower of the ascetics, having instructed the captives by thy teaching, thou didst bring them unto Christ, O Eustratius; and deliver us who are captive unto passions by thy supplications, and show us to be emulators of thine honorable life, who cry aloud: Rejoice, thou that didst sweetly drink the cup of Christ's sufferings!

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*[^169]
Thee, O Mother of God, we the faithful perceive as a noetic furnace: for as the Supremely Exalted One saved the three youths, so the Almighty One renewed the world in thy womb, the praised and supremely glorified God of our fathers.

*Troparia:*
Nailed upon the cross, O father, thou didst reprove the foolish Jewish command from prophetic writings, and didst not desire to see the legal passover, desiring to be a partaker of the Passover which was sacrificed for us in the world, Christ God the blessed.

Citing from the prophets the destruction of the curse that came from the tree, O blessed one, and preaching the entrance of life in the Life hanging upon the wood of the cross before their eyes, thou didst confound the Jews, teaching them to sing: Blessed art Thou, O God!

*To Eustratius of the Caves*

Unto the Jews that crucified thee thou didst prophesy, O venerable father: Vengeance will soon come upon you for my blood and for that of the other Christians whom ye have innocently slain, and your feasts shall be turned into mourning: for the Lord hates your piety; unto whom thou wast prepared to sing: Blessed art Thou, O Christ God!

*Glory:*
Thy words were sharp arrows unto the lawless ones, wherewith being pierced they could not endure, but pierced thy ribs with a spear; and thou didst depart unto Him Who poured forth life unto the whole world from His pierced side, to sing: Blessed art Thou, O God!

*Both now: Theotokion:*
Pierced with the spear of sin by fierce Belial, make haste to apply the plaster of thy mercy, O Virgin Mother and Bride of God, that being healed I may ever sing unto thee, O Lady: Blessed art thou!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*[^170]
In the furnace the Israelite youths, as in a smelter, shone purer than gold with the beauty of piety, saying: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord; sing and supremely exalt Him unto all ages.

*Troparia:*
Honor, ye people, the good sufferer, who having formerly crucified all his members mentally within himself, afterward, being visibly crucified by the Jews, cried aloud unto Christ: I bear Thy wounds upon my body, and I sing of Thee unto all ages.

When thou wast borne up unto heaven, O father, on a chariot and horses of fire, the choirs of martyrs and righteous ones came forth to meet thee, and receiving thy God-praised wounds of hands and feet, and thy pierced side, they received thee into the habitation of the saints, to praise the Lord unto ages.

A voice was brought unto thee from the majestic glory, O holy one: Behold, the good heavenly citizen, of whom the whole world was not worthy, ascends from this earthly world unto the heavenly habitations, to sing unto the Lord unto all ages.

*We bless the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit: the Lord.*
The lawless Hebrew, wishing to hide the priceless treasure of thy relics, O most blessed one, cast them into the sea; but by the providence of God they were found here in the cave: bowing before which, we glorify the Lord Who glorified thee with incorruption and wonders, unto all ages.

*Both now: Theotokion:*
O priceless treasure of the most precious Jesus, who didst proceed from maternal loins: deliver my soul from the captivity of the voluptuous world, that I may sing of thee *Service to the Holy Venerable-Martyr*

...here, and in the unwaning light unto the ages.

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*[^171]
The unburnt bush showed forth an image of thy pure childbirth: and now we pray thee to extinguish the furnace of temptations against us, that we may unceasingly magnify thee, O Mother of God.

*Troparia:*
Shine, shine, O Monastery of the Caves of the Mother of God: for in thee marvelous Eustratius grew up as a flourishing tree, adorned with the sweet-smelling flowers of suffering: that we also may become partakers of his beauty, we bow before his relics and magnify him with hymns.

God Who reveals secrets showed the wretched murderers unto the pious Emperor,[^172] how the Jews hypocritically kill Christians in secret; and unrighteousness came upon their head: for now they openly received retribution according to thy prophecy, O most blessed father.

Thy blood, poured forth innocently by the Jews, O father, like Abel's cried out unto God, Who by a righteous judgment brought vengeance for it; and thy tormentor, having been hanged upon a tree, received the lot of Judas in Gehenna; while the Lord took thee as His child, Whom bowing down before today we magnify.

*Glory:*
Thy blood, O holy one, and thy unfailing prophecy afterward brought many unto faith: for seeing the fulfillment thereof, having truly repented they believed in Christ, and having been baptized they lived well, and were vouchsafed blessed life; of which let not us also be deprived, as we bow before the shrine of thy relics and magnify thee.

*Both now: Theotokion:*
O blameless Mother, who didst embrace eternal Life in thy womb: direct us from temporal life unto eternal life through thy mediation, and grant unto all to behold the unwaning day of the Kingdom of thy Son.

*Exapostilarion:*
Foreseeing eternal life, O blessed one, thou didst take no thought for the temporal, bravely enduring all things inflicted by the Jews; and refusing to taste of the legal passover, thou wast nailed to the cross, and ascending from earth upon a fiery chariot, didst testify: Christ is the Passover of incorruption, the salvation of the world.

*Glory, both now: Theotokion:*
Thou art the foreshadowed sanctuary of the Ever-Existing Light, O Virgin Bride of God: stand forth unto us that dwell in darkness, and direct our life, that without stumbling we may attain to Eustratius of the Caves*

...to where Christ is, the Passover of incorruption, the salvation of the world.

*At the Praises, Stichera, Tone 6:*
Having reddened thy robe, dyed purple with martyr's blood, O venerable one, worthily hast thou entered into the Holy of Holies, O glorious one, where is the unwaning light, where is divine glory, where is the voice of them that feast: from Whom for the labors of thy suffering thou hast received the victories, and an unfading crown, and everlasting good glory, and the life of paradise, O all-wise Eustratius; with boldness pray for our souls. *(Twice)*

The tempests of torments did not shake thy soul, nor did the long confinement in the dark prison hide thee, O glorious sufferer; but like a light thou didst shine forth in darkness as a beacon unto the pious, whom thou didst reveal as sons of the day through the laver of baptism, by rebirth of water and salvation, and by divine abiding: as a God-pleasing sufferer, O all-wise Eustratius.

Who shall worthily recount the victories of thy virtues? What lips shall utter thy sufferings and endurance? For in both thou didst well-please Christ, O Eustratius; and now cease not praying, O servant of God, to save our souls, as having great boldness, O ever-memorable one.

*Glory: Tone 8:*
The Cherub, seeing thee ascending unto heavenly realms on a fiery chariot, with his flaming sword did not forbid thee to taste of the tree of life, but with joy received thee, O martyr of Christ, Eustratius. Therefore now enjoying the food of paradise, forget not us that honor thy memory, but stretch forth thy palms, wherewith thou wast nailed to the tree, and pray for all unto the Trinity and Christ, that we also may become partakers with thee, O most blessed father.

*Both now: Theotokion.*
*Great Doxology, and dismissal.*

*(Woodcut Engraving: Saint Niphont, Bishop of Novgorod (*С: Нїфо́нтъ Епⷭ҇копъ Новгородскій*), depicted in full episcopal vestments with sakkos, omophorion, and mitre, holding a pastoral crosier in his left hand and blessing with his right; rays of divine light emanate from a cloud above; behind him on the left rises the high multistoried church of the Kyiv-Pechersk Monastery.)*[^173]

---

# MONTH OF APRIL

*On the 8th Day: Service to our Father among the Saints, Niphont, Bishop of Novgorod.*[^174]

*At Vespers, on "Lord, I have cried," Stichera, Tone 4. Podoben: "As one valiant" (Ꙗ҆́кѡ до́блѧ):*[^175]
The adornment of hierarchs and zealot of piety, come, ye people, let us honor today with songs, weaving praiseful crowns with love *To Niphont, Bishop of Novgorod*

...and bowing before the shrine of the relics of the worthily-blessed father, with suppliant voices let us cry aloud: O holy hierarch of Christ, Niphont, as having boldness toward God, forget not, diligently praying, ever to intercede for thy flock.

The tongue of thy honey-flowing lips, O most blessed teacher Niphont, being the pen of the Holy Spirit, cast down the pride of them that hate thee: and now smite the enemies that rise up in pride against thy flock, and strengthening us in orthodox dogmas, save us by thine intercession, that with a pure conscience we may offer praise unto God concerning thee in love.

Illuminated by the light of God's grace, Thou didst shine forth like the sun in the world, and with the rays of thy virtues didst enlighten many lands; therefore by direction from on high, Thou wast chosen by all with one accord as shepherd unto the rational flock; for which also after thy departure diligently pray, that it may be preserved unassailed by enemies.

*Glory: Tone 2:*
Come, ye assemblies of the Orthodox of Novgorod, keeping festival today in the yearly memory of the most blessed father, having formed a choir with us in love, let us give glory unto God the Deliverer, for He gave unto us a shepherd and teacher and intercessor for our souls.

*Both now: Theotokion, or Stavrotheotokion.*
*At the Aposticha, in the Triodion.*
*Glory: Tone 4:*
Having made thy heart a temple for the Holy Spirit, thou didst erect houses of prayer for the increase of God's glory, which having splendidly adorned, thou didst gather thy rational sheep as into a heavenly sheepfold, and didst set before them the pasture of thy sweet and God-inspired teaching, instructing and guiding them unto the path of piety and salvation; whereon also direct us by thy prayers, O venerable father, that we may ever glorify the one God, and Savior of our souls.

*Both now: Theotokion, or Stavrotheotokion.*

*Troparion, Tone 3:*
As by disposition a true defender of the Orthodox traditions of the God-bearing Church, thou wast vouchsafed to see Theodosius the great among the fathers after his death, as alive, announcing unto thee the inheritance of the Heavenly Kingdom, whose ineffable goods thou now inseparably enjoyest with him; pray also for the salvation of thy children, O most praised Niphont.[^176]

*Glory, both now: Theotokion.*

*Service to the Hierarch*

*Canon to the Hierarch, Tone 1. Ode 1. Heirmos:*[^177]
Thy victorious right hand has been divinely glorified in power, O Immortal One: for being almighty, it shattered adversaries, fashioning a new path through the deep for the Israelites.

*Troparia:*
May Thy right hand, O my Christ, guide me on this present day to sing the memory of Thy servant, driving away the enemies that hinder me, and renewing the path grown old with passions within my heart.

Having sent the grace of Thy Comforter unto Thy servant, O Christ, Thou didst call him to run after Thee: who immediately renounced the world and loved Thy law, renewing the path unto Thy love.

Guided by Thine arm, O Lord, Thy venerable one came unto the untroubled haven, into the most honorable Caves Monastery of Thy Mother, and became a monk, living the monastic life in virtuous conduct.

*Glory:*
Far and wide did thy virtue shine forth, O father, seeing which many magnified thee, and glorified the Heavenly Father Who gave unto thee such grace; of which let not us also be deprived, through thy righteous deeds, O father.

*Both now: Theotokion:*
Be thou my guide, O Virgin Bride of God, leading me upon the path of salvation, and renewing my soul that is grown old with passions, O Mary, by thy compassions.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*[^178]
O Thou Who alone knowest the weakness of human nature, and didst mercifully take form therein: gird me with power from on high, that I may cry aloud unto Thee: Holy art Thou, O animate temple of Thine ineffable glory, O Lover of mankind!

*Troparia:*
Seeing the one right way, to take up Thy cross in this life and to follow Thee his Master, Thy faithful servant immediately hastened eagerly thereto: therefore now he is satisfied with Thine ineffable glory, O Lover of mankind.

Unceasingly sending up thy prayers unto the one God from a pure heart, thou didst break the wiles of the feeble enemy, striking him down with the spear of faith; therefore, O hierarch, thou hast received the crown of glory from Christ the Lover of mankind.

Soaring on high through vigilance and fasting, thou didst not choose to incline thy mind below unto fleeting things, and through such action didst acquire the vision of God's glory *To Niphont, Bishop of Novgorod*

...which that we also may obtain, pray thou unto Jesus the Lover of mankind.

*Glory:*
Thy life shone forth before men, so that seeing thy virtues, O father, they chose thee for the archiepiscopal throne of Novgorod, unto which thou wast also a shepherd, leading thy sheep unto Jesus the Chief Shepherd, the Lover of mankind.

*Both now: Theotokion:*
O pure Mother of the Chief Shepherd: guide me, a straying sheep, unto the right path, and direct my life, that guided by thee I may be vouchsafed to be filled with the glory of Jesus the Lover of mankind.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 4:*[^179]
Having labored with great zeal for Orthodoxy, thou didst multiply the glory of God in thy flock; now therefore, being glorified where the voice of them that feast is unceasing, pray for them that honor thy memory, O Niphont, most sacred father.

*Glory, both now: Theotokion:*
As thou didst bear the Lord of glory Who promised to deliver us from inglorious standing: vouchsafe us to be partakers of the glory of the saints, and glorify them that honor thy childbirth, O pure one.

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*[^180]
Beholding with foresighted eyes thee as a mountain overshadowed by the grace of God, Habakkuk proclaimed that the Holy One of Israel should come forth from thee, for our salvation and renewal.

*Troparia:*
Them that walk upon the mountains of high-mindedness thou didst humble, O blessed one, not permitting them to wage internecine strife; but thou didst bless them with peace, bringing them unto salvation and the renewal of love.[^181]

Following the apostolic words, in season and out of season thou didst press urgently, O father, with all thy might striving that thy sheep should not depart from the commandments of the Lord, and from the canonical traditions of His Church, for these are for our salvation and renewal.

Who can recount thy boldness, O hierarch? For thou didst speak of the testimonies of the Lord before princes, not being ashamed before the diadem; therefore thou wast a second Forerunner, rebuking lawless deeds.[^182]

*Glory:*
The princes who gave over their body not unto Christ but unto fornication through an unlawful marriage, thou didst boldly rebuke, apostolically commanding thy church and all the clergy to turn away from such as walk disorderly.

*Both now: Theotokion:*
Reclaim me who live lawlessly, and the perverted *Service to the Hierarch*

...passion-storms in me humble, O Mother of God, that being freed from them through thee, I may turn back: for thou art the only salvation and renewal of us all.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*[^183]
O Christ Who didst enlighten the ends of the world with the radiance of Thy coming, and illuminated them with Thy Cross: enlighten with the light of Thy divine knowledge the hearts of them that sing of Thee in orthodox manner.

*Troparia:*
As a second Theodore the Studite,[^184] a hierarch zealot, the Church sings of thee, O blessed one, for thy boldness and confession, and calls unto spiritual rejoicing them that lovingly praise thee, O father.

Thou didst withstand them that walk uncanonically, O venerable one, in nowise consenting that without the blessing of the Ecumenical Patriarch of Constantinople a hierarch should be advanced to the throne, and an ungodly wolf be given as shepherd unto the sheep that piously glorify Christ.[^185]

Worthy of honor art thou, O father, for when they elevated Clement to the dignity of the hierarch's office by the head of Saint Clement,[^186] thou didst not consent, but rather, having rebuked the men-pleasers, didst condemn their council.

*Glory:*
Being a true son of the eastern ecumenical throne, thou didst not consent to abandon its primacy for destructive councils, but didst worthily maintain that consecration must be confirmed from the beginning, that through the guilelessness of its natural shepherd, the Patriarch of Constantinople, all things should be done.

*Both now: Theotokion:*
O thou who didst crown the human race with blessing: vouchsafe unto us the blessing of the chosen, and grant unto them that in right faith praise thee to behold the glory of thy most sweet Son.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*[^187]
