The lowest abyss has surrounded us, and there is none to deliver; we are accounted as sheep for the slaughter: save Thy people, O our God, for Thou art the strength of the weak and their restoration.

*Troparia:*
The hierarch who hated righteousness surrounded thee with malice, for thou didst not communicate with him, since he had unrighteously usurped the throne; but thou, O father, wast not terrified of him, having hope in God, Who is the strength of the weak and their restoration.

Scheming to restrain thee who couldst not be restrained, O blessed one, the hierarch plotted with the prince to send thee into exile; and he restrained thy person, but could not restrain thy tongue, for thou didst rebuke him for unlawfully usurping the throne.[^188]

Thy words were a sword unto the lawless, wherewith thou didst cut down their frenzy; for this reason they hated thee *To Niphont, Bishop of Novgorod*

...they hated thee, and separated thee from the throne; but thou didst not lose heart, O father, hoping for the beatitude of them that are persecuted for righteousness' sake.[^189]

*Glory:*
Thy rebuke, O hierarch Niphont, was a wound unto the ungodly, for it consumed them as transgressors of the law; and their hearts swelled, gnashing their teeth, wishing to tear thee to pieces.

*Both now: Theotokion:*
Noetic beasts have surrounded us, gnashing their teeth and rejoicing at our ruin; but do thou, O Virgin mighty in strength, break their jaws, and strengthen us to stand against them: for thou art the strength of the weak and their restoration.

*Kontakion, Tone 5:*[^190]
Emulating the zeal of the ancient fathers in the steadfast keeping of true dogmas, O firm-minded Niphont, thou didst not consent to concelebrate with Clement who arbitrarily usurped the throne of the metropolis; but submitting to the one chosen for it by all,[^191] thou didst serve with him in sacred splendor, and having ended thy life, didst find the unending Kingdom of Christ.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*[^192]
Thee, O Mother of God, we the faithful perceive as a noetic furnace: for as the Supremely Exalted One saved the three youths, so the Almighty One renewed the world in thy womb, the praised and supremely glorified God of our fathers.

*Troparia:*
Thou, O most blessed father, dwelling in the most marvelous monastery as in a heavenly bridal chamber, rejoicing, didst offer thanksgiving unto the King of all and unto her most sweet Son, singing: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

Having stood boldly for Orthodoxy, O venerable one, thou wast deprived of thy throne by transgressors of the law; but a throne was prepared for thee in the heavenly sheepfold, as for a true witness of righteousness.

When the Chief Shepherd saw thy sheep troubled by thy quieting, He gave thee unto them again as shepherd; for he that worked evil, by the righteous judgment of God, himself received double evil, and he that removed thee from the throne himself fell from the throne.

*Glory:*
The pious Prince George,[^193] having righteously driven Izyaslav from the throne of the principality, released thee with great honor unto thy throne; whereon seeing thee, O venerable father, thy sheep cried aloud with joy: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Both now: Theotokion:*
The human race which had righteously fallen from God's grace thou didst raise up by thy most pure childbirth, O Mother of God; therefore joyfully we sing unto thee who art blessed throughout all generations.

*Service to the Hierarch*

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*[^194]
In the furnace the Israelite youths, as in a smelter, shone purer than gold with the beauty of piety, saying: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord; sing and supremely exalt Him unto all ages.

*Troparia:*
When the Ecumenical Patriarch of Constantinople heard that thou wast unburned in the furnace of temptations and in the flame of flattery of the transgressors of the law, he called thy courage blessed, O father, and numbered thee among the ancient confessors, with whom thou now singest of the Lord unto ages.[^195]

Seeing thy boldness written in the book of the living, the Patriarch of Constantinople sent his written epistle unto thee, wherein he called blessed thy zeal for Orthodoxy, which remembering, the sons of Rus' also supremely exalt the Lord unto ages.

Kindled with double desire, O hierarch, thou didst come in haste unto the city of Kyiv, desiring to bow before the Mother of God in the Caves Monastery and before the venerable fathers, and likewise to receive a blessing from the coming hierarch; therefore thou wast vouchsafed to see a marvelous vision, how Saint Theodosius said unto thee: From now on thou shalt be inseparably with us, singing of the Lord unto ages.

*Glory: We bless the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit: the Lord.*
Like a fledgling the swallow's nest built on the hill of the Caves by the appointed dove,[^196] lovingly didst thou desire to abide therein without departing; and though such desire drew thee, yet God's providence prepared Thy departure from temporal things unto eternal.

*Both now: Theotokion:*
A fledgling am I born in thy sheepfold, O my Lady, yet I have fallen down, ensnared by demonic assaults; yet I pray thee, bring me again into it: that where my beginning was, there also I may receive a good end, and with the holy fathers supremely exalt thy Son unto ages.

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*[^197]
The unburnt bush showed forth an image of thy pure childbirth: and now we pray thee to extinguish the furnace of temptations against us, that we may unceasingly magnify thee, O Mother of God.

*Troparia:*
By the image of a marvelous vision the Master of all assured thee, O father, that after thy labors thou shouldest depart unto peaceful places, where the angelic hosts unceasingly glorify the All-Holy Trinity, Whom we also with them magnify.

Brought down into a light sleep, O father, thou didst seem to stand in the Caves church, stretching forth supplication unto the Most Pure Mother of God, that thou mightest see the face of blessed Theodosius; and it came to pass even as thou didst desire.

According to thy desire, in the vision one of the blessed took thee, O venerable one, and brought thee unto blessed *To Niphont, Bishop of Novgorod*

...Theodosius; seeing whom joyfully, thou didst fall at his feet, and wast vouchsafed a blessing; held by desire of whom, we also magnify the All-Holy Trinity.

*Glory:*
O most sweet voice! O good greeting of our father! For he said: Henceforth thou shalt be with us; and he showed thee a scroll wherein was written: Behold, I and the children whom God has given me.[^198] That we also may hear this voice, we pray unto the Mother of God, and ever with thee, O father, we magnify Him.

*Both now: Theotokion:*
Rejoice, O scroll, most pure Virgin, for in thee by the finger of God the Pre-eternal Word was inscribed; do thou write us also upon the scroll with thy servants: ever with the father we pray unto thee, and with songs magnify thee.

*Exapostilarion:*
Having lived angelically upon earth, thou didst appear as a companion of the angels; for having been translated from lower things, thou didst depart unto the highest, where standing together before the God of all, pray for them that honor thy memory, O blessed Niphont.

*Glory, both now: Theotokion:*
O thou that art higher than the heavenly ranks: depart not from us on earth, but ever be with us, delivering us from all assaults: for while thy help is steadfastly with us, no one shall rise up against us.

*At the Praises, Stichera, Tone 4:*
On the auspicious day of the memory of the God-bearing father, come, ye people named after Christ, let us leap for joy, offering praiseful songs, and regaling ourselves with spiritual sweetness, in love let us cry aloud: O Niphont, most sacred father, pray unto Christ God for the salvation of our souls.

We bring unto thee, O venerable father, on this present day songful gifts in love, remembering the achievements of thy virtues, and we pray unto Christ God, that having escaped harmful thoughts, we may direct our life in good deeds, and not be deprived of heavenly honors through thy prayers.

Who can worthily praise Thy holy deeds, O God? For being wounded with Thy love, they despised all earthly things, and being in the flesh, conquered the bodiless enemy, and now rejoice with the bodiless powers in heaven; of whose choir vouchsafe us also to be partakers, through the prayers of Thy teacher.

*Service to the Hierarch*

*Glory: Tone 6:*
The adornment of hierarchs and glory of fathers, the wellspring of wonders and speedy helper of the faithful, come, ye feast-loving people today, having assembled in the most honorable temple let us hymn with praises, saying: Rejoice, O good ascetic! Rejoice, O watchful shepherd of the rational sheep! Rejoice, thou that didst drive away the noetic wolf from thy flock by the rod of faith, and didst lead it wholly unto thy Chief Shepherd; unto Whom ever pray for us, that we may be preserved unharmed from all enemies, and may become heirs of eternal life.

*Both now: Theotokion: O Mother of God, thou art the true vine:*

*Great Doxology, and dismissal.*
*At the Liturgy, the common service to a Hierarch.*

*(Woodcut Engraving: Saint Simon, Bishop of Vladimir and Suzdal (*С: Сїмо́нъ Епⷭ҇кпъ Владимирескій и Сꙋждалскій*), depicted standing in episcopal vestments with sakkos, omophorion, and mitre, blessing with his right hand and holding a pastoral crosier in his left; a dove representing the Holy Spirit descends from heaven above; in the background on the left is the Dormition Cathedral of Vladimir and monastic dwellings, with clergy and people in procession on the right.)*[^199]

---

# MONTH OF MAY

*On the 10th Day: Service to our Father among the Saints, Simon, Bishop of Vladimir and Suzdal.*[^200]

*At Vespers, "Blessed is the man," the first antiphon.*
*On "Lord, I have cried," Stichera, Tone 5. Podoben: "Rejoice, life-bearing Cross" (Ра́дꙋйсѧ живоно́сный):*[^201]
Rejoice, adornment of the Church, beauty of hierarchs, most sacred father, good shepherd: who having loved Jesus the Chief Shepherd from thy youth, with thy whole soul didst ever serve Him; us who with passions *Service to the Hierarch*

...us who are enslaved by passions, who from the soul fall down before thee, restore unto the former freedom by thy prayers.

Rejoice, most fervent lover of Christ thy Lord, O worthily-marvelous Simon: who by the warmth of spiritual grace being exceedingly kindled into the love of God, didst despise all the fair things of this world, and didst captivate thy mind into the obedience of Christ; therefore now, dwelling as free in free habitations, free our minds that are captivated by the assaults of the enemy, O blessed father, and deliver us from them through thy mediation with God, as we pray and honor thy memory, O father.

Them that were held fast by the turmoils of this life, and were going astray upon the paths of passions, O hierarch Simon, by wise words, instructing and guiding them by thy wise teaching, thou didst lead unto the path of salvation, having become their guide unto the heavenly height; where now dwelling thyself with the angels, direct our souls into life and salvation by thine invisible governance.

*Glory: Tone 6:*
Thy sweet tongue for teaching even now stirs up the hearts of the slothful, and calls them unto the doing of the Lord's commandments; and thy wilderness flute proclaims God-revealed words, whereby, as by a ladder, they that hear are lifted up and brought unto God; therefore, O Simon, venerable father, as having boldness toward Christ God, cease not praying that we may be enlightened with the light of understanding and good deeds, who lovingly honor thy holy memory.

*Both now: Theotokion.*
*Entrance. Prokeimenon of the day. Three readings for a hierarch from the Discourses of Venerable Ephraim the Syrian (see fol. 47v).*[^202]

*At the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 1. Podoben: "All-praised martyrs" (Прехва́льнїи мч҃нцы):*[^203]
As a rational trumpet of theology and an honorable mirror of virtues, as an initiate of grace, having come together today with hymns we praise thee, O Simon most sacred, and we pray thee: pray thou unto Christ God to grant our souls peace and great mercy.

*Verse:* Thy priests shall clothe themselves with righteousness, and Thy venerable ones shall rejoice.[^204]

*To Simon, Bishop of Suzdal*

Having received illumination from the noetic Light, O blessed one, thou wast a radiant beacon in the midst of the ascetic choir; from whence by election from on high being entrusted with the episcopal throne, thou wast a good shepherd of the rational sheep of Christ God; before Whom now standing, pray thou to grant our souls peace and great mercy.

*Verse:* The mouth of the righteous shall meditate on wisdom, and his tongue shall speak of judgment.[^205]
By thy many labors, O consecrated and blessed hierarch, thou hast ascended unto the unwaning Light, and dwellest rejoicing with thy first founders;[^206] with whom also pray for us, that we may be vouchsafed to abide in eternal life with thee and with all the saints.

*Glory: Tone 6:*
The image of thy radiant life, O most sacred Simon, directed many unto virtues, and the river of thy saving teaching stirred up unto vigilance souls held fast by despondency, and raised the slothful unto the praise of God; therefore honoring thy all-honorable memory, we fall down and pray, that through thy guidance we may direct our life in peace, and receive great mercy from Christ God.

*Both now: Theotokion: Save from perils thy servants, O all-holy Mother of God:*

*Troparion, Tone 4:*
O true writer of the lives of the venerable fathers of the Caves, all-blessed hierarch of Christ Simon: with them diligently pray unto Christ God to direct our life in peace, and to inscribe us in the books of life, saving our souls.[^207]
*Glory, both now: Theotokion.*

*At Matins, on "God is the Lord," Troparion to the Hierarch, twice. Glory, both now: Theotokion.*
*After Kathisma 1, Sessional Hymn, Tone 4. Podoben: "Thou hast appeared" (Ꙗ҆ви́лсѧ є҆сѝ):*[^208]
As with a gold-woven robe, by thy orthodox teaching the Church of Vladimir is clothed, and the ranks of monastics are adorned by thy guidance; while laboring in the one priesthood, they ever glorify thee, O hierarch Simon.
*Glory, both now: Theotokion:*
O most pure Virgin, Mother of God: despise not us who run unto thy goodness and seek thy speedy defense, but incline unto supplication, help in our needs, and deliver our souls from perils.

*After Kathisma 2, Sessional Hymn, Tone 3. Podoben: "The beauty of virginity" (Красотѣ̀ дѣ́вства):*[^209]
A noetic treasure from our first-founding fathers *Service to the Hierarch*

...having received, O venerable father Simon, all-wise hierarch, thou didst multiply it into threefold talents, and left it abundantly unto them that came after thee; and thyself as a good servant didst enter into the joy of thy Lord; of which also vouchsafe us who honor thy memory with praises, through thy prayers.[^210]

*Glory, both now: Theotokion:*
The talents entrusted unto us by God we have ruined through sloth, and we are ashamed to appear empty-handed before our Lord: unto thee do we flee, O Lady; grant at least one mite from the unsearchable treasury of thy mercy, that we be not put to shame in the day of the dread examination, and may unceasingly magnify thee with praises.

*After the Polyeleos, Sessional Hymn, Tone 5. Podoben: "Co-unoriginate Word" (Собеꙁнача́лное Сло́во):*[^211]
The wise hierarch and unswerving shepherd, faithful writer of the lives of the saints and their pious emulator, Simon most sacred, come, ye people, let us bless in hymns: for he now stands on high before the throne of God amid the unceasing vision of the radiant ones, and unceasingly prays for them that lovingly sing his memory and glorify Christ God.

*Glory, both now: Theotokion:*
O sacred Mother of the Word co-unoriginate with the Father, speedy helper of sinners and intercessor for them that are in perils: stretch forth thy most pure hands, wherewith thou didst embrace and carry in time as an infant Him Who is timeless, and entreat His goodness for us who are much-sinning, that we may be delivered from impending needs and sorrows, that we may magnify thy blameless childbirth.

*Gradual Hymns, the 1st Antiphon of Tone 3.*
*Prokeimenon:* My mouth shall speak wisdom, and the meditation of my heart understanding.[^212]
*Verse:* Thy priests shall clothe themselves with righteousness, and Thy venerable ones shall rejoice.
*Let every breath praise the Lord.*
*The Gospel from John, pericope 36.*[^213]
*After Psalm 50, Sticheron, Tone 6:*
O venerable father, thou wast an example unto them that live the monastic life: for who beheld thy boundless humility and was not corrected in his life? Or who, having seen thy murmuring-free obedience, did not marvel at thy patient endurance? Thou didst teach all by thyself, and even after thy departure thou ceasest not to guide and instruct thy sheep with diligent admonition; therefore as our shepherd and teacher, and companion in character with the venerable, honoring thy memory, we bless thee.

*To Simon, Bishop of Suzdal*

*Canon of the Mother of God, with the Heirmos to 6, and of the Hierarch to 6.*
*Canon to the Hierarch, Tone 8. Ode 1. Heirmos:*[^214]
The wonderworking rod of Moses, striking the sea crosswise and dividing it, submerged the chariot-mounted Pharaoh; while it saved fugitive Israel fleeing on foot, as they sang a song unto God.

*Troparia:*
Direct me, I pray, O Christ, as I strive to sail across the sea of the virtues of Thy hierarch in order to hymn them with praises; and having calmed my mind from the tempestuous thoughts of this life, save me as I sing a song unto Thee my God.

Rude and senseless though I am, yet drawn by love toward thee, I make bold to praise thy memory, O Simon, hierarch of Christ; do thou assist me by thy prayers, and be my guide as I now send up song concerning thee unto my God.

Who shall not marvel, reading thy life in hymns and seeing thy zeal to emulate the first Caves fathers? Therefore I also, marveling, humbly send up a song concerning thee unto my God.

*Glory:*
Having received the angelic habit in the blessed Caves Lavra, thou didst also embrace the angelic life, following Anthony the Great and Theodosius the marvelous; with whom now living thou rejoicest, unceasingly singing hymnody unto God.

*Both now: Theotokion:*
I have changed my habit, yet I have not changed my deeds, O Mother of God; by the grace of Him Who was born of thee and saved the human race, according to thy grace save me as together concerning thee I send up my song unto God.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*[^215]
O Lord, Artificer of the celestial vault and Builder of the Church: do Thou confirm me in Thy love, O summit of desires, confirmation of the faithful, O only Lover of mankind.

*Troparia:*
Thy virtues shone forth like the stars of heaven, O father, with whose rays enlighten us also who walk in the darkness of passions, that concerning thee we may sing unto the Creator of the heavenly vault: Glory to Thee, O only Lover of mankind.

Wishing to be a partaker of the unwaning Light, thou didst depart from the captives of the world, and having received the angelic habit, didst angelically offer praise unto God, crying: Glory to Thee, O only Lover of mankind.

Thou didst come unto the great fathers: Anthony and Theodosius of the Caves *Service to the Hierarch*

...thou didst appear, and like a radiant star didst shine forth in this life, O venerable one, through humility and purity; marveling at which, we sing: Glory to Thee, O only Lover of mankind.

*Glory:*
Thy obedience, O blessed one, was like a mirror in the midst of the assembly of the brethren, and through this thou didst especially draw near unto Jesus Who became obedient even unto death;[^216] with Whom now living in the midst of heavenly citizens, thou singest: Glory to Thee, O only Lover of mankind.

*Both now: Theotokion:*
I hear the commandments of my Lord, yet I disobey them; but do thou, O pure Virgin who didst say: Behold the handmaid of the Lord,[^217] make me a servant of obedience, that I may glorify thee, the only Mother that loves mankind.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 8:*[^218]
Having found the ladder of virtues, thou didst ascend the mountain of dispassion, and seeing the deceit of this world, thou didst renounce the voluptuous kingdom which passes away in the twinkling of an eye, and bowedst not before the world-ruler, but before the only King of ages; therefore with songs we honor thee, O Simon, most blessed father.

*Glory, both now: Theotokion:*
Having thee as the hope of salvation and helper in needs, O Virgin Bride of God, we fear not the assaults of enemies, but in thee we take courage: for thou art our strength and confirmation.

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*[^219]
Thou art my strength, O Lord, Thou art my power, Thou art my God, Thou art my rejoicing, Who didst not leave the bosom of the Father, yet visitedst our poverty: therefore with the Prophet Habakkuk I cry unto Thee: Glory to Thy power, O Lover of mankind!

*Troparia:*
By vigilance and action drawing near unto the noetic Light, O venerable one, thou didst show thyself radiant in life, through which many being guided glorified God, saying: Glory to Thy power, O Lover of mankind!

The report of thy deeds went forth into diverse lands, O father, and many desired to have thee as leader and instructor, that being guided by thee, they might glorify the Heavenly Father, Who glorified thee with such grace.

More than others the Church of Vladimir eagerly called thee, O blessed one, that by thy coming thou mightest gladden her that was troubled, and shepherd her with the rod of thy governance.

*Glory:*
The citizens of Suzdal also set thee forth, forming choirs of spiritual songs, and rejoicing they elevate thee to the archiepiscopal throne; having ascended which, thou wast a good shepherd unto them.

*To Simon, Bishop of Suzdal*

*Both now: Theotokion:*
O sacred Mother of the Good Shepherd: gather thy rational sheep scattered by the noetic wolf, and do thou thyself shepherd us close at hand, that no longer may the wolf of hell dare to approach thy flock.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*[^220]
Why hast Thou cast me away from Thy presence, O unwaning Light, and an alien darkness has covered me, the wretched one? But turn me back, and direct my ways unto the light of Thy commandments, I pray.

*Troparia:*
Unto thee, O Simon, even as unto that beloved disciple Simon,[^221] Jesus the Chief of shepherds entrusted the pasturing of sheep, which having received, thou didst well guide unto the heavenly height; whereto also direct my ways by thy prayers.

From youth thou didst love the Master of all, from Whom in return for love thou didst receive to shepherd the sheep redeemed by His precious blood; of this same Good One I also am a sheep: look upon me, O hierarch Simon, and direct my ways, I pray thee.

The pastoral pipe of thy teachings, O father, gladdened the rational sheep, so that no longer did they prefer corruptible food, feeding on spiritual pasture; nourish me who hunger with thy teaching, and direct my ways, I pray thee.

*Glory:*
With the rod of governance thou didst well guide the flock entrusted unto thee, setting thyself as an example unto them, which written instruction I also read, yet am not able to emulate it; do thou, O good shepherd, that I may become an imitator thereof, direct my ways by thy prayers.

*Both now: Theotokion:*
O nature more sacred than all earth-born through thy most holy childbirth, O Virgin Mary: lift up our mind crawling among earthly trifles unto higher things, and by thy prayers direct our paths to seek them.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*[^222]
Cleanse me, O Savior, for many are mine iniquities, and lead me up from the depth of evils, I pray: for unto Thee have I cried, and do Thou hear me, O God of my salvation!

*Troparia:*
Sanctified by the grace of the Holy Spirit, as a faithful sacred initiate of mysteries, thou didst serve the throne of God on earth in holiness and righteousness, O Simon; therefore now living with the faithful servants in the joy of thy Lord, pray, and be an intercessor with God for my salvation.

Hearing the call, O hierarch, thou wast vouchsafed as one who took upon himself the promised labors: for attending unto pastoral deeds *Service to the Hierarch*

...equal unto the angels, thou didst never abandon monastic ascetic labors; therefore now living in the honor of hierarchs, pray for me that I may not be deprived of my salvation.

Ever remembering the labors of the venerable Caves fathers, O holy one, thou didst learn to emulate them, showing thyself of like character and like-minded with them; thou didst pass from the lesser to the better, that thou mightest be bound with a double crown.

*Glory:*
Through the doing of the Lord's commandments thou didst mortify thy flesh, yet by the grace of God thou didst quicken thy soul, and through knowledge having found the spiritual ladder, thou didst ascend by noetic elevation unto God; before Whom standing, pray for them that sing of thee, O blessed Simon.

*Both now: Theotokion:*
Action through sloth and knowledge through the darkening of passions have I ruined, and possessing neither, I have fled from the bond of good ordering; yet unto thee do I flee, O Mother of God: as thou art strong and all-powerful, correct both, and be the helper of my salvation.

*Kontakion, Tone 8:*[^223]
Though thou wast worthily vested with the vesture of hierarchs, yet thou wast not exalted thereby, but desiredst rather as a hierarch to submit before the brethren of the Caves Lavra, O humble-minded Simon; therefore, not as possessions left outside, but as a treasure within the holy cave wast thou vouchsafed to be laid, and now standing in heaven before the Lord, pray for us that honor Thee.[^224]

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*[^225]
The fire was put to shame by God's condescension in Babylon of old: therefore the youths in the furnace with joyful step, dancing as in a meadow, sang: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparia:*
The grace of God worked in thee, O hierarch: for streams of holy teachings ever went forth from thy mouth, watering the hearts of the slothful, who budding forth fruits of virtues by thy sprinkling cried aloud: Blessed is the God of our fathers!

Thou didst sow the seed of the word of God in the garden of thy flock, planting the lily of purity, prayer like the incense of Lebanon, mercy toward neighbors like an olive tree, and thoughts ever concerning God like a cypress: which things having done thyself, thou didst instruct others also to do.

Having well inscribed the virtues of the Caves fathers upon the tables of thy heart, thou didst not hide them under the bushel of worldly turmoil, O father, but moved by zeal, didst turn thy whole mind to write down their lives for the profit of others; which we now reading *To Simon, Bishop of Suzdal*

...cry aloud: Blessed is the God of our fathers![^226]

*Glory:*
Like a wise bee, having gathered noetic flowers, the lives of those near to thee, thou didst give them unto us that live after thee like a sweet honeycomb, wherewith sweetening our souls, with thee, O Simon, we sing: Blessed is the God of our fathers!

*Both now: Theotokion:*
Having tasted of the honeycomb of passions, I still have lips filled with bitterness: do thou, O Lady who didst bear the most sweet Jesus, sweeten my soul by thy grace, that I may sing: Rejoice, dweller of holy sweetness and blessing of our fathers!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*[^227]
Sevenfold did the Chaldean tyrant frantically kindle the furnace for the pious; but having seen them saved by a greater power, he cried unto the Creator and Deliverer: Ye youths, bless; ye priests, hymn; ye people, supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparia:*
The head of the seven-headed serpent thou didst crush by the sevenfold spiritual gifts, O father, and wounded, he trembles even until now at thy name; which remembering on this present day, concerning thee we cry: Ye people, supremely exalt Christ unto all ages!

Through love toward the Most Holy Mother of God and the Caves fathers, thou seemedst to be separated from them in body only, yet in spirit thou didst ever abide with them and emulate their life; therefore visit us in thy love, and pray that we may supremely exalt Christ unto all ages.

Truly, even as Simon was a most fervent lover of Christ Himself, so thou also, O hierarch Simon, didst show thyself a zealous lover toward the Mother of God and her monastery; and with those whom thou didst love, with them thou now dwellest unto ages.

*We bless the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit: the Lord.*
Accounting thy glory and honor not equal to the dust of the Caves monastery, thou didst desire, O father, that it were better for thee to be cast out for one day as dead in the house of the Mother of God, than to be glorious upon a throne for a thousand years, by reason of thy marvelous humility; therefore we bless thee unto all ages.[^228]

*Both now: Theotokion:*
By thy help, O pure one, I came unto cohabitation with the Caves fathers; yet having fallen through the sight of laxity I turned aside, and the enemy draws me back again into the world unto former things. O Mother of God, shame me into abiding here and taking heed to my salvation, lest falling away I perish unto ages.

*Service to the Hierarch*

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
Heaven was dismayed at this, and the ends of the earth were amazed, that God appeared in the flesh unto men, and thy womb became more spacious than the heavens: therefore thee, O Mother of God, do the ranks of angels and men magnify.

*Troparia:*
Emulating that life of thine, O most blessed Simon, we glorify thy struggles, and we honor thy memory, which now with the angels we magnify.

Leaving lower things, thou didst depart unto the highest; and thy body according to thy desire was brought from thy throne into the cave, and shows how great love thou hadst toward this place, wherein we all magnify thee.[^229]

The cave rejoices holding thy relics, O hierarch, and convenes assemblies of monks and feast-lovers, that with songs of praise all may magnify thy memory on this present day.

*Glory:*
Thou didst depart thyself, O honorable one, yet thy memory abides with us without departing: as thy children burning with love toward thee, we ever remember and magnify thee.

*Both now: Theotokion:*
Abide with us without departing, O most pure Virgin, with our fellow citizens in memory of thy eternal servant; strengthen the people, drive away sloth, and enlighten our understanding, that unitedly we all may magnify thee and thy Son.

*Exapostilarion:*
Let us praise the God-wise father who drank in all-wise words: for his honorable teachings are like the morning dew, sweet honeycomb, bread of life, heavenly nectar, a wellspring of living water, drinking of which one is filled with spiritual sweetness, and glorifies thy memory, O most blessed Simon.[^230]

*Glory, both now: Theotokion:*
Having brought forth, O Virgin, the wellspring of living water higher than the heavens, which cannot be comprehended: give drink unto me who thirst, O thy mother, that refreshed I may glorify thee.

*At the Praises, Stichera, Tone 1. Podoben: "Joy of the heavenly ranks" (Небе́сныхъ чинѡ́въ):*
Thou wast an initiate of sacred mysteries, O blessed one, while living in the body in this world; and translated unto a better life, thou wast vouchsafed the very Vision of God, and clearly seest the Inaccessible Light: of which do thou make us also worthy to behold, by thy fervent prayers unto God.[^231]

They that attend unto thy words and cleave unto thy admonition *To Simon, Bishop of Suzdal*

...unto thy admonition, O venerable Simon, perceive profit in their life, and appear filled with correction; they receive spiritual deeds and draw grace through thy teaching: wherewith enrich us also who on this present day honor thy memory with praises.

A divine instrument of the Holy Spirit, and a trumpet proclaiming spiritual corrections, the primate of Suzdal, Simon of sacred utterance, let us the people today honor with songs, bowing down before the reliquary of his relics: for as a hierarch and fervent intercessor he stands before the throne of the Master, praying that we may be saved.

*Glory: Tone 6:*
At the radiance of thy life and thy episcopal direction, angels and men marveled together: for in election thou wast shown from on high to be an initiate of sacred mysteries, and wast an excellent mirror unto ascetics; therefore as a worthy servant of God, O Simon, most blessed hierarch, with love in songs we magnify thee.

*Both now: Theotokion: O Mother of God, thou art the true vine:*

*Great Doxology, and dismissal.*
*At the Liturgy, the common service to a Hierarch.*

*(Woodcut Engraving: Saint Agapitus the Unmercenary (*Пр: Агапитъ Безмездникъ*), depicted standing in monastic habit with cowl and epitrachelion, blessing with his right hand and holding a cup of healing herbs in his left; in the background are the cells of the Kyiv Caves Monastery, a kneeling ascetic, and an angel descending from heaven with a heavenly crown. Below, a secondary narrative panel depicts the repose of Saint Agapitus upon a bier surrounded by brethren, while the Armenian physician kneels in repentance (*По смерти Агапита крестися Арменинъ по пророчеству его*).)*[^232]

---

# MONTH OF JUNE

*On the 1st Day: Service to the Venerable Agapitus of the Caves, Unmercenary Physician.*[^233]

*At Vespers, on "Lord, I have cried," Stichera, Tone 4. Podoben: "As one valiant among martyrs" (Ꙗ҆́кѡ до́блѧ):*
Having abundantly received from on high the gift of spiritual grace, O venerable Agapitus, as with river streams thou dost water all, and by thy prayers thou grantest healing unto all that run unto thee with faith;[^234] therefore we also, having taken boldness *To the Caves Unmercenary Physician*

...having taken boldness, bow down before the reliquary of thy relics, and pray: Grant healing unto our souls and bodies.

Having by purity of mind and unceasing standing in prayers subdued irrational passions unto the spirit, O all-blessed one, thou didst receive the grace of healing, and dost grant it ungrudgingly unto all that ask help of thee. Therefore we also, honoring thy dormition, diligently pray thee: As an unmercenary physician, grant healing unto our souls and bodies.

The reliquary of thy relics, O venerable father Agapitus, is revealed as a radiant heaven, showing forth wonders like light-bearing stars, and granting gracious healing unto them that run unto it with hope. Therefore we also, possessing expectation, diligently pray thee: Grant help in our passions unto our souls and bodies.

*Glory: Tone 6:*
Having received the grace of healing from on high while yet living in this temporal life, O most blessed Agapitus, thou didst ungrudgingly grant healing unto all that asked it; and now vouchsafed greater grace, standing before the throne of the Highest, send down from thy gifts unto us that bow down before thy many-healing relics and honor thy memory with praises, that through thy prayers unto God, O father, we may receive healing for our sicknesses.

*Both now: Theotokion, or Stavrotheotokion.*

*At the Aposticha, Stichera:*
*Glory: Tone 3:*
O glorious one, who bringest forth the embers of spiritual gifts and healings unto them that are held fast by many passions and need thy defense: grant speedy help from on high, cooling and driving away with the streams of thy wonders the sicknesses brought on by the evil one; that having all alike drawn healing, we may glorify the Heavenly Father, and magnify His Co-eternal Son, with His Co-essential Spirit, and all may call thee blessed, O father Agapitus.

*Both now: Theotokion, or Stavrotheotokion.*

*Troparion, Tone 5:*
Emulating the humble-mindedness of the glorious Anthony, as with a medicine through edible herbs thou didst heal the sick, O venerable Agapitus; therefore having also convinced the unbelieving physician, thou didst guide him onto the path of salvation: heal our sicknesses also, and pray unto the Lord for them that sing of Thee.[^235]

*Service to the Venerable Agapitus*

*Canon to the Venerable One.*
*Tone 6. Ode 1. Heirmos:*[^236]
As on dry land did Israel walk through the abyss with their footsteps, and beholding Pharaoh the pursuer submerged, cried aloud: Let us sing a song of victory unto God!

*Troparia:*
As of old by a touch Thou didst make the mouth of the dumb to speak rightly, so also unto me, O my Christ, loosen my tongue and cleanse my mind, that I may bring praise in memory of Thy pleasing one, singing with love.

I cannot speak according to thy grace, O venerable one, unless thou by thy prayers grant me help, that I may glorify the Good One Who gave thee the grace of healings, singing in praise.

Called by a call, O father, thou wast summoned to walk after thy Master, and following Him, leaving all the fair things of the world, in love thou didst come unto the great Anthony, and with him didst live, singing unceasing praise unto God.

*Glory:*
Thou didst appear an angel unto the earthly and a heavenly man, and didst acquire the angelic life thyself, O venerable father. Therefore we honor thy achievements, and glorify the Master of all, singing a song concerning thee unto Him.

*Both now: Theotokion:*
O thou who art ever glorified by the earthly and the heavenly powers: on this present day preserve from all assaults them that make bold to bring praise unto thee, that we may sing of thee who art full of grace.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*[^237]
There is none holy as Thou, O Lord my God, Who hast exalted the horn of Thy faithful, O Good One, and established us upon the rock of Thy confession.

*Troparia:*
Through many years thou didst pass through exceeding labors, O Agapitus, struggling in vigilance and fasting, until thou wast shown to be of like character with thy instructor, and wast established by him upon the rock of a steadfast life.

Possessing naught else save poverty, thou didst follow Christ Who became poor for our sake, O father, and keeping His commandments, didst serve the sick, visiting and giving them rest, and granting them health by thy prayer.

By conjoined life thou didst acquire venerableness, O laudable Agapitus, and wast vouchsafed the gifts of healings, for by prayer and edible herbs thou didst grant health unto the sick; therefore also thou wast called an unmercenary physician.

*Glory:*

*To the Caves Unmercenary Physician*

...Thou didst glorify God in thy members, O venerable one; therefore God also glorified thee with the grace of healing, wherewith heal our sicknesses both of soul and body, that we may be established upon the rock of the confession of God.

*Both now: Theotokion:*
O thou who didst bud forth the Tree of Life: by its fruit quicken us who are slain by sins, and grant unto us, O pure Mother, a firm confession of thy goodness.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 1:*
Freely having received from God the gift of healing, freely dost thou grant healing unto them that suffer, who with faith run unto thee. Therefore we also, bowing down before the reliquary of thy relics, O venerable father, pray to receive from thee healing for our sicknesses.

*Glory, both now: Theotokion:*
Running unto thy loving-kindness in sorrows, we pray, O all-immaculate one: for the sake of thy eternal servant, grant speedy healing unto us that are held fast by sicknesses.

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*[^238]
Christ is my power, God and Lord, the honorable Church divinely sings, crying from a pure mind, keeping festival in the Lord.

*Troparia:*
Christ chose the foolish things of this world to put to shame the wise, which thing came to pass also in thee, O all-blessed father: for by grace and not by art didst thou put to shame the all-wise physician. Therefore celebrating in the Lord, we glorify thee.

He who formerly deceived the sick was wont to deceive, foretelling unto them the day and the hour of death: reproaching whom, O venerable one, thou didst heal the sick man with prayer and edible herbs, saying: Give glory unto God, Who has the key of life and death.[^239]

Wounded by the arrow of envy, the ungodly Armenian reproached thee, O father, and having sent a man condemned to death, commanded him to be slain before thee with a deadly potion. But thou, having healed him with prayer and herbs, didst put to shame the heretic who wished to put thee to shame.

*Glory:*
The exceeding great grace given unto thee became an occasion of stumbling, O most blessed one, for the ungodly Armenian arming himself yet more against thee, came upon thee to give thee poison; but even drinking deadly poison, thou wast unharmed, for the grace of God preserved thee unto greater wonders.[^240]

*Both now: Theotokion:*
Rejoice, O vessel that didst bear the Myrrh which healed the human race: quicken me who am slain by the poison of sin, that having lived the rest of my time whole, I may praise thee, O Virgin.

*Service to the Venerable Agapitus*

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*[^241]
Enlighten with the love of Thy divine light, O Good One, the souls of them that rise early unto Thee with love, I pray, that they may know Thee, O Word of God, as the true God, Who callest them forth from the darkness of sin.

*Troparia:*
Having received from God the grace of healing, Thou didst heal the prince, who though treated by the Armenian found no relief; but having tasted of thy herbs, he was delivered from his sickness.[^242]

Behold, ye monastics, and emulate the virtuous patient endurance of the God-bearing father, how with zeal he renounced the will of the world, and never went outside the gates of the monastery: which thing beholding, emulate him.

Possessing one love toward all and not regarding persons, whether prince or commoner came unto thee, O venerable one, unto all that came thou didst grant healing freely, seeking reward from God alone.

*Glory:*
Having received healing through thee, O all-blessed one, the prince understood that thou art a true servant of God, and desired to see thee; therefore he came unto thee with gifts, but having God alone, thou wouldest receive none of those gifts.

*Both now: Theotokion:*
We praise thee, O most pure Virgin, for thou openest unto us the doors of thy mercy, and grantest unto all an ungrudging wellspring of healings, delivering us from the venom of sin.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*[^243]
Beholding the sea of life surging with the tempest of temptations, fleeing unto Thy calm haven, I cry unto Thee: Lead up my life from corruption, O Greatly-merciful One!

*Troparia:*
Leaving all the corruptible things of this world, thou didst cast the gold brought unto thee before the gates of thy cell, O blessed one, casting out together the grasping enemy and the shoot of his soul-harming head.[^244]

Having acquired such precious lack of envy, thou didst account all other things as refuse, O father, and thereby didst purchase for thyself a habitation in the heavens, which we also pray to attain by thy prayers, O father.

Fasting through many years, thou didst grow weak in body, O venerable one, yet in spirit thou didst remain firm as a pillar, enduring all things brought upon thee, and giving thanks unto God, unto Whom thou didst cry: Lead up my life from corruption, O Greatly-merciful One!

*Glory:*
Ailing in body, in spirit thou didst contend against the ungodly Armenian, who foretold death unto thee after three days; whom thou didst conquer, O blessed one, proclaiming thy departure unto God after three months.[^245]

*To the Caves Unmercenary Physician*

*Both now: Theotokion:*
O Word Who didst dwell in thy womb, O all-immaculate Virgin: Thou didst deify the human race in the heavens. Entreat Him to translate me from the corruption of this life, thither where the saints are nourished with the torrent of delight.

*Kontakion, Tone 6:*[^246]
Being an excellent physician, O marvelous one, thou wast shown to be ungrudging in thy faith, reproving the unbelief of the Armenian and bringing him unto piety; and being thyself near unto death, thou didst ask from God prolongation of life, and having convinced him by this wonder, didst bring him unto Christ; before Whom standing in gladness, pray for us, O venerable one.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*[^247]
An angel made the furnace produce dew for the venerable youths, while the burning command of God consumed the Chaldeans, persuading the tyrant to cry aloud: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparia:*
Possessing the gift from on high to heal sicknesses, O venerable one: heal me who am sick through neglect of good deeds, even as of old thou didst heal him that was brought in sickness, granting him health by prayer, and crying: Blessed is the God of our fathers!

He that made thee wrought a miracle: suddenly He set thee forth healthy before the ungodly one, who seeing it marveled at thy speedy change from sickness into health. By thy visitation from on high, O father, lead me who am tormented with wounds of shameful deeds unto the direction of correction, that I may sing: Blessed is the God of our fathers!

Having received thy sickness and edible herbs, the physician came to confess his ungodliness, whom thou didst rebuke, for he dared to condemn thy mouth, saying: Behold, I also make bold to approach thee, O father; but I pray thee, cast me not away in my needs, that I may sing: Blessed is the God of our fathers!

*Glory:*
Having gathered the fruits of thy labors with gladness, as a good husbandman, by a blessed end thou didst depart unto the highest, to receive reward from Christ God; and thou didst receive from Him unending joy, O much-marvelous Agapitus.

*Both now: Theotokion:*
Unto thy haven do we flee, O Bride of God, Virgin, carried along by the waves of worldly passions, which do thou calm, that we may sing unto thee: Rejoice, haven of worldly voyagings.

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*[^248]
From the flame Thou didst pour forth dew for the venerable ones, and burned up the sacrifice of the righteous one with water: for Thou doest all things, O Christ, only by Thy willing: Thee do we supremely exalt unto all ages.

*Service to the Venerable Agapitus*

*Troparia:*
A most marvelous physician wast thou, O father, healing bodily sicknesses in this life while living in the flesh, and after thy dormition granting healing of soul, as thou didst show this in the Armenian, giving his soul whole and instruction unto piety.

Seeing thee in a marvelous vision, O father, the Armenian reported openly that thou art holy and venerable; and turning from unbelief unto faith, by thy direction he became a monk: with whom forget not us who sing of the Lord unto ages.

Possessing a great gift, thou didst practice the art of healing the sick; and now vouchsafed greater gifts, apply a plaster unto the wounds of my soul, that having received relief, I may supremely exalt the Lord unto ages.

*We bless the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit: the Lord.*
From the light resembling flame, grant rays of grace by the mouth, O Christ God; quench with the dew of Thy compassions the passions that burn me, dispersing the darkness of passions: whom the venerable one entreats with us, that we may supremely exalt Thee, the Lord, unto all ages.

*Both now: Theotokion:*
O thou who didst enlighten the world by thy most pure childbirth: disperse the gloom of my evil deeds, pouring forth upon me for the doing of good the rich mercy of thy compassions, that I may supremely exalt thee, the Mother of God, unto ages.

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*[^249]
It is impossible for men to see God, upon Whom the ranks of angels dare not gaze; yet through thee, O all-pure one, the Word was made flesh and appeared unto men: Whom magnifying, with the heavenly hosts we call thee blessed.

*Troparia:*
Possessing a God-given healer, let no people go unto vain physicians, who harm possessions and health; but let him that is in need come with faith unto the reliquary of the God-bearing father, and he shall straightway receive healing.

The noetic robbers having now wounded us, we possess incurable wounds; therefore without doubt we fall down before thy most marvelous reliquary, O venerable one, and pray: Grant healing unto them that need it, that we all may praise and magnify thee.

Having ascended in soul into the highest unto the all-radiant bridal chambers, thou didst leave the reliquary of thy relics in the cave, O all-blessed one: bowing down before which ever, grant healing unto our sicknesses, that we may magnify thee in songs.

*To the Caves Unmercenary Physician*

*Glory:*
Despise not them that ask help of thee in sicknesses, but by thy visitation grant healing of soul together with body, O most blessed Agapitus, as thou art an excellent physician, that we may ever magnify thee.

*Both now: Theotokion:*
Despise not them that need help from thee, O Virgin, but grant it unto them that run unto thee with faith: for thou art an inexhaustible wellspring of healings, that with the heavenly powers we may ever magnify thee.

*Exapostilarion:*[^250]
Having left the corruptible things of the world and striving to receive the incorruptible as an emulator, thou didst hasten to exchange the worse for the better, sanctifying by love-contempt of passions; and enriched with the gift of healings, grant this also unto us who today honor thy memory, O all-blessed Agapitus.

*Glory, both now: Theotokion:*
Being saved through thee, we confess thee as the pure Mother: for thou didst bear Him Who destroyed death by the Cross, and ascending on high, drew all things unto Himself, and granted us great mercy.

*At the Praises, Stichera, Tone 4. Podoben: "Thou hast given a sign" (Да́лъ є҆сѝ зна́менїе):*[^251]
Having withered the passionate stirrings of the flesh by ascetic labors, and having ascended unto the height of virtues, thou didst appear brighter than the sun, shining with the grace of healings, and enlightening with its rays them that come unto thee. Therefore we pray thee, O venerable one: Unto them that lovingly honor thy memory, grant healing for sicknesses.

Having received from the only Lover of mankind on high a rich gift, thou didst distribute it ungrudgingly unto them that needed it, granting them healing. And now also unto them that fall down before the reliquary of thy relics, send down healing in sicknesses, as a true physician, O most blessed one, and intercessor for our souls.

Gathered together in faith and love in memory of the worthy-of-blessedness father, let us the people praise him with songful voices, that we may be vouchsafed healings in our sicknesses from him *Service to the Venerable Agapitus*

...from him: for he is swift to incline, and grants healing as an unmercenary physician and speedy helper in sorrows.

*Glory: Tone 6:*[^252]
Endless is Thy grace, O Christ the King, and great is the gift which Thou hast given unto Thy venerable one: for not only while living in the body did he heal sicknesses, but even after death by Thy divine power the reliquary of his incorrupt relics grants healing unto them that come with faith. Therefore we pray Thee, O Good Master, bringing Thy holy one unto intercession: according to Thy manifold goodness, deliver us from sicknesses of soul and body, and save our souls, as Thou art the Lover of mankind.

*Both now: Theotokion.*

*(Woodcut Engraving: Saint John the Much-Suffering (*Іоаннъ многострадальный*), depicted buried in the ground up to his shoulders during Great Lent, enduring demonic assaults with a skull and cross beside him; an angel descends from heaven holding a crown (*Іоанъ благословен мощи Моисея приложи, змий опали ему главу*). Below, a secondary panel depicts the saint attacked by a fire-breathing demonic serpent in the cave.)*[^253]

---

# MONTH OF JULY

*On the 18th Day: Service to our Venerable Father John the Much-Suffering.*[^254]

*At Vespers, "Blessed is the man," the 1st antiphon. On "Lord, I have cried," Stichera to 6, Tone 8. Podoben: "O most marvelous wonder" (Ѽ пресла́внагѡ чꙋдесе):*[^255]
O father John, God-wise one, running the much-afflicted path of this life, thou didst trample down thorn-bearing fleshly passions, and through many ascetic struggles didst attain unto the summit of noetic desire, where now rejoicing *Service to the Venerable One*

...where now rejoicing, pray for us, O venerable one, that we who run unto thee may trample down lying stumbling-blocks, and may attain thither where is the unending gladness of all that rejoice.

O father John, blessed one, desiring to adorn thy soul with blood by the corrections of pure thoughts, thou didst despise this temporal life, dwelling in a dark cave, and being buried therein alive, desiring to be dissolved from the body and to be with Christ; with Whom now dwelling, forget not us also who honor thy holy memory.

O father John, venerable one, kindled by the desire of eternal good things, thou didst bind the heavy passions of thy flesh with the laying on of heavy bonds, and didst make no relief for thyself, until thou didst receive relief from the Divine Light; wherein now rejoicing, pray for us, to help our spirit against passions, and lightly to pass over unto unending life.

*Glory: Tone 2:*
O father John, when the desire of the love of God was kindled in thy holy soul, then the enemy, the author of evil, striving to extinguish it, brought upon thee a surging tempest of fleshly burnings; which having perceived, thou didst stand upon the rock of firm suffering, and didst remain unmoved, until *To John the Much-Suffering*

...until thou didst calm these things by the manifestation of the Divine Light; and now rejoicing in the heavenly courts, send down grace unto dispassion for us also through thy prayers unto God.

*Both now: Theotokion: The shadow of the law has passed:*

*Entrance. Prokeimenon of the day. Three readings (see fol. 38v on the reverse).*[^256]

*At the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 2. Podoben: "When from the Tree" (Е҆гда̀ ѿ дре́ва):*[^257]
O father John, God-wise one, an image unto monastics by the pure form of virginity: for the sake of purity alone Thou didst endure great temptation, and didst remain unharmed, preserved by the grace of God; of which do Thou grant that we also may not be deprived, through thy prayers.

*Verse:* Blessed are all they that fear the Lord, that walk in His ways.[^258]
Beholding thy good contest, O all-blessed one, and thy expectation toward the good, the Creator of creation sent speedy help from on high in the light of spiritual grace, whereby the darkness of demonic assault was straightway driven away from thee; and having received peace in thy soul, thou didst cleave unto Christ with warmer love; unto Whom may we also be united through thy prayers.

*Verse:* The righteous man shall flourish like a palm tree, and like a cedar in Lebanon shall he be multiplied.
After the gloom of much-afflicted passions, light from the noetic Light was kindled for thee, and joy was brought unto thy rejoicing, which feeling in the heart of thy much-enduring body, thou wast filled with ineffable gladness: of this do thou vouchsafe us also, O John the much-suffering, through thy speedy prayers unto God.

*Glory: Tone 7:*
What praise shall we bring unto thee, O venerable father? Or with what songs shall we hymn thy ascetic struggles, O all-wise John? For being in the flesh, by thy struggles thou didst put to shame the bodiless enemy, and didst keep thy purity, for the sake of which having suffered much, thou now dwellest in the place of rest with the virgins; pray unto Christ the Master to grant peace unto the world, and to save our souls.

*Both now: Theotokion: That which was spoken by Isaiah is fulfilled:*

*Troparion, Tone 4:*
Truly an unshakable pillar of patient endurance, and an unassailable rampart against the arrows of the enemy, an equal of angels and our much-suffering father John: having thee, we the unworthy pray, fervently falling down before thy honorable relics: Deliver us from soul-corrupting fleshly passions, and save us by thy prayers.[^259]

*Glory, both now: Theotokion: When Gabriel announced unto thee:*

*At Matins, on "God is the Lord," Troparion of the Saint, twice. Glory, both now: Theotokion.*
*After Kathisma 1, Sessional Hymn, Tone 8. Podoben: "The angelic choir" (Ли́къ а҆́гг҃льскїй):*[^260]
Having a life filled with good deeds, O venerable one, thou wast envied with lusts by the crafty enemy, who fell upon thee in the likeness of a huge-jawed serpent, and breathing flame with evil mouth, wishing to devour the noetic magnanimity; but the grace of God preserved thee unharmed: wherewith grant us also to obtain help, O John the much-suffering, through thy intercessions.

*Glory, both now: Theotokion:*
Having a life filled with shameful deeds, I have become prey for the crafty enemy; yet unto thee do I cry, O pure Virgin: Break the teeth of the insatiable dragon, and deliver me from unworthy passions, that I may ever cry unto thee with the voice of rejoicing: Rejoice, thou that didst deliver us from foul deeds.

*After Kathisma 2, Sessional Hymn, Tone 8. Podoben: "That which was commanded mystically" (Повелѣ́нное та́инственнѡ):*[^261]
Unceasingly envying the purity of thy virginity, O venerable father, the all-evil enemy cast upon thee arrows of unclean burnings, which striking against thee, were shattered upon the rock of thy patient endurance; and in this *Service to the Venerable One*

...and in this thou didst acquire thy reward, O most blessed one; of which may we also be partakers through thy prayers.

*Glory, both now: Theotokion:*
The all-crafty enemy has cast me down into the streams of sinful passions, and I lie in the mire of unworthy deeds; but do thou, O most pure Virgin, by thy speedy intercession bring forth for me water of compunction, that having washed away the stench of many sins, I may sing: Rejoice, laver that washest away sin.

*After the Polyeleos, Sessional Hymn, Tone 8. Podoben: "The Word co-unoriginate" (Собеꙁнача́лное Сло́во):*[^262]
Adorned with beauty and lover of purity, who shall not call thee blessed, O all-wise John? Or who will not hymn with songs thy manifold ascetic struggles? How in purity thou didst contend against the invisible enemy, and having conquered him, wast shown to be a crown-bearer; and now standing before Christ God in the Inaccessible Light, pray for them that honor thy much suffering.

*Glory, both now: Theotokion:*
Fair among women, and only immaculate one, who by purity didst surpass the angels and archangels: who will not praise thee, O all-pure Virgin? Or who will not honor with songs the birthgiving of thee, unwedded Mother, whom all generations harmoniously magnify?

*Gradual Hymns, the 1st Antiphon of Tone 4.*
*Prokeimenon, Tone 4:* Marvelous is God in His saints, the God of Israel.[^263]
*Verse:* In the congregations bless ye God, the Lord from the wellsprings of Israel.
*Let every breath praise the Lord.*
*Gospel of a Venerable One.*
*Sticheron after the Gospel, Tone 6: What praise shall we bring unto thee:*
*(see at the Aposticha, Glory).*

*Canon of the Mother of God, with the Heirmos to 6, and of the Venerable One to 8.*
*Canon to the Saint, Tone 8. Ode 1. Heirmos:*[^264]
Having traversed the water as dry land, and escaped the malice of Egypt, the Israelite cried aloud: Let us sing unto our Deliverer and God!

*Troparia:*
Thou Who of old didst give water in the wilderness unto Thy people that thirsted: unto me also, O my Christ, who thirst to bring praise unto Thee, give the water of grace, that in memory of Thy eternal servant I may sing a song: For He is glorified!

The narrow way and many afflictions Thou didst show forth Thyself, O Lord, unto them that walk toward Thy Kingdom: desiring which, Thy eternal servant gladly endured afflictions in this life, that there he might rejoice, singing: For He is glorified!

*To John the Much-Suffering*

Unceasingly remembering the voice of perpetual rejoicing and the sound of them that keep festival, O father John, thou didst leave worldly pleasures that call unto themselves; and taking upon thee Christ's yoke, thou didst bear that light burden in hope of speedy transformation, singing unto God: For He is glorified!

*Glory:*
Having departed far from the passionate world, O blessed one, thou didst run like a hart unto the Caves hill, where being watered by the arrows of the spiritual protection of the blessed fathers, thou didst sing unto God: For He is glorified!

*Both now: Theotokion:*
Being overwhelmed with the ship of soul in the sea of passions, I cry unto thee, O pure one: Help me by thy mercy, and deliver me from sinking; for thou art the ship for them that desire to be saved, and the calm haven of worldly voyagings.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*[^265]
Thou art the confirmation of them that run unto Thee, O Lord; Thou art the light of the darkened, and my spirit sings of Thee.

*Troparia:*
Having escaped the worldly abysses, thou didst run unto the calm haven of monastics, O father, living with whom thou didst love the one God, crying unto Him: O my Christ, confirm me in Thy love!

Desiring to follow the pure and blameless Lamb in purity, O blessed one, the foul enemy seeing thee attacked thee with fleshly burnings, against whom thou didst stand, subduing thy flesh by fasting and thirst, and in prayer singing: O my Christ, confirm me!

Remembering the fall of him that brings light unto fornicators, O venerable one, thou didst dwell in a dark cave, and didst keep silence beside the tomb of the great Anthony, ever remembering thine own departure; and thus thou didst find peace in thy bones.[^266]

*Glory:*
Death is not death, but a translation unto a better life for the saints of God: for though they are laid in tombs, yet they ever live unto God and utter a voice, even as unto thee, O father John, a voice came from the tomb of the great Anthony, calling thee unto silence.

*Both now: Theotokion:*
I remember the hour of judgment, yet I have no change from passions unto a dispassionate life, O pure Mother of God: for the sake of the venerable virgin John, wash me from the love of passions, and confirm me in the love of thy Son.

*Service to the Venerable One*

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 8:*[^267]
Emulating the beatitude of them that have not defiled themselves with women, that thou mightest follow the Lamb upon Mount Sion, thou didst resolve to keep thy virginity undefiled, and didst preserve it whole, unharmed by many demonic temptations; therefore thou singest a new song, O venerable John, and thy spirit rejoices with the angels.

*Glory, both now: Theotokion:*
Not having emulated the beatitude of virgins, wretch that I am, having defiled the robe of my baptism with fornicating corruptions, I have departed far from Thy grace, O immaculate Lamb: O Son of God, for the sake of the sufferings of Thy saints and of the all-pure Mother of God, grant me correction and have mercy on me.

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*[^268]
Thou hast set toward us a steadfast love, O Lord: for Thou didst give Thine Only-begotten Son unto death for our sake; therefore giving thanks we cry unto Thee: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!

*Troparia:*
Thou didst establish thy life in a dark cave without departing, O father, until the passionate gloom was changed into light through silence and unseenness of sensible light.

Thou didst put on many chains, O father, contending against passions as with fierce beasts, firmly bridling their harsh attacks in thy bodily frame by fasting and vigilance.

Unable to cool thy flesh burned with the fire of Ethiopian assaults, O blessed one, thou didst choose to live naked in a narrow place, struggling there through freezing cold unto spiritual warmth.[^269]

*Glory:*
Desiring to subdue thy fierce and untamed body as a wild beast, thou didst bind it with heavy irons, O venerable one, that it might not lust against the spirit, but rather be subject unto the spirit.[^270]

*Both now: Theotokion:*
Unable to quiet the passions of much intemperance that rise up against me, unto thee do I flee, O Lady: do thou, O all-pure one, subdue their impurity, and save me from their assault.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*[^271]
Thou didst appear invisible upon the earth, and didst willingly dwell with men, O Incomprehensible One; and rising early unto Thee, we sing of Thee, O Lover of mankind.

*Troparia:*
Who among all that live upon earth will not marvel at thy patient endurance, O father? For having dug a pit for thyself, and entering naked, thou didst bury thyself up to thy shoulders, subduing with earth the fleshly surge moved by the enemy adversary.[^272]

Thy whole body oppressed with earth was growing faint, yet the enemy was vigilant *To John the Much-Suffering*

...yet the enemy was vigilant, rising up fiercely in warfare and assailing thee with terrors, wishing thus to turn thee away, lest having escaped he should fall before thee, O venerable one.

The devil seeing thy mind soaring, O John, burning toward God, with crafty flame burned thy members and entrails from below; yet thou, neglecting this pain, didst choose to be burned up for the Lord's sake, rather than be mocked by the demon.

*Glory:*
Satan prepared to strike thee with the venom of asps in weakness, O blessed one; yet he transformed himself into a fire-breathing serpent, that at least by terrifying thee he might overcome thee, an unconquerable warrior of Christ.[^273]

*Both now: Theotokion:*
The ashen venom of passionate assaults, wherewith the enemy wounds my heart: heal with the medicine of thy mercy, O all-immaculate Virgin, that being saved from them, I may desire to bring forth fruit of repentance.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*[^274]
The lowest abyss of sins has surrounded me, and my spirit fails; but stretch forth Thy high arm, O Master, and save me as Thou didst Peter, O Guide!

*Troparia:*
The lowest abyss opened its maw: for the devil transforming himself into an unclean serpent, armed himself breathing with fiery gaping, wishing to swallow thee; but thou, O father, wast like an immovable pillar, singing: O my God, my Guide, save me!

Standing upon the rock of firm faith, O venerable one, thou didst cover thyself with the shield of hope, and accounting the crafty enemy as naught by faith, thou didst direct thyself against his head; and being in his maw, thou wast not terrified, crying unto Christ: O my Guide, save me!

The noetic beast thrust thy head into his maw, O blessed one, and thy hair was burned; yet thy heart was not burned with faint-heartedness, O father, therefore thou didst cry aloud; and the Lord heard thee, as a good Helmsman and Savior Who accepts all without regard to persons.[^275]

*Glory:*
Thy prayer, O John, was as lightning arrows against the power of the adversary: for thou didst say: My God, why hast Thou forsaken me? And straightway the fierce serpent vanished, and no longer approached thee, being driven away by the Divine Light.

*Both now: Theotokion:*
The arrows of the noetic adversary have wounded my heart with passions; therefore from the bitterness *Service to the Venerable One*

...from the bitterness of my soul I cry unto thee, O pure one: Do thou quench them by thy boldness, as thou didst bear Christ the Physician of my soul.

*Kontakion, Tone 8:*[^276]
Being an unconquerable pillar of chastity in battles of contention, as a vigilant pillar of much suffering thou hast left us thy body, O all-honorable John: bowing down before which in sicknesses of soul and body, we pray thee, O venerable one: Pray unceasingly unto Christ God for us all.

*Ikos:*[^277]
Having loved the divine commandments of Christ, O venerable one, and hated the pleasures of this world, thou didst come eagerly unto Him, and wast a radiant beacon, enlightening the ends of the earth with spiritual radiance; therefore falling down I pray thee: Enlighten my spiritual eyes to sing of thy ascetic struggles, thy fasting, vigilance, tears, sicknesses, and the exhaustion of thy body, for the sake of the blessed future life, which thou now enjoyest: pray unceasingly for us all.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*[^278]
Even as of old Thou didst bedew the three pious youths in the Chaldean flame with the bright fire of the Godhead: illuminate us also who cry: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparia:*
Even as dust before the wind and as smoke from a thick cloud perishes, so also when the Divine Light arrived the evil one vanished; and thou, O father, didst bow down before the Lord, singing: Blessed art Thou, O God!

The Lord was near when thou didst call Him unto help, O father; whose grace having perceived, with joyful soul from the depth of thy heart thou didst cry aloud: Blessed art Thou, O God!

*Glory:*
Even as a wise master lays a great task upon a strong servant, so also the Lord permitted thee to endure the day and night heat of fleshly burnings; and thou didst bear it with patient endurance, singing: Blessed art Thou, O God!

*Both now: Theotokion:*
As a most wise Master, Thy Son, O Virgin, permits me who am slothful to be constrained by light passions; yet before they even arrived, I grew faint; therefore unto thee do I cry: Help me, O Mother of the Word: for thou art blessed.

*To John the Much-Suffering*

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*[^279]
United with the unbearable fire, the youths standing for piety, unharmed by the flame, sang a divine song: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparia:*
In the unbearable fire of fleshly burnings, a voice from on high commanded thee, O blessed one, to pray unto a dead man who was able to help them that are in miserable passions and others, that they may sing of the Lord unto ages.[^280]

He that did the same as Joseph of old, when being held captive he obeyed not the defilement of the woman: he also helped thee, O father, by intercession unto God, whereby having heard him of corrupt passions, thou now singest of the Lord unto ages.[^281]

Seeing thy magnanimity, O father, ten thousand times brighter than the sun, the eye of God sent down the light of grace, and the gloom of ruinous fornication was utterly driven away from thy bones: and having lived the rest of thy life in stillness, thou now singest of the Lord unto ages.

*We bless the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit: the Lord.*
The Master Who grants noetic gladness unto the dispassionate granted it unto thee also, O venerable John, after thy many labors; which having received from the Divine Light, thou didst rejoice, and now rejoicest in heaven with them that sing of the Lord unto ages.

*Both now: Theotokion:*
O Virgin who without passions didst conceive, and without fleshly lust didst bear the Word of God: destroy the assaults that begin in the darkness of the evil one, that having escaped foulness, I may be vouchsafed to sing of the Lord unto ages.

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*[^282]
A new wonder and divinely-befitting: for through the shut door of the virgin the Lord plainly passes, naked in His entrance, and God was shown forth flesh-bearing in His departure, and the door remains shut: her ineffably do we magnify as the Mother of God.

*Troparia:*
A new life didst thou begin, when after much suffering the tempest of passions grew calm within thee, O father, and thou didst live the rest of thy time in stillness, magnifying the power of God.

Standing upon the firm rock of patient endurance, thou wast as a strong pillar against the face of the enemy, whose arrows striking against thee were shattered; therefore thee, O John, do we magnify.

Having lived pure in soul and body upon earth, thou didst depart unto the heavenly ones, with the pure in heart to behold the Face of God; enjoying the vision of Whom, pray for us, O venerable one, that with thee we may magnify the Trinity.

*Glory:*
Blessed is thy life, O John, blessed is thy dormition, but most blessed is thy life in the unwaning light, for the sake of which thou didst endure many afflictions; now rejoicing, pray for us that honor thy memory, O all-wise one.

*Both now: Theotokion: Of life:*

*Service to the Venerable One*

O Virgin who didst bear seedlessly from thy blood the Prince of life: for the sake of the prayers of the celibate John, direct unto eternal life us who bow down before thee, that we may magnify thee who didst bear the Wellspring of life.[^283]

*Exapostilarion:*[^284]
Having lived in sorrow for the sake of thy purity, O father, thou didst sow many tears; but now with rejoicing reap blessed joys! Pray for us, O venerable one, with the holy fathers, that we also may be partakers of your beatitude; for we are your children, and the spiritual sheep of your teachings.

*Glory, both now: Theotokion:*
O pure Virgin, Gate of the Word, Mother of Christ our God: pray that we thy servants may be saved.

*At the Praises, Stichera, Tone 4. Podoben: "Thou hast given a sign" (Да́лъ є҆сѝ зна́менїе):*[^285]
Thou hast given desire, and unacceptable longing toward purity unto Thy servant, O Lord; and having received chastening from Thee, he endured much torment from enemies, yet having conquered them unto the end, he was shown to be a crown-bearer. For his sake do Thou also preserve us unharmed, O Jesus, Lover of mankind, and save our souls.

Having pure oil in the vessel of thy heart, O all-wise John, and having kindled the fire of noetic desire, with the wise virgins thou didst enter into the heavenly bridal chamber, unto the marriage of the immaculate Lamb, with whom now rejoicing, remember us also who honor thy venerable dormition, O most blessed father.

The flame of thy love toward God and thy daily desire burned up the head of the serpent that scorched thee, and drove him away by the power of the Light on high, so that he dared not approach thy place. Beholding thy much-enduring relics therein, we bow down and pray: Pray for us, O glorious John, as thou art an unceasing intercessor for our souls.

*Glory: Tone 6:*[^286]
Come, ye people, let us call blessed John the much-suffering, a virginal vessel and lover of purity, praising him with songs and hymns; for he crushed the head of the multifaceted serpent. Having not scorched his body with the flame of lust, pure he went forth with a pure heart, to stand before the immaculate Lamb, Christ God. And now serving the Light brighter than the sun, he prays fervently for us who lovingly honor his memory, and grants healing unto them that run unto him with faith.

*Both now: Theotokion: Deliver from perils:*
*Great Doxology, and dismissal.*
*At the Liturgy, the service is of the Venerable Father.*

*(Woodcut Engraving: Saint Moses the Hungarian (*ПР: МОѴСЕЙ ОУГРИНЪ*) flanked by four narrative hagiographical panels from his life and contest: 1. Moses tonsured in the Polish lands (**Въ Заморъ постриженъ Пр: Моѵсей Оугринъ**); 2. Polish noblewoman buys Moses (**Купуетъ его мужи Моѵсея**); 3. The woman kisses Moses (**Лобзаше жена Моѵсея**); 4. She commands Moses to be beaten (**Повеле Моѵсеа бити**); 5. Dormition of the Venerable Moses the Hungarian (**Преставленіе Пр: Моѵсея Оугрина**); 6. Moses brought before King Bolesław (**Приведе ко Королю Болеславу**).)*[^287]

*On the 26th Day of the Month of July:*
*Service to our Venerable Father Moses the Hungarian.*[^288]

*At Vespers, on "Lord, I have cried," Stichera to 6, Tone 1. Podoben: "Joy of the heavenly ranks" (Небе́сныхъ чинѡ́въ):*[^289]
Cleaving unto purity of soul, thou didst preserve bodily purity, O our father Moses, adorned with which thou dost now follow the immaculate and all-pure Lamb, the Son of God, Who was slain for the whole world; unto Whom *Service to the Venerable One*

...pray for us, O venerable one, to preserve our souls below undefiled unto this life, and to enter into the heavenly bridal chamber.

Thou wast likened unto Moses who beheld the Bush, O father; for noetically beholding God in thy soul as in the Bush, thou didst nail thy mind unto His fear, and having that ever before thine eyes, thou wast kindled with desire as with a flame into the love of God, from which thou didst never depart. And now standing before the throne of majesty on high, pray for us, that we may pass through our life blamelessly, and appear before the Face of God.[^290]

The grace of the All-Holy Spirit, having taken up dwelling in thy holy soul, made thee unconquerable by all the assaults of the murderer of man; gaining victory over whom, by the help of Christ thou didst cut down his craftiness, and wast shown to be an unvanquished warrior. And now standing before the throne of the Master, pray that He grant us peace and great mercy.

*Glory: Tone 6:*
Come, ye assemblies of feast-lovers, having formed a spiritual choir, let us praise in summer celebration the memory of our bush-bearing father Moses, the steadfast sufferer; for he contended steadfastly against the invisible enemy who wished to burn his purity with unclean passions, and suffered much for the sake of purity; yet having hope in God, he was shown to be a crown-bearer, and now standing before the Holy Trinity, he prays for our souls.[^291]

*Both now: Theotokion: Who will not call thee blessed:*
*Entrance, Prokeimenon of the day, and three readings (see fol. 38v on the reverse).*[^292]

*At the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 3. Podoben: "O all-lauded martyrs" (Прехва́льнїи мꙋ́ченицы):*[^293]
Faithfully serving the all-lauded passion-bearer, the Russian prince Boris, O our father Moses, thou didst learn to serve also the Heavenly King in holiness and righteousness; standing before Whom now, forget not to pray for us also, that He may spiritually loose our bonds, and that we may receive crowns of heavenly honor.[^294]

*Verse: Blessed is the man that fears the Lord, in His commandments [he shall greatly delight].*[^295]
Following the Master God Who was slain, O our most blessed father Moses, thou didst learn meekness and humility, and for His love wast ready to lay down thy soul, in nothing resisting visible enemies, but only standing against invisible foes; whom having cut down unto the end, pray for us also unto the Master on high *To Moses the Hungarian*

...unto the Master on high, that He may swiftly cut down every need and the spirits of wickedness under heaven from us.

*Verse: The venerable ones shall boast in glory, and shall rejoice upon their beds.*[^296]
The grace of the Most High preserved thee, O venerable one: when thy prince was being slain, the all-honorable right hand of Christ God covered thee, and preserved thee as the apple of an eye, until He made thee an eternal servant unto Himself; Whom having faithfully served in this life, thou didst receive the joyful day of eternal life: pray that we who honor thy memory may not be deprived thereof.[^297]

*Glory: Tone 8:*
Come, ye assembly of Russian monastics and lovers of purity, unto the holy cave, and behold the many-healing relics of our most glorious father Moses, and learn from him humility and purity, patient endurance and magnanimity, and glorify the Heavenly Father, Who grants great mercy unto His servants.[^298]

*Both now: Theotokion:*
O Mother of the Creator of all who art serving, deliver from every necessity and sorrow us who sing today in thine all-honorable temple, which thou didst build as a habitation for thyself, and didst enrich with many miracles.

*Troparion, Tone 3:*[^299]
Praising thee with sacred songs as another Joseph, O great lover of purity and chastity, all-honorable and equal-to-the-angels Moses, we sinners fervently pray thee: Entreat Christ God, that He may heal all our passions, and grant great mercy.

*Another Troparion, the same Tone:*[^300]
Beholding thy labors and ascetic contests with sufferings for thy purity, O pure one, who will not marvel?
