For receiving hunger, prison, and wounds, thou didst not enslave thyself unto the pleasures of the Polish woman, but as a king didst master the passions; now and then thou wast glorified by Him Who gave human hearts, from Whom having received grace to champion all, champion us also who honor thy memory, O venerable Moses.

*Glory, both now: Theotokion.*

*At Matins, on "God is the Lord," Troparion of the Venerable One. Glory, both now: Theotokion.*
*After Kathisma 1, Sessional Hymn, Tone 4. Podoben: "Speedily anticipate" (Ско́рѡ предварѝ):*[^301]
The all-evil enemy, envying the beauty of thy countenance *Service to the Venerable One*

...beholding that he might deceitfully steal away spiritual beauty, surrounded with passions the mind of an unlawful woman, who by all deceits strove to shake thee, O venerable one; but thou, having understood his wiles, as another Joseph wast delivered from the hands of the adulteress, and didst preserve both beauties unharmed. Therefore thou now dost enjoy in heaven the beauty of the consubstantial Trinity.[^302]

*Glory, both now: Theotokion:*
Beholding thy speedy protection and intercession, and thine unreckonable goodness and compassion, O pure one, all sinners run unto thee and receive the kisses of repentance; but I, wretch that I am, having despised all these things, alone have been deprived of thy help. Look down, therefore, unto thy goodness, and let me not be shown as the prey of the destroyer, but accept me who fall down before thee, O Sovereign Lady, and guide me unto the way of repentance.

*After Kathisma 2, Sessional Hymn, the same Tone. Podoben: "Thou hast appeared today" (Ꙗ҆ви́лсѧ є҆сѝ):*[^303]
At thy patient endurance and courage, O venerable one, all the angelical choirs marveled: how being surrounded by the fire of the passion of adultery, thou wast not scorched by the defiled woman, neither didst thou submit unto her desire; for having a heart kindled with the fire of the love of God, thou didst quench the flame of passion.[^304]

*Glory, both now: Theotokion:*
Speedily stand up unto our help, O all-immaculate Sovereign Lady: for enemies have risen up against us, warring against us day and night; and unconquered, we have no protection where to flee from the hands of the destroyers. Unto thee do we pray, as the Mother of God: Stretch forth thy strong right hand, and strike them down by thy power, and preserve us unharmed, that we may bring unto thee the angelic song: Rejoice, thou who art full of grace, the Lord is with thee.[^305]

*After the Polyeleos, Sessional Hymn, Tone 5. Podoben: "The Word co-unoriginate" (Собеꙁнача́лное Сло́во):*[^306]
Come, ye people, let us hymn in songs the good sufferer and unconquerable warrior, the boast of monastics and lover of purity, our God-bearing father Moses, praising God Who strengthens His servants and grants victory over visible and invisible enemies: by Whose help this venerable one conquered all the regiments of the ruler of this world, and now standing before the throne of the Master in the heavenly habitations, he entreats for us also, that from all *To Moses the Hungarian*

...that from all assaults of the enemy and from his temptation we may be delivered.

*Glory, both now: Theotokion:*
O All-Holy Sovereign Lady, more honorable than all creation: turn not away thy face, but accept the prayers of thy servants who run today into thine all-honorable temple and bow down before thine all-pure icon; stretch forth the all-holy hands wherewith thou didst bear the Creator of all, and entreat Him as the Lover of mankind, that He may gird us with His power from on high, wherewith having cut down all that rise up against us, we may magnify good things, thy speedy intercession and protection.

*Gradual Hymns, the 1st Antiphon of Tone 3.*
*Prokeimenon: Precious in the sight of the Lord is the death of His venerable ones.*[^307]
*Verse: What shall I render unto the Lord for all that He hath rendered unto me?*[^308]
*Let every breath praise the Lord.*
*Gospel from Matthew, composite section 43 [Matt. 11:27–30].*[^309]
*After Psalm 50, Sticheron, Tone 6: Come, ye assemblies of feast-lovers:*
*(see above in the service at "Lord, I have cried").*

*Canon to the Venerable One, Tone 2. Ode 1. Heirmos:*[^310]
Come, ye people, let us sing a song unto Christ God, Who divided the sea, and guided the people whom He led forth from the bondage of Egypt, for He is glorified!

*Troparia:*
Come, ye people, let us bring praise unto God, Who glorifies His servant and hath shown him unconquered in all temptations, and let us sing unto Him a song: Gloriously is He glorified!

The all-evil enemy brought death upon thy lord, the blessed prince Boris, through the accursed fratricide, and wished to destroy thee with him; but saved from the wicked, thou didst sing: Glory to God for all things; blessed be the Name of the Lord![^311]

Saved from death by the hand of God, thou didst come to Kyiv unto Predslava, the sister of Yaroslav, where seized with grief for thy lord, thou didst recount all things concerning him, how much he suffered, and as a voiceless lamb not resisting, surrendered himself into the hands of them that sought his blood; and thou didst sing: Our God is gloriously glorified![^312]

*Service to the Venerable One*

*Glory:*
For a certain time thou didst hide thyself for fear, O venerable one, showing forth what is human concerning thyself, until the Lord beheld thee as a beacon, for an example unto all that run the honorable path, which thou didst preserve pure through many temptations, singing: O my God, be Thou gloriously glorified in my weak flesh![^313]

*Both now: Theotokion:*
O Sovereign Queen who canst do all things from God: stand up speedily for them that offer supplication unto thee, and behold how many soul-destroyers have risen up against us; whom do thou utterly destroy by the cast of thy power, that we may unceasingly cry unto thee: For great and terrible unto our enemies is thy name, gloriously glorified!

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*[^314]
Having established me upon the rock of faith, Thou hast enlarged my mouth against mine enemies, for my spirit rejoices when I sing: There is none holy as our God, and none righteous besides Thee, O Lord!

*Troparia:*
Having established thy spiritual feelings upon the firm rock of faith, thou didst go into captivity, having hope in the One Jesus Who endured the heaviest wounds and the Cross, knowing that He would deliver thee from the snare of the fowler; therefore in every place thou didst sing: There is none righteous besides Thee, O Lord![^315]

Bound with heavy irons upon hands and feet, and having previously borne five years of narrow confinement, thou didst not grow weak in mind, but having an unbound tongue, in all things that befell thee in those places thou didst give thanks unto God, singing: Righteously hast Thou brought all things upon us, for there is none righteous besides Thee, O Lord![^316]

Seeing thee fair of face and yet more radiant of soul, a certain young widow of noble women was wounded in bonds with the arrow of thy love, and deceiving thee with many words, spake; but thou didst answer her: In no wise shall I submit unto thy will, for God willed it thus; unto Whom I sing: There is none righteous besides Thee, O Lord![^317]

The adulteress marveling at thy bodily beauty, and especially at thy chaste words, sought many times to ensnare thee with her flattering words; but thou, O blessed one, possessing a firmer mind, didst wisely say: Who, having ever listened to a woman, was saved? And thou didst cry unto God: Save me, O God, from this assault, for there is none righteous besides Thee, O Lord![^318]

*To Moses the Hungarian*

*Glory:*
Unto the defiled woman who many times pressed thee with enticements, many times thou didst answer: Being redeemed with the blood of my Christ, in no wise shall I become a slave unto a shameless woman, whom I knew not from my birth; and let it not be unto me to destroy my labor in these bonds, for the sake of which I hope to attain deliverance from eternal bonds, if I remain thus enduring, and thankfully singing: There is none righteous besides Thee, O Lord![^319]

*Both now: Theotokion:*
Beholding me ensnared by the cords of grievous transgressions and shut up in the prison of noetic darkening, O Sovereign Lady, loose me speedily by thine intercession; and by the rays of thy grace enlighten my mind, and turn me to sing: There is none other more speedy unto help, besides thee, O all-holy Virgin Mary!

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 5:*[^320]
The adulteress, being scorched with the fire of thy beauty, hastened unto a deceitful counsel; for she thought: If she ransom thee with a price from him that took thee captive, she might easily bend thee unto her will; but thou, O venerable one, having the fear of God in thy heart, didst unceasingly cry aloud: Save me, O God, from such madness, for unto Thee, my Master, do I flee!

*Glory, both now: Theotokion:*
O thou that didst receive the Divine Fire unscorched into thy most pure womb: upon me who am burned by the fire of passions, sprinkle at least a drop of thy grace, O Sovereign Lady, that I may unceasingly sing: Save me, for unto thee, my Sovereign Lady, do I flee!

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*[^321]
Thou didst come from a Virgin, not an intercessor, nor an angel, but the Lord Himself incarnate, and didst save me, the whole man. Therefore I cry unto Thee: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!

*Troparia:*
The woman, held captive by passion for the captive, gave a ransom for thee, O blessed one, and taking thee, wished to bind thy soul with sinful fetters; but thou, having fenced thyself with the Cross, didst go into her house, singing psalm-like: Though I walk through the midst of the shadow of death, I will fear no evil, for God is with me; unto Whose power be glory![^322]

Thou wast likened unto thy Master: for even as the Jews, having bought Him, prepared Him for the Passion, so also the defiled adulteress prepared thee: if she could not ensnare thy soul with passions, she would burden thy body with suffering. Therefore thou didst choose rather to suffer in body than in soul, singing: Glory to Thy power, O Lord![^323]

*Service to the Venerable One*

That thou mightest not feel carnal burning in thy body, thou didst cast away from thyself every sweet food and soft garments, and in their stead didst clothe thyself in fasting and prayer, choosing dry bread and water with purity, rather than drinking diverse meats and wine with defilement; and unconquered by the enemy, thou didst sing: Glory to Thy power, O Lord![^324]

Behold a second Joseph, O brethren! For as he, having left his garment in the hand of the adulteress, fled from sin, so also this one, having stripped himself of the beauty of garments, fled from the hand that drew him unto defiled mingling, and thereby put to shame the invisible enemy who wished to defile the soul and body of the sufferer, yet accomplished nothing, for he sang: Glory to Thy power, O Lord![^325]

*Glory:*
The enemy, having found a second Egyptian woman, sought to ensnare the blessed one, for he could not bear to behold the purity of heart nor the beauty of countenance, at which even the angels marveled, crying: Glory to Thy power, O Lord![^326]

*Both now: Theotokion:*
The Heavenly King, desiring thy beauty, by the counsel of the Father and the overshadowing of the All-Holy Spirit, came down as rain upon the fleece into thy most pure womb, O Virgin; raise up again the fallen beauty of my mind, O Sovereign Lady, that I may unceasingly sing unto thee: Glory to Thy power, O Lady Mother of God![^327]

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*[^328]
O Lord, Giver of light and Creator of the ages: guide us in the light of Thy commandments, for besides Thee we know no other God!

*Troparia:*
The senseless woman, unable to deprive thee of the noetic Light, devised a murderous intent to seize thee, O venerable one: therefore she cast thee into a dark prison, where she tormented thee with hunger and thirst; but thou didst cry aloud: O my God, forsake me not, for besides Thee I know no other![^329]

He Who of old fed Elijah and Paul of Thebes in the wilderness, showed marvelous mercy unto thee also; for He inclined one of that woman's servants unto compassion, and commanded him to feed thee secretly; and thou, accounting this as a divine visitation, didst cry aloud: Besides Thee I know no other God![^330]

Many, not knowing the fire of divine grace working within thee, exhorted thee to submit unto the will of thy mistress, that thou mightest be master of her and of all her possessions; yet all these things thou didst despise for the sake of the love of God, saying: Besides Thee I will know no other![^331]

*To Moses the Hungarian*

Not corruptible beneficence, but mastery over fleshly passions didst thou seek, O our most blessed father Moses; therefore God granted thee, that not only didst thou enslave thine own passions unto the spirit, but didst also receive grace to assist others in temptations; for this cause thou didst cry aloud: Naught else besides the love of God will I know![^332]

*Glory:*
Thou didst mock the folly of the adulteress when she commanded thee to ride openly with glory upon horses and be loosed from things, and that they should bow down before thee as unto their lord; but with a loud voice thou didst utter words of chastity: All this is before her together with her that commanded you to do this; for I, being a servant of God, will know nothing thereof![^333]

*Both now: Theotokion:*
Put to shame the rejoicing enemy who hath ensnared us with the pleasures of sin-loving, O Lady, and gladden us who are imperiled in the storm of worldly prayers; and grant help to reach the calm haven of thy protection, for besides thee we know no other helper in sorrow.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*[^334]
Wallowing in the abyss of sin, I call upon the unfathomable abyss of Thy compassion: Lead me up from corruption, O God!

*Troparia:*
Thou didst not sink in the abyss of passions, nor didst thou dip thy garment into defilements, O venerable one; therefore a monk guided by God secretly clothed thee who didst desire it in the angelic habit, and taught thee how to preserve it blameless, neither to fear terror nor turn thy back unto the enemy; for God is He that delivers His servants from corruption.[^335]

The adulteress, sinking in the abyss of sinful burning and deprived of her hope in thee, laid heavy wounds upon thee, O venerable one; for having stretched thee out, she commanded that thou be beaten severely with rods, so that even the earth was filled with thy blood; yet thou, enduring these things with thanksgiving, didst cry aloud: Lead me up from corruption, O my God![^336]

Wishing to terrify thee who wast unterrified, they declared how many torments thou must endure, O blessed one, if thou wouldest not perform the will of thy mistress; yet thou, being ready for all things, didst boldly answer: Neither fire nor wounds shall be able to separate me from the love of God, in Whom I hope that He will deliver me from corruption![^337]

Thou didst not submit unto the woman darkened with shamelessness, but rather didst rebuke her, O venerable one, as one that had surpassed the fear of God, and shamelessly compelled thee unto defiled mingling *Service to the Venerable One*

...thou didst spit upon her, fearing neither her fury nor her wrath; for thou didst have hope in God, O father, that He would deliver thee from her hands.

*Glory:*
The woman, seeing herself put to shame by thee, O father, brought thee before the prince, from whom she received final judgment to do unto thee whatsoever she wished, having power after many torments to deliver thee unto death. But thou, prophetically foreseeing their destruction, didst answer: Ye yourselves shall soon go into eternal destruction, but Christ my God shall deliver me from corruption![^338]

*Both now: Theotokion:*
Ever do I stand before the world-loving prince, and as a captive hear judgment against myself, that lustful passions may work their will upon me; which having gained power over me, are laid bare in my senses, and by force ever draw me to commit adultery with them in thought. But before the ax of death cut me down in this world, do thou who art the Mother of the peaceful Heavenly King drive them away, and deliver me from corruption, O Mother of God![^339]

*Kontakion, Tone 3:*[^340]
Seeking the One God in the highest, thou didst despise all earthly things; taking no care for thy body, thou didst manfully surrender thyself unto torments; hunger, thirst, and bonds, being shut up in prison, thou didst gallantly endure; cuttings, shedding of blood, and the cutting off of members for the sake of thy chaste virginity thou didst manfully bear. And now standing before the All-Holy Trinity with the choirs of virgins, pray that from all temptations they may be delivered who cry unto thee: Rejoice, Moses, our most blessed father!

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*[^341]
The God-opposing decree of the lawless tyrant raised a high flame; but Christ spread a spiritual dew for the pious youths, He Who is blessed and supremely praised!

*Troparia:*
The adulteress having received the God-opposing decree, shamelessly dragged thee unto defiled mingling; for she wished to be sated with thy beauty, adorned with purity, whereat even the angels marveled, crying unto Him Who wrought this in thee: Blessed art Thou, O God the Creator![^342]

The enemy shot all his passionate arrows against thee, O father, wishing to wound thee; but wounded himself, he groaned, seeing that thou wast unconquerable, but rather crying: Blessed art Thou, O God!

In vain dost thou labor, O woman! thou didst say, O most blessed one; in no wise shall I yield unto thy defilement *To Moses the Hungarian*

...unto thy final defilement, foreseeing God ever at my right hand; neither doth my flesh play wanton, for the fear of the Lord crushes my bones; therefore I cry: Blessed art Thou, O God![^343]

Wishing to soften the firmness of thy mind, the adulteress commanded that thou be beaten with clubs, and that a hundred stripes be laid upon thee every day; yet even so was it not shaken, for thou didst dedicate thy flesh in the love of God, unto Whom thou didst bring forth hundredfold fruit, crying: Blessed art Thou, O God![^344]

*Glory:*
Having received grievous cuttings and the severing of members, O father, thou wast as dead, and lying scarcely alive; yet thou didst cut off the head of the standing enemy, showing him dead and inactive, crying aloud: Blessed art Thou, O my God, my Strength![^345]

*Both now: Theotokion:*
Having fallen into grievous misfortunes, I am in no wise able to lift myself up; do thou, my Sovereign Lady, reach forth thy strong hand unto me, and set me again upon my feet, that I may walk straight in Thy paths, crying unto thee: From generation to generation, blessed be thy name!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*[^346]
Unto God Who descended into the fiery furnace for the Hebrew children, and turned the flame into dew: sing, ye works, as the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparia:*
Drawn into the fiery furnace of lustful sin, thou didst call upon the One God, O blessed one, saying with David: O God, give ear unto my help; and having entered upon the flame, thou didst remain unscorched, rather scorching the multifaceted head of the enemy, who wills not to sing of the Lord unto ages![^347]

Unable to scorch thy purity with the fire of adultery, the enemy raised up a bitter persecution against the ranks of monastics; and all were driven from the province of the unmerciful prince, whom the wrath of God quickly overtook according to thy prophecy, O father, because he would not have in all his dominion them that sing of the Lord unto ages.[^348]

When the prince suddenly died an evil death, there was rebellion and clamor among the people; for the servants rose up and slew their masters, with whom also the adulteress who tormented thee perished, and her blood was upon her own head; yet thou, O venerable one, didst sing: In all works bless the Lord unto ages![^349]

Having given praise unto God Who freed Thee, Thou didst come into the cave unto the venerable Anthony *Service to the Venerable One*

...unto the venerable Anthony, bearing the wounds of martyrdom upon thy body; seeing which, all cried aloud: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages![^350]

*We bless the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit: the Lord.*
Desiring to hide thy purity brighter than the sun, O father, thou didst dwell in a dark cave, wherein thou didst exercise thyself in fasting and all-night standing; therefore God revealed concerning thee how great grace thou hast to assist all in temptations, that His Name may be glorified unto all ages.[^351]

*Both now: Theotokion:*
Thinking to hide the shame of my sin before the All-Seeing Eye, burned daily with carnal scorching, I add iniquity unto iniquity. But before my deeds are exposed before the angelic and human assembly, help me, O pure one, to escape this madness, that I may glorify thy name unto all ages.

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*[^352]
God the Word from God, Who by ineffable wisdom came to renew Adam who had fallen grievously into corruption by food, ineffably incarnate of the Holy Virgin for our sake: Him, ye faithful, let us magnify with songs in one accord!

*Troparia:*
God the Word Who descended from God glorified thee while yet in this life with wonderworking gifts, O father; for when a certain man was warred upon by lust, by the touch of thy staff his passions were mortified; and receiving a commandment from thee never to converse with a woman, having done so, he lived the rest of his years in peace, magnifying thee.[^353]

From generation to generation thy purity is glorified, for the sake of which having suffered much, thou didst receive the beatitude of the pure in heart; and translated from this temporal life, thou dost purely behold the All-Holy Trinity. Remember us also, O venerable one, who venerate thy memory.[^354]

The Lord named Thee a healer, for thou didst mortify carnal lusts by marvelous patient endurance, and hast power to quiet the passions of them that fall down before thy relics, even as thou didst for John the much-suffering, who having received peace in his bones, magnified God Who glorifies His saints.[^355]

Rejoice, O mountain that hast in the cave the incorrupt relics of the marvelous Moses! Sing praises, ye assemblies of monastics *To Moses the Hungarian*

...sing praises, ye assemblies of monastics; for behold, ye have an assistant and intercessor in afflictions; bow down before his reliquary, touching it with your lips, and ye shall find rest in your members.[^356]

*Glory:*
By the parable of glorious labor, seeing that He gave us the luminary of the world, our father Moses, celebrating whose summer memory, the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit, in one Godhead, we faithful magnify in one accord.

*Both now: Theotokion:*
Ever do we glorify thee, O pure Virgin, for thou hast gathered thy faithful servants into this place, where many receive healing who bow down before thine icon without doubt, and invoke our Caves fathers in prayer.

*Exapostilarion:*[^357]
O Tri-hypostatic Light, Creator and true God: enlighten the darkness of our souls; for behold, we bring Thy favored one unto Thee in prayer, and with him we pray: Calm the tempest of our sins, and deliver us all from sorrow!

*Glory, both now: Theotokion:*
O Mary, Mother of God, more radiant than the sun: by grace enlighten all that bow down before thee and invoke thy name, and preserve this monastery from every evil; and fill them that dwell therein with everlasting rejoicing and spiritual joy, for thou art the mediatress of our joy!

*At the Praises, Stichera, Tone 4. Podoben: "As one noble" (Ꙗ҆́кѡ до́блѧ):*[^358]
Gathered together today with the voice of rejoicing, ye feast-lovers, let us offer songs of praise unto God, glorifying the most blessed father, and falling down before the reliquary of his relics, let us cry: Forget us not as thou standest before the Master, but pray that we who lovingly honor thy memory may be delivered from all temptations and grievous circumstances.

Who can worthily hymn thee, O venerable father? For in this temporal life, though in a mortal body, yet thou didst live spiritually, being outside the world and flesh, having an unceasing desire to be dissolved from the flesh and to be with Christ; with Whom now reigning, remember us also who glorify thee.[^359]

Rejoice, all-honorable choir of God-bearing Caves fathers, for ye have acquired a good luminary *Service to the Venerable One*

...a luminary shining, the summit of virgins, and the beauty of martyrs, a pure habitation of the Holy Spirit, Moses the noetic beholder of God; crowning whose memory with praises, we pray you: Entreat Christ God and Master, that we your children may be delivered from tribulations, and may not be deprived of the Kingdom on high.[^360]

*Glory: Tone 6:*[^361]
As a true follower of Christ and faithful hearer of His Gospel, we praise thee today, O venerable father; for looking unto eternal recompense, thou didst account all the fair things of this world as refuse, and didst choose to be a captive, that thou mightest keep thy mind free from sin; taking no care for thy body, thou didst surrender thyself unto torments, that with streams of blood thou mightest drown the tormenting noetic enemy. Thyself now abiding in a place of rest and enjoying the joy of the saints, enrich us also from on high with thy gifts, who lovingly celebrate thy memory.

*Both now: Theotokion: O Mother of God, thou art the true vine:*
*Great Doxology, and dismissal.*

*(Ornamental Baroque woodcut tailpiece) (Woodcut Engraving: Saint Pimen the Much-Ailing of the Kyiv Caves (*Пр: Пиминъ Печерскій*); below, secondary narrative panel: The Angels tonsuring the sick Pimen (**Болнаго Пимина постригоша Аггѣлы**).)*[^362]

---

# MONTH OF AUGUST

*On the 7th Day: Service to our Venerable Father Pimen the Much-Ailing of the Caves.*[^363]

*At Vespers, "Blessed is the man," the 1st antiphon. On "Lord, I have cried," Stichera, Tone 4. Podoben: "Joy of the heavenly ranks" (Небе́сныхъ чинѡ́въ):*[^364]
He Who foreknew thee from thy mother's womb unto beatitude, by His providence gave an image of salvation, permitting upon thee the sickness of Job, that being tried thereby as gold in the furnace, thy body *Service to the Venerable One*

...thou didst exhaust in much-ailing sickness; and now being in painless rest, remember us who are sick with spiritual passions, O most blessed father Pimen![^365]

Having hated the world, O venerable one, and life that drags down by carnal delight, thou didst ask of the Lord to exchange it for prolonged illness, wherein thou didst ever utter thanksgiving unto God, saying psalm-like: My soul hath cleaved after Thee, that Thy right hand may uphold me, O Christ, guiding me unto the right path and eternal life.[^366]

Unto thee, O venerable father Pimen, after the multitude of painful temptations which thou didst painlessly offer up in the body, consolations in the unapproachable Light now gladden thy soul; for thou dost stand before the throne of the Master with the angelic ranks, where pray for us also, that we may obtain peace from on high and deliverance from passions.[^367]

*Glory: Tone 5:*
Come, ye choirs of monastics, let us praise the venerable Pimen, who became an emulator of the great Anthony; for as he received the monastic habit from angels, so this one also was vouchsafed to be clothed in the monastic habit by an angel's hand, with whom now wearing incorruptible garments, he rejoices with the righteous, keeps festival with the venerable, and prays with them unto Christ God for our souls.[^368]

*Both now: Of the Feast.*[^369]
*Entrance, Prokeimenon of the day, and three readings (see fol. 30 on the reverse).*[^370]

*At the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 4. Automela:*[^371]
The candle given unto thee by the angels prefigured the brightness of thy soul, O venerable father; therefore thy blameless life shone forth brighter than the sun before men. Which venerating, we magnify, and glorify the Heavenly Father with the Son and the Holy Spirit.[^372]

*Verse: Precious in the sight of the Lord is the death of His venerable ones.*[^373]
Beholding the desire of thy heart and thy love toward heavenly things, the Sovereign King on high sent His angels unto thee, O father, to clothe thee in the monastic habit and to sweeten thee with heavenly singing, that having completed the course of this painful life with patient endurance, thou mightest obtain painless rest through sickness.[^374]

*Verse: Blessed is the man that fears the Lord, in His commandments he shall greatly delight.*[^375]
Renunciation of the world and poverty thou didst choose, blameless virginity, and measureless patient endurance *To Pimen the Much-Ailing*

...patient endurance, and perfect love toward God, are truly great virtues, wherewith as upon a swift-coursing chariot thou didst ascend unto heavenly things, and stand before the Creator of all and God; unto Whom pray for us also, O venerable one, that we may complete our course without stumbling, and obtain mercy in the day of judgment.[^376]

*Glory: Tone 8:*
We, the multitudes of monastics, run unto thy reliquary, O father; we bow down before thine incorrupt relics, we draw healings for diseases, we receive strength against the enemies that war against us, and find victory over our adversaries; forgiveness of sins is granted us, and we are vouchsafed the mercy of the Lord through thy prayers.[^377]

*Both now: Of the Feast.*

*Troparion, Tone 5:*[^378]
Emulating the humble-mindedness of the God-bearing Anthony, and likened in patient endurance unto Job, thou didst not seek healing from sickness, but rather its prolongation for thyself, O patient-spirited Pimen, lest having become healthy and torn away by thy parents from this place, thou shouldest be deprived of the fellowship of the venerable ones and of their rank; therefore beyond hope thou wast tonsured by the very angels, with whom pray unto Christ God to save our souls.

*Glory, both now: Of the Feast.*

*At Matins, on "God is the Lord," Troparion of the Feast. Glory: of the Venerable One. Both now: of the Feast.*
*After Kathisma 1, Sessional Hymn, Tone 5. Podoben: "The Word co-unoriginate" (Собеꙁнача́лное):*[^379]
Exceeding great patient endurance didst thou possess, O father: for lying upon the bed of sickness for many years, covered with sores from head to foot, thou didst give thanks unto God, choosing in this life to rot entirely, that in that other life thou mightest be without corruption, and enduring the perception of stench, that thou mightest obtain the fragrance of Christ; make us also patient, therefore, by thy prayers!

*Glory, both now: Of the Feast, or this Theotokion:*
Lustful poisons have encompassed me, and I lie upon the bed of mine evil deeds; but do thou who didst bear without pain, wither away my diseases, and raise me up unto virtues, nailing me whole by thy prayers.[^380]

*After Kathisma 2, Sessional Hymn, Tone 3. Podoben: "Speedily anticipate" (Ско́рѡ предварѝ):*[^381]
Speedily did God give ear unto thee when thou didst pray, O blessed one, and fulfilled the desire of thy heart, sending His angels to clothe thee in the angelic habit; therefore be thou also speedy unto help for us, and fulfill the desire of our heart, entreating the angelic *Service to the Venerable One*

...Master of the angels, have mercy upon us from on high by His compassion!

*Glory, both now: Of the Feast, or this Theotokion:*
Speedily give ear unto the supplications of thy servants, O Sovereign Lady, and anticipate to cover us with the shelter of thy wings, lest we be enslaved unto sin unto the end; but that we may zealously serve thy Son, our Lord, Who grants us great mercy.

*After the Polyeleos, Sessional Hymn, Tone 8. Podoben: "Having reclined on Wisdom" (Премꙋ́дрости возле́гъ):*[^382]
Thy many sicknesses, O blessed Pimen, became the cause of health for many; for ailing thyself, thou didst heal the sick; lying thyself in disease, thou didst raise up the bedridden; by a word thou didst drive away every weakness. Therefore now running unto thine incorrupt relics, we pray thee: Pour forth health for bodies, and grant healing for souls; entreat Christ God to grant forgiveness of sins unto them that lovingly honor thy holy memory![^383]

*Glory, both now: Of the Feast.*
*Gradual Hymns, the 1st Antiphon of Tone 4.*
*Prokeimenon, Tone 7: The venerable ones shall boast in glory, and shall rejoice upon their beds.*[^384]
*Verse: Sing unto the Lord a new song.*[^385]
*Let every breath praise the Lord.*
*Gospel common for a Venerable Monk.*[^386]
*After Psalm 50, Sticheron: Come, ye choirs of monastics:*
*(see at "Lord, I have cried").*

*Canon to the Venerable One, Tone 2. Ode 1. Heirmos:*[^387]
In the deep of old, the overwhelming power laid low the whole host of Pharaoh; and the incarnate Word destroyed all-wicked sin, the supremely glorified Lord, for He is gloriously glorified!

*Troparia:*
Held fast by the depth of perplexity, I pray Thee, the Depth of Wisdom, O Jesus: Raise up my mind, and move my tongue, to praise the great patient endurance of Thy favored one Pimen!

In the depth of dispassion, O venerable one, thou didst drown the passions, and didst cross the sea of the world with dry feet, so that not even thy mind touched sinful corruption; from which preserve me also by thy prayers.[^388]

*Glory:*
In the depth of thy tears thou didst drown the noetic Pharaoh, and now in the heavenly haven thou dost sing the song of victory, where pray for me also, O venerable one!

*Both now: Theotokion:*
When I am drowned in the depth of evils, O Sovereign Lady, reach forth *To Pimen the Much-Ailing*

...reach forth a hand of help, even as thy Son did unto Peter, and lead me unto the haven of salvation.[^389]

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*[^390]
The wilderness blossomed as a lily, O Lord, the barren church of the Gentiles, by Thy coming, wherein my heart was established!

*Troparia:*
The Caves monastery blossomed with venerable fathers as a garden with fragrant lilies, and in their midst thou also, O venerable one, didst blossom as a lily, by holiness in life, and after thy dormition by incorruption.[^391]

Thou didst blossom by thy God-pleasing life as a flower of the field, O venerable one; and now thy holy relics in the cave send forth sweet fragrance as a lily of the valleys, and pour forth grace unto them that run unto thee with faith.[^392]

*Glory:*
A lily among thorns, Pimen blossomed in purity through the patient endurance of sicknesses; who, transplanted into the gardens of Paradise, now makes us fragrant by his prayers.[^393]

*Both now: Theotokion:*
O fragrant Flower, All-Holy Virgin: drive away the stench of my sin, and show me forth as a sweet fragrance of Christ by thy prayers.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 2:*[^394]
Bold was thy prayer unto God, for when the Caves fathers prayed for thy healing, thou didst ask of the Lord an increase of sicknesses for thyself, and the supplication of thee alone overcame the prayers of all. We believe, therefore, that now also thy holy prayers can do much![^395]

*Glory, both now: Theotokion:*
A mother's prayer avails much unto the winning over of the Master; pray therefore unto Him for us, O Virgin Mother, and make known our sorrow unto Him, that having sent thee, He may be merciful unto us, and turning our sorrow into joy, grant eternal life.[^396]

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*[^397]
I sing of Thee, for by hearing, O Lord, I have heard and was afraid; for Thou didst come unto me, seeking me who was gone astray; therefore do I glorify Thy great condescension toward me, O Greatly-merciful One!

*Troparia:*
Grant us worthily to sing and glorify God, O venerable father, by thy prayers, that we may ever call thee blessed!

The fathers that were near heard angelic singing at midnight, when they clothed thee in the garment of salvation; wherewith vouchsafe us also to enjoy angelic songs *Service to the Venerable One*

...vouchsafe us to enjoy, by thy intercession, O blessed one!

*Glory:*
I sing unto thee fervently, O father; I call blessed, O venerable one, thy great innocence and thy meekness; I praise thy patient endurance, I marvel at thine incorruption, and I bow down before thy healing relics.[^398]

*Both now: Theotokion:*
Save thy singers, O All-Hymned Mother, delivering them from eternal weeping; by thy mercy wipe away every tear from mine eyes, and vouchsafe unto eternal joys.[^399]

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*[^400]
Thou hast become a Mediator between God and men, O Christ God; for through Thee, O Master, we have introduction unto the Source of Light, Thy Father, out of the night of ignorance.

*Troparia:*
By thine intercession, O father, preserve from every assault of the enemy this monastery, wherein thou didst live in a God-pleasing manner, and be a helper unto them that live as monastics, for we put our trust in thy prayers.[^401]

Be an intercessor for me unto the righteous Judge, O blessed one; for behold, I have been negligent even as that schema-monk whom thou didst show unto the brethren at thy dormition, who had the great habit but small deeds. Therefore his schema is kept incorrupt, unto his reproof and condemnation.[^402]

*Glory:*
Intercede for salvation for me who am in despair, O blessed one; guide a sinner unto repentance, O venerable one; grant amendment unto the perverse, O father, that without shame I may hope in thy prayers.

*Both now: Theotokion:*
O Mediatress unto the Creator, All-Pure Virgin: cleanse me who am wholly defiled by sins, through thy prayers; for placing all my hope in thee, I shall not be put to shame forever.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*[^403]
Wallowing in the abyss of sin, I invoke the unfathomable abyss of Thy compassion: Lead me up from corruption, O God!

*Troparia:*
In the abyss of evils lies the whole world, for which cause thou didst renounce it, that thou mightest please God in goodness; unto Whom vouchsafe me also to be well-pleasing, O favored one of God![^404]

Lying in the abyss of grievous sicknesses, neglected many times and abandoned by them that served thee, thou didst have God alone visiting thee; leave us not, O father, who are in sorrow, destitute of thy prayers, for we are continually ailing.[^405]

*To Pimen the Much-Ailing*

*Glory:*
Lest I be condemned unto the abyss of Hades by reason of the multitude of my sins, do thou, O father, stand before the righteous Judge, and entreat Him to deliver me from His righteous judgment.

*Both now: Theotokion:*
As thou hast borne the Abyss of mercy, exhaust the abysses of my measureless iniquities, O Mother of compassion, I pray thee.

*Kontakion, Tone 6:*[^406]
Come, having assembled together, let us praise Pimen the patient-enduring, who thankfully endured prolonged sickness; for he from whom men once fled because of the stench in his illness, now pours forth sweet fragrance from his relics unto them that approach, and drives away the foul stench of passions from them that cry: Come unto us, O Pimen, and the enemy shall accomplish nothing against us!

*Ikos:*[^407]
Ye that are held fast by diverse sicknesses and passions, having gathered together unto Pimen the much-ailing, marvel at his patient endurance! For remaining many times hungry and thirsty, he grieved not against them that served him, but with joy endured all things, giving thanks unto God; and having spent twenty years in his suffering, he was tried as gold in the furnace, and was found worthy before God, and received from Him the gift to heal infirmities and to drive away passions from them that run unto him with faith. Unto him, therefore, let us also run, and invoke him, crying: Come unto us, O Pimen, and the enemy shall accomplish nothing against us!

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*[^408]
The god-opposing command of the lawless tyrant raised up a high flame; but Christ spread forth spiritual dew unto the pious youths, He Who is blessed and supremely glorified!

*Troparia:*
Thou didst hate the god-opposing world, O father, knowing Him Who said: Whosoever would be a friend of the world becomes an enemy of God; grant us therefore also to flee the world and all its lusts.[^409]

The god-opposing law of mine evil custom forcibly draws me unto evil deeds, and what I would, that I do not, but what I would not, that do I commit; do thou therefore, O Pimen, deliver me from it by thy prayers.[^410]

*Glory:*
The god-opposing command of reigning sin in my members *Service to the Venerable One*

...in my members compels me often to anger God; unto thee do I flee, O father: deliver me from compulsive sins!

*Both now: Theotokion:*
O Virgin who didst bear God: grant us to fulfill the commandments of thy Son, and let us in nothing be found opposed unto Him, by thy help.

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*[^411]
The fiery furnace of old in Babylon divided its operation by the divine command, scorching the Chaldeans, but bedewing the faithful, who sang: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Troparia:*
That thou mightest quench the furnace of passions, O father, thou didst ask of God an increase of sicknesses, wherein bodily refreshment was granted thee; which grant us also to obtain by thy prayers, that with thee we may sing unto the All-Pure God: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

Though the furnace of many years of sickness scorched thy body, yet it could in no way harm the mind of thy soul; for in all these things that befell thee thou didst not sin, but remaining unconsumed in the midst of temptations, as in the midst of flame, thou didst sing with the children: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord![^412]

*We bless the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit: the Lord!*
As in a furnace, I have kindled the fire of lusts in my flesh, and multiplied the flame of impurity by mine intemperance; O God Who sendest down dew, cool the flame, quench the fire, and bedew the furnace of my passions by the prayers of Thy favored one Pimen, that with him I may sing unto Thee, God glorified in the Trinity: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Both now: Theotokion:*
The fiery furnace of old that did not scorch the youths prefigured thy childbirth, O All-Pure Lady; for having conceived the Divine Fire, thou didst remain unconsumed; and I therefore pray thee, O Sovereign Lady: preserve me unburnt by the adverse fire, that praying I may sing unto thee: Sing unto the Lord, all ye works of the Lord, and supremely exalt His all-holy name unto the ages!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*[^413]
The Son of the unoriginate Begetter, God and Lord, incarnate of the Virgin, hath appeared unto us, to enlighten the darkened and to gather together the dispersed; therefore the All-Hymned Theotokos do we magnify!

*Troparia:*
O Son of the unoriginate Begetter: we bring unto Thee in prayer Thy favored one Pimen, who from the beginning of his monastic life loved Thee with all his soul *To Pimen the Much-Ailing*

...loved Thee with all his soul and with all his strength served Thee; look upon his ascetical struggles, and for their sake hear his supplication for us, and grant us a good end!

O Son, co-unoriginate with the Father and the Spirit: we present unto Thee in prayer Thy good and faithful servant Pimen; look upon his prolonged and grievous sicknesses, and for their sake have mercy upon us at his supplication, and be merciful unto us!

*Glory:*
O Holy Spirit, co-ever-existing with the Father and the Son: we offer unto Thee the fervent prayers of the blessed Pimen; give ear unto him, and for his sake have mercy upon us!

*Both now: Theotokion:*
O Mother of God, with the blessed Pimen and with all the venerable Caves fathers who pleased God: pray unto the All-Holy and Undivided Trinity to have mercy upon Thy servants who continually glorify thee.

*Exapostilarion:*[^414]
The bridal chamber of the Savior is open unto Thee, O venerable Pimen; for thou hast the wedding garment of the angelic habit laid upon Thee by angels, wherein having entered into the marriage supper of the Lamb, forget not us who honor Thee!

*Glory, both now: Of the Feast, or this Theotokion:*
O undefiled Bridal Chamber, All-Pure Virgin: cover us who have no wedding garment with thy precious robe, and lead us into the heavenly bridal chamber!

*At the Praises, Stichera, Tone 8. Podoben: "O marvelous wonder" (Ѽ пресла́внагѡ чꙋдесѐ):*[^415]
O marvelous wonder! O marvelous operation of the Holy Spirit upon thee, which wrought in thy infirm flesh, O venerable one! For while ailing thyself, thou didst heal others, and didst lead them unto amendment by thy patient endurance. Therefore show thy mercy unto them that praise thee, O father, and entreat of Christ God healing in our infirmities, for them that lovingly honor thy holy dormition!

O God-wise father Pimen: from childhood thou didst give thyself over unto sicknesses through prayer, and by grievous sufferings didst wear away thy body, that thou mightest preserve the purity of the flesh and present thy soul blameless unto God; standing before Whom in painless life, as one able to assist by thy prayer, pray continually for us who sing with faith of thy holy falling asleep!

*Service to the Venerable One*

O venerable father Pimen: let everyone that ails in this prolonged life learn to emulate thy patient endurance, and send up thanksgiving unto God, from Whom we receive trials as chastisement for the correction of spiritual sicknesses; but be an advocate for all that run unto the reliquary of thy relics, and entreat of Christ God deliverance from spiritual and bodily infirmity!

*Glory: Tone 2:*
A truly mystical myrrh, unto the healing of the sick, does the reliquary of thy holy relics pour forth, making fragrant them that approach with faith, granting them rest in necessities; grant therefore unto us also, O all-blessed father, from the ever-flowing fountain of thy love, deliverance from spiritual and bodily illnesses, as we praise thy holy falling asleep with the beauties of hymns!

*Both now: Of the Feast, or this Theotokion: Save Thy servants from perils...*
*Great Doxology, and Dismissal.*
*(Ornamental baroque tailpiece) (Woodcut engraving: Above in the clouds, the Venerable Basil holding a cross/staff (*Пр: Василій.*) and the Venerable Theodore of the Caves (*Пр: Ѳео́доръ.*); below in two panels: demons tempting Basil to slander Theodore (*Бѣси поучают Василіа оклеветати Ѳеодора.*) and the appearance of the earthly treasure to Theodore (*Или Ѳеодору сокровище явися и обрати(ся)*).)*

*Month of August, on the 11th Day:*
*Service to our venerable fathers Theodore and Basil of the Caves.*
*At Vespers, "Blessed is the man," the 1st antiphon. On "Lord, I have cried," Stichera to the Venerable Theodore, Tone 6. Podoben: "Having laid all aside" (Всѐ ѿло́жше):*[^416]
Having gathered all corruptible treasure, and followed the word of Christ, O our most blessed father Theodore, thou didst distribute it unto them that were in need, and by their hands didst deposit it in an unstealable place, whence by the light *Service to the Venerable Ones*

...being enriched by the light of grace, thou didst shine forth in the world as the sun; and now standing with boldness before the Master, enlighten with the rays of thy radiant gifts them that sing of thy memory!

Desiring to receive noetic riches, thou didst disregard sensory wealth, and delivering it into the hands of the poor, thou didst choose in poverty to follow Christ, Who became poor for our sakes; and taking His cross upon thy shoulder, thou didst traverse the narrow way, and after a blessed end didst inherit spacious places, where dwelling with the angels, pray for them that praise thee, O most blessed Theodore![^417]

Having left all the beautiful things of this world, thou didst joyfully receive the monastic habit, and having struggled well therein, thou didst settle in a dark cave, where by much abstinence and labors thou didst subdue thy flesh unto the spirit; and having wounded the adversary that wounded thee, thou didst drive him away by the weapon of the Cross. And now dwelling in unapproachable light, remember us also who celebrate thy honorable dormition![^418]

*Other Stichera, to the Venerable Basil, Tone 4. Podoben: "Thou hast given a sign" (Да́лъ є҆сѝ зна́менїе):*[^419]
Fulfilling the law that is in the Scriptures, O venerable Basil, thou didst acquire love toward God, and didst love thy neighbor as thyself, and didst establish him, O blessed one, not to be shaken by the invisible enemy, against whom Jesus the Lover of mankind and Savior of our souls granted thee strength.[^420]

Having in thy lips, O most blessed one, speech seasoned with the salt of grace, thou didst again confirm toward virtue the heart that was corrupted with the love of money, and didst perceive the serpent hiding in the hut, and didst drive him far away by the name of Christ, Whom the opposing powers dread.[^421]

Bound together by a firm bond of love, O most blessed fathers, ye were found even in this life as a strong city, which the deceitful ministers of cruel Belial could not undermine; and feigning God-pleasing counsel, they fell wounded, while ye sang in praise: Our power and strength is Jesus, the Lover of mankind and Savior of our souls![^422]

*Glory: Tone 6:*
O most blessed fathers, noble warriors of Christ: by the word of unceasing prayer, having taken up the shield of unwavering faith, and girded yourselves with hope in God, ye conquered the demonic hosts *To Theodore and Basil of the Caves*

...conquered the demonic hosts, and having cleansed your robes in the blood of your sufferings, ye entered into the heavenly bridal chamber; whence forget us not, but be compassionate toward us, asking for us from Christ God peace and great mercy![^423]

*Both now: Of the Feast.*
*Entrance, Prokeimenon of the day, and three readings (see fol. 38 on the reverse).*[^424]

*At the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 2. Podoben: "When from the Tree" (Е҆гда̀ ѿ дре́ва):*[^425]
When the grace of the All-Holy Spirit kindled Thy heart with the fire of warm love toward God, O venerable one, then despising all the beautiful things of this world and following the Master's footsteps, thou didst choose to walk the narrow path in this life, bearing the Cross of Christ upon Thy shoulder; and through long-suffering thou didst enter into spacious places; whence forget not us who lovingly bless Thy honorable memory in faith, O Basil![^426]

*Verse: The venerable ones shall boast in glory, and shall rejoice upon their beds.*[^427]
When having rejected all the rumor and turmoil that enslaves the human mind in trial, O most blessed one, thou didst depart unto a silent place, thou didst settle in a dark cave, and didst make it as a certain most beautiful paradise; and having lived therein by many labors, thou didst overcome the invisible enemies, and wast shown to be a converser with angels. And now standing with them before the Master Christ, pray for us also, O father Theodore, that we may be delivered from all turmoil and calamity![^428]

*Verse: Precious in the sight of the Lord is the death of His venerable ones.*[^429]
Beholding the ladder, O most venerable fathers, set up from the Caves mountain unto heaven, whereon the Mother of God placed her habitation, and the angelic choirs with the saints ascended upon it unto heaven: ye followed their footsteps as though bodiless; and having ascended by contemplation and action, ye now stand before the Holy Trinity; where remember us also who bow down before your relics, O most blessed ones![^430]

*Glory: Tone 7:*
Having flourished in the world by many ascetical struggles, O holy martyrs, marvelous Theodore with courageous Basil: ye were clothed in a richly embroidered robe, and now live in the unwaning Light; whence looking down upon the earth, by the rays *Service to the Venerable Ones*

...by the rays of your radiant gifts enlighten us who are in distress in the darkness of this life, and entreat of Christ God great mercy!

*Both now: Of the Feast.*

*Troparion, Tone 4:*[^431]
Bound together by the bond of love, O venerable ones, ye trampled down every bond of the enemy's wiles, and nobly endured suffering and guiltless death from the money-loving prince; therefore we pray you that lived together and received martyrs' crowns: Pray unto the Lord for us, that having lived well in abundant love, faith, and hope, we may ever call you blessed, O Theodore and Basil, gloriously victorious!

*Glory, both now: Of the Feast.*
*(Ornamental baroque headpiece)*

*At Matins, on "God is the Lord," Troparion of the Feast. Glory: of the Venerable Ones. Both now: of the Feast.*

*After Kathisma 1, Sessional Hymn, Tone 4. Podoben: "Thy tomb" (Гро́бъ тво́й):*[^432]
Come, ye people, let us honor in hymns the beautiful pair of all-blessed fathers; for having been greatly tried, they were shown forth in the world purer than gold; and now standing before the Master's throne, they assist them that are in distress in many tribulations as helpers, and as a quiet haven unto them that are tempest-tossed, that they may receive deliverance from all temptations.[^433]

*Glory, both now: Of the Feast, or this Theotokion:*
O speedy Helper of all the world, and quiet Haven of them that are tempest-tossed: bring us who wander upon the waves of voluntary passions into the speedy stillness of thy goodness, and deliver from perils them that sing unto thee: Rejoice, the Joy and Salvation of all!

*After Kathisma 2, Sessional Hymn, Tone 4. Podoben: "Thou hast appeared" (Ꙗ҆ви́лсѧ є҆сѝ):*[^434]
Having labored with many sweats, and received guiltless martyrdom, O God-wise fathers, ye have come unto the place of rest, where keeping festival with the angels, pray unto the All-Bountiful God, that we who suffer may also receive protection from enemies; for we are your children, and the rational sheep of your teachings.[^435]

*Glory, both now: Of the Feast, or this Theotokion:*
Appear unto us at this hour, O All-Pure Virgin, and receiving the praises offered unto thee, carry them unto thy Son Whom thou didst bear mercifully upon thine arms, asking for us the remission of sins; for we are thy servants, having *To Theodore and Basil of the Caves*

...having thee as our speedy advocate and intercessor for our souls.

*After the Polyeleos, Sessional Hymn, Tone 4. The same Podoben:*
The enclosed garden of the Mother of God brought forth for us two unfading flowers, Theodore and Basil, the noble martyrs, wherewith monastics are filled with joy, and the marvelous monastery is made fragrant; whom the people honor with praises, receiving healing from their relics, and having them as fervent intercessors unto God.[^436]

*Glory, both now: Of the Feast, or this Theotokion:*
Fervent Advocate of them that are in sorrows, and Guarantor of sinners, O Pure Virgin: despise not our tears and sighing, but incline unto thy man-befriending goodness, and save thy servants from all assaults of the demons, cruel and fierce!

*Gradual Hymns, the 1st Antiphon of Tone 4.*
*Prokeimenon: Precious in the sight of the Lord is the death of His venerable ones.*[^437]
*Verse: What shall I render unto the Lord for all that He hath rendered unto me?*[^438]
*Let every breath praise the Lord.*
*Gospel from Matthew, pericope 38 (Matthew 10:32-33, 37-38; 19:27-30).*[^439]
*The Lord said unto His disciples: Whosoever shall confess Me before men...*
*After Psalm 50, Sticheron, the evening headpiece, Tone 6: O most blessed fathers...*

*Canon to the Venerable Ones, Tone 2. Ode 1. Heirmos:*[^440]
In the deep of old, the overwhelming power laid low the whole host of Pharaoh; and the incarnate Word destroyed all-wicked sin, the supremely glorified Lord, for He is gloriously glorified!

*Troparia:*
Desiring by life to walk the path of ascetical fasting, the glorious Theodore distributed all the riches of silver he possessed of this world unto the poor, and alone acquired Christ of the destitute, unto Whom he also sings: He is gloriously glorified![^441]

Seeing, O blessed one, that the world lies in evil, thou didst not desire to look upon its corruption; therefore thou didst settle in a cave, remaining many years, applying thyself to abstinence and labors, looking unto God alone.[^442]

Envying thee, the all-crafty enemy cast thee into despair, O venerable one, through repentance for the distributed possessions, darkening thee for many days; but thou didst correct thyself again through confession, and didst beguile him that sought to beguile thee.[^443]

*Glory:*
As a perfect monk having perfect non-possessiveness, thou didst comfort Theodore, O venerable *Service to the Venerable Ones*

...venerable Basil, not to repent, for the fashion of this world passes away, but to look upon heavenly things, for they abide unto ages.[^444]

*Both now: Theotokion:*
Wither the flesh that drags me down unto corruption, O Sovereign Lady, and establish me to look upon incorruptible things, and guide our life, as thou art a speedy helper and correction unto men.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*[^445]
The bow of the mighty is broken by Thy strength, O Christ, and the weak have girded themselves with power!

*Troparia:*
The bow of Belial strung against thee was broken, O father; for having received the words of prayer as a sword, thou didst cut him down, who had previously sickened with love of money, darkened; and afterward being girded with divine strength, thou wast a wonder unto the enemy.[^446]

Thou didst hear, O venerable one, of the woman of old weeping over distributed alms because gold and life had perished; thou didst weep bitterly, and having thereby escaped the disease of the love of money, didst cry aloud: I glorify Thy measureless and unfathomable mercy![^447]

As born of one womb ye loved one another, Basil and Theodore; and having beguiled Belial who hates advancement in the commandments of the Lord, establish me also unto their advancement, that together with you I may glorify the unfathomable mercy of God!

When thou hadst departed upon obedience, O venerable Basil, the enemy found the time, and coming in thy form, he armed Theodore again with the attraction of distributed possessions, so that he remembered not the prayer of Job, unshaken in misfortunes.[^448]

*Glory:*
Let him that thinks he stands take heed lest he fall: as came to pass unto the blessed Theodore; for thinking he had driven away the adversary, he received him again, and his last state would have been worse than the first, had not the unfathomable grace of God saved him from these things.[^449]

*Both now: Theotokion:*
Being insensible, I thought that I stood in virtues, not knowing that I have fallen; but do thou, O speedy Helper, raise me up, O Pure Virgin, and teach me zealously to do the commandments of God, that I may glorify thine ineffable mercy, O Mother of God.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 8:*[^450]
Having been wounded by the adversary, the author of malice, O father, conversing excessively with him concerning unprofitable things, not remembering that evil communications corrupt good manners: yet all this was permitted by God, that thy trial might be shown purer than gold, and obtain a greater crown for thee, which having received afterward, pray unto Christ God that we also may not be deprived thereof, who lovingly honor thy holy memory![^451]

*To Theodore and Basil of the Caves*

*Glory, both now: Of the Feast, or this Theotokion:*
The arrows of the crafty one, steeped in the burning of the flesh, have wounded my heart, and I lie dead, in despair of life; apply unto me the plaster of thy healing, O Pure Virgin, as thou art the healer of all the Christian race.

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*[^452]
I heard, O Lord, of Thy glorious dispensation, and I glorified Thine unfathomable power, O Lover of mankind!

*Troparia:*
When the all-destroyer heard that in conversation thou didst not mention the name of the Lord Jesus, O Theodore, he took boldness, and advised thee to ask of God that a multitude of gold and silver be given thee for the sake of thy distributed wealth; but thou didst not know that they that will to be rich fall into temptation and an unceasing snare.[^453]

The demon having assumed the guise of holy counsel, O venerable one, Thou didst see thyself in sleep as an angel in great brightness, showing thee a treasure in the cave; which having dug out a little and found, Thou didst almost lose the Pearl of great price, Christ, by Whose grace, having fallen, Thou wast raised up again![^454]

The devil desiring to cause the root of the love of money to sprout branches in thee, through good thoughts suggested to thee, and straightway appearing in the form of Basil, advised thee to flee with the found gold into a foreign land, and purchase estates for thy need, that so thou mightest depart from this holy place, O venerable one, having fallen away from the grace of God.[^455]

*Glory:*
Terrifying thee with the prolongation of life and the onset of old age, the enemy drew thee toward the desire for possessions, O Theodore, wishing to take away from thee remembrance, that thou mightest not remember Him Who said: Take no thought for the morrow, for God cares for you![^456]

*Both now: Theotokion:*
The crafty one gives me remembrance, that I should not remember Him Who said: Take no thought for your soul; and so he leads me into a soul-harming land, that having become destitute of the grace of God, I may perish there. But, O Pure Virgin, suffer me not to fall into this final perdition![^457]

*Service to the Venerable Ones*

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*[^458]
The Coal revealed unto Isaiah, the Sun, hath shone forth from the virginal womb, granting the enlightenment of the knowledge of God unto them that were wandering in darkness!

*Troparia:*
Still having the coal scorching thy conscience with shame, O blessed one, thou didst answer the demon: I promised to end my life here in the cave; how then shall I now be a fugitive? Nay, I will live here, and perform all things commanded by thee.[^459]

Again the adversary suggests that the found treasure will be discovered, and threatens its confiscation, with which he advises thee to flee from this place; which counsel having heeded, thou didst prepare thyself for flight, had not the prayers of the venerable fathers, and rather the very right hand of the Mother of God, held thee back, O Theodore![^460]

A true disciple and non-possessor of wealth, the blessed Basil, thou didst drive away, O Theodore, having been deceived into believing that he had beguiled thee with demonic deception; but he, when driven away, did not contradict, but rather prayed unto God to deliver thee from satanic snares.[^461]

*Glory:*
In the presence of many witnesses, that thou mightest be justified, O Basil, thou didst come with the brethren unto him that was deceived, assuring him that *To Theodore and Basil of the Caves*

...that not with thee, but with the demon in thy form who was here yesterday and before, spreading his wiles, had he conversed.

*Both now: Theotokion:*
The multi-crafty enemy, wishing to trip me up, transforms himself into diverse shapes, and operates in my thoughts, that I may depart from thy shelter, O All-Pure Mother; but, O Theotokos, suffer not such temptation to come upon me!

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*[^462]
Having heard the voice of prayerful words from an ailing soul, deliver me from grievous things; for Thou alone art the Author of our salvation!

*Troparia:*
When the flatterer heard the voice of the words of Job's prayer, he dared not approach thee, O Theodore, but being smitten, fled from thy cave; and thou, strengthened by the power of that prayer, didst not open the doors unto anyone that came unto thee until he confessed that the Lord Jesus Christ is the Author of our salvation![^463]

Being delivered from such a pit of perdition, O Theodore, thou didst dig a deep pit, and didst cast the buried treasure into it, and covered up also the memory of the desire for possessions, saying within thyself: There is nothing so soul-harming unto monastics toward the knowledge of God as the desire for possessions.[^464]

With many labors thou didst weary thyself, O Theodore, lest being idle thou shouldest fall into fearlessness and negligence; thou didst set up millstones, and grinding wheat for the brethren, didst have unceasingly in thy mouth the prayer of Job, and so didst strengthen thyself against the invisible enemy.[^465]

*Glory:*
The adversary wishing to terrify thee, O blessed one, made a noise as of thunder, and ground at the millstones; but thou, commanding his action into servitude, didst forbid him, and he became as a captive, obeying and grinding, until he ground five cartloads of grain in a single night![^466]

*Both now: Theotokion:*
When I am in negligence, the enemy ever takes me captive, and prompts me unto unprofitable deeds; but do thou, O Mary, Mother of my God, drive him far away, and teach me to enslave the flesh unto the spirit, and to serve my God in holiness and righteousness.

*Kontakion, Tone 2:*[^467]
A good counselor wast thou shown to be unto the blessed Theodore, O God-wise Basil; thou didst deliver him by thy counsel from the deception of the devil, and didst guide him unto the light of the knowledge of God; therefore afterward thou didst receive a blessed end, being unjustly shot through the belly by the money-loving prince; and now standing before the Lord, pray unceasingly for us all!

*Ikos:*[^468]
By the exceeding multitude of your virtues, O venerable ones, ye are filled as a beautiful garden; and as roses ye have blossomed, by fasting and prayers, making all fragrant with the sweet odor of your many labors and ascetical struggles, and your monastic sweats, wherein ye passed over in good character unto endless life, and were crowned with victorious crowns; pray unceasingly for us all!

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*[^469]
When the golden image was served on the plain of Dura, the three children disregarded the godless command, and cast into the midst of the flame, being bedewed they sang: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparia:*
The blessed fathers, desiring to act prudently, revealed their thoughts one to another, and both discerned whether their thoughts were well-pleasing unto God. Emulating them, O brethren, test your thoughts by fraternal discernment, which of them are from God.[^470]

Emulating the struggles and labors of the venerable Theodore *Service to the Venerable Ones*

...let us not give our labor unto others; but even as he himself carried firewood unto his cell, so let us also labor for ourselves, if we will that the enemy entangle us not through idleness.[^471]

The demons hating the blessed one wrought mischief against him; for as much as he with great labor carried timber up the mountain, so much did they cast down again from the mountain; but while assigning labor unto the elder, they themselves endured labor and torment.[^472]

Trepidation unto the heart is unceasing prayer from a pure heart, wherewith the blessed Theodore bound the malicious spirits, and they became obedient unto him as captives, carrying timber for the rebuilding of the ancient monastery that had burned down before.[^473]

*Glory:*
Tormented and dishonored by many labors, the crafty spirits cried aloud against the blessed ones, boasting and saying: Cruel adversaries unto us are Theodore and Basil, with whom we shall not cease to war until we deliver them unto death!—not knowing that they would be the cause of a most honorable crown for them.[^474]

*Both now: Theotokion:*
Beholding me strengthened by thee, O Sovereign Lady, the demons gnash their teeth, unceasingly warring against me until they overcome; but do thou, my Protectress, deliver me not into their hands, but rescue me from their assaults!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*[^475]
Not accepting the golden likeness, the thrice-blessed youths, beholding the unchangeable and living image of God, cried aloud in the midst of the fire: Let all creation sing of the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparia:*
By the semblance of all their crafty malice, the demons began themselves to incite men, that they might destroy thee, O Theodore; therefore having hired slanderers with much reproach, they slandered thee unto the judge, O father; but thou, taking no thought for this, didst cry aloud: Let all creation sing of the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages![^476]

In the likeness of the blessed Basil, the demon came again unto a certain inhuman boyar, and slandered Theodore concerning the found treasure, saying that he wished to flee with it into another land; but that wicked one knew not that the saints possess Christ alone as their treasure, Whom they supremely exalt with praises unto ages.[^477]

In the likeness of Basil, the wicked boyar brought the demon unto the prince, who lied unto him also concerning the treasure against thee, O father, and advised that they seize thee, and by many tortures extort the gold from thee; but thou, O blessed one, fearing nothing, didst arm thyself strongly against the adversary who is estranged from God forever.

*To Theodore and Basil of the Caves*

*We bless the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit: the Lord!*
With fury, as upon a hunt, coming himself against a certain strong warrior, the prince seized thee, O blessed one; and having brought thee into his house, at first with flatteries questioned thee concerning the treasure; unto whom thou didst reply, O father: I found a treasure, and saw a multitude of golden vessels, which I buried again, as it was unprofitable for me, and I prayed unto the Lord that I might forget it forever![^478]

*Both now: Theotokion:*
I have buried the talent entrusted unto me, as a slothful servant, and in no wise remember that I must give an account concerning its increase unto my Master; therefore I pray thee, All-Hymned, All-Pure Virgin: help me to make the talent well-pleasing, that I may find my Lord merciful forever![^479]

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*[^480]
Thou Who alone art Desire, Who art Sweetness, O Word of God, Son of the Virgin, God of gods, holy and all-holy Lord: therefore do we all magnify Thee with her that bore Thee!

*Troparia:*
Desiring to be loosed from carnal bonds and to be with Christ, thou wast bound by the prince guiltlessly; and being severely tormented, thou didst torment the tormenting enemy, O most blessed Theodore![^481]

From thy flesh streams of blood flowed, and reddened the hair-shirt, wherein clad as in a most precious purple robe, Thou didst enter into the heavenly bridal chamber, where with her that bore God Thou dost magnify the Trinity!

Being suspended and choked with smoke, and then severely scorched with fire, thou wast, O blessed one; but the right hand of God preserved thee whole, so that the fire did not even touch thy hair-shirt; but standing in the flame as in a cool place, thou didst glorify Christ with her that bore Him.[^482]

Believing the demonic deception, the prince dared to go against the mystical victor over passions, Basil, and wishing to have a witness against Theodore, questioned him concerning the hidden gold; but thou without wavering didst reprove him and them that stood by, as deceived by the demon.[^483]

*Glory:*
Being heated with wine, not enduring thy reproof, the foolish prince wounded thee with an arrow, O father Basil; and thou, O blessed one, didst prophesy unto him, saying: Thou thyself shalt be wounded by the very same arrow *Service to the Venerable Ones*

...thou thyself shalt be wounded by the very same arrow, and thy word was fulfilled speedily, O blessed one![^484]

*Both now: Theotokion:*
Our enemies have armed themselves, O Sovereign Lady, and compassed us round about, shooting lustful arrows at us; but do thou, O Protectress, being a strong city, an impregnable wall, and a mighty shield, defend and preserve them that magnify thee with thy Son!

*Exapostilarion:*[^485]
Cast into prison, O blessed fathers, after many tortures, the Lord led your souls out of carnal bonds, and brought them into the unwaning Light, to confess His holy name, where rejoicing with angelic choirs, remember us also who honor your yearly memory!

*Glory, both now: Of the Feast, or this Theotokion:*
Ensnared by sin-loving nets by the adversary, and cast into the prison of heavy blindness, I see not the sun of the commandments of my Lord; but unto thee, O Pure Virgin, do I cry: Cause the Sun of repentance to dawn upon me, and warm my frozen heart with the rays of thy goodness, that I may glorify thy speedy help!

*At the Praises, Stichera, Automela, Tone 1:*[^486]
Who can worthily praise your labors and ascetical struggles, O most blessed fathers? For in bodily frames ye lived as bodiless ones, and having winged your minds toward God, ye flew up unto the heavens; and now standing before the throne of the Most High, earnestly pray to bring peace unto the world and to save our souls!

Who will not marvel at your patient endurance, O fathers, how being guiltlessly slandered by the enemy, ye endured many torments from the enraged prince, and having endured unto the end, ye invisibly conquered him that warred against you, and received the crown of victory from the hand of the Lord; from Whom pray that we also may receive this!

Blessed is the earth that drank in your blood, O noble confessors of Christ; blessed is the cave that preserves your relics as a precious treasure, lying not under a bushel, but openly in the tomb, wherein while yet in the body, in souls ye stand before the angelic choirs; and upon earth ye drive away crafty spirits, from whom preserve us also, and pray unto Christ God to save our souls![^487]

*To Theodore and Basil of the Caves*

*Glory: Tone 3:*
Come, brethren, let us praise our most blessed fathers, Basil with the marvelous Theodore; for having first renounced every passionate attachment, and striving to attain unto dispassion, they took no thought for this life, and chose for the sake of their Lord to suffer death from the money-loving prince; and appearing as victors, they conquered the warrior and adversary enemy, and are now crowned with glory and honor by the Most High Master, Whom they also unceasingly entreat to grant peace unto the world, and great mercy unto our souls!

*Both now: Of the Feast, or this Theotokion:*
O blessed Hope of the world, Virgin: forget not us that sing of thee, but pray ever for thine unprofitable servants, that we may be delivered from all perils and tribulations!

*Great Doxology, and Dismissal.*
*At the Liturgy, the service is common for Venerable-Martyrs.*[^488]
*(Ornamental baroque tailpiece) (Woodcut engraving: The Venerable Alypius the Iconographer (*Прпⷣніи Алѵпіи їконописецъ*) painting the icon of the Mother of God in his cell with an angel assisting; with middle inscription: "They saw a dove, the Holy Spirit, flying on high; an angel painted the icon of the Mother of God" (*Видѣша голубя Дух Стый горѣ лѣтающ Аггелъ списа Икону Бцы*); and lower panel depicting his dormition surrounded by the Caves brethren.)*

*Month of August, on the 17th Day:*
*Service to our venerable father Alypius the Iconographer of the Caves.*
*At Vespers, on "Lord, I have cried," Stichera, Tone 4. Podoben: "Called from on high" (Зва́нный свы́ше):*[^489]
Having the image of the glory of God ever in thy virtuous mind, O most blessed father Alypius, desiring to show thy soul beautiful unto the Triune Glory by renewal, whence having received the pigments of spiritual grace, thou wast an exceptional wonderworker; and Whose countenance thou didst depict in thy heart, with Him thou now reignest; entreat Him for us, O venerable one, to grant us peace, and the enjoyment of His glory![^490]

*To Alypius the Iconographer of the Caves*

Desiring to be a beholder of the face of God, thou didst set in thy mind, O father, ever to behold the sweetest countenance of Jesus; therefore having laid the stone of ultimate humility, washing in the baths of unceasing prayers, mingling them with streams of tears, thou didst inscribe upon the fleshy table of the heart the vision of the invisible glory of the All-Holy Trinity; standing before Whom now, pray for us, O father, that we may obtain mercy and the sweet vision of the face of God![^491]

Being inflamed with the desire to depict the images of the saints, the uncircumscribed glory of God, thou didst depict with the pigments of good deeds upon the tablets of thy heart the image of unceasing love toward the All-Pure Mother of God; Whom having served by thine art, thou wast vouchsafed wonderworking grace, and didst receive the reward of unending recompense; and now dwelling with the angels, pray for us, O venerable one, to bring peace unto the world and to save our souls![^492]

*Glory: Tone 8:*
The boastful serpent who once through the deceit of tasting dishonored the human image, is now filled with shame before thee, O father, who hast received divine nobility from God; for he could not bear to look upon the icons of thine art, which dispel the darkness with wonderworking rays, and grant the gifts of healing unto all that run unto them with faith: make us also partakers of these, on this present day celebrating thy memory, O wise Alypius![^493]

*Both now: Of the Feast.*

*At the Aposticha, Stichera of the Feast.*
*Glory: Tone 3:*
Having the spiritual depiction of thy soul luminous, O our venerable father Alypius, thou wast vouchsafed bright grace even sensibly, which the painting of thine icons gave forth, granting healing unto them that needed in diseases; therefore we pray thee, all-blessed one: correct the deformity of our souls by virtues, and heal our passionate sores with the pigments of thy prayers, even as thou didst the leper of old, that we may praise thee with hymns, O beauty of the venerable![^494]

*Both now: Of the Feast.*

*Service to the Venerable One*

*Troparion, Tone 8:*[^495]
Depicting the likeness of the saints visibly upon boards, O Alypius, truly praising their God-pleasing deeds as a sincere artist, thou didst inscribe them upon the tablets of thy heart; and therefore, adorned as an image divinely beautiful, thou wast overlaid with the grace of the priesthood as with gold, by Christ God and the Savior of our souls.

*Glory, both now: Of the Feast.*

*(Ornamental headpiece band)*

*Canon of the Feast and to the Venerable One, Tone 8. Ode 1. Heirmos:*[^496]
Let us, the people, send up a hymn unto our marvelous God, Who freed Israel from bondage, singing a song of victory and crying aloud: We sing unto Thee, the only Master!

*Troparia:*
Grant help from on high unto Thy servants, O Christ, to send up a hymn, that we may offer praise from warm love in the memory of Thy pleaser, unto Thee, the only Master!

As grace multiplied, faith flourished, and the host of venerable ones multiplied upon the Caves mountain; desiring to be a companion with them in life, the venerable one came to serve the one Christ and Master.[^497]

From thy youth, O venerable one, thou wast given over by thy parents to be instructed in icon-painting; diligently applying thyself thereto, thou didst inscribe chastity and purity in thy heart, so that through both thou wast a servant unto thy Master.[^498]

*Glory:*
Thy marvelous obedience showed thee forth, O father, as a beholder of the midday wonder; for thou didst see the image of the Sovereign Lady depicted not by human hands, and kindled from this vision with greater love for the contemplation of God, thou didst prepare thy heart as a tablet for inscribing the remembrance concerning Christ the Master.[^499]

*Both now: Theotokion:*
