O Virgin Mother, tablet having the unutterable image of the hypostasis of the Father inscribed by the Holy Spirit: transform the deformity of my evil belief into the fair beauty of good works, that I may be prepared in mind to serve Christ, my God and Master!

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*[^500]
There is none holy as the Lord, and none righteous as our God, of Whom all creation sings: there is none righteous above Thee, O Lord!

*Troparia:*
There is none like unto Thee in godheads, O Christ; for by the operation of Thy Pre-Holy Spirit Thou didst depict the icon of Thy Mother not made by hands, and a great wonder took place, whereof Thy venerable one was an eyewitness, O Lord![^501]

*To Alypius the Iconographer of the Caves*

He Who wrought wonders wonderfully: [the dove] flew out from the icon not made by hands in the likeness of a dove, which Thy venerable one seeing moving within the church, O Christ, he despised all things, and loved nothing in this world more than Thee, O Lord![^502]

Having seen the image of the glory of God, O venerable one, thou didst straightway change thy worldly manner of life, becoming a monk, and didst well inscribe the portrayal of virtue; which do thou also portray in us by thy warm prayers!

*Glory:*
From thine artistic art, O father, thou didst learn to portray spiritual actions; for having well smoothed the scroll of thy heart with streams of tears, thou didst inscribe the thought of the remembrance of God.[^503]

*Both now: Theotokion:*
Rejoice, O scroll, Bride-Unwedded Virgin, upon which by the pen of God the Father's Word was written: inscribe upon the scroll of my heart the remembrance of the dread judgment, and restrain it from evil deeds by the coming torments, I pray thee!

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 4:*[^504]
Who will not praise thee with hymns together with us, O venerable one, as a beholder of the glory of God and a most wise maker of icons? For Thou didst see the Holy Spirit in part even sensibly, and now standing before the throne of the Most High, not in a mirror, but face to face, Thou seest the unapproachable glory of God, whereof may we also be made partakers by thy prayers, O father Alypius!

*Glory, both now: Theotokion:*
O Propitiatory of the Fatherly Trinity: stand quickly before us that are gathered on this present day in thy most honorable temple, and grant unto them that glorify thee, O Virgin, to behold the glory of thy venerable ones through thy prayers!

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*[^505]
From the overshadowed mountain, O Word, of the only Theotokos, the Prophet with God-beholding vision foresaw Thee about to become incarnate, and with fear he glorified Thy power!

*Troparia:*
From the mountain Thou didst call Thy venerable one to run after Thee, O Christ, and having cast away worldly entreaties, he cleaved unto Thee with his whole soul, and continually portrayed Thy power in his mind.

As a radiant star thou didst shine forth, O father, upon the Caves mountain; for virtues from thee spread forth as rays *Service to the Venerable One*

...O blessed one, and enlighten on this day them that magnify thee; therefore we also glorify thy grace!

Not for wages, but moved by zeal, didst thou apply thyself unto thy work, and from this thou didst unsparingly distribute for fair beautification unto the churches of God, so that all, looking upon thine icons, might turn their hearts toward the love of God.[^506]

*Glory:*
A good laborer wast thou shown to be, O blessed one, of the vineyard of Christ, so that it is proper for thee to say with the Apostle: I ate not bread for naught, for my hands served me and others; therefore thou didst receive the denarius from God, and with the good servants dost behold the ever-existing glory.[^507]

*Both now: Theotokion:*
Having from the treasure of thy womb given the Denarius that ransomed the world, thou didst thereby redeem all from the ancestral debt, O Mother of God; unto thee do I flee: redeem me from spiritual creditors, that I may continually glorify thy goodness!

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*[^508]
Rising early at dawn we cry unto Thee, O Lord: Save us, for Thou art our God, besides Thee we know no other!

*Troparia:*
Raise up from despondency them that rise early with praise unto thee, O father, and by thy prayers lead up our minds from dark deeds unto the light of the contemplation of God, for we know thee as a speedy helper unto us.

The work of thy hands, O father, for the honor of the one God, thou didst set apart into a threefold division: for the need of icon-painting, for the labor of the monastery, and for alms unto the poor; and by this thou didst acquire the better, ever-desired habitation of paradise.[^509]

The day in the labor of thy hands, and the night in vigilance, prayer, and prostrations didst thou ever spend, O blessed one; and having thus put thy hand to the plow, thou wast made fit for the Kingdom of God; there remember us also by thine intercession unto God.[^510]

*Glory:*
The sun shone forth, and saw thy labors, O father; for humility, non-possessiveness, purity, patient endurance, and fasting didst thou temper with love toward all; and by this ascent thou didst ascend unto the height of the contemplation of God, and wast vouchsafed heavenly gifts.

*Both now: Theotokion:*
O noetic Dawn, who didst bring forth into the world the great Sun: illumine our mind and heart, and show us forth as children of the light who pray unto thee, O Bride-Unwedded Virgin; for thou art our enlightenment and our hope!

*To Alypius the Iconographer of the Caves*

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*[^511]
Grant me a radiant robe, Thou that deckest Thyself with light as with a garment, O greatly merciful Christ our God!

*Troparia:*
Having adorned the robe of the angelic habit with the pigments of monastic labors, thou didst ascend unto the high degree of the priesthood by the counsel of thy guide; and standing thereon as a beacon, thou didst enlighten many with the light of thy deeds.[^512]

Unto me that have defiled the robe of baptism with the mire of sin, grant compunction, O father, by thy prayers, that therein I may wash it and appear pure before the face of God; for behold, standing before thee, I sing thy praise with clay lips.

Having received the vesture of the priesthood according to the likeness of the Great Hierarch, the Son of God, thou didst enter, O father, into the inner veil, to offer sacrifice unto Him that sacrificed Himself for the sin of the whole world; by thy prayers now as by a sacrifice, reconcile also the Father the Creator concerning us!

*Glory:*
Clothed with the vesture of the priesthood, thou wast vouchsafed greater gifts, so that thou didst heal even them that were clothed with leprosy by thy prayers; heal also us that are spiritually ailing, O most blessed intercessor![^513]

*Both now: Theotokion:*
Extinguish them that have wounded us with the arrow of sin-loving passion, O All-Pure Virgin, and let the demonic hosts flee struck down with shame; for thou art an incurable wound unto the enemies!

*Kontakion, Tone 2:*[^514]
Having in thyself from youth the divine impress, thou wast given over to be instructed in icon-painting; and when the heaven-like church of the Mother of God was being painted, thou didst assist therein, and didst see the radiance of the Holy Spirit, from Whom thou didst receive the operation of wonders, so that thine icons wrought miracles; therefore we honor and call thee blessed, O marvelous Alypius!

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*[^515]
The Hebrew children in the furnace boldly trampled upon the flame, and turned the fire into dew, crying aloud: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God, forever!

*Troparia:*
Upon him that childishly trifled with doubt in the faith, the chastisement of God righteously came; for leprosy covered his face; whom thou, O father, didst heal, and he departed singing: Blessed is the God of our fathers![^516]

*Service to the Venerable One*

Unto him that confessed the sin of his unbelief, thou didst first apply the plaster of repentance, O blessed one; and when his soul was healed, his face was also healed by the anointing of thy waters; and he cries out concerning thee: Blessed is the God of our fathers![^517]

He who was previously covered with sores through the sin of smell, having despaired of the hope of salvation by the counsel of the devil: behold, he again was healed by turning unto God through thy teaching, O father; and having been delivered from both sores, he cried aloud: Blessed is the God of our fathers![^518]

*Glory:*
Marveling at thy many wonders, O venerable one, we cry aloud with one accord: Rejoice, new Elisha, who didst heal the unbelieving one of leprosy as of Naaman, and concerning thee he cries the children's song: Blessed is the God of our fathers![^519]

*Both now: Theotokion:*
Who shall correct the deformity of my soul, if not thou, O Mother of God, transforming its face into fair beauty with the pigments of thy goodness? And receiving goodly beauty, I will sing: Rejoice, thou that didst portray the human image through life-bearing painting!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*[^520]
Praise the Lord, Who was glorified upon the holy mountain and revealed the mystery of the Ever-Virgin unto Moses by fire in the bush, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparia:*
The Creator of all showed thee forth, O father, upon the Caves mountain as a fair-flowering tree unto healing; for by the fruits of thy prayers thou didst unceasingly grant healing unto all that came unto thee, and they departed healthy, supremely exalting the Lord unto all ages!

The innocence of thy heart deprived Thee not, O venerable one, of great gifts; for never didst thou become embittered against them that offended Thee, nor didst thou render evil for evil; therefore Christ also glorified Thee here and there forever.[^521]

Darkened by deadly gloom, they slandered thee before men, but God brought forth thy righteousness as the light, and thine innocence as the noonday; and they that dug a pit for thee fell into it, and all remember their judgment forever.[^522]

*We bless the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit: the Lord!*

O God One in Trinity, operating in seven gifts: by the establishment of seven icons Thou didst command them to be made not by hands, and didst show forth the venerable one possessing the seven gifts of Thy Paraclete; grant these unto us also, O All-Bountiful King, that we may supremely exalt Thee unto all ages![^523]

*To Alypius the Iconographer of the Caves*

*Both now: Theotokion:*
Having received the incorruptible robe of all gifts, arrayed in golden fringes, thou hast stood before the Life-Originating Trinity; stand therefore, lifting up thy most pure hands, and pray for us who are stripped bare of good deeds; for thou art the all-immaculate one who coverest our sins!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*[^524]
Thee, the unwedded Mother of God the Most High, who beyond understanding didst give birth by the Word unto God in truth, higher than the most pure hosts, with unceasing hymns of praise do we magnify!

*Troparia:*
Thee that didst glorify Him, O father, God glorified; for fire never overcame the icons of thy painting, but they remained whole and undamaged from the conflagration; which wonders seeing, with unceasing praises concerning thee we magnify Christ![^525]

From all sides the people assembled to see the most glorious wonder, how the fiery flame consumed all other things, yet in no wise harmed the icons of thy painting; which we also hearing, magnify with praises God Who wrought wonders in Thee![^526]

Thou didst keep innocence and didst look upon uprightness, spending thy life in all holiness; therefore God glorified thee over injustice and delivered thee from every misfortune; deliver us also from this by thy prayers, O father, that we may continually magnify thee with hymns!

*Glory:*
The city of Rostov rejoices, possessing the icon painted by the behest of God, and as with a costly treasure is delighted from all passions; kissing which, they remember how much God wrought through thee, O blessed one; honoring this wonderworking today, we also marvel, and magnify with praises.[^527]

*Both now: Theotokion:*
Having thee as a tower of strength against the face of our enemies, O Theotokos Virgin, we thy servants, being persecuted, flee unto thee, and falling down we pray: Preserve us that are in perils, for thou art a strong tower unto us against the face of the enemy, and an invincible wall![^528]

*Exapostilarion:*[^529]
He that ever preserves thee in tribulations, O venerable one, came instead of thee before thy face to paint the icon of the Mother of God, which having depicted radiantly in three hours, he gave for fair beautification unto the most honorable church for the feast; and he took thee into the heavenly temple to celebrate forever with the holy angels, where now celebrating with the voice of rejoicing, remember us also, O Alypius, most blessed father![^530]

*Service to the Venerable One*

*Glory, both now: Theotokion:*
Being the temple of the Most High created by the Daystar: raise up the temple of my soul that is fallen through sin, O Mother of God; renew it by thy visitation, and grant unto them that unslothfully offer glory unto thee, unceasingly to sing unto thee: Rejoice, temple of the Lord, created as a refuge for Christians!

*At the Praises, Stichera, Tone 4. Podoben: "As one valiant" (Ꙗ҆́кѡ до́блѧ):*[^531]
Let us the people sing after the manner of David on this day in thy memory, O all-blessed father: thou wast planted in the house of the Lord, and didst flourish in the court of the Mother of God as a fragrant tree, receiving from which the fruits of healing, we glorify our God Who glorified thee, and we earnestly pray to obtain mercy through thine intercession, and healing for our soul-passions.[^532]

The righteous shall flourish like a palm tree—in thee, O our father Alypius, this was fulfilled; for at the streams of the living water of the teachings of the God-bearing fathers, the paths of thy strivings poured forth; looking upon whose shade, we run unto the shrine of thy relics, and embracing it with love we pray: Grant that through thy prayers we may obtain peace from Christ, and direction for our life![^533]

Who can worthily praise thy labors and ascetical struggles, O venerable one? For thou didst not give sleep unto thine eyes, nor slumber unto thine eyelids, until thou didst adorn thy soul with the drops of virtues; joining the initial fathers, inscribe also into our memory the zeal of their lives, we earnestly pray, that we also may not be deprived of heavenly dwellings with them![^534]

*Glory: Tone 8:*
Having the image of the glory of God continually in thy mind, thou didst inscribe in thy soul the zeal ceaselessly to walk after the manner of our initial fathers, whose life following, in a mortal body thou wast assimilated unto the bodiless ones in holiness; with whom now standing before the throne of the Most High, pray, O venerable father, that like-mindedness be granted unto our monastery, which honors thy holy memory with love!

*Both now: Of the Feast.*

*Great Doxology, and Dismissal.*

*(Woodcut engraving: Full-page composition showing the Venerable-Martyr Kuksha (*Прпⷣный Кꙋ́кша*) and the Venerable Pimen the Faster (*Прпⷣный Пи́минъ*) standing full-length in monastic habit with nimbi, scrolls, and prayer ropes; across the Dnipro river stands the Kyiv-Pechersk Lavra under divine rays and clouds; below are two predella panels: on the left, "Saint Kuksha slain with his disciple" (*Сты́й Кꙋ́кша ᲂу҆бїе́нъ со ᲂу҆ченико́мъ*); on the right, "Saint Pimen foresaw the slaying of Kuksha" (*Сты́й Пи́минъ Прозрѣ́ ᲂу҆бїе́нїе Кꙋ́кшы*).)*[^535]

*Month of August, on the 27th Day:*
*Service to the Venerable-Martyr Kuksha and Pimen the Faster of the Caves.*[^536]

*At Vespers, on "Lord, I have cried," Stichera, Tone 2. Podoben: "When from the Tree" (Е҆гда̀ ѿ дре́ва):*[^537]
Goodly concord convenes the assemblies of the faithful piously and wisely to praise the memory of the God-bearing fathers, Kuksha the holy and Pimen the emulator of fasters, as fervent intercessors with faith *Service to the Venerable Ones*

...for them that run unto them; for they speedily intercede, ever praying, and for them that lovingly sing their holy memory.

Come now, ye loving people, let us make a voice of rejoicing and festival, proclaiming with harmonious melodious beauties; let us bring majesty unto the marvelous fathers, rendering yearly praises and honoring the day of their departure unto God, before Whom they now stand in gladness, and pray for our souls.[^538]

Offering honorable sacrifices unto God at first, O Kuksha, most blessed father, afterward thou didst offer thyself also as a living sacrifice, as an unblemished lamb, with thine honorable disciple, being cut down by the sword in the preaching of the Gospel teaching; for which thou didst suffer rejoicing, and standest before Christ ever praying for our souls![^539]

*Glory: Tone 8:*
Lifted up toward God by a unanimous desire, having fashioned the ship of your soul by virtues, O venerable fathers, ye lightly traversed the surging sea of the world, and reached the quiet harbor; where being, ye subdued carnal arrows unto the spirit, and were united unto Christ, before Whom now standing in heaven, earnestly pray for our souls!

*Both now: Theotokion, or Stavrotheotokion.*

*At the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 6.*

*Glory to the Venerable Ones: Tone 6:*
Teaching the mysteries of the divine faith unto the unbelieving Vyatichi people, O most blessed Kuksha, with thy disciple, ye reproved the deception of evil belief, and they that obeyed your teaching received holy baptism; but now those blinded by malice attempted to take away from you the sensory light, cutting off your heads with the sword; but, O most holy fathers, now standing before the unapproachable Light, unceasingly pray that our souls may be saved![^540]

*Both now: Theotokion, or Stavrotheotokion.*

*Troparion, Tone 3:*[^541]
Honorable Kuksha, who with thy blessed disciple wast honorably adorned with martyr's blood in the preaching of the Gospel; and Pimen, who didst flourish radiantly in fasting: in one day and hour ye entered into the unwaning Light, where pray ye unto the Lord, that He may grant us great mercy!

*To Kuksha and Pimen of the Caves*

*Canon to the Venerable Ones, Tone 4. Ode 1. Heirmos:*[^542]
Having traversed of old the red deep of the sea with dryshod feet, Israel conquered the power of Amalek in the wilderness by the cross-shaped hands of Moses!

*Troparia:*
Who can praise the equally-honored memory of the fathers? For their death is precious before Thee, as of Thy friends, O God; yet according as I have received, I offer praise unto them, and I pray Thee, O Good One: remove hindrances far from me, and strengthen me by Thy power![^543]

As sacred ones with the disciple, O sacred father, ye carried the name of Christ before the unbelievers, whom having astonished with signs and wonders, ye brought unto the knowledge of God; that I also may be a vessel of honor unto my Master, help me by your prayers, O fathers!

*Glory:*
Daring today to honor the choir of the three: Kuksha with his disciple, and Pimen the clairvoyant, with praises, I pray: stand before the Holy Trinity praying for me, that being delivered from three enemies—the world, the flesh, and the devil—I may appear as a good warrior, having conquered them![^544]

*Both now: Theotokion:*
O thou who didst deliver mankind from the tyranny of the enemy by thy most pure childbearing: deliver me from impure thoughts, I pray thee, and save me, as the Mother of the God Who loves mankind!

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*[^545]
Thy Church rejoices in Thee, O Christ, crying aloud: Thou art my strength, O Lord, and refuge, and establishment!

*Troparia:*
The Great Lavra of the Caves rejoices, having nurtured you in her bosom, O venerable fathers, and cries with love: For the sake of Kuksha and Pimen, O my Christ, be Thou my refuge and establishment in sorrow![^546]

Unto thy many virtues, O father, many gifts from on high followed; for what is greater than to sanctify an unbeliever into the faith? This in truth is greater than raising the dead.[^547]

*Glory:*
Coming as an apostle unto the unbelieving people, thou didst astonish them with signs and wonders, and having thereby turned many unto God, O Kuksha, thou didst bring them from darkness unto light; establish me who am unstable, O venerable one, by thy prayers!

*Service to the Venerable Ones*

*Both now: Theotokion:*
O glory of the Apostles and beauty of the Martyrs: deprive me not of a portion of heavenly glory, and make me a partaker of thy beauty through the intercession of Pimen and Kuksha, O Virgin Mother and Maiden!

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 2:*[^548]
Receiving the gifts of Thy love, O Christ, the fathers offered them as a fragrant sacrifice unto Thee: Pimen purity and abstinence, as an acceptable olive; and Kuksha the blood poured out for the Gospel's sake, as a cluster of the vine; therefore they were worthily vouchsafed heavenly honor, as good ascetics!

*Glory, both now: Theotokion:*
Behold, I have not offered love unto Thee, O my God, therefore I feel myself unworthy to find mercy from Thee; but for the sake of Thy goodness, O my Christ, and through the intercession of Thy most pure Mother, have mercy and save me!

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*[^549]
The Church, beholding Thee, the Sun of righteousness, lifted up upon the Cross, stood in her rank, worthily crying aloud: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!

*Troparia:*
Rousing yourselves in battle against the ruler of the world, ye set yourselves in array, O all-blessed fathers, and drove the enemies far from men, and by your wonderworking ye brought many unto Christ, unto Whom we sing: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!

Bedewed from on high with the drops of the grace of God, O hieromartyr Kuksha, thou didst bring down rain-bearing clouds, and they poured down abundant drops upon the earth; by the drops of thy prayers bedew also us that sing: Glory to Thy power, O Lord![^550]

*Glory:*
O Pimen, seer by the Spirit of God of human deeds that take place far off: bring unto our remembrance the secret deeds of our hearts that have remained in forgetfulness; for though Thou art far off now, yet by the grace of God Thou canst intercede for them that call thee unto help and sing: Glory to Thy power, O Lord![^551]

*Both now: Theotokion:*
Be Thou a speedy visitation unto me in sorrow, O All-Pure Virgin; for thou didst bear Joy beyond nature, the Dayspring from on high having visited us, enlightened by Whom, we offer Rejoice unto thee!

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*[^552]
Thou, O Lord my Light, art come into the world, a holy Light turning from the darkness of ignorance them that sing of Thee in faith!

*Troparia:*
Thou, O venerable Kuksha, didst dry up the lake of evil belief, and its turbid waters dried up, when the rays of true faith shone forth among the Vyatichi; stay the streams of malice by thy prayers for us also, who sing of thee in faith![^553]

*To Kuksha and Pimen of the Caves*

Even as thou didst heal the sick by thy word, so also visit us by the grace given thee from God, and give rest in spiritual ailments unto them that lovingly sing of thy memory, O Pimen![^554]

*Glory:*
Let us the people give glory unto God Who is in the highest, Who glorified His saints, and let us pray that through their prayers we also may not be deprived of heavenly glory, joyfully singing of their memory!

*Both now: Theotokion:*
Who can recount thy glory, O Pure Virgin? For the heavenly hosts glorify it, and we the earthly, falling down, glorify thee and magnify thee, singing: Rejoice, pure glory of the Orthodox!

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*[^555]
I will sacrifice unto Thee with the voice of praise, O Lord, the Church cries unto Thee, having been cleansed from the blood of demons by the blood that flowed for mercy's sake from Thy side!

*Troparia:*
Thou didst rescue the unbelieving people about to be swallowed by the noetic sea-monster, O father, through holy baptism; and having built a church from them, thou didst cleanse them from demonic sacrifices through the blood that flowed by thy prayers![^556]

They shall lay hands on the sick, and they shall recover, as the Scripture said; so according to the power given thee from Christ, by thy word thou didst heal the ailing brethren, who departed glorifying Him Who gave thee such authority; therefore visit also us that are sick in soul, and heal us by thy word, O most blessed Pimen![^557]

*Glory:*
Who can worthily praise you, O blessed fathers, who labored much here? For ye are praised in heavenly glory, and rejoice upon your beds in the ever-existing light; pray that we also may be with you, having been cleansed by the blood that flowed from the most pure side of the Savior!

*Both now: Theotokion:*
All Christian generations praise and call thee blessed, and cry with love: O Sovereign Lady, stand before us who are submerged in the mire of foul deeds; for though we have defiled our souls, yet thou canst wash us with the precious blood shed by thy Son!

*Service to the Venerable Ones*

*Kontakion, Tone 3:*[^558]
Gathering together, O ye people, let us sing with praises of the lights of the world; for ye shone forth in this world: Pimen by many ascetical struggles, and Kuksha with the disciple by the preaching of the Word; and these three, standing with one mind before the Holy Trinity, shine as the sun and enlighten them that praise their memory with love!

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*[^559]
In the Persian furnace, Abraham's children, kindled by love of piety rather than by the flame, cried aloud: Blessed art Thou in the temple of Thy glory, O Lord!

*Troparia:*
As a like-minded assembly, O holy fathers, though standing far apart in body, yet united in spirit ye ran unto the Lord, crying together: Blessed art Thou in the temple of Thy glory, O Lord!

Standing far off, O blessed Kuksha, Pimen foresaw thy beheading with the disciple by the unbelieving Vyatichi, and with a loud voice in the midst of the Caves church he cried: Our brother Kuksha has been slain this day, at the hour of the sacrifice being offered: Glory to Thy power, O Lord![^560]

*Glory:*
Having foretold by the Holy Spirit the slaying of Kuksha and the disciple by the unbelievers, he also surrendered his spirit into the hands of God; having received them, for the sake of the venerable ones, receive also our spirits into Thy glory, O Lord![^561]

*Both now: Theotokion:*
O thou who didst conceive in thy womb by the descent of the Holy Spirit: in the hour when we are to surrender our spirits unto the Lord and stand before His judgment seat, stand by through thine intercession and entreat His goodness concerning us, for nothing is impossible unto thine intercession!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*[^562]
Daniel, stretching out his hands, stopped the mouths of the lions in the pit; and the youths, lovers of piety, girded with virtue, quenched the power of the fire, crying aloud: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Troparia:*
Stretching out your hands unto God, pray earnestly for us who sing of your memory, O God-bearing fathers, that through your intercession we may be found in the ever-existing light to behold the Lord!

Thou, O hieromartyr Kuksha, having with thine honorable disciple offered thy blood as mingled wine: pray that we may be delivered from the assault of enemies that seek our blood, and in peace henceforth bless the Lord!

*We bless the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit: the Lord!*

O God-wise Pimen, seeing far off and foretelling things to come as a prophet of God: [deliver us] from the evils coming upon us for our transgressions, by thy speedy prayer unto the Master, that we may worthily bless the Lord in the heavenly dwellings unto all ages![^563]

*To Kuksha and Pimen of the Caves*

*Both now: Theotokion:*
Truly did the venerable one of God foresee thee, that thou art a helper unto people living in the world; we pray thee, O Sovereign Lady: help also us the infirm, that girded with power from thee, we may be able to bless the Lord unto all ages!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*[^564]
The Cornerstone, Christ, was cut out without hands from thee, the unhewn mountain, O Virgin, uniting disparate natures; therefore rejoicing, we magnify thee, O Mother of God!

*Troparia:*
Christ preached by thee unto the disciples, O Kuksha, became a stone of stumbling unto the unbelievers, for they reckoned thy reproof of their evil belief as an offense and vexation; for this cause those senseless ones, falling upon thee with thy disciple, cut off your heads with the sword; therefore we magnify you together, as having shed your blood for Christ![^565]

Thou, O most blessed Pimen, having established thyself by patience upon Christ the Rock, didst pass without stumbling through the seductive nets of the stumbling enemy, or rather didst tear them asunder like a cobweb, and having passed unto heavenly things, thou dost enjoy eternal glory; therefore we magnify thee![^566]

*Glory:*
United in spirit, dwelling in the heavenly habitations, look down upon us below, O venerable fathers; for we are in perils from the cruel wolf; pray that we may be delivered from him, as we magnify your memory with hymns!

*Both now: Theotokion:*
O thou who didst receive the mystery of the Unwaning Light and didst reveal the incarnate Word of God unto the world, Mary, Bride of God and Intercessor: cleanse me from my secret faults, that I be not exposed before angels and men on that day when thy Son shall come to judge the world![^567]

*Exapostilarion:*[^568]
Today an auspicious time is shown unto us, wherein the holy cave sings of the God-bearing ones, in which we also, having gathered together, sing of Kuksha with his glorious disciple, and of Pimen, and bowing down, we praise the one God in Trinity!

*Glory, both now: Theotokion:*
O unwedded Virgin, who in time didst bear Him Who placed times and seasons in His own authority: order aright by the prayers of the venerable ones our years that are woven like a cobweb, that we may glorify Him Who was born of thee, and lovingly magnify thee![^569]

*Service to the Venerable Ones*

*At the Aposticha, Stichera from the Octoechos, and to the Venerable Ones, Tone 4:*

*Verse: Many are the afflictions of the righteous, and out of all of them the Lord will deliver them.*[^570]
By a good fight, O glorious Pimen, and by martyr's blood, O most blessed Kuksha, with the disciple that followed thee, ye were enrolled in the army of the Heavenly King, and became mighty armor-bearers in the noetic warfare; for by a firm battle array ye cast them down utterly, and receiving crowns as victors, ye rejoice in the heavenly habitations; whence look down upon us who honor your holy memory![^571]

*Glory: Tone 6:*
Wise disciples were ye shown to be of the most wise Teacher, Christ our God; for ye fulfilled the Word by deed: in this world ye despised your souls, took no care for the body, cast away every earthly attachment, set yourselves in array against the ruler of the world, and led by the Spirit ye conquered carnal passions—O Kuksha, being cut down with the sword together with the disciple, and O Pimen, who by fasting labors didst cast down the enemy's strength! But, O all-blessed fathers, help us by your prayers to cast down the adversary that continually wars against us, and entreat great mercy for our souls!

*Both now: Theotokion, or Stavrotheotokion, and Dismissal.*

*(Ornamental baroque tailpiece) Troparion, Tone 4:*[^572]
Let us on this day honor the noetic sun and the pair of lights of the initial Caves fathers, with the whole assembly of the venerable ones; for they firmly establish the Church, enlighten them that are in the darkness of passions, and through their prayers bestow help from Christ God in all tribulations, and entreat deliverance for our souls!

*(Woodcut engraving: Monumental assembly of the Kyiv Caves Venerable Fathers (*Собо́ръ Преподо́бныхъ Ѻ҆тє́цъ Пече́рскихъ*); above, the Mother of God Great Panagia with the Christ Child in an oval mandorla cloud surrounded by cherubim; in the center, the Great Dormition Church of the Kyiv-Pechersk Lavra with cupolas; below, the holy assembly of all the Caves ascetics with nimbi, led in front by Saint Anthony on the left and Saint Theodosius on the right holding inscribed monastic scrolls, surrounded by hierarchs, hieromonks, and monastics, enclosed within an ornate foliate border.)*[^573]

*Kontakion, Tone 8:*[^574]
O chosen pleasers of God from our race, holy venerable ones of the Caves, who flourished with virtues upon these hills: the earth did not conceal you, but heaven and the habitation of paradise were opened unto you; therefore in your memory we offer songs of praise unto God Who glorified you; and do ye, as having boldness, defend by your prayers from all perils them that honor your assembly, as our protectors and intercessors unto God!

*Troparion, Tone 8:*[^575]
Having ascended unto virtue, having from youth loved the monastic life, having valiantly attained unto thy desire, thou didst settle in a cave; and having adorned thy life with fasting and brightness, in prayers thou didst abide as bodiless, having shone forth as a radiant light in the land of Rus: O father Theodosius, pray unto Christ God to save our souls!

*(Woodcut engraving: Full-page hagiographical icon of Venerable Theodosius of the Caves (*Прпⷣный Ѳеѡдо́сїй Пече́рскїй*) standing in monastic habit with schema and open scroll; flanked by four roundels: "His mother urges him to desist" (*Мт҃и є҆го̀ ᲂу҆вѣщава́етъ є҆го̀ да ѡ҆ста́вится*); "Angels brought gold to the laborers" (*А҆́гг҃ли ноша́хꙋ трꙋжда́ющуся зла́то*); "Before his death he instructs the brethren" (*Пред̾ сме́ртїю наказу́етъ бра́тїю*); "Departure of Saint Theodosius" (*Ѿхо́дъ ст҃а́гѡ Ѳеодо́сїа*); and a broad lower predella panel depicting the Translation of his relics (*Перенесе́нїе тѣлесѐ прпⷣнагѡ ѻ҆ц҃а̀ на́шегѡ Ѳеодо́сїа Пече́рскагѡ Кїевскагѡ*).)*[^576]

*Kontakion, Tone 3:*[^577]
Let us today honor the star of Rus, which shone forth from the East and came unto the West, and enriched all this land with wonders and goodness, and by the establishment and grace of the monastic rule of all of them, the blessed Theodosius!

*Month of August, on the 28th Day:*
*Service to the Venerable Fathers of the Caves whose incorrupt relics repose in the Far Cave.*[^578]

*At Vespers, "Blessed is the man," 1st Antiphon.*[^579]

*At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera, Tone 8. Podoben: "O most marvelous wonder" (Ѡ҆ пресла́внагѡ чꙋдесѐ):*[^580]
The God-wise Theodosius, voivode of the Good King, gathered a regiment of spiritual warriors, the venerable fathers; whose ascetical struggles blessing, let us offer them songs of praise, and cry aloud, saying: As ye have boldness unto the Master of all and God, ask great mercy for our souls, and pacification for all the world![^581]

Having spiritual wings, the spiritual warriors, all-blessed fathers, come from heaven and hasten unto our help, setting themselves in array against the ruler of the world; unto whom let us fall down, crying: We call upon you, our heaven-coursing defenders: hasten to deliver us from our encroaching enemies, and to give us deliverance from sorrows!

A marvelous vision is seen by noetic eyes: like fiery warriors surrounding thy flock *Service to the Venerable Fathers of the Caves*

...like fiery warriors surrounding thy flock; for they ever conspire to destroy it by evil incursions; but do thou, as one speedy in help, grant defense unto them that flee unto thee and ask thy deliverance, and withhold not thy visitation in sorrows!

*Glory: Tone 8:*
Standing before the thrice-sunlit throne of the Godhead, and richly illumined with unapproachable splendors, O our all-blessed fathers: deliver from passions and perils them that lovingly stand upon the earth and sing of your assembly on this day with voices, as our continual intercessors unto God and supplicants for our souls!

*Both now: Theotokion:*
*"The Heavenly King"* (Dogmatikon, Tone 8).[^582]

*Then the Entrance, Prokeimenon of the day, and three readings for the venerable ones (seek at the beginning of the book on fol. 4 on the reverse).*[^583]

*At the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 2. Podoben: "O House of Ephratha" (До́ме є҆ѵфра́ѳовъ):*[^584]
God-planted laborers, our venerable Caves fathers: ye planted a spiritual vineyard of virtues upon earth, from which a worthy fruit of eternal life is rendered unto you!

*Verse: The venerable ones shall boast in glory, and shall rejoice upon their beds.*[^585]
Ye sowed tears upon the mountain, that abundant fruit, even joy, might flourish in the courts of the Lord; enjoying which forever, pray for us unto the Lord![^586]

*Verse: Blessed are all they that fear the Lord, that walk in His ways.*[^587]
Desiring to be delivered from worldly turmoils, ye settled in the cave, where having lived laboriously, ye entered into eternal rest: pray there for us unto Christ the Master![^588]

*Glory: Tone 1:*
Having escaped the wave-like turmoils of life, and having reached the calm stillness of the holy cave, and having laboriously pleased the Lord therein, ye are now citizens of the Zion on high, and are become sons of the Kingdom of God; pray, O God-bearing fathers, that they who ever honor your assembly with love may obtain a portion of your glory!

*Both now: Theotokion:*
Joy of the heavenly ranks, mighty protection for men upon earth, O All-Pure Virgin: save us who flee unto thee, for after God we have placed our hope in thee, O Mother of God!

*Of the Far Cave*

*Troparion, Tone 4:*[^589]
Let us on this day honor the noetic sun and the pair of lights of the initial Caves fathers, with the whole assembly of the venerable ones; for they firmly establish the Church, enlighten them that are in the darkness of passions, and through their prayers bestow help from Christ God in all tribulations, and entreat deliverance for our souls!

*Glory, both now: Theotokion:*
*"The mystery hidden from the ages..."* (Theotokion Dismissal Hymn, Tone 4).[^590]

*(Ornamental divider)*

*At Matins, on "God is the Lord," the same Troparion and Theotokion.*

*After Kathisma 1, Sessional Hymn, Tone 4. Podoben: "Thou hast appeared" (Ꙗ҆ви́лся є҆сѝ):*[^591]
Through many tribulations ye traversed the path of this life, and having reached the summit of spiritual desire, ye settled in the bridal chamber of divine glory; and now rejoicing with the King of all and God, pray that we also who honor your assembly may be made partakers of your gladness, O all-blessed fathers!

*Glory, both now: Theotokion:*
O full of grace, intercede by thy prayers, and ask for our souls an abundance of compassions, and cleansing of transgressions, for all that sing of thee as the pure Mother and Virgin!

*After Kathisma 2, Sessional Hymn, Tone 3:*[^592]
Having heard of the marriage of the Lamb in the heavenly chamber, and the calling of the Heavenly King being addressed unto all, ye left behind all worldly attachments, O God-bearing fathers; and having cleansed your robes with streams of tears, ye entered in unto the banquet of Christ God; but before the doors of that entrance be shut, pray that we also, having entered through repentance, may be made partakers with you![^593]

*Glory, both now: Theotokion:*
Seeing continually the bridal chamber adorned with ever-existing glory, we desire to enter together into it, but not having a garment, we are ashamed lest we be bound by the angels and cast out; do thou who art the garment of boldness for the naked, O Virgin Mary, adorn us, and lead us into the Kingdom of Christ![^594]

*Then "Praise ye the name of the Lord" (Polyeleos) and the Magnification:*[^595]
We magnify you, O our venerable Caves fathers, and we honor your holy memory; for ye pray for us unto Christ our God!

*Verse 1: Rejoice in the Lord, O ye righteous: praise is seemly for the upright.*[^596]

*Service to the Venerable Ones of the Caves*

*Verse 2: They that sow in tears shall reap in joy.*[^597]
*Verse 3: The venerable ones shall boast in glory, and shall rejoice upon their beds.*[^598]
*Verse 4: Be glad in the Lord, and rejoice, ye righteous: and boast, all ye that are upright of heart.*[^599]
*Glory, both now: And Alleluia, thrice.*
*After the Polyeleos, Sessional Hymn, Tone 8. Podoben: "Of Wisdom" (Премꙋ́дрости):*[^600]
Following the calling of your Lord, taking up the Cross as a staff into your hand, ye were not afraid of the terror that stands before in darkness, but by hope ye passed unto a tranquil haven, and having found the recompense of your ascetic deeds, ye rejoice in eternity that passes not away: therefore we honor you, O all-blessed fathers, as ever praying Christ God to grant remission of transgressions unto them that lovingly celebrate your holy memory![^601]
*Glory, both now: Theotokion:*
Having thee, O Virgin unwedded, as the only blessed one among women, and the boast and adornment of Adam, an invincible help in battles, the establishment of the infirm, and a speedy visitation unto them that are in sorrows: on this day in thy most honorable temple we fall down, and our supplications do we bring unto thee; that thou mayest pray Christ God to grant remission of transgressions unto them that piously worship thy most holy Offspring![^602]
*Gradual, the 1st Antiphon of Tone 4:*
*"From my youth..."*[^603]
*Prokeimenon:*
*Precious in the sight of the Lord is the death of His venerable ones.*[^604]
*Verse:*
*What shall I render unto the Lord for all that He hath rendered unto me?*[^605]
*Let every breath praise the Lord.*
*Verse:*
*Praise God in His saints.*[^606]
*Gospel according to Matthew, Pericope 43:*[^607]
*The Lord said unto His disciples: All things are delivered unto Me of My Father...* (Matt. 11:27–30).[^608]
*After Psalm 50, Sticheron:*
*"Standing before the throne of the thrice-sunlit Godhead..." (seek at "Lord, I have cried").*[^609]
*(Ornamental divider)*
*Canon to the Mother of God, with the Heirmos, to 6;*
*And to the Venerable Ones, to 8.*
*Tone 8. Ode 1. Heirmos:*
*Having traversed the water as dry land, and having escaped the Egyptian evil, the Israelite cried aloud: Let us sing unto our Deliverer and God!*[^610]
*Troparia:*
*To the Venerable Fathers of the Far Caves:*
Wishing to cross the water of hymnographic words in your memory, O God-bearing fathers, direct me ever in stillness of mind, and restrain me from perilous passions, that in your praise with singing I may magnify the name of my God![^611]

*Of the Far Cave*

Having gone forth into the grassy field of the spiritual paradise of the cave, O fathers, I am sweetened by the fragrant spices which your relics pour forth, O venerable fathers, and joyfully do I chant a hymn in my mind unto God the Creator![^612]
A garden enclosed art thou, O holy cave; for in thee the Planter on high planted a multitude of trees, the God-bearing fathers, like cedars of Lebanon, which have reached unto the height of heaven; marveling at whom, I sing a hymn unto my God![^613]
I give thanks unto the watchful guard Longinus,[^614] who had the gift to see beforehand the thoughts of them that enter, and who obtained the prize of the high calling: enter and accept praises from the God-bearing fathers, in whose memory we sing a hymn unto our God!
O Ignatius,[^615] shepherd of monastics and healer of the sick: help us who honor thee in our infirmities, that in thy memory we may offer a hymn of praise unto our God!
*Glory:*
O thou who ever didst quench distress,[^616] lover of purity and keeper of the gardens: quench with thy prayer, O gracious one, them that wish to steal both the sensory and noetic fruits; and save us also from their assaults, as we pray!
*Both now: Theotokion:*
Encompassed by many thieves, I perceive them not ever, and the enemies rob me of the fruits of repentance; but by thy watchful prayer and immeasurable goodness, O Mother of God, deliver me, I pray!
*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
*O Lord, Creator of the celestial vault and Builder of the Church: do Thou establish me in Thy love, O summit of desires, confirmation of the faithful, O only Lover of mankind!*[^617]
*Troparia:*
The Creator of the heavenly and earthly circle created this place, and placed you therein as good citizens, O all-blessed fathers: number us into your inextinguishable habitation, and pray that we may reach the desired summit of mercy, we pray you!
O blessed Agathon,[^618] renowned for the sweetness of speech, beauty of fasters, unerring prophet, and healer of the infirm: tell us who are ignorant whether there be a way of iniquity in us, and do thou *Service to the Venerable Ones of the Caves*

—and do thou guide us by thy prayers unto the everlasting way!
We sing of the daily Zeno,[^619] radiant with fasting, and the blessed namesake Makary;[^620] for the sake of these two fathers, prayerful and healing from passions, O only Lover of mankind!
The evil one relaxed against the servant of sacrifice,[^621] and as a true faster we bring him unto Thee in prayer, O Christ: for his sake deliver us from the smiting of belly-passions, and teach us his temperance, O only Lover of mankind!
O Hypatius,[^622] servant unto the infirm, who hadst the gift to heal the sick by the laying on of hands: we pray thee, visit from on high us who are held fast by spiritual infirmities, and save us by thy prayers!
As ye were lovingly bound together by a union in this life, and after death were laid together in a single tomb, even so now living together in the incorruptible life, O Paphnutius and Mercurius:[^623] by your mediation unto God, establish us in oneness of mind and love, we pray!
*Glory:*
O Lawrence,[^624] beacon unto fasters, and Moses,[^625] God-bearer: enlighten us who are in the darkness of passions and walk therein, and guide us by your prayers, that in our God-bearing path we may not dash our feet against a stone of stumbling![^626]
*Both now: Theotokion:*
The darkness of passions hath encompassed us, and we have turned aside into impassable paths of pleasures, and see not the light of correction; O instructress, guide us, and make the light of repentance to shine upon us: for thou art unto all an all-radiant beacon that enlightens our souls!
*Sessional Hymn, Tone 2:*[^627]
Who is able to praise the pure life and labors of the venerable Caves fathers, who blossomed through fasting and radiant ascetic deeds? Yet out of love toward them do we offer hymnographic gifts: for standing before God with the angels, they ever pray unto the Lord for all of us!
*Glory, both now: Theotokion:*
Being a fountain of a multitude of mercies, O Mother of God, look down mercifully upon a people that hath sinned; show forth as ever thy power: for hoping in thee, we cry aloud unto thee: Rejoice! as once did Gabriel, the archistrategos of the bodiless hosts!

*Of the Far Cave*

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
*I have heard, O Lord, the mystery of Thy dispensation; I have understood Thy works, and I have glorified Thy Godhead.*[^628]
*Troparia:*
Following the steps of the ancient fathers, ye turned aside not even a little from their labors; therefore receiving on this day from Christ equal rewards of eternal life, with the angels ye glorify His eternal Godhead!
Marvelous Hilarion,[^629] who hadst the rank of the Great Schema, and according to thy rank zealously imitating the life of the venerable Theodosius, eating only once in the week, wast strong to sing in doxology: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!
Ascetic army of the fatherland, in which Dionysius,[^630] the radiant star, grew forth, who now shines in the glory of the saints, and glorifies the one Godhead!
Arsenius,[^631] an evident model for the industrious, namesake unto courage, who lived in labors day and night: he received as recompense the gift of wonderworking, from which he abundantly bestows upon all that have need!
O Poemen,[^632] of one mind with fasters, who didst labor with painstaking labors in the obedience of the brethren, carrying firewood, grinding at the millstones, every night unceasingly singing unto God and His one Godhead!
O unshakeable pillar, Athanasius the Recluse:[^633] being desirable in seclusion, thou didst not need sensory brightness, since the spiritual brightness illumined thee; by it, O blessed one, pray that they who honor thee may be enlightened!
*Glory:*
Glory to Thee, O Most Holy Trinity, Who hast glorified an earthly place, like unto the heavenly citizens; in whose memory, by the singing offered by us, we sinners praise Thee, O Father, Son, and Holy Spirit, with slavish veneration, and we extol Thy one Godhead!
*Both now: Theotokion:*
Glory to thee, O pure boast of the Orthodox: the human race with the angels doth glorify thee, and falling down cries aloud: for thy glory, O Mother of God, hath extended unto all for salvation!
*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
*Enlighten us by Thy commandments, O Lord, and by Thy high arm grant Thy peace unto us, O Lover of mankind!*[^634]
*Troparia:*
They have enlightened all the Russian land by the grace given unto them, *Service to the Venerable Ones of the Caves*

—by the grace given unto them, O all-blessed fathers, and the darkness of evil belief hath now utterly perished; extend their rays upon us for the sake of their supplications, we pray Thee, O only Lover of mankind!
O marvelous Sisoes,[^635] and Gregory,[^636] who hadst a name of vigilance, both having bridled your passions by fasting: calm the raging lust of our flesh also; for grace hath been given unto you to help us in passions!
O lover of temperance, blessed Paul,[^637] with the two canonarchs:[^638] offer prayers for us unto the Lord, that by His grace we may be restrained from gluttony of the belly, and may become heirs with you in temperance!
The Word of God, Who makes men wise, taught thee, O father Nestor,[^639] not unlettered wisdom, but that which is on high, whose angels thou didst behold during prayers, and didst foresee thine end; make us also partakers thereof, we pray thee, as we honor thy memory!
All-valiant Titus,[^640] having once served as a warrior, the adversaries smote thy head with a weapon, and the wound became the cause whereby thou camest unto the Caves mount, and didst weep there for thy sins; concerning which, having received assurance of forgiveness, thou didst depart joyfully unto the heavenly mansions on high, O venerable one!
I dare to honor the memory of the obedient one,[^641] and I marvel: for he endured much for the sake of obedience from the unbelievers and for the faith in bonds; yet caught up by angels and transported, he was found in his cell, and upon him was fulfilled that obedience delivers from death!
*Glory:*
I have fallen into the hands of spiritual enemies, and am bound by them with heavy bonds, from which a brother shall not deliver me;[^642] Thou, O Most Holy Trinity, as having shown compassion, remember Thy creation, and by Thy goodness free me from mortal bonds!
*Both now: Theotokion:*
I am bound with many cords of passions, and have no hope of release; but do thou have mercy upon me, as the Mother of the God on high, and loose me from them: for after God thou alone hast power to loose the cords of sinners!
*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
*I will pour out my prayer unto the Lord, and unto Him will I declare my sorrows; for my soul is filled with evils, and my life hath drawn near unto hades, and like Jonah I pray: Lead me up from corruption, O God!*[^643]

*Of the Far Cave*

*Troparia:*
Our prayers do we offer unto you, O God-bearing fathers, for ye are intercessors and supplicants for us unto God: therefore by your mediation deliver from the corruption of sin them that honor your honorable assembly!
Having a guileless spiritual vision toward God, ye were guileless as infants, yet ye had an eye against demons, so that they tremble even at your name; we pray you, let our prayer cause the assaults of the enemy to turn back with shame from us!
O Zechariah,[^644] namesake unto the remembrance of the Lord, who according to thy name didst also acquire thy life: for having valiantly armed thyself against enemies by fasting, so that in all thy life thou wast satisfied being fed with herbs; therefore, driving away demonic actions, hasten unto our help by thy name, we humbly pray thee!
Blessed Theodore,[^645] who didst account the glory of thy nobility as dust, exchanging thy princely rule for the monastic habit, wherein having well pleased thy God, and now living with the angels in the heavens: pray for us, that we may become partakers of incorruptible glory!
Venerable Sophronius,[^646] who didst enclose thyself in a dark place, for unceasingly in psalms didst thou sing unto God; and now enjoying angelic singing in the spacious mansion on high, pray that we also may be vouchsafed to hear the same!
In holiness and righteousness of priesthood unto the Highest, O marvelous Pancratius,[^647] thou wast enriched with wonderworking gifts, from which thou didst abundantly bestow upon them that had need; grant these therefore also unto us who fervently come unto thee in sicknesses!
*Glory:*
For the sake of the glory of Thy name, O supersubstantial Trinity, Thou hast glorified Thy venerable ones and this place; grant therefore of Thy goodness also unto us, O Good One, to see the glory of the saints in Thy glory, and with them to glorify Thee, the one God in three Hypostases!
*Both now: Theotokion:*
He that glorifies thee not, O All-Pure Virgin, let him not see the glory of eternal life; but grant unto us to see it, for we know thee to be glorified by all *Service to the Venerable Ones of the Caves*

—of all the heavenly hosts, and this do we offer unto thee: Rejoice, thou known boast of all!
*Kontakion, Tone 8:*[^648]
O chosen servants of God from among generations, venerable Caves fathers, who have shone forth in virtues upon these mountains: the earth hath not concealed you, but heaven and the paradise habitation have opened unto you! Therefore we offer songs of praise in your memory unto God Who hath glorified you: and do ye, as having boldness, protect by your prayers them that honor your assembly from all perils, as our defenders and intercessors unto God!
*Ikos:*[^649]
Who is able to praise Thy saints one by one, O Good One? If I should number them, they would be multiplied more than the sand; but do Thou Thyself, O Master Christ, Who countest the multitude of the stars, and callest them all by name: accept all their supplications for us, and manifest Thy compassions upon Thy people; for we know that they have great boldness toward Thy majesty, and intercede unto Thee as our continual supplicants for our souls!
*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
*The Hebrew youths in Babylon once trampled the flame of the furnace with faith in the Trinity, singing: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!*[^650]
*Troparia:*
Thou art fertile and well-watered, O mount of the Caves, for among thy citizens the Highest was well-pleased to place His habitation; therefore we also sing with them: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!
Filled with myrrh and like unto the hill of Lebanon art thou, O mount of the Caves, trickling with the myrrh of the God-bearing fathers for the healing of corrupting sicknesses: receiving whose beneficence, we sing: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!
O Ammon and Mardarius,[^651] who became an active model of lovers of poverty, and have now acquired imperishable wealth: pray that we also may acquire the same in the realms on high, where they sing: Blessed art Thou, O God!
We sing of Rufus,[^652] the mirror for fasters and the industrious, with blessed Benjamin, who gave away all his possessions unto the poor that he might singly gain Christ: whose imitators may we be, singing: Blessed art Thou, O God!
O Theophilus,[^653] adornment of hierarchs, who held the see of Novgorod and beheld the Lord Himself: entreat His goodness, that we also *Of the Far Cave*

—that we also may behold His face with thee, and sing: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!
O beacon among deacons and model among the industrious, Martyrius,[^654] lover of purity, who hadst the gift to drive away evil spirits from men and to heal infirmities: drive away from us the spirit of malice and of all deceit, that we may purely sing: Blessed art Thou, O God!
*Glory:*
O Most Holy Trinity, Who hast most gloriously glorified Thy saints with the glory which Thou preparedst from the age for Thy servants: make us also worthy to sing: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!
*Both now: Theotokion:*
O Mother and Virgin, full of exceeding joy: make them that offer joyful hymns unto thee worthy of eternal joy, and open the gates of paradise unto all Christians as they ever sing unto thee: Blessed art thou, O Mother of God!
*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
*The King of heaven, Whom the angelic hosts sing of: praise and highly exalt Him unto all ages!*[^655]
*Troparia:*
A dwelling of the Queen of heaven art thou, O sheltered mountain, which covers a multitude of saved venerable fathers; who, all having cleansed weapons, even prayers against the ruler of the world, sing a hymn unto God: Let us sing and highly exalt Him unto all ages!
The North sees in thine enclosure, O mount of the Caves, a fountain welling with the myrrh of miracles of the God-bearing fathers, poured out abundantly unto all, who sing and highly exalt the Lord unto all ages!
What praise shall we bring unto you, O God-bearing fathers? How shall we declare your labors and ascetic deeds with our poor tongue? We know not. But do thou, O silent Evagrius,[^656] teach us, that with thee we may sing of the Lord unto all ages!
Radiance of fasters and of the industrious, O Cassian and blessed Pior,[^657] both shining by your life like celestial beacons: endow us with the brightness of the grace given unto you, that we may be vouchsafed to sing with you of the Lord unto all ages!
Ever remembering the beatitude of them that mourn, O Paphnutius,[^658] thou didst ever weep; and now having inherited joyful places, pray that we also may obtain that weeping-free shelter, that with thee we may highly exalt the Lord unto all ages!
With marvelous Joseph,[^659] all we saints honor your memory, and besides, your labors whereby ye well pleased God:

*Service to the Venerable Ones of the Caves*

—whereby ye well pleased God. And we pray that we may find protection through you from all perils, and may be vouchsafed to highly exalt the Lord unto all ages!
*Let us bless the Father, Son, and Holy Spirit, the Lord.*[^660]
I traverse in mind the enclosure of the holy cave, and behold upon its firmament a multitude of venerable fathers like stars, whom it is impossible to number; therefore rendering praise unto God for all of them, we pray that He may grant us to highly exalt Him with them unto all ages!
*Both now: Theotokion:*
Being pursued by the serpent that loves the world, I flee unto thine enclosure, O Pure One: protect me under the shelter of thy mercy, and deliver me from his savageries, that being saved, I may highly exalt thee, the Mother of God, unto all ages!
*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
*Truly do we confess thee as the Mother of God, who are saved through thee, O pure Virgin, magnifying thee with the bodiless choirs.*[^661]
*Troparia:*
Truly of old this holy mountain was a sorrowful place of idols and a den of thieves, but now it is the house of God, and they that dwell therein are blessed, for it was cleansed by you, O God-bearing fathers: therefore we all magnify you!
The voice of gladness and the sound of them that feast shall open to praise your assembly, O Caves fathers: receiving therefore their praises, save us from all tribulations and sorrows, that we may ever magnify you!
Having taken up the yoke of the Cross with joyful soul, and having tilled this mountain with the plow of labors, ye planted spiritual gardens of your honors, O God-bearing fathers; embracing whose fruits, we gladden our souls and magnify all of you!
Walking in the single path of the Lord's commandments, ye sowed diverse seeds: one purity, another obedience, another fasting, another poverty, labors, and tears; yet all of you alike reap the sheaves of joy, from which do ye bestow upon us also, that we may ever magnify all of you!
Where do ye pasture now in a grassy place, O all-blessed fathers, and what good thing are ye now deprived of? For the torrent of sweetness refreshes you; by even a single drop thereof, sweeten the bitterness of our life by your prayers, that we may joyfully sing unto our God!
Having passed from a sorrowful place unto a place without sorrow, we pray you, O all-blessed fathers: deliver us who together honor your memory from every tribulation, that we all may magnify you!
*Glory:*

*Of the Far Cave*

*Glory:*
Thou art the glory of the choir of all-hymned fathers, O Trinity, and imperishable gladness; for in Thee having overcome the world and the flesh with the invisible enemy, they have received crowns and rejoice with the angels, ever magnifying Thee!
*Both now: Theotokion:*
We glorify thee, O all-glorious City of God, for He Who became the Cause Himself gave thee as a refuge unto all that flee from the face of the enemy: be thou also a refuge unto us, that we may ever magnify thee!
*Exapostilarion:*[^662]
Royal priesthood, chosen generation, holy hierarchs, priests, monastics, virgins, wonderworkers, and the whole ascetic assembly of the venerable Caves fathers: gather together on this day in prayer, that for your sake the King of glory may save the EMPRESS[^663] and all the people who piously honor your assembly!
*Glory, both now: Theotokion:*
Thee, holier than the holy, do we glorify, O pure Virgin, and we magnify the Word born of thee: do thou receive this doxology from us, and grant us to glorify thee where all the saints glorify thee as the Mother of God!
*At the Praises, Stichera, Tone 4. Podoben: "Thou hast given a sign" (Да́лъ є҆сѝ зна́менїе):*[^664]
Ye enslaved carnal mindedness by the subjection of the mind unto the obedience of Christ, O all-blessed fathers, and having traversed the sea of passions, ye reached the summit of desire; therefore God, the Lover of mankind and Savior of our souls, granted you the honors of the high calling!
Ye quenched the furnace of worldly passions and voluntary turmoils with streams of tears and utter poverty, O venerable fathers; and as in a bright bridal chamber ye abode in dark caves, angelically glorifying Jesus, the Lover of mankind and Savior of our souls!
Having cleansed your mind from the depth of earthen passions, sorrows, and worldly cares, O God-bearing fathers, by the hope of the life to come ye despised all corruptible things; and now being in the incorruptible life, pray for us unto Jesus, the Lover of mankind and Savior of our souls!
*Glory: Tone 6:*[^665]
Desiring the beatitude of the blameless, ye ran in the way of the Lord's commandments, and seeking after the testimonies of the will of God, *Service to the Venerable Ones of the Caves*

—and seeking after, ye settled in mountains, in dens, and in dark caves; ye crucified the world with its lusts, and cutting down the demonic regiments, ye now inherit endless life and glory! Pray for us, we pray you, O venerable fathers, that we may obtain communion with you in the Jerusalem on high, and salvation for our souls!
*Both now: Theotokion:*[^666]
Thou art truly the vine that hast budded forth for us the Fruit of life; unto thee do we pray: Pray, O Mother of God, with the holy apostles and with all the saints, that our souls may be saved!
*The Great Doxology, and Dismissal.*
*At the Liturgy, the Beatitudes from the Canon, Odes 3 and 6.*
*Prokeimenon, Tone 7:*
*Precious in the sight of the Lord is the death of His venerable ones.*[^667]
*Verse:*
*What shall I render unto the Lord for all that He hath rendered unto me?*[^668]
*Epistle to the Galatians, Pericope 213 (Gal. 5:22–6:2).*[^669]
*Alleluia, Tone 6:*
*Blessed is the man that fears the Lord, he shall delight exceedingly in His commandments.*[^670]
*Verse:*
*His seed shall be mighty upon the earth.*[^671]
*Gospel according to Matthew, Pericope 43 (Matt. 11:27–30); or Luke, Pericope 24 (Luke 6:17–23).*[^672]
*Communion Hymn:*
*The righteous shall be in everlasting remembrance.*[^673]
*(Ornamental woodcut engraving of floral cartouche) COMMON SERVICE TO A VENERABLE FATHER:*

*At Vespers, the conclusion of Stichera at "Lord, I have cried":*[^674]
—having been an instructor of monastics: pray that our souls may be saved![^675]
*Glory: Tone 8:*[^676]
We honor thee, our father [Name], as the instructor of a multitude of monastics; for by thy path we have learned truly to walk uprightly: blessed art thou having labored for Christ, and thou didst expose the power of the enemy, O converser with angels, companion of the venerable and the righteous; with them pray unto the Lord that our souls may be shown mercy!
*Both now: Theotokion:*
*"O Virgin unwedded, who hast conceived God..." (Seek at the end of this book).*[^677]
*If it be not a feast, say this Theotokion:*[^678]
O pure Virgin, Gate of the Word, Mother of our God: pray that we may be saved!
*Stavrotheotokion:*[^679]
Not enduring the wonder, seeing Thee suspended upon the Tree, O Bestower of pride unto all; that as of old by the fruit of transgression they that had fallen into mortal sleep might receive divine and saving vigilance: the Virgin weeping spoke, whom we magnify!
*Troparion from the Typikon.*
*If there be no Typikon, say this:*
*If there be a Polyeleos, at the Praises, Stichera, Tone 6:*[^680]
For unto all, O all-blessed [Name], thou didst subject carnal mindedness by ascetic deed, and didst establish thy soul by sufferings: shining like gold tried in the furnace, thou wast a dwelling place of the Holy Spirit; having gathered multitudes of monastics, by thy teachings as by an ascending ladder thou didst lead them up unto the height of virtues: ever remember them that honor thy memory, O sacred minister, and pray that our souls may be saved!
*Prosomoia, to 2:*
On this day the all-radiant calling and spiritual memory, O all-glorious [Name], hath called together the assembly of monastics and regiments of the venerable; and angels with men sing in the Trinity of Christ our God: therefore fleeing unto the holy shrine of thy relics, we abundantly receive gifts of healing, and we glorify the Savior of our souls Who hath crowned thee!
O God-bearing, all-blessed father [Name], prophetically with tears *Service to a Venerable Father*

—sowing the fruit upon earth with tears, O sacred father, thou didst receive endless life after death: therefore on this day the Church of Christ celebrates thy most marvelous memory, O adornment of the venerable! *(Twice).*
*Glory, both now: Theotokion:*
Guide us into the way of repentance, who ever turn aside into evil trackless paths, and provoke the most good Lord to anger, O blessed Mary unwedded, refuge of despairing men, dwelling-place of God!
*Gradual, the 1st Antiphon of Tone 4:*
*"From my youth..."*
*Prokeimenon:*
*Precious in the sight of the Lord is the death of His venerable ones.*
*Verse:*
*What shall I render unto the Lord for all that He hath rendered unto me?*
*Let every breath praise the Lord.*
*Gospel according to Matthew, Pericope 43 (Matt. 11:27–30).*
*After Psalm 50, Sticheron, Tone 6:*
O venerable father, into all the earth hath gone forth the sound of thy right deeds: therefore in the heavens thou hast found the reward of thy labors; thou didst destroy the demonic regiments, and didst reach the angelic ranks, whose life thou didst blamelessly emulate: as having boldness unto Christ God, entreat peace for our souls!

*Service to a Venerable Father*

*If thou celebratest with a Polyeleos:*
*Canon to the Mother of God, to 4;*
*And to the Saint, to 8.*
*Tone 8. Ode 1. Heirmos:*
*Having traversed the water as dry land, and having escaped the Egyptian evil, the Israelite cried aloud: Let us sing unto our Deliverer and God!*[^681]
*Troparia:*
Adorning thyself with good dispositions, thou didst cleave unto Christ from youth, having slain the carnal passions by temperance, and thou didst pass over unto life, O venerable one!
Having become a fulfiller of the words and laws of God, thou wast filled, O all-wise father, with divine gifts and miracles, and unto all that have faith thou art richly inexhaustible!
Having been strengthened by the might of Christ, thou didst cast down the dominion and power of the enemy; and thou didst receive the praise of victorious honor, O venerable one, through radiant miracles!
*Glory:*
Good counsel hadst thou, and a spiritual eye looking unto God; therefore inner prayer joined Thee of old unto the venerable!
*Both now: Theotokion:*
Still the passions of my flesh and the incurable motions of my mind, I pray thee, O All-Pure Virgin: for thou didst bear God for us!

*Service to a Venerable Father*

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
*Thou art the establishment of them that flee unto Thee, O Lord; Thou art the light of them that are darkened, and my spirit sings of Thee!*[^682]
*Troparia:*
Having sown thyself into eternal life, O venerable father, thou reapest the fruits of divine enjoyment, O ever-memorable one!
With drops of tears thou didst drive away the sinful gloom, and thou gushest forth rivers of healings, O ever-memorable one!
Thy shrine is shown forth as a fountain overflowing with the graces of the Holy Spirit, O venerable father, pouring forth miracles!
*Glory:*
Thou didst lay aside carnal mindedness, and didst subject the body unto the mind by fasting, O venerable father!
*Both now: Theotokion:*
O spiritual heavens, as an ear of corn upon the furrows hast thou brought forth Him that feeds all creation, the God of all, O Ever-Virgin!
*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
*Rising early we cry aloud unto Thee, O Lord, save us: for Thou art our God, besides Thee we know no other!*[^683]
*Troparia:*
With a cleansed mind, O all-glorious one, we cry aloud unto thee who art worthy of wonder: of ineffable Christ, the God of all of us!
As Elijah upon a chariot, O father, by thy virtues thou didst ascend unto heaven, clothed in the Spirit!
*Glory:*
Having subdued thy body by temperance and purity, thou didst reach unto the spacious mansions on high, O father!
*Both now: Theotokion:*
Heal the darkening of my ailing mind, O all-immaculate Mother of God, who didst bear the Physician Christ!
*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
*I will pour out my prayer unto the Lord, and unto Him will I declare my sorrows; for my soul is filled with evils, and my life hath drawn near unto hades, and like Jonah I pray: Lead me up from corruption, O God!*[^684]
*Troparia:*
Having slain the serpent, thou didst slay the passions of thy flesh ever by temperance, O venerable one:

*Service to a Venerable Father*

—therefore, O divinely-blessed one, from his malice also are they loosed who receive from thee signs, being unenlightened; for thou art an all-faithful servant of God, and wast known to be faithful!
Having tilled thy soul with the plow of labors, O sacred father, wisely didst thou cast into it virtuous, fruitful seeds; and thou hast reaped rich ears of manifold healings!
*Glory:*
Having been strengthened by the power of the Spirit, O father, thou didst cast down the dominion and might of the enemy; and thou didst receive the praise of victorious honor, O venerable one, through radiant miracles!
*Both now: Theotokion:*
The Lord is with thee, O Pure One, even as He was well-pleased to be, O Maiden: by thine intercession He hath delivered all of us from the power of the captor; therefore do we now bless thee, generations of generations!
*Kontakion from the Typikon.*
*If there be no Typikon, say this Kontakion, Tone 2. Podoben: "Seeking the things on high" (Вы́шнихъ и҆ща̀):*[^685]
Having divinely armed thyself with purity of soul, and having pierced the demonic regiments with unceasing prayers as with a spear: pray Christ God unceasingly for all of us![^686]

*Service to a Venerable Father*

*Ikos:*[^687]
Let us all hear how the venerable one obtained great gifts, and for this cause mortified his flesh: and made his heart pure by temperance and many ascetic deeds. And he became a temple of the Holy Spirit, and by His operation shone forth: for in heaven he stands with boldness before the throne of the Highest, praying Christ God unceasingly for all of us!
*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
*The Hebrew youths in Babylon once trampled the flame of the furnace with faith in the Trinity, singing: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!*[^688]
*Troparia:*
Illumined by divine grace, thou didst drive away the darkness of passions, and unto the people that honor thee thou wast manifestly shown forth as a light, as they sing: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!
Thou didst drive away the winter of sin with fervent zeal toward God, and didst bring forth the fruit of temperance, singing: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!
*Glory:*
Thou didst make thy heart pure, O father, and becamest a spiritual temple unto the Holy Trinity: before Whom standing, thou singest: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!
*Both now: Theotokion:*
Willingly didst thou bear the timeless Son, Who is glorified with the Father and the Spirit; Whom do thou pray, O Pure One, that our souls may be saved!
*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
*The furnace was terrified of the youths, and was consumed by fire, wherein the young men sang: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord, and highly exalt Him unto all ages!*[^689]
*Troparia:*
Thy precious shrine abundantly gushes forth healing by the Spirit of God, and heals the passions of them that are heavily burdened, O father, who come unto thee, and it drives away fierce evil spirits; and it raises up the faithful to praise thy radiant ascetic deeds!
Like a great sun unto us, by the greatness of thy deeds thou didst shine forth, and didst illumine all the ends of the earth, O divinely wise one. Having died, thou didst pass from light unto the Light that hath shone forth: therefore we cry aloud unto thee: Enlighten our thoughts, O venerable father!
Thy patient body, O blessed one, girt with chains, heals incurable passions by touch: God and the Savior having exceedingly glorified thee, O all-wise and marvelous father, *Service to a Venerable Father*

—O all-wise and marvelous father [Name], for by thy good works thou wast glorified, O venerable one!
*Glory:*
Thou didst blossom in the ascetic valleys, O our all-praised father, like a fragrant rose, and like a lily thou didst breathe the sweetness of the virtues and miracles of the faithful: therefore, O venerable one, drive away from us stinking passions!
*Both now: Theotokion:*
Enlighten, O pure Virgin, my heart that is ever quenched by transgressions and many worldly cares; leave me not to be a joy unto enemies, that I may glorify thee and lovingly sing of thee, O all-hymned one!
*Exapostilarion. Podoben: "Give ear, ye women" (Жены́ ᲂу҆слы́шите):*[^690]
Thou didst blossom like a palm tree according to David, O father,[^691] and wast shown forth as a dwelling place of the Most Holy Spirit, and He revealed thee glorious unto the universe; unceasingly pray for us who honor thy most honorable memory with faith, O venerable [Name]!
*Theotokion:*
With unceasing hymns do we bless thee, O Virgin, for thou, O Mother of God, didst bear One of the Trinity, and bearest upon thy divine arms the exceeding rich Word, without alteration and without change!
*If thou celebratest with a Polyeleos, at the Praises, Stichera, Tone 6:*
O God-bearing, all-blessed father [Name], thou didst subject carnal mindedness, and didst establish thy soul by sufferings; shining like gold tried in the furnace, thou wast a dwelling place of the Most Holy Spirit: having gathered multitudes of monastics, by thy teachings as by a certain ladder ascending unto heaven, thou didst lead them up unto the height of virtues! Ever remember us who honor thy sacred memory, and pray that our souls may be saved! *(Twice).*
On this day thy most radiant and unfading memory, O all-glorious [Name], hath shone forth, calling together the numerous assembly of fasters and the choirs of the venerable; and angels with men truly praise Christ our God Who is known in the Trinity: therefore fleeing unto the sacred shrine of thy relics, we abundantly receive gifts of healing, and we glorify Christ Who hath crowned thee, the Savior of our souls!
O God-bearing, all-blessed father [Name], prophetically with tears *Service to a Venerable Father*

—prophetically with tears didst thou bedew the earth, and gavest no sleep unto thine eyes, nor slumber unto thy eyelids;[^692] manifesting a heartfelt desire toward Christ Whom thou didst love! Therefore thou becamest a model for monastics, and didst fulfill every kind of virtue; therefore we bless thee, magnifying Him Who hath glorified thee!
*Glory: Tone 8:*[^693]
O venerable father, having diligently trained thyself in virtue from infancy, thou wast an instrument of the Holy Spirit; and having received from Him the operation of miracles, thou didst persuade men to despise pleasant things: and now being most purely illumined with divine Light, enlighten our thoughts also, O [Name], our father!
*Both now: Theotokion:*
All my hope do I place in thee, O Mother of God: keep me under thy shelter!
*The Great Doxology, and Dismissal.*
*At the Liturgy, the Beatitudes from the Canon, Odes 3 and 6.*
*Prokeimenon, Tone 7:*
*Precious in the sight of the Lord is the death of His venerable ones.*[^694]
*Verse:*
*What shall I render unto the Lord for all that He hath rendered unto me?*[^695]
*Epistle to the Galatians, Pericope 213 (Gal. 5:22–6:2).*[^696]
*Alleluia, Tone 6:*
*Blessed is the man that fears the Lord, he shall delight exceedingly in His commandments.*[^697]
*Verse:*
*His seed shall be mighty upon the earth.*[^698]
*Gospel according to Matthew, Pericope 43 (Matt. 11:27–30); or Luke, Pericope 24 (Luke 6:17–23).*[^699]
*Communion Hymn:*
*The righteous shall be in everlasting remembrance.*[^700]
*(Ornamental woodcut engraving of two angels holding a wreath) RESURRECTIONAL THEOTOKIA OF THE EIGHT TONES:*
*At Vespers, at "Lord, I have cried," and at the Aposticha.*[^701]

*Tone 1. At "Lord, I have cried":*[^702]
Let us sing of the Virgin Mary, the glory of the universe, who budded forth from men and bare the Master, the heavenly gate, the song of the bodiless hosts, and the adornment of the faithful! For she was shown forth as heaven and the temple of the Godhead: she broke down the middle wall of enmity, brought in peace, and opened the Kingdom! Having her therefore as the confirmation of faith, we have as champion the Lord born of her. Take courage therefore, take courage, O people of God: for He will conquer the enemies, as He is almighty!
*At the Aposticha, Theotokion:*[^703]
Behold, the prophecy of Isaiah is fulfilled; for a Virgin hast thou given birth, and after giving birth hast remained as before childbirth: for He that was born was God; therefore nature also was transformed anew. But O Mother of God, despise not the supplications of thy servants brought unto thee in thy temple: but as bearing the Compassionate One in thy hands, take pity upon thy servants, and pray that our souls may be saved!
*Tone 2. Theotokion:*[^704]
The shadow of the law hath passed away now that grace hath come: for as the bush was not consumed while burning with fire, even so a Virgin hast thou given birth, and a Virgin hast thou remained; instead of a pillar of fire, the Sun of Righteousness hath shone forth; instead of Moses, Christ, the Salvation of our souls!
*At the Aposticha, Theotokion:*[^705]
O new wonder of all ancient wonders! For who hath known a Mother having given birth without a husband, and bearing in her arms Him that contains all creation? It is the good pleasure of God that was born; Whom as an Infant having borne in thy hands, O Pure One, and having motherly boldness toward Him: cease not praying for them that honor thee, to have compassion and to save our souls!

*Tone 3. Theotokion:*[^706]
