How should we not marvel at thy God-man childbirth, O all-honorable one? For without having accepted the trial of man, O all-immaculate one, thou hast given birth without a father unto a Son in the flesh, Who was born before ages from the Father without a mother, Who in no wise endured change, or mingling, or division, but preserved intact the property of each essence. Therefore, O Mother and Virgin, Lady, entreat Him that the souls of them that in an orthodox manner confess thee as the Mother of God may be saved!

*At the Aposticha, Theotokion:*[^707]
Without seed from the Divine Spirit, yet by the will of the Father, thou didst conceive the Son of God, Who from the Father without a mother was before ages; and for our sake having become without a father from thee, thou hast given birth unto Him in the flesh, and hast nourished the Infant with milk. Therefore cease not to pray, that our souls may be delivered from perils!

*Tone 4. Theotokion:*[^708]
He that for thy sake is the ancestor of God, the Prophet David, harmoniously proclaimed concerning thee unto Him that worked great things for thee: At Thy right hand stood the Queen![^709] For as a Mother who is mediatress of life did God show thee forth, Who was well-pleased without a father to become man from thee, that He might renew His own image corrupted by passions; and having found the sheep that went astray upon the mountains, taking it upon His shoulder, He might bring it unto the Father, and according to His own will might join it with the heavenly hosts; and that Christ, Who possesses great and rich mercy, might save the world, O Mother of God!

*At the Aposticha, Theotokion:*[^710]
Look down upon the supplications of thy servants, O all-immaculate one, quenching the fierce uprisings against us, delivering us from all sorrow: for thee alone do we have as a firm and sure establishment, and thine intercession have we acquired, that we who call upon thee may not be put to shame, O Lady; make haste unto the entreaty of them that faithfully cry aloud unto thee: Rejoice, O Lady, the help of all, joy and protection, and the salvation of our souls!

*Tone 5. Theotokion:*[^711]
In the Red Sea the image of the unwedded Bride was inscribed of old: there Moses was the divider of the water; here Gabriel was the minister of the miracle. Then the deep was traversed dryshod by Israel; and now the Virgin hath given birth without seed unto Christ. The sea after the passage of Israel remained impassable; the immaculate one after the childbirth of Emmanuel remained incorrupt.

—remained incorrupt.[^712] O Thou Who art and Who wast before, and hast appeared as man: O God, have mercy upon us!

*At the Aposticha, Theotokion:*[^713]
A temple and a door art thou, a palace and the throne of the King, O all-honored Virgin: through whom my Deliverer, Christ the Lord, appeared unto them that sleep in darkness, being the Sun of Righteousness, wishing to enlighten that which He created according to His own image with His own hand. Therefore, O all-hymned one, as having acquired motherly boldness toward Him, unceasingly pray that our souls may be saved!

*Tone 6. Theotokion:*[^714]
Who shall not call thee blessed, O Most Holy Virgin? Who will not sing of thy most pure childbirth? For the only-begotten Son Who shone forth timelessly from the Father, the Same came forth from thee who art pure, having become incarnate in an ineffable manner: being God by nature, and for our sake having become man by nature, not divided into two persons, but in two natures unconfusedly known. Entreat Him, O pure and all-blessed one, that our souls may be shown mercy!

*At the Aposticha, Theotokion:*[^715]
My Creator and Deliverer, O most pure one, Christ the Lord, having come forth from thy womb and having clothed Himself in me, delivered Adam from the former curse. Therefore unto thee, O all-immaculate one, as unto the Mother of God and Virgin in truth, we cry aloud unceasingly: Rejoice, like the angel! Rejoice, O Lady, intercession and protection, and the salvation of our souls!

*Tone 7. Theotokion:*[^716]
Thou wast known as a Mother beyond nature, O Mother of God, and hast remained a Virgin beyond word and understanding; and the miracle of thy childbirth the tongue cannot declare. For thy conception being most glorious, O pure one, incomprehensible is the manner of giving birth: for where God wills, the order of nature is overcome! Therefore, knowing thee all as the Mother of God, we fervently pray thee: Entreat Him that our souls may be saved!

*At the Aposticha, Theotokion:*[^717]
Fleeing beneath thy shelter, O Lady, all of us earthborn cry aloud unto thee: O Mother of God, our hope, deliver us from immeasurable transgressions, and save our souls!

*Tone 8. Theotokion:*[^718]
The King of heaven, for His love toward mankind, appeared upon earth and lived with men: for from the pure Virgin —having taken pure flesh, and having come forth from her with the assumption, He is one Son, twofold in nature, but not in hypostasis. Therefore truly proclaiming Him as perfect God and perfect man, we confess Christ our God: Whom entreat, O unwedded Mother, that our souls may be shown mercy!

*At the Aposticha, Theotokion:*[^719]
O unwedded Virgin, who hast ineffably conceived God in the flesh, Mother of God the Most High: receive the supplications of thy servants, O all-immaculate one, granting unto all cleansing of transgressions! Receiving our supplications now, entreat that we all may be saved!

*(Ornamental woodcut engraving of floral cartouche) RESURRECTIONAL THEOTOKIA,*
*Dismissal [Theotokia] after the Troparia of the Saints, of the Eight Tones:*[^720]

*Tone 1. Theotokion:*[^721]
When Gabriel announced unto thee, O Virgin: Rejoice! with the voice the Master of all became incarnate in thee, the holy ark, as the righteous David said:[^722] thou wast shown forth more spacious than the heavens, having borne thy Creator! Glory to Him Who made His abode in thee! Glory to Him Who came forth from thee! Glory to Him Who delivered us through thy childbirth!

*Tone 2. Theotokion:*[^723]
All beyond mind, all most glorious are thy mysteries, O Mother of God: sealed in purity and preserved in virginity, thou wast known as a true Mother, having given birth unto the true God! Entreat Him that our souls may be saved!

*Tone 3. Theotokion:*[^724]
Thee, who didst intercede for the salvation of our race, do we sing of, O Mother of God and Virgin: for having assumed flesh from thee, Thy Son and our God, having accepted suffering through the Cross, delivered us from corruption, as the Lover of mankind!

*Tone 4. Theotokion:*[^725]
The mystery hidden from the age and unknown unto angels, through thee, O Mother of God, was revealed unto them that are upon the earth: God becoming incarnate in unconfused union, and willingly accepting the Cross for our sake; whereby having raised up the first-created, He saved our souls from death!

*Tone 5. Theotokion:*[^726]
Rejoice, impassable Gate of the Lord! Rejoice, wall and protection of them that flee unto thee! Rejoice, tempest-tossed haven, and unwedded one, who hast given birth in the flesh unto thy Creator and God! Cease not to intercede for them that sing of and worship thy childbirth!

*Tone 6. Theotokion:*[^727]
Having called Thy Mother blessed, Thou camest unto suffering by voluntary intent, —shining forth upon the Cross, wishing to seek out Adam, saying unto the angels: Rejoice with Me, for the lost drachma is found![^728] O our God, Who hast wisely arranged all things: Glory to Thee!

*Tone 7. Theotokion:*[^729]
As the treasury of our resurrection, lead up from the pit and depth of transgressions them that hope in thee, O all-hymned one: for thou didst save them that are guilty of sin, having given birth unto our Salvation; who before childbirth wast a Virgin, and in childbirth a Virgin, and after childbirth again remainest a Virgin!

*Tone 8. Theotokion:*[^730]
O Good One, Who for our sake wast born of a Virgin and didst endure crucifixion, Who by death didst overthrow death and as God didst manifest the Resurrection: despise not those whom Thou hast fashioned with Thy hand! Manifest Thy love toward mankind, O Merciful One! Accept the Mother of God who bare Thee as she intercedes for us, and save a despairing people, O our Savior!

*The end, and glory be to God.*[^731]

*(Ornamental woodcut engraving of floral cartouche) (Vacat — Fol. 128v facing page: Unnumbered blank sheet) (Flyleaf — Archival binding sheet)*
Archival stamp of the State Museum of the Book and Printing of Ukraine (Державний музей книги і друкарства України) at the lower left margin.

*(Flyleaf — Archival binding sheet)*
Rear endpaper pastedown with penciled institutional cataloguing shelfmarks: (МКДУ 2353 / Сд 115).

*(Rear Cover — Blind-stamped leather binding)*
Eighteenth-century brown calfskin binding over heavy wooden boards. The outer border is blind-tooled with a decorative ornamental frame and corner fleurons. In the center is an embossed diamond-shaped lozenge. The surface exhibits historical patina, age wear, edge scuffs, and binding cord remnants at the corners.

[^1]: The front flyleaf recto contains 19th-century cursive library provenance inscriptions detailing the transfer of this volume of the Pechersk Menaion to the Trinity Church on November 23, 1879, with shelfmarks from the Kyrylivsky and St. Cyril Hermitage collections.

[^2]: Empress Catherine II ("the Great"), who reigned 1762–1796. This 1763 edition was printed in the second year of her reign.

[^4]: Archimandrite Zosimas (Valkevych), Archimandrite of the Kyiv-Pechersk Lavra from 1762 to 1786.

[^37]: Cf. John 12:26: "If any man serve Me, let him follow Me; and where I am, there shall also My servant be." The printed text reads `внѣ́шнѣхъ` (literally "of external things/words"), which in this liturgical context appears to be a typesetting corruption for `бж҃е́ственныхъ` ("divine") or denotes the externally heard words of the Holy Gospel.

[^38]: 1 Kings 2:30 [LXX] (1 Sam. 2:30): "For them that honor Me will I honor, and he that despises Me shall be lightly esteemed."

[^39]: Cf. Ps. 91:13 [LXX] (Ps. 92:12): "The righteous man shall flourish like a palm tree, and like a cedar in Lebanon shall he be multiplied."

[^40]: Ps. 76:12 [LXX] (Ps. 77:11): "I remembered the works of the Lord; for I will remember Thy wonders from the beginning."

[^41]: Ps. 76:15 [LXX] (Ps. 77:14): "Thou art the God that doest wonders; Thou hast made known Thy power among the peoples."

[^42]: Ps. 111:1 [LXX] (Ps. 112:1).

[^44]: The melodic model "Being Called from on High" (`Зва́нный свы́ше` / Ὁ κληθεὶς ἄνωθεν) is canonically an automelon of Tone 4; in this edition the rubric specifies `Гла́съ, а҃` (Tone 1), reflecting local Ruthenian chant practice or a typographical variant.

[^45]: A diplomatic rendering of the printed imperial titulature `І҆МПЕРАТРИ́ЦѢ на́шей` (unto our Empress), invoking Empress Catherine II (the Great, reigned 1762–1796) during the publication year of this codex (1763).

[^46]: The printed Slavonic rubric specifies `Подо́бїе: Что́ васъ нарече́мъ ст҃і́и` (Tone 2), which is the standard prosomoion model derived from the commemoration of holy martyrs.

[^47]: The printed Slavonic text reads `покори́въ дх҃ъ тѣле́снымъ страсте́мъ` ("having subjected the spirit unto bodily passions"), which is an epigraphic/compositor invertendo slip for `покори́въ тѣ́ло дꙋхо́внымъ страсте́мъ/по́двигомъ` ("having subjected the body to the spirit / spiritual struggles").

[^48]: Ps. 91:13 [LXX] (Ps. 92:12).

[^49]: The rubric specifies `Подо́бенъ: Ли́къ а҆́гг҃лскїй` ("The Angelic Choir"), an automelon of Tone 1 closely aligned with `Небе́сныхъ чинѡ́въ`.

[^50]: Melodic model `Премꙋ́дрости возше́дъ` (Tone 8), a standard variant/incipit of the model `Премꙋ́дрости и҆ Сло́ва` ("Having Ascended the Wisdom / In Wisdom and the Word").

[^51]: The Slavonic rubric reads `г҃-гѡ гла́са` (of Tone 3 or Tone 4; the context and following prokeimenon establish Tone 4).

[^52]: Ps. 67:36 [LXX] (Ps. 68:35).

[^53]: Ps. 67:27 [LXX] (Ps. 68:26).

[^54]: Printed `Правосла́вной І҆МПЕРАТРИ́ЦѢ` ("unto the Orthodox Empress"), referring diplomatically to Empress Catherine II (the Great, reigned 1762–1796).

According to the Kyiv-Pechersk Paterikon (Discourse 23), Venerable Athanasius died after prolonged illness, lay dead for two days, and on the third day was found sitting upright and weeping, having been raised back to life by the Lord to preach repentance and secluded vigil.

[^63]: Historical feast day of Ven. Athanasius the Recluse, December 2 (O.S.). In modern calendars commemorated Dec 2 and on the Synaxis of the Near Caves (Sept 28).

[^73]: Historical commemoration of Ven. Nikon the Dry on December 30 (O.S.).

[^96]: Woodcut engraving of Saint Gregory the Wonderworker of the Caves (*Прⷣ: Григо́рїй Чꙋдотво́рецъ Пече́рскїй*). According to Discourse 25 of the Kyiv-Pechersk Paterikon, St. Gregory was gifted with discernment and prophecy; when Prince Rostislav Vsevolodovich was marching on an expedition against the Polovtsi in 1093, his servants mocked the elder, who prophesied that the prince and his warriors would drown in the Stuhna River. Enraged, Rostislav had Gregory bound hand and foot with a heavy stone tied around his neck and cast into the Dnipro River. The prophecy was fulfilled shortly thereafter at the Battle of the Stuhna.

[^97]: Historical feast day of Ven. Gregory the Wonderworker of the Caves on January 8 (O.S.). In modern calendars commemorated Jan 8 and on the Synaxis of the Near Caves (Sept 28).

[^108]: Podoben model incipit: Slavonic 'Нб҃ныхъ чинѡ́въ' (Tone 1), canonical Greek 'Τῶν οὐρανίων ταγμάτων'.

[^109]: Woodcut engraving of Saint Ephraim, Bishop of Pereyaslavl (*Ѡ С͠тъмъ Е҆фремѣ Е҆пⷭ҇копѣ Переꙗславскомъ*). Disciple of Saint Anthony of the Caves and former boyar and treasurer to Grand Prince Izyaslav Yaroslavich of Kyiv. After being tonsured by St. Anthony, he undertook a pilgrimage to Constantinople and the holy places, whence he brought back copies of the Studite Rule (*Typikon*) that governed monastic life at the Kyiv-Pechersk Lavra. Consecrated Bishop of Pereyaslavl in 1089, he adorned the city with monumental stone churches (including the Cathedral of St. Michael) and built the first recorded public infirmaries and bathhouses in Kievan Rus', where the poor and sick were treated without fee.

[^110]: Feast of our Venerable Father Ephraim, Bishop of Pereyaslavl, commemorated on January 28 (O.S.). In modern general usage commemorated on January 28 and on the Synaxis of the Near Caves (September 28).

[^111]: Composite Patristic Paremia 1 from the Proverbs of Solomon (Proverbs 10:7, 3:13–16; 8:4–21 LXX), traditional reading appointed for holy hierarchs and teachers.

[^112]: Composite Patristic Paremia 2 from the Wisdom of Solomon (Proverbs 10:31–32; Wisdom of Solomon 4:7–15; 6:12–21; 7:22–30; 8:2–21; 9:1–14 LXX), appointing the prayer of Solomon for the wisdom that sits beside the throne of God.

[^113]: Composite Patristic Paremia 3 from the Wisdom of Solomon (Wisdom of Solomon 4:7–15; 2:12–22; 3:1–9 LXX), celebrating the immortality of the righteous and prophesying the unjust persecution of Christ and His saints.

[^126]: Woodcut engraving of Saint Prokhor the Weed-Eater of the Caves (*Прпⷣ: Про́хоръ Ло́бодникъ*), Discourse 31 of the Kyiv-Pechersk Paterikon. A native of Smolensk, Prokhor received tonsure at the Kyiv-Pechersk Monastery and adopted an extreme rule of ascetic fasting, gathering wild orache (chenopodium) during the summer, grinding it into flour, and baking bread without ever tasting normal wheat or rye bread. During the severe famine in Rus' under Grand Prince Svyatopolk Izyaslavich (1093–1113), St. Prokhor baked orache bread for all the starving citizens, which by his blessing tasted sweeter than honey. When the merchant guild created an artificial salt monopoly and salt became unavailable, he gathered ashes from the cells and distributed them to the poor, where they miraculously turned into pure white salt. Prince Svyatopolk, deeply moved by these miracles, repented of his extortions and vowed never to harm the monastery; upon Prokhor's repose in 1107, the prince himself carried the saint's body to the caves for burial.

[^127]: Historical feast day of our Venerable Father Prokhor the Wonderworker of the Caves on February 12 (O.S.). In modern general calendars commemorated on February 10 or 12 and on the Synaxis of the Near Caves (September 28).

[^128]: Podoben model incipit: Slavonic 'Да́лъ є҆сѝ ꙁна́менїе' (Tone 4), canonical Byzantine Greek 'Ἔδωκας σημείωσιν'.

[^138]: Woodcut engraving of Saint Isaac the Recluse of the Caves (*Прпⷣ: І҆са́кїй Пече́рскїй*), Discourse 36 of the Kyiv-Pechersk Paterikon. A wealthy merchant from Toropets named Chern, he gave away his entire fortune to the poor and monasteries, arrived at the Kyiv Caves, and was tonsured by St. Anthony. Entering into strict reclusion in a narrow cave cell for seven years, he was severely deceived by demons who manifested themselves in radiant glory, falsely proclaiming one of their number to be Christ. Falling down and worshipping the apparition, Isaac was overwhelmed by demonic mockery and dancing, being left comatose and near death. Nursed back to physical and mental health over two years by St. Anthony and St. Theodosius, he subsequently embraced the asceticism of holy folly (*yurodstvo*), endured extreme cold and bodily mortification, and gained complete spiritual mastery over demonic assaults, becoming a wonderworker.

[^139]: Feast of our Venerable Father Isaac the Recluse of the Caves, commemorated on February 14 (O.S.). In modern general usage commemorated on February 14 and on the Synaxis of the Near Caves (September 28).

[^140]: Podoben model incipit: Slavonic 'Ѡ҆ пресла́внагѡ чꙋдесѐ' (Tone 8), canonical Byzantine Greek 'Ὢ τοῦ παραδόξου θαύματος'.

[^142]: Heirmos of Ode 3 (Canon to St. Isaac): 1 Samuel 2:2 (1 Kings 2:2 LXX; Song of Hannah) and Habakkuk 3:3.

[^143]: Sessional Hymn to St. Isaac, Tone 8.

[^144]: Heirmos of Ode 4 (Canon to St. Isaac): Habakkuk 3:1–19 (The Prayer of Habakkuk).

[^151]: Woodcut engraving of Saint Eustratius the Venerable-Martyr of the Caves (*Пр: Єѵстратїй Печерскій*), Discourse 29 of the Kyiv-Pechersk Paterikon. A wealthy Kievan who gave away his wealth and took monastic tonsure at the Caves, Eustratius was taken captive during the Polovtsian raid on Kyiv in 1096 and sold into slavery with fifty other Christians to a Jewish merchant in Chersonesus (Korsun). When the master attempted to force the captives to renounce Christ, Eustratius exhorted them to endure starvation rather than apostatize; all fifty captives died of hunger over fourteen days, while Eustratius survived through prayer. Enraged by the loss of his captives and money, the master crucified St. Eustratius on Pascha, mocking him as his ancestors mocked Christ. Pierced through the ribs with a spear from which his soul ascended to heaven in a fiery chariot, his holy relics were cast into the Black Sea, miraculously washed ashore, and translated to the Near Caves of the Lavra.

[^152]: Feast of the Holy Venerable-Martyr Eustratius of the Caves, commemorated on March 28 (O.S.). In modern calendars commemorated on March 28 and on the Synaxis of the Near Caves (September 28).

[^153]: Podoben model incipit: Slavonic 'Нб҃ныхъ чинѡ́въ' (Tone 1), canonical Byzantine Greek 'Τῶν οὐρανίων ταγμάτων'.

[^172]: Reference to the Byzantine Emperor (Alexis I Comnenus), who ordered the punishment of the Jewish merchant and his conspirators in Korsun/Chersonesus after the martyrdom of St. Eustratius, fulfilling the saint's prophecy; Paterikon Discourse 29.

[^173]: Woodcut engraving of Saint Niphont, Bishop of Novgorod (*С: Нїфо́нтъ Епⷭ҇копъ Новгородскій*), Discourse 14 of the Kyiv-Pechersk Paterikon. Tonsured at the Kyiv Caves Monastery where he shone in obedience and asceticism, he was consecrated Bishop of Novgorod in 1130. A zealous champion of canonical Orthodoxy, he refused to sanction the uncanonical marriage of Prince Svyatoslav Olgovich, firmly withstood Prince Izyaslav Mstislavich's attempt to appoint Clement Smolyatich as Metropolitan of Kyiv without the blessing of the Ecumenical Patriarch of Constantinople, and suffered imprisonment in the Caves for his fidelity to the canons. Returning to the Caves before his death, he was granted a vision of St. Theodosius shortly before falling asleep in the Lord on April 8, 1156; his incorrupt relics repose in the Near Caves.

[^174]: Feast of our Father among the Saints Niphont, Bishop of Novgorod, commemorated on April 8 (O.S.). Also commemorated on the Synaxis of the Near Caves (September 28) and the Synaxis of the Novgorod Hierarchs (February 10).

[^175]: Podoben model incipit: Slavonic 'Ꙗ҆́кѡ до́блѧ' (Tone 4), canonical Byzantine Greek 'Ὡς γενναῖον ἐν μάρτυσιν'.

[^176]: Paterikon Discourse 14 recounts that three days before his death in the Kyiv Caves, St. Niphont had a marvelous vision of St. Theodosius of the Caves, who appeared to him holding a scroll and welcoming him into the heavenly habitations.

[^177]: Heirmos of Ode 1 (Canon to St. Niphont): Exodus 15:6, 15:1–19 (The Song of Moses at the Red Sea).

[^178]: Heirmos of Ode 3 (Canon to St. Niphont): 1 Samuel 2:1–10 (1 Kings 2:1–10 LXX; Song of Hannah) and Habakkuk 3:3.

[^179]: Sessional Hymn to St. Niphont, Tone 4.

[^180]: Heirmos of Ode 4 (Canon to St. Niphont): Habakkuk 3:3, 3:1–19 (The Prayer of Habakkuk).

[^181]: St. Niphont repeatedly reconciled warring Rus' princes (including the princes of Kyiv, Chernihiv, and Novgorod in 1135 and 1141), preventing bloodshed and defending civil peace.

[^182]: Psalm 118:46 (Psalm 119:46 LXX). St. Niphont courageously rebuked Prince Svyatoslav Olgovich of Novgorod when the prince sought to contract an uncanonical marriage, refusing to crown him or allow his clergy to perform the ceremony.

[^183]: Heirmos of Ode 5 (Canon to St. Niphont): Isaiah 26:9.

[^184]: St. Theodore the Studite (759–826), renowned champion of ecclesiastical independence and defender of the sacred canons against imperial and uncanonical ecclesiastical usurpations.

[^185]: In 1147, Grand Prince Izyaslav Mstislavich convened a council of Rus' bishops in Kyiv to elevate Clement Smolyatich as Metropolitan of Kyiv without the confirmation or blessing of the Patriarch of Constantinople. St. Niphont firmly refused to participate or recognize Clement, defending the canonical order and unity of the Church.

[^186]: To bypass the requirement of patriarchal consecration, Onuphrius, Bishop of Chernihiv, proposed consecrating Clement Smolyatich using the relic of the head of St. Clement of Rome, preserved in Kyiv. St. Niphont protested that this was entirely uncanonical and contrary to apostolic tradition.

[^187]: Heirmos of Ode 6 (Canon to St. Niphont): Jonah 2:1–10; Psalm 43:23 (Psalm 44:22 LXX).

[^188]: Prince Izyaslav detained St. Niphont in Kyiv and confined him to the Kyiv Caves Monastery, forbidding him to return to his see in Novgorod until Prince Yuri Dolgoruky took Kyiv.

[^189]: Matthew 5:10.

[^190]: Kontakion to St. Niphont, Tone 5.

[^191]: Reference to Constantine I, consecrated Metropolitan of Kyiv by the Patriarch of Constantinople in 1156, whom St. Niphont was traveling to meet when he fell ill and reposed in the Kyiv Caves.

[^192]: Heirmos of Ode 7 (Canon to St. Niphont): Daniel 3:26–56 (The Three Holy Children in the Furnace).

[^193]: Prince Yuri Vladimirovich Dolgoruky, Grand Prince of Kyiv (1149–1151, 1155–1157), who drove out Prince Izyaslav Mstislavich and restored St. Niphont with great honor to the see of Novgorod.

[^194]: Heirmos of Ode 8 (Canon to St. Niphont): Daniel 3:57–88 (The Song of the Three Holy Children).

[^195]: Patriarch Michael II Kourkouas (or his successor Nicholas IV Mouzalon) sent an encyclical to Rus' praising Bishop Niphont's unbending fortitude, likening him to the ancient holy fathers.

[^196]: An allegorical allusion to the Great Church of the Kyiv Caves Monastery, revealed by the Mother of God (the mystical Dove) to St. Anthony and St. Theodosius.

[^197]: Heirmos of Ode 9 (Canon to St. Niphont): Exodus 3:2 (The Burning Bush).

[^198]: Isaiah 8:18; Hebrews 2:13; Paterikon Discourse 14.

[^199]: Woodcut engraving of Saint Simon, Bishop of Vladimir and Suzdal (*С: Сїмо́нъ Епⷭ҇кпъ Владимирескій и Сꙋждалскій*), principal author of the Kyiv-Pechersk Paterikon (Discourses 1–13, his Epistle to St. Polycarp). Tonsured at the Kyiv Caves, he was appointed igumen of the Nativity Monastery in Vladimir and in 1214 was consecrated the first Bishop of Vladimir and Suzdal. Despite his exalted episcopal rank, he maintained fervent devotion to the Kyiv Caves, declaring in his famous epistle: "I would rather be swept out as dung in the house of the Mother of God, and be trodden upon by men, or be one of the poor before the gates of that holy Great Church of the Caves, than possess this fleeting glory." He reposed in 1226; his holy relics rest in the Near Caves of the Lavra.

[^200]: Feast of our Father among the Saints Simon, Bishop of Vladimir and Suzdal, commemorated on May 10 (O.S.). In modern calendars commemorated on May 10 and on the Synaxis of the Near Caves (September 28).

[^201]: Podoben model incipit: Slavonic 'Ра́дꙋйсѧ живоно́сный крⷭ҇те' (Tone 5), canonical Byzantine Greek 'Χαίροις ἀσκητικῶν'.

[^202]: Physical leaf coordinate: Leaf p52 (Fol. 47v), appointed Paremias for a Hierarch from the Homilies of St. Ephraim the Syrian.

[^203]: Podoben model incipit: Slavonic 'Прехва́льнїи мч҃нцы' (Tone 1), canonical Byzantine Greek 'Πανεύφημοι μάρτυρες'.

[^204]: Psalm 131:9 (Psalm 132:9 LXX).

[^205]: Psalm 36:30 (Psalm 37:30 LXX).

[^206]: St. Anthony and St. Theodosius of the Caves, whose monastic rule, lives, and miracles St. Simon recorded and emulated.

[^207]: Troparion to St. Simon, Bishop of Vladimir and Suzdal, Tone 4.

[^208]: Podoben model incipit: Slavonic 'Ꙗ҆ви́лсѧ є҆сѝ дне́сь' (Tone 4), canonical Byzantine Greek 'Ἐπεφάνης σήμερον'.

[^209]: Podoben model incipit: Slavonic 'Красотѣ̀ дѣ́вства' (Tone 3), canonical Byzantine Greek 'Τὴν ὡραιότητα'.

[^210]: Matthew 25:21.

[^211]: Podoben model incipit: Slavonic 'Собеꙁнача́лное сло́во' (Tone 5), canonical Byzantine Greek 'Τὸν συνάναρχον Λόγον'.

[^212]: Psalm 48:4 (Psalm 49:3 LXX).

[^213]: John 10:9–16 (The Good Shepherd pericope).

[^214]: Heirmos of Ode 1 (Canon to St. Simon): Exodus 15:1–19, 15:4 (The Song of Moses at the Red Sea).

[^215]: Heirmos of Ode 3 (Canon to St. Simon): 1 Samuel 2:2 (1 Kings 2:2 LXX; Song of Hannah).

[^216]: Philippians 2:8.

[^217]: Luke 1:38.

[^218]: Sessional Hymn to St. Simon, Tone 8.

[^219]: Heirmos of Ode 4 (Canon to St. Simon): Habakkuk 3:1–19 (The Prayer of Habakkuk).

[^220]: Heirmos of Ode 5 (Canon to St. Simon): Psalm 87:15 (Psalm 88:14 LXX); Isaiah 26:9.

[^221]: John 21:15–17 (The commissioning of Simon Peter: "Feed My sheep").

[^222]: Heirmos of Ode 6 (Canon to St. Simon): Jonah 2:1–10 (The Prayer of Jonah).

[^223]: Kontakion to St. Simon, Bishop of Vladimir and Suzdal, Tone 8.

[^224]: Reference to St. Simon's famous testament in Paterikon Discourse 13, wherein he begged to be buried in the Kyiv Caves near St. Anthony and St. Theodosius rather than in his glorious cathedral in Vladimir.

[^225]: Heirmos of Ode 7 (Canon to St. Simon): Daniel 3:26–56 (The Three Holy Children in the Babylonian Furnace).

[^226]: Allusion to St. Simon's composition of the foundational discourses of the Kyiv-Pechersk Paterikon.

[^227]: Heirmos of Ode 8 (Canon to St. Simon): Daniel 3:19–30, 57–88 (The Song of the Three Youths in the Sevenfold Furnace).

[^228]: Psalm 83:11 (Psalm 84:10 LXX): "For better is one day in Thy courts than a thousand elsewhere. I chose rather to be cast down in the house of my God than to dwell in the tents of sinners." Quoted by St. Simon in Paterikon Discourse 13.

[^229]: Fol. 76v (p81): Saint Simon, Bishop of Vladimir and Suzdal (†1226), author of the primary epistles of the Kyiv Caves Paterikon, requested in his letters to the monk Polycarp that upon his death his body be translated from Vladimir to the Kyiv Caves Monastery to be interred among the venerable fathers, which pious desire was fulfilled by his flock.

[^230]: Fol. 76v (p81): An allusion to Saint Simon's epistolary narratives in the Kyiv Caves Paterikon, which record the lives, ascetic struggles, and miracles of the first Caves ascetics.

[^231]: Fol. 76v (p81): Church Slavonic: "сама́гѡ Бг҃овидѣ́нїѧ сподо́билсѧ є҆сѝ, и҆ ꙁри́ши ꙗ҆́снѡ свѣ́тъ непристꙋ́пный" (thou wast vouchsafed the very Vision of God, and clearly seest the Inaccessible Light).

[^232]: Fol. 77v (p82): Visual description of the 1763 Lavra woodcut engraving for the feast of Saint Agapitus the Unmercenary Physician of the Caves (June 1), portraying the saint with medicinal herbs and the subsequent baptism of his theological and medical rival, the Armenian physician.

[^233]: Fol. 77v (p82): June 1 marks the commemoration of our Venerable Father Agapitus of the Caves, Unmercenary Physician (†ca. 1095), who healed the sick without fee using monastic herbs and prayers, famously curing Prince Vladimir Monomakh of Chernihiv.

[^234]: Fol. 77v (p82): The melodic model is the Tone 4 prosomoion "As one valiant among martyrs" (*Ꙗ҆́кѡ до́блѧ* / *Ὡς γενναῖον ἐν μάρτυσιν*).

[^235]: Fol. 78r (p82): The troparion highlights Agapitus's ascetic reliance on the vegetable diet of Saint Anthony of the Caves (*ꙁе́лїемъ снѣ́днымъ*) as a vehicle of miraculous healing, and his conversion of the Armenian physician who converted to Orthodoxy and was tonsured as a Caves monk after Agapitus's death.

[^236]: Fol. 78v (p83): Canon of Saint Agapitus, Tone 6, Ode 1 Heirmos: "Ꙗ҆́кѡ по сꙋ́хꙋ пѣшеше́ствовавъ І҆и҃ль..." (*Ὡς ἐν ἠπείρῳ πεζεύσας ὁ Ἰσραήλ*).

[^237]: Fol. 78v (p83): Ode 3 Heirmos: "Нѣ́сть ст҃ъ, ꙗ҆́коже ты̀ Гдⷭ҇и Бж҃е мо́й..." (*Οὐκ ἔστιν ἅγιος ὡς σύ, Κύριε ὁ Θεός μου*).

[^238]: Fol. 79r (p83): Ode 4 Heirmos: "Хрїсто́съ моѧ̀ си́ла, Бг҃ъ и҆ Гдⷭ҇ь..." (*Χριστός μου δύναμις, Θεὸς καὶ Κύριος*).

[^239]: Fol. 79r (p83): Reference to Discourse 27 of the Kyiv Caves Paterikon, where the Armenian physician predicted that a sick boyar would die within eight days; Agapitus healed the boyar with monastery herbs, rebuking the Armenian for presuming to know the divine boundary between life and death.

[^240]: Fol. 79r (p83): According to the Paterikon, the envious Armenian gave Agapitus a deadly poison, but the saint drank it and remained unharmed, fulfilling Christ's promise in Mark 16:18 ("if they drink any deadly thing, it shall not hurt them").

[^241]: Fol. 79v (p84): Canon of Saint Agapitus, Tone 6, Ode 5 Heirmos: "Бж҃їимъ свѣ́томъ твои́мъ Бл҃же..." (*Τῷ θείῳ φέγγει σου, Ἀγαθέ*).

[^242]: Fol. 79v (p84): Referring to the miraculous healing of Prince Vladimir Vsevolodovich Monomakh (†1125), Prince of Chernihiv (and later Grand Prince of Kyiv), who was dying of an illness that the Armenian physician could not cure. Saint Agapitus sent him herbs boiled in monastery soup through Igumen John, which instantly cured the prince.

[^243]: Fol. 79v (p84): Ode 6 Heirmos: "Жите́йское мо́ре воздвиза́емое зрѧ̀..." (*Τοῦ βίου τὴν θάλασσαν, ὑψουμένην καθορῶν*).

[^244]: Fol. 79v (p84): When Prince Vladimir Monomakh came in person to the Caves Monastery with rich gifts of gold and silver to reward Agapitus, the saint refused to receive them and threw the gold before the gate of his cell, telling the prince to distribute it to the poor.

[^245]: Fol. 79v (p84): When Saint Agapitus fell sick, the Armenian physician came to his cell, took his pulse, and announced that he would die within three days; Agapitus answered that God had revealed to him that he would depart only after three months, which came to pass exactly as prophesied.

[^246]: Fol. 80r (p84): Kontakion, Tone 6, emphasizing Agapitus's victorious contest against the Armenian physician and his post-mortem conversion of the Armenian to the Orthodox Christian faith and monastic habit.

[^247]: Fol. 80r (p84): Ode 7 Heirmos: "Росода́тельнꙋ ᲂу҆́бѡ пе́щь содѣ́ла а҆́гг҃лъ..." (*Δροσοβόλον μὲν τὴν κάμινον εἰργάσατο*).

[^248]: Fol. 80r (p84): Ode 8 Heirmos: "И҆зъ пла́мене прпⷣбнымъ ро́сꙋ и҆сточи́лъ є҆сѝ..." (*Ἐκ φλογὸς τοῖς ὁσίοις δρόσον ἐπήγασας*).

[^249]: Fol. 80v (p85): Ode 9 Heirmos: "Бг҃а человѣ́кѡмъ невозмо́жно ви́дѣти..." (*Θεὸν ἀνθρώποις ἰδεῖν ἀδύνατον*).

[^250]: Fol. 81r (p85): Exapostilarion to Saint Agapitus, summarizing his ascetic flight from worldly vanity to spiritual riches.

[^251]: Fol. 81r (p85): The melodic model is the Tone 4 prosomoion "Thou hast given a sign" (*Да́лъ є҆сѝ зна́менїе* / *Ἔδωκας σημείωσιν*).

[^252]: Fol. 81v (p86): The Doxastikon (*Сла́ва*) at the Praises highlights the ongoing bodily incorruption and wonderworking power of Saint Agapitus's relics in the Near Caves (Saint Anthony's Caves).

[^253]: Fol. 82r (p86): Visual description of the 1763 Lavra woodcut engraving for the feast of Saint John the Much-Suffering of the Caves (July 18), portraying his ascetic endurance buried up to his neck during the forty days of Great Lent and the fiery demonic serpent that assailed him in the cave.

[^254]: Fol. 82r (p86): July 18 marks the commemoration of our Venerable Father John the Much-Suffering of the Caves (†1160), who struggled thirty years in severe bodily mortification and chastity against the spirit of fornication, burying himself in a pit during Lent.

[^255]: Fol. 82r (p86): The melodic model is the Tone 8 prosomoion "O most marvelous wonder" (*Ѽ пресла́внагѡ чꙋдесе* / *Ὢ τοῦ παραδόξου θαύματος*).

[^256]: Fol. 82v (p87): Cross-reference to the three Old Testament paremias for a Venerable monastic (Wisdom 3:1–9; Wisdom 5:15–6:3; Wisdom 4:7–15) located at folio 38v on the reverse (physical leaf p43).

[^257]: Fol. 82v (p87): Melodic model: Tone 2 prosomoion "When from the Tree" (*Е҆гда̀ ѿ дре́ва* / *Ὅτε ἐκ τοῦ ξύλου*).

[^258]: Fol. 82v (p87): LXX Psalm 127:1 versification.

[^259]: Fol. 83r (p87): Troparion, Tone 4 to Saint John the Much-Suffering, summarizing his unyielding struggle against carnal temptations and spiritual defense of the Caves brethren.

[^260]: Fol. 83r (p87): Melodic model: Tone 8 prosomoion "The angelic choir" (*Ли́къ а҆́гг҃льскїй* / *Χορὸς ἀγγελικός*), recounting the manifestation of the devil as a fire-breathing serpent attacking Saint John in the cave during Great Lent.

[^261]: Fol. 83r (p87): Melodic model: Tone 8 prosomoion "That which was commanded mystically" (*Повелѣ́нное та́инственнѡ* / *Τὸ προσταχθὲν μυστικῶς*).

[^262]: Fol. 83v (p88): Melodic model: Tone 8 prosomoion "The Word co-unoriginate" (*Собеꙁнача́лное Сло́во* / *Τὸν συνάναρχον Λόγον*).

[^263]: Fol. 83v (p88): LXX Psalm 67:36, 27 versification.

[^264]: Fol. 83v (p88): Canon of Saint John the Much-Suffering, Tone 8, Ode 1 Heirmos: "Во́дꙋ проше́дъ ꙗ҆́кѡ сꙋ́шꙋ..." (*Ὑγρὰν διοδεύσας ὡσεὶ ξηράν*).

[^265]: Fol. 84r (p88): Ode 3 Heirmos: "Ты̀ є҆сѝ ᲂу҆твержде́нїе притека́ющихъ къ тебѣ̀ Гдⷭ҇и..." (*Σὺ εἶ τὸ στερέωμα τῶν προστρεχόντων σοι, Κύριε*).

[^266]: Fol. 84r (p88): Reference to Discourse 29 of the Kyiv Caves Paterikon, where Saint John the Much-Suffering shut himself in the Near Cave beside the relics of Saint Anthony to pray against temptations of the flesh; a divine voice issued from Anthony's tomb directing him to persevere in silence and reclusion.

[^267]: Fol. 84v (p89): Sessional Hymn, Tone 8, citing Revelation 14:4 ("These are they which were not defiled with women; for they are virgins. These are they which follow the Lamb wherever He goes").

[^268]: Fol. 84v (p89): Canon of Saint John the Much-Suffering, Tone 8, Ode 4 Heirmos: "Положи́лъ є҆сѝ къ на́мъ тве́рдꙋю любо́вь Гдⷭ҇и..." (*Ἔθου πρὸς ἡμᾶς κραταιὰν ἀγάπησιν, Κύριε*).

[^269]: Fol. 84v (p89): Discourse 29 of the Kyiv Caves Paterikon relates that to overcome fleshly heat, Saint John spent three years in extreme cold, stripping himself naked in the winter freeze and binding his body with heavy iron chains.

[^270]: Fol. 84v (p89): The iron chains (*вериги*) of Saint John the Much-Suffering, worn throughout his thirty years of ascetic confinement in the cave, are preserved as miraculous relics in the Kyiv Caves Lavra.

[^271]: Fol. 84v (p89): Ode 5 Heirmos: "На землѝ неви́димый ꙗ҆ви́лсѧ є҆сѝ..." (*Ἐπὶ γῆς ἀόρατος ὤφθης*).

[^272]: Fol. 84v (p89): For the forty days of Great Lent, Saint John dug a deep pit in the floor of the cave, stepped into it, and had himself covered with earth up to his breast and shoulders, enduring intense pain and demonic apparitions while praying unceasingly.

[^273]: Fol. 85r (p89): Paterikon narrative: On the night of Holy Pascha, a huge demonic serpent appeared in the cave, spewing flames and sparks, and attempted to devour the ascetic in his earthen pit.

[^274]: Fol. 85r (p89): Ode 6 Heirmos: "Бе́ꙁдна послѣ́днѧѧ грѣхѡ́въ ѡ҆бы́де мѧ̀..." (*Ἄβυσσος ἐσχάτη ἁμαρτιῶν ἐκύκλωσέ με*).

[^275]: Fol. 85r (p89): The serpent took Saint John's head into its jaws, scorching his hair and beard; the saint cried out to Christ, whereupon a flash of divine lightning drove the monster away.

[^276]: Fol. 85v (p90): Kontakion, Tone 8 to Saint John the Much-Suffering, praising his lifelong victory over spiritual and physical warfare.

[^277]: Fol. 85v (p90): Ikos to Saint John, summarizing his ascetic regime of prolonged reclusion, fasting, tears, and bodily exhaustion.

[^278]: Fol. 85v (p90): Ode 7 Heirmos: "Ꙗ҆́коже дре́вле бл҃гочести́выѧ три̑ ѻ҆́троки ѡ҆роси́лъ є҆сѝ..." (*Ὥσπερ πάλαι τοὺς εὐσεβεῖς τρεῖς παῖδας ἐδρόσισας*).

[^279]: Fol. 86r (p90): Ode 8 Heirmos: "И҆нтерпѣ́нномꙋ ѻ҆гню̀ соедини́вшесѧ..." (*Τῷ ἀστέκτῳ πυρὶ ἑνωθέντες*).

[^280]: Fol. 86r (p90): Paterikon account: After years of torment, a divine voice told Saint John to pray to the newly reposed monk Saint Moses the Hungarian (†1043), who had preserved perfect chastity during seven years of severe captivity in Poland.

[^281]: Fol. 86r (p90): John went to Saint Moses's tomb in the cave, took a bone from the relics, and touched it to his ailing body, after which all carnal passion was permanently extinguished and divine light enveloped his cell.

[^282]: Fol. 86r (p90): Ode 9 Heirmos: "Но́вое чꙋ́до и҆ Бг҃олѣ́пное..." (*Καινὸν θαῦμα καὶ θεοπρεπές*).

[^283]: Fol. 86v (p91): Theotokion concluding Ode 9 of the Canon to Saint John the Much-Suffering.

[^284]: Fol. 86v (p91): Exapostilarion (Свѣти́ленъ) to Saint John the Much-Suffering, reflecting on Psalm 125:5 ("They that sow in tears shall reap in joy").

[^285]: Fol. 86v (p91): Stichera at the Praises, Tone 4. Podoben model: "Thou hast given a sign" (*Ἔδωκας σημείωσιν*).

[^286]: Fol. 86v (p91): Doxasticon (Glory) at the Praises in Tone 6, honoring the victory of Saint John over carnal warfare and demonic assault.

[^287]: Fol. 87r (p91): Woodcut engraving depicting Saint Moses the Hungarian (†1043), with six biographical scenes illustrating his refusal of the Polish noblewoman's advances, his imprisonment, torture, and eventual monastic tonsure in the Kyiv Caves.

[^288]: Fol. 87r (p91): Commemoration of our Venerable Father Moses the Hungarian, feast celebrated on July 26.

[^289]: Fol. 87r (p91): Stichera at "Lord, I have cried," Tone 1. Podoben model: "Joy of the heavenly ranks" (*Τῶν οὐρανίων ταγμάτων*).

[^290]: Fol. 87v (p92): Typological comparison of Saint Moses the Hungarian to the Prophet Moses and the burning bush (Exod. 3:2), reflecting the preservation of his purity amid the burning furnace of lust.

[^291]: Fol. 87v (p92): Doxasticon (Glory) on "Lord, I have cried" in Tone 6, calling the faithful to celebrate the summer feast of the Caves ascetic.

[^292]: Fol. 87v (p92): Rubrical cross-reference directing the reader to Fol. 38v on the reverse for the three Old Testament readings of a venerable monk (Wisdom 3:1–9; Wisdom 5:15–6:3; Wisdom 4:7–15).

[^293]: Fol. 87v (p92): Aposticha Stichera, Tone 3. Podoben model: "O all-lauded martyrs" (*Πανεύφημοι μάρτυρες*).

[^294]: Fol. 87v (p92): Saint Moses the Hungarian had been a faithful bodyguard and servant to the holy passion-bearer Prince Boris of Rostov until the latter's martyrdom on the Alta River in 1015.

[^295]: Fol. 87v (p92): LXX Psalm 111:1.

[^296]: Fol. 88r (p92): LXX Psalm 149:5.

[^297]: Fol. 88r (p92): Following the murder of Prince Boris and his brother George (Moses's own brother), Moses escaped the massacre and found temporary refuge with Predslava, sister of Prince Yaroslav the Wise, in Kyiv.

[^298]: Fol. 88r (p92): Doxasticon (Glory) at the Aposticha in Tone 8, inviting the Russian monastics to venerate the incorrupt relics of Saint Moses in the Near Caves.

[^299]: Fol. 88r (p92): First Dismissal Troparion, Tone 3, comparing Saint Moses to the Patriarch Joseph in Egypt (Gen. 39).

[^300]: Fol. 88r (p92): Second Dismissal Troparion, Tone 3, recounting his endurance of starvation, fetters, and castration at the hands of the Polish noblewoman.

[^301]: Fol. 88r (p92): Sessional Hymn after Kathisma 1, Tone 4. Podoben model: "Speedily anticipate" (*Ταχὺ προκατάλαβε*).

[^302]: Fol. 88v (p93): Conclusion of Sessional Hymn after Kathisma 1, recounting the attempted seduction and Moses's steadfast preservation of virginity.

[^303]: Fol. 88v (p93): Sessional Hymn after Kathisma 2, Tone 4. Podoben model: "Thou hast appeared today" (*Ἐπεφάνης σήμερον*).

[^304]: Fol. 88v (p93): Moses's steadfastness is portrayed as an unquenchable divine fire that overcame the physical fire of carnal temptation.

[^305]: Fol. 88v (p93): Theotokion quoting the Archangel Gabriel's greeting to the Mother of God (Luke 1:28).

[^306]: Fol. 88v (p93): Sessional Hymn after the Polyeleos, Tone 5. Podoben model: "The Word co-unoriginate" (*Τὸν συνάναρχον Λόγον*).

[^307]: Fol. 89r (p93): LXX Psalm 115:6.

[^308]: Fol. 89r (p93): LXX Psalm 115:3.

[^309]: Fol. 89r (p93): Common Matins Gospel for a Venerable Monastic: Matthew composite section 43 (Matt. 11:27–30).

[^310]: Fol. 89r (p93): Canon to Saint Moses the Hungarian, Tone 2, Ode 1 Heirmos: "Грѧди́те лю́дїе, пои́мъ пѣ́снь..." (*Δεῦτε λαοί, ᾄσωμεν ᾆσμα*).

[^311]: Fol. 89r (p93): Job 1:21 ("The Lord gave, and the Lord has taken away; blessed be the name of the Lord").

[^312]: Fol. 89r (p93): Reference to Kyiv Caves Paterikon Discourse 30: Moses escaped the 1015 fratricidal massacre by Svyatopolk the Accursed and reported Prince Boris's martyrdom to Yaroslav's sister Predslava in Kyiv.

[^313]: Fol. 89v (p94): Moses initially fled to Kyiv after Prince Boris was killed, concealing himself until God called him to witness through suffering.

[^314]: Fol. 89v (p94): Ode 3 Heirmos: "На ка́мени мѧ̀ вѣ́ры ᲂу҆тверди́въ..." (*Ἐν πέτρᾳ με τῆς πίστεως στερεώσας*).

[^315]: Fol. 89v (p94): In 1018, King Bolesław I the Brave captured Kyiv and took Moses captive to Poland, where he was bound in chains for five years.

[^316]: Fol. 89v (p94): Allusion to the Prayer of Azariah (Dan. 3:27; LXX Dan. 3:31: "Righteous art thou in all that thou hast done to us").

[^317]: Fol. 89v (p94): Discourse 30 of the Kyiv Caves Paterikon relates that a wealthy Polish noblewoman fell in love with Moses upon seeing his youthful beauty in chains.

[^318]: Fol. 89v (p94): Moses's steadfast resistance to the noblewoman, citing biblical examples of men led into ruin through women (e.g. Adam, Samson, Solomon).

[^319]: Fol. 90r (p94): Moses declared himself bought with the precious Blood of Christ (1 Cor. 6:20; 7:23), refusing to trade spiritual freedom for worldly comfort.

[^320]: Fol. 90r (p94): Sessional Hymn after Ode 3, Tone 5.

[^321]: Fol. 90r (p94): Ode 4 Heirmos: "Прише́лъ є҆сѝ ѿ Дѣ́вы, не хода́тай..." (*Ἐλήλυθας ἐκ Παρθένου, οὐ πρέσβυς*).

[^322]: Fol. 90r (p94): LXX Psalm 22:4 ("For though I should walk in the midst of the shadow of death, I will fear no evils: for Thou art with me"). The noblewoman paid a large ransom to purchase Moses from his captor and brought him into her estate.

[^323]: Fol. 90r (p94): Typological parallel between Christ being sold for thirty pieces of silver and Moses being purchased to be subjected to carnal temptation and physical torture.

[^324]: Fol. 90v (p95): In the noblewoman's palace, Moses refused rich clothing and delicacies, choosing coarse sackcloth, dry bread, and water to quell carnal passions.

[^325]: Fol. 90v (p95): Gen. 39:12 (Joseph leaving his garment in the hand of Potiphar's wife).

[^326]: Fol. 90v (p95): The Polish noblewoman is likened to Potiphar's wife, the "Egyptian woman" of the Book of Genesis.

[^327]: Fol. 90v (p95): LXX Psalm 71:6 ("He shall come down like rain upon a fleece").

[^328]: Fol. 90v (p95): Ode 5 Heirmos: "Свѣ́та пода́телю и҆ вѣкѡ́мъ тво́рче Гдⷭ҇и..." (*Ὁ τοῦ φωτὸς χορηγός, καὶ τῶν αἰώνων ποιητής*).

[^329]: Fol. 90v (p95): When Moses repeatedly refused her propositions, the noblewoman had him thrown into a dark dungeon and starved.

[^330]: Fol. 90v (p95): Allusion to 1 Kings 17:4–6 (Elijah fed by ravens) and the Life of Saint Paul of Thebes; one of the noblewoman's servants took pity on Moses and brought him secret sustenance.

[^331]: Fol. 90v (p95): Friends and fellow captives urged Moses to yield to the noblewoman's desires and become ruler over her vast estates, which he firmly rejected.

[^332]: Fol. 91r (p95): Having achieved mastery over carnal passions through extreme ascetical endurance, Saint Moses is invoked throughout Eastern Slavic monasticism as a heavenly patron against sins of the flesh.

[^333]: Fol. 91r (p95): The noblewoman attempted to force Moses into luxurious clothing and seated him beside her in a chariot to tour her lands, but he openly derided the vain spectacle.

[^334]: Fol. 91r (p95): Ode 6 Heirmos: "Въ бе́зднѣ грѣхо́внѣй валя́ѧсѧ..." (*Ἐν ἀβύσσῳ πταισμάτων κυκλούμενος*).

[^335]: Fol. 91r (p95): While imprisoned in Poland, a traveling priest-monk from Mount Athos visited Moses and secretly tonsured him into the monastic schema.

[^336]: Fol. 91r (p95): Enraged by his monastic tonsure, the noblewoman ordered Moses stretched out upon the ground and severely flogged with rods until his blood saturated the soil.

[^337]: Fol. 91r (p95): Rom. 8:35, 38–39 ("Who shall separate us from the love of Christ? Shall tribulation, or distress, or persecution, or famine, or nakedness, or peril, or sword?").

[^338]: Fol. 91v (p96): Moses was brought before King Bolesław I, whom he warned that both the king and the noblewoman would soon face sudden divine judgment (fulfilled in the Polish civil uprising of 1030–1031).

[^339]: Fol. 91v (p96): Theotokion of Ode 6, reflecting on internal spiritual warfare as a court before an earthly prince.

[^340]: Fol. 91v (p96): Kontakion, Tone 3 to Saint Moses the Hungarian.

[^341]: Fol. 91v (p96): Ode 7 Heirmos: "Бг҃опроти́вное велѣ́нїе..." (*Ἀντίθεον πρόσταγμα*).

[^342]: Fol. 91v (p96): Allusion to Dan. 3:52 ("Blessed art thou, O Lord God of our fathers").

[^343]: Fol. 92r (p96): LXX Psalm 15:8 ("I foresaw the Lord always before my face, for He is on my right hand, that I should not be moved").

[^344]: Fol. 92r (p96): Paterikon account: The noblewoman commanded that Moses be given one hundred lashes daily with cudgels to break his resolve.

[^345]: Fol. 92r (p96): Finally, the noblewoman ordered Moses castrated; left for dead, he miraculously survived and recovered his strength.

[^346]: Fol. 92r (p96): Ode 8 Heirmos: "Въ пе́щь ѻ҆́гненнꙋю ко ѻ҆трокѡ́мъ є҆вре́йскимъ..." (*Εἰς τὴν κάμινον τὴν πυρὸς καταβάντα*).

[^347]: Fol. 92r (p96): LXX Psalm 69:2 ("O God, draw nigh unto my help: O Lord, make haste to help me").

[^348]: Fol. 92r (p96): King Bolesław expelled monastics from Poland; soon thereafter, Bolesław died suddenly in 1025, and violent revolts engulfed Poland.

[^349]: Fol. 92r (p96): During the Polish rebellion, peasants and servants revolted against the nobility; the noblewoman who tormented Moses was killed by her own serfs.

[^350]: Fol. 92v (p97): Moses escaped Poland and arrived in Kyiv, where Saint Anthony received him into the Caves monastery; Moses bore the scars of his torture as marks of Christ (Gal. 6:17).

[^351]: Fol. 92v (p97): Moses lived ten years in seclusion in the Kyiv Caves, renowned as a spiritual physician for those struggling with carnal passions.

[^352]: Fol. 92v (p97): Ode 9 Heirmos: "Ѿ Бг҃а Бг҃а Сло́ва..." (*Τὸν ἐκ Θεοῦ Θεὸν Λόγον*).

[^353]: Fol. 92v (p97): Paterikon account: A monk struggling with carnal temptations came to Moses; the saint touched him with his staff, and all lustful desires immediately vanished.

[^354]: Fol. 92v (p97): Matt. 5:8 ("Blessed are the pure in heart: for they shall see God").

[^355]: Fol. 92v (p97): Allusion to the healing of Saint John the Much-Suffering through the relics of Saint Moses (cf. Discourse 29).

[^356]: Fol. 93r (p97): The uncorrupted relics of Saint Moses the Hungarian rest in the Near (Antony) Caves of the Kyiv Caves Lavra.

[^357]: Fol. 93r (p97): Exapostilarion (Свѣти́ленъ) to the Holy Trinity invoking the intercession of Saint Moses.

[^358]: Fol. 93r (p97): Stichera at the Praises, Tone 4. Podoben model: "As one noble" (*Ὡς γενναῖον ἐν Μάρτυσιν*).

[^359]: Fol. 93r (p97): Phil. 1:23 ("Having a desire to depart, and to be with Christ; which is far better").

[^360]: Fol. 93v (p98): Praises stichera conclusion honoring Saint Moses the Hungarian as a noetic visionary and protector against impure thoughts.

[^361]: Fol. 93v (p98): Doxasticon (Glory) at the Praises, Tone 6, synthesizing Moses's physical sufferings with his ascetical triumph.

[^362]: Fol. 94r (p98): Large woodcut engraving of Saint Pimen the Much-Ailing (†1110), depicting his lifelong illness and his miraculous tonsure by angels in the Dormition Cathedral of the Caves monastery.

[^363]: Fol. 94r (p98): Commemoration of our Venerable Father Pimen the Much-Ailing of the Caves, celebrated on August 7.

[^364]: Fol. 94r (p98): Stichera on "Lord, I have cried," Tone 4. Podoben model: "Joy of the heavenly ranks" (*Τῶν οὐρανίων ταγμάτων*).

[^365]: Fol. 94v (p99): Discourse 35 of the Kyiv Caves Paterikon relates that Saint Pimen had been sickly and bedridden from infancy, yet gave thanks to God and prayed that his illness might not cease, fearing that physical health would lead him away from monastic life.

[^366]: Fol. 94v (p99): LXX Psalm 62:9 ("My soul has cleaved after Thee: Thy right hand has upholden me").

[^367]: Fol. 94v (p99): Pimen's voluntary acceptance of infirmity transformed his sickbed into a spiritual altar.

[^368]: Fol. 94v (p99): Doxasticon (Glory) at "Lord, I have cried," Tone 5. When Pimen's parents brought him to the Caves monastery to be healed, he prayed instead for his illness to continue so he could remain; angels appeared at night in the guise of monks, holding candles, and tonsured him into the monastic habit.

[^369]: Fol. 94v (p99): The feast of Saint Pimen (August 7) falls within the Forefeast of the Transfiguration or Afterfeast of the Holy Cross.

[^370]: Fol. 94v (p99): Rubrical cross-reference to Fol. 30v for the three Old Testament readings of a venerable monk.

[^371]: Fol. 94v (p99): Aposticha Stichera in Tone 4, Automela (sung to their own melody).

[^372]: Fol. 94v (p99): Paterikon narrative: The angels who tonsured Pimen left behind the hair from his tonsure and a burning candle upon the tomb of Saint Theodosius as proof of their celestial visitation.

[^373]: Fol. 94v (p99): LXX Psalm 115:6.

[^374]: Fol. 94v (p99): For twenty years Pimen remained bedridden in the monastery infirmary, rotting with purulent ulcers, yet comforting others who were sick.

[^375]: Fol. 94v (p99): LXX Psalm 111:1.

[^376]: Fol. 95r (p99): Sessional sticheron conclusion emphasizing Pimen's patient endurance as a fiery ascent to God.

[^377]: Fol. 95r (p99): Doxasticon (Glory) at the Aposticha in Tone 8, praising the healing grace flowing from Pimen's relics in the Near Caves.

[^378]: Fol. 95r (p99): Dismissal Troparion, Tone 5, comparing Saint Pimen's patient endurance to that of the Righteous Job.

[^379]: Fol. 95r (p99): Sessional Hymn after Kathisma 1, Tone 5. Podoben model: "The Word co-unoriginate" (*Τὸν συνάναρχον Λόγον*).

[^380]: Fol. 95r (p99): Theotokion following Kathisma 1 Sessional Hymn, invoking the painless birthgiving of the Virgin Mary to soothe painful illnesses.

[^381]: Fol. 95r (p99): Sessional Hymn after Kathisma 2, Tone 3. Podoben model: "Speedily anticipate" (*Ταхὺ προκατάλαβε*).

[^382]: Fol. 95v (p100): Sessional Hymn after the Polyeleos, Tone 8. Podoben model: "Having reclined on Wisdom" (*Премꙋ́дрости возле́гъ* / *Τῇ σοφίᾳ προσκλιθείς*).

[^383]: Fol. 95v (p100): Paradox of Saint Pimen: though bedridden and rotting with sores himself, God granted him the miraculous gift of healing others of severe sicknesses.

[^384]: Fol. 95v (p100): LXX Psalm 149:5.

[^385]: Fol. 95v (p100): LXX Psalm 149:1.

[^386]: Fol. 95v (p100): Common Matins Gospel for a Venerable Monastic (Matt. 11:27–30).

[^387]: Fol. 95v (p100): Canon to Saint Pimen the Much-Ailing, Tone 2, Ode 1 Heirmos: "Во глубинѣ́ постла̀ и҆ногда̀..." (*Ἐν βυθῷ κατέστρωσέ ποτε*).

[^388]: Fol. 95v (p100): Typological parallel between Israel crossing the Red Sea dryshod (Exod. 14) and Pimen traversing the sea of worldly passions unharmed by sin.

[^389]: Fol. 96r (p100): Matt. 14:30–31 (Christ stretching forth His hand to save the sinking Peter).

[^390]: Fol. 96r (p100): Ode 3 Heirmos: "Процвѣла̀ є҆́сть пꙋсты́нѧ..." (*Ἐξήνθησεν ἡ ἔρημος*).

[^391]: Fol. 96r (p100): Isaiah 35:1 ("The wilderness shall blossom as the lily").

[^392]: Fol. 96r (p100): Song of Songs 2:1 ("I am the rose of Sharon, and the lily of the valleys").

[^393]: Fol. 96r (p100): Song of Songs 2:2 ("As the lily among thorns, so is my love among the daughters").

[^394]: Fol. 96r (p100): Sessional Hymn after Ode 3, Tone 2.

[^395]: Fol. 96r (p100): Paterikon account: Whenever the brethren prayed for Pimen's recovery, he prayed that God would rather keep him ill, and his individual prayer prevailed over that of the community.

[^396]: Fol. 96r (p100): Traditional liturgical troparion formula: "A mother's prayer avails much unto the winning over of the Master" (*Πολλὰ ἰσχύει δέησις μητρός*).

[^397]: Fol. 96r (p100): Ode 4 Heirmos: "Пою́ тѧ, слꙋ́хомъ бо Гдⷭ҇и ᲂу҆слы́шахъ..." (*ᾌδω σοι, ἀκοῇ γὰρ Κύριε εἰσακήκοα*).

[^398]: In the 1763 Church Slavonic text: `вели́чїю неѕло́бїе`, where `вели́чїю` functions intensively/adverbially for `вели́кое неѕло́бїе` (thy great innocence).

[^399]: Cf. Revelation 7:17 and 21:4: "God shall wipe away every tear from their eyes."

[^400]: Heirmos of Ode 5, Tone 2; cf. 1 Timothy 2:5.

[^401]: The Caves monastery of Kyiv.

[^402]: An episode from Discourse 34 of the Kyiv Caves Patericon: St. Pimen the Much-Ailing foresaw the death of a negligent monk who had assumed the great schema without spiritual labor; after death, the monk's schema remained intact in the tomb as an enduring witness of his reproof.

[^403]: Heirmos of Ode 6, Tone 2; cf. Jonah 2:2–6.

[^404]: Cf. 1 John 5:19: "The whole world lies in evil."

[^405]: Reference to the historical narrative in the Patericon wherein the attending brethren, weary of Pimen's stench and protracted infirmity, abandoned him for days without bread or water.

[^406]: Kontakion of St. Pimen the Much-Ailing, Tone 6.

[^407]: Ikos of St. Pimen, commemorating his twenty years of unmurmuring patience on the bed of sickness.

[^408]: Heirmos of Ode 7, Tone 2; cf. Daniel 3.

[^409]: James 4:4: "Whosoever therefore will be a friend of the world is the enemy of God."

[^410]: Romans 7:19: "For the good that I would I do not: but the evil which I would not, that I do."

[^411]: Heirmos of Ode 8, Tone 2; cf. Daniel 3:19–27.

[^412]: Cf. Job 1:22: "In all this Job sinned not, nor charged God foolishly."

[^413]: Heirmos of Ode 9, Tone 2.

[^414]: Cf. Matthew 22:11–12 and Revelation 19:9: the garment of the wedding feast of the Lamb.

[^415]: Podoben melodic model of Tone 8: Ѽ пресла́внагѡ чꙋдесѐ (O marvelous wonder).

[^416]: Podoben melodic model of Tone 6: Всѐ ѿло́жше (Having laid all aside); cf. Matthew 6:19–20.

[^417]: Cf. 2 Corinthians 8:9: Christ became poor for our sakes; Matthew 16:24 and 7:14: taking up the cross on the narrow way.

[^418]: 1 Timothy 6:16: dwelling in unapproachable light.

[^419]: Podoben melodic model of Tone 4: Да́лъ є҆сѝ зна́менїе (Thou hast given a sign); cf. Psalm 59:6 [LXX].

[^420]: Matthew 22:37–39: loving God and neighbor as oneself.

[^421]: Colossians 4:6: speech seasoned with salt; and Discourse 33 of the Kyiv Caves Patericon regarding St. Basil discerning the demon that appeared in his own form to deceive St. Theodore.

[^422]: Cf. Proverbs 18:19: a brother helped by a brother is as a strong city; 2 Corinthians 6:15: Belial.

[^423]: Ephesians 6:16: the shield of faith; Revelation 7:14: robes washed in the blood of the Lamb.

[^424]: Rubrical cross-reference to the common readings for venerable martyrs on fol. 38v.

[^425]: Podoben melodic model of Tone 2: Е҆гда̀ ѿ дре́ва (When from the Tree).

[^426]: Cf. Psalm 65:12 [LXX]: "Thou broughtest us out into a wealthy place."

[^427]: Psalm 149:5 [LXX].

[^428]: The monastic cell of Theodore in the Kyiv Caves transformed into a spiritual paradise.

[^429]: Psalm 115:6 [LXX].

[^430]: Allusion to Jacob's ladder (Genesis 28:12) spiritually linking the Kyiv Caves mountain to heaven.

[^431]: Troparion of the Holy Venerable-Martyrs Theodore and Basil of the Caves, Tone 4.

[^432]: Podoben melodic model of Tone 4: Гро́бъ тво́й Сп҃се (Thy tomb, O Savior).

[^433]: Job 23:10: tried as gold.

[^434]: Podoben melodic model of Tone 4: Ꙗ҆ви́лсѧ є҆сѝ дне́сь (Thou hast appeared today).

[^435]: Spiritual flock of the Kyiv Caves monastery.

[^436]: Song of Songs 4:12: "A garden enclosed is my sister, my spouse."

[^437]: Psalm 115:6 [LXX].

[^438]: Psalm 115:3 [LXX].

[^439]: Matthew 10:32 ff., Slavic pericope 38.

[^440]: Heirmos of Ode 1, Tone 2.

[^441]: Discourse 33 of the Kyiv Caves Patericon: St. Theodore gave away all his immense earthly wealth to the poor before becoming a monk.

[^442]: 1 John 5:19.

[^443]: The demon tempted Theodore with sorrow and regret over the wealth he had given away; Theodore confessed his temptation to Basil, who healed him through holy counsel.

[^444]: 1 Corinthians 7:31: "For the fashion of this world passes away."

[^445]: Heirmos of Ode 3, Tone 2; cf. 1 Samuel 2:4 [LXX 1 Kings 2:4].

[^446]: Ephesians 6:17: the sword of the Spirit, the word of God.

[^447]: Patericon Discourse 33: Basil told Theodore the instructive story of a woman who wept over lost charity to awaken Theodore's soul from the demonic trance of avarice.

[^448]: The demon appeared to Theodore transformed into the likeness of Basil, urging him to seek treasure and flee from the monastery.

[^449]: 1 Corinthians 10:12: "Let him that thinks he stands take heed lest he fall"; Matthew 12:45.

[^450]: Sessional Hymn of the Venerable-Martyrs, Tone 8.

[^451]: 1 Corinthians 15:33: "Evil communications corrupt good manners."

[^452]: Heirmos of Ode 4, Tone 2; cf. Habakkuk 3:2 [LXX].

[^453]: 1 Timothy 6:9: "They that will be rich fall into temptation and a snare." In the Patericon, the demon noticed that Theodore failed to seal his speech with the Jesus Prayer.

[^454]: Matthew 13:46: the Pearl of great price. Theodore unearthed an ancient Varangian hoard of gold hidden in the cave.

[^455]: The demon in Basil's guise urged Theodore to take the Varangian gold, buy villages, and abandon the Kyiv Caves.

[^456]: Matthew 6:34: "Take no thought for the morrow"; 1 Peter 5:7: "Casting all your care upon Him, for He cares for you."

[^457]: Matthew 6:25.

[^458]: Heirmos of Ode 5, Tone 2; cf. Isaiah 6:6.

[^459]: Theodore, ashamed to abandon his vows yet tempted by the hoard of gold, sought to remain in the cave while succumbing to the demon's bidding.

[^460]: Divine preservation of St. Theodore through the patronage of the Kyiv-Pechersk Theotokos.

[^461]: Discourse 33 of the Kyiv Caves Patericon: Theodore, deceived by the demon in Basil's likeness, drove away the true Basil when he returned.

[^462]: Heirmos of Ode 6, Tone 2.

[^463]: Cf. Job 1:21: Job's prayer; and the formula demanded by Theodore: "The Lord Jesus Christ is the Author of our salvation!"

[^464]: St. Theodore re-buried the Varangian gold in an undisclosed pit in the caves, where it remains hidden.

[^465]: Theodore toiled at the heavy hand-mill to crush all demonic laziness through bodily labor.

[^466]: A famous episode in Discourse 33 of the Kyiv Caves Patericon: the demon was forced under holy obedience to grind five carts of grain.

[^467]: Kontakion of the Venerable-Martyrs Theodore and Basil, Tone 2.

[^468]: Ikos of the Venerable-Martyrs.

[^469]: Heirmos of Ode 7, Tone 2; cf. Daniel 3:1–23.

[^470]: Patristic practice of manifestation of thoughts (discretio / rozsudzhennia).

[^471]: Monastic self-sufficiency and rejection of spiritual acedia through physical manual labor.

[^472]: Demons rolling the firewood down the Pechersk ravine, which Theodore patient-enduringly carried up again.

[^473]: The binding of demons to haul heavy oak timber for the restoration of the Pechersk monastery buildings.

[^474]: Prophecy of the demons in the Patericon that they would incite Prince Mstislav Svyatopolkovych to torture the saints.

[^475]: Heirmos of Ode 8, Tone 2; cf. Daniel 3.

[^476]: Incitement of Prince Mstislav against St. Theodore regarding the hidden Varangian treasure.

[^477]: The demon in Basil's likeness told Prince Mstislav's boyar that Theodore possessed hidden gold.

[^478]: Discourse 33 of the Kyiv Caves Patericon: Prince Mstislav Svyatopolkovych of Novgorod and Kyiv personally interrogated Theodore regarding the Varangian hoard.

[^479]: Matthew 25:25–26: parable of the buried talent.

[^480]: Heirmos of Ode 9, Tone 2.

[^481]: Philippians 1:23: "Having a desire to depart, and to be with Christ."

[^482]: Prince Mstislav ordered Theodore hung up in smoke and roasted with fire; his hair-shirt miraculously remained unharmed.

[^483]: St. Basil was brought from the cave and reproved the prince for believing a demon's lies.

[^484]: St. Basil drew the prince's arrow from his own body and prophesied that Mstislav would die by that very arrow; Prince Mstislav was killed by an arrow shot through the neck during the siege of Volodymyr in 1099.

[^485]: Exapostilarion of the Venerable-Martyrs Theodore and Basil.

[^486]: Automela of the Praises, Tone 1.

[^487]: Matthew 5:15: a candle not under a bushel. The relics of Theodore and Basil resting in the Near Caves of Kyiv.

[^488]: Rubrical direction appointing the Common Service for Venerable-Martyrs from the General Menaion.

[^489]: Podoben model of Tone 4: Зва́нный свы́ше (Called from on high).

[^490]: Discourse 34 of the Kyiv Caves Patericon: St. Alypius the Iconographer (+1114), who learned icon painting from the Byzantine masters of the Great Church of the Caves.

[^491]: 2 Corinthians 3:3: "In fleshy tables of the heart."

[^492]: An angel miraculously completed the icon of the Dormition for Alypius when the saint was dying.

[^493]: The miracle of St. Alypius's icons remaining unharmed in fires and driving away demons.

[^494]: St. Alypius healed a man from Kyiv suffering from severe leprosy by washing his sores with the water and paints used for holy icons; cf. Patericon Discourse 34.

[^495]: Troparion to St. Alypius the Iconographer of the Caves, Tone 8: *Зрими изобразуя святых на дщицах...* Alypius (Alipius) reposed c. 1114.

[^496]: Heirmos of Ode 1, Tone 8: *Пѣснь возсылаемъ людїе...* (Heirmologion Tone 8).

[^497]: Historical note on the expansion of the Kyiv-Pechersk monastic community under Hegumen Theodosius (reposed 1074) and Hegumen Nikon (reposed 1088).

[^498]: In the Kyiv Caves Paterikon (Discourse 34), Alypius's parents brought him to the monastery and apprenticed him to the Greek iconographers sent from Constantinople to adorn the Great Church of the Caves.

[^499]: Allusion to the miracle during the decoration of the sanctuary altar when a white dove, symbolizing the Holy Spirit, flew from the mouth of the icon of the Theotokos.

[^500]: Heirmos of Ode 3, Tone 8: *Нѣсть святъ, яко Господь...* (1 Samuel [1 Kings LXX] 2:2).

[^501]: Refers to the miraculous depiction of the icon of the Dormition in the sanctuary of the Great Church of the Kyiv-Pechersk Lavra.

[^502]: The Paterikon relates that as the Greek masters worked in the apse, a radiant dove flew forth from the mouth of the icon of the Virgin, flew into the mouths of Christ and the saints depicted in the icon, and hovered throughout the entire church.

[^503]: Classic monastic and biblical metaphor: comparing the heart washed clean by tears of repentance unto an unblemished writing tablet or parchment scroll (*свитокъ*).

[^504]: Sessional Hymn to St. Alypius, Tone 4: *Божїя славы зрителя...*

[^505]: Heirmos of Ode 4, Tone 8: *Изъ горы приосѣненныя, Слове...* (Habakkuk [Habacuc LXX] 3:3).

[^506]: The Kyiv Caves Paterikon records that St. Alypius refused payment for painting icons, dividing whatever patrons freely gave into three portions: for icon paints and wood, for alms to the poor, and for the monastery treasury.

[^507]: Scriptural allusion to the Apostle Paul's words in 2 Thessalonians 3:8 and Acts 20:34, as well as the Parable of the Laborers in the Vineyard (Matthew 20:1–16).

[^508]: Heirmos of Ode 5, Tone 8: *Утренююще вопїемъ ти Господи...* (Isaiah 26:9).

[^509]: Explicit celebration of the tripartite division of earnings practiced by St. Alypius throughout his monastic life.

[^510]: Scriptural allusion to Luke 9:62: "No man, having put his hand to the plow, and looking back, is fit for the kingdom of God."

[^511]: Heirmos of Ode 6, Tone 8: *Ризу мнѣ подаждь свѣтлу...* (Psalm 103 [104]:2).

[^512]: Historical reference to Hegumen Nikon ordaining Alypius as a hieromonk on account of his exemplary asceticism and virtues.

[^513]: Miracle recorded in the Paterikon: a wealthy citizen of Kyiv stricken with incurable sores and leprosy was healed when St. Alypius anointed his face and body with the water and paints used for his icons.

[^514]: Kontakion to St. Alypius, Tone 2: *Отъ юности божественное въ себѣ имѣя начертанїе...*

[^515]: Heirmos of Ode 7, Tone 8: *Отроцы еврейстїи въ пещи...* (Daniel 3).

[^516]: The Kyiv man, before coming to St. Alypius, had squandered his wealth on physicians and magicians who only aggravated his leprosy; his repentance brought immediate healing.

[^517]: The Paterikon emphasizes that Alypius instructed the leper to confess his sins first and receive Holy Communion before washing his face with icon water.

[^518]: Mystical exposition of the physical senses being corrupted through sin, particularly illicit scents and carnal desires, contrasted with spiritual health.

[^519]: Biblical typological parallel: the cleansing of Naaman the Syrian by the Prophet Elisha in the Jordan River (4 Kings [2 Kings] 5).

[^520]: Heirmos of Ode 8, Tone 8: *На горѣ святѣй прославльшася...* (Exodus 3:2).

[^521]: Narrative of a client who commissioned seven icons from St. Alypius; two slanderous monks stole the boards and accused Alypius of neglect before Prince Vladimir Monomakh, yet Alypius bore their deceit with total meekness.

[^522]: Scriptural citation of Psalm 7:16 [LXX 7:15]: "He made a pit, and digged it, and is fallen into the ditch which he made."

[^523]: The vindication of St. Alypius: an angel miraculously painted the seven icons and brought them directly to the patron, confounding the slanderers.

[^524]: Heirmos of Ode 9, Tone 8: *Тя неискусобрачную Матерь Бога вышняго...*

[^525]: Miracle of the great fire in Podil (Kyiv) in 1124: while the wooden church was reduced to ashes, the icons painted by Alypius were recovered completely untouched by fire.

[^526]: The people of Kyiv assembled in amazement to witness the five icons of the Deisis painted by St. Alypius intact amid the glowing embers.

[^527]: The icon of the Dormition sent by Prince Vladimir Monomakh to Rostov: when the Rostov cathedral collapsed during a fire, the icon was found completely unhurt in the ruins.

[^528]: Scriptural citation of Psalm 60:4 [LXX 60:3]: "For Thou hast been a shelter for me, and a strong tower from the enemy."

[^529]: Exapostilarion to St. Alypius: *Присно храняй тя преподобне...*

[^530]: The saint's final miracle: being near death and unable to finish a commissioned icon of the Dormition for the feast, an angel in radiant white appeared, painted the icon in three hours, and carried it to the church, whereupon Alypius reposed in peace.

[^531]: Stichera at the Praises to St. Alypius the Iconographer, Tone 4. Podoben: *Яко добля...*

[^532]: Scriptural citation of Psalm 91:14 [LXX 91:13]: "Planted in the house of the Lord, they shall flourish in the courts of our God."

[^533]: Psalm 91:13 [LXX 91:12]: "The righteous shall flourish like the palm tree: he shall grow like a cedar in Lebanon."

[^534]: Scriptural citation of Psalm 131:4 [LXX 131:4]: "I will not give sleep to mine eyes, or slumber to mine eyelids."

[^535]: Woodcut engraving on Fol. 110r depicting St. Kuksha the Hieromartyr and St. Pimen the Faster standing full-length before the Kyiv-Pechersk Lavra, with predella panels depicting St. Kuksha's martyrdom and St. Pimen's contemporaneous vision.

[^536]: Commemoration of the Hieromartyr Kuksha and the Venerable Pimen the Faster on August 27 in the Kyiv Caves liturgical menology.

[^537]: Stichera at "Lord, I have cried," Tone 2. Podoben: *Егда от древа...*

[^538]: St. Kuksha, an ascetic of the Kyiv Caves, was sent as an enlightened missionary to the pagan Vyatichi tribe in the early 12th century.

[^539]: St. Kuksha was beheaded along with his disciple (Nikon) by pagan Vyatichi priests c. 1114 after performing countless miracles and baptisms.

[^540]: Historical context from the Kyiv Caves Paterikon (Discourse 33): St. Kuksha was renowned for his preaching along the Oka River, baptizing thousands before being martyred.

[^541]: Troparion to Saints Kuksha and Pimen, Tone 3: *Мученическою кровїю священне Кукшо...*

[^542]: Heirmos of Ode 1, Tone 4: *Моря чермную пучину...* (Exodus 14–15; Heirmologion Tone 4).

[^543]: Scriptural citation of Psalm 115:6 [LXX 115:5]: "Precious in the sight of the Lord is the death of His saints."

[^544]: The hymnographer pairs the martyred St. Kuksha and his disciple with St. Pimen the Faster, invoking them as a triple intercessory shield against the world, the flesh, and the devil.

[^545]: Heirmos of Ode 3, Tone 4: *Веселится о тебѣ церковь твоя Христе...* (1 Samuel [1 Kings LXX] 2:1).

[^546]: The Kyiv Caves Lavra is praised as the mother and nurturer of both martyrs and ascetic luminaries.

[^547]: Traditional patristic teaching holding that bringing a soul from pagan darkness unto salvation in Christ is an even greater spiritual miracle than physical bodily resurrection.

[^548]: Sessional Hymn to Saints Kuksha and Pimen, Tone 2: *Дары любве отцы твои прїемше...*

[^549]: Heirmos of Ode 4, Tone 4: *Вознесена тя видѣвши церковь...* (Habakkuk [Habacuc LXX] 3:11).

[^550]: Miracle of St. Kuksha recorded in the Paterikon: during a severe drought among the Vyatichi, he prayed and brought down rain from heaven, causing the pagans to accept Christ.

[^551]: St. Pimen the Faster was endowed with clairvoyance; on the exact day and hour Kuksha was slain hundreds of versts away, Pimen cried out in the Caves church: "Our brother Kuksha has been slain this day!"

[^552]: Heirmos of Ode 5, Tone 4: *Ты Господи мой свѣтъ въ міръ пришелъ еси...* (John 12:46).

[^553]: Miracle of St. Kuksha: the saint dried up a pagan sacred lake and drove out demonic apparitions that had held the Vyatichi in fear.

[^554]: St. Pimen the Faster was renowned for curing physical and spiritual diseases by his word of prayer and holy water.

[^555]: Heirmos of Ode 6, Tone 4: *Пожру ти со гласомъ хваленїя Господи...* (Jonah 2:10 [LXX 2:9]).

[^556]: Metaphorical reference to the abolition of bloody pagan sacrifices among the Vyatichi following their conversion to Christianity.

[^557]: Scriptural citation of Mark 16:18: "They shall lay hands on the sick, and they shall recover."

[^558]: Kontakion to Saints Kuksha and Pimen, Tone 3: *Свѣтила мѵрская сошедшеся...*

[^559]: Heirmos of Ode 7, Tone 4: *Въ пещи авраамстїи отроцы...* (Daniel 3; Heirmologion Tone 4).

[^560]: Discourse 33 of the Kyiv Caves Paterikon recounts that St. Pimen the Faster, gifted with clairvoyance, announced St. Kuksha's martyrdom in the midst of the Caves church at the very moment Kuksha was beheaded by the Vyatichi hundreds of versts away.

[^561]: Immediately after prophesying Kuksha's martyrdom and the hour of the sacrifice, St. Pimen foretold his own death, lay down in his cell, and reposed in peace on the same day (August 27).

[^562]: Heirmos of Ode 8, Tone 4: *Руцѣ распростеръ Данїилъ...* (Daniel 6, 3).

[^563]: Elliptical syntax in the printed Slavonic text (*яже скорымъ твоимъ моленїемъ ко Владыцѣ*); the governing imperative verb [deliver us] is elided and supplied in brackets.

[^564]: Heirmos of Ode 9, Tone 4: *Камень нерукосѣчный...* (Daniel 2:34, 45).

[^565]: Scriptural citation of 1 Peter 2:8 and Isaiah 8:14 concerning Christ as a stone of stumbling unto unbelievers.

[^566]: Ascetical metaphor of demonic snares being rendered as insubstantial as spiders' webs through patience and hope in Christ.

[^567]: Scriptural allusion to Psalm 18:13 [LXX 18:12]: "Cleanse Thou me from secret faults."

[^568]: Exapostilarion to Saints Kuksha and Pimen: *Днесь благонарочитое намъ показася время...*

[^569]: Scriptural citation of Acts 1:7: "The times or the seasons, which the Father hath put in His own power."

[^570]: Psalm 33:20 [LXX 33:19]: "Many are the afflictions of the righteous: but the Lord delivers him out of them all."

[^571]: Stichera at the Aposticha uniting the distinct ascetical paths of Pimen the Faster and Kuksha the Hieromartyr.

[^572]: Troparion to the Synaxis of the Caves Fathers, Tone 4: *Мысленное солнце, и свѣтилу двухъ первоначальныхъ печерскихъ...* Commemorates SS. Anthony and Theodosius as the two great luminaries alongside the entire synaxis of venerable fathers.

[^573]: Full-page woodcut engraving on Fol. 114r depicting the General Synaxis of the Caves Fathers beneath the Dormition Mother of God and the Lavra's Great Church.

[^574]: Kontakion to the Synaxis of the Caves Fathers, Tone 8: *Избраннїи от роднаго угодницы Божїи...*

[^575]: Troparion to St. Theodosius of the Caves, Tone 8: *Возвысився на добродѣтель...* (Common troparion to St. Theodosius, Hegumen of the Kyiv Caves).

[^576]: Woodcut engraving on Fol. 114v depicting St. Theodosius of the Caves surrounded by biographical scenes (his mother's entreaty, angels bringing gold, final instruction to brethren, dormition) and the Translation of his relics in 1091.

[^577]: Kontakion to St. Theodosius of the Caves, Tone 3: *Звѣзду россїйскую днесь почтимъ...*

[^578]: Primary headpiece title on Fol. 115r: August 28 marks the Synaxis of the Venerable Fathers of the Kyiv Caves who repose in the Far (Theodosius) Caves (*Дальнїя Пещеры*).

[^579]: Rubric prescribing the 1st Kathisma of the Psalter ("Blessed is the man", Psalms 1–3) at Great Vespers.

[^580]: Stichera at "Lord, I have cried," Tone 8. Podoben: *О преславнаго чудесе...*

[^581]: St. Theodosius of the Caves is invoked as the spiritual general (*воевода*) who established the cenobitic rule (Studite Typikon) and gathered the monastic host of the Far Caves.

[^582]: Standard Dogmatikon Theotokion of Tone 8 at Great Vespers: *Царь небесный за человѣколюбїе на земли явися...*

[^583]: Cross-reference rubric directing the reader to Fol. 4v at the beginning of the volume for the three Old Testament readings (Paremias) appointed for the Caves Fathers.

[^584]: Stichera at the Aposticha, Tone 2. Podoben: *Доме евфраѳовъ...*

[^585]: Psalm 149:5: "The venerable ones shall boast in glory, and shall rejoice upon their beds."

[^586]: Scriptural citation of Psalm 125:5 [LXX 125:5]: "They that sow in tears shall reap in joy."

[^587]: Psalm 127:1 [LXX 127:1]: "Blessed are all they that fear the Lord; that walk in His ways."

[^588]: Celebrates the ascetical seclusion of the Far Caves (*Феодосїевы пещеры*) where ascetics lived in absolute prayer and solitude.

[^589]: General Troparion to the Synaxis of the Far Caves Fathers, Tone 4: *Мысленное солнце, и свѣтилу двухъ первоначальныхъ печерскихъ...*

[^590]: Standard Dismissal Theotokion of Tone 4: *Еже отъ вѣка утаеное...*

[^591]: Sessional Hymn after Kathisma 1, Tone 4. Podoben: *Явился еси...*

[^592]: Sessional Hymn after Kathisma 2, Tone 3: *Бракъ агнчїй въ чертозѣ небесномъ...*

[^593]: Scriptural allusions to the Parable of the Wedding Feast (Matthew 22:1–14) and the Marriage Supper of the Lamb (Revelation 19:7–9).

[^594]: Scriptural allusion to the wedding garment required for the heavenly banquet (Matthew 22:11–13).

[^595]: Polyeleos Psalms (Psalms 134–135: "Praise ye the name of the Lord") and the Magnification (*Величанїе*) to the Caves Fathers.

[^596]: Psalm 32:1 [LXX 32:1]: "Rejoice in the Lord, O ye righteous: for praise is comely for the upright."

[^597]: Ps. 125:5 (LXX).

[^598]: Ps. 149:5 (LXX).

[^599]: Ps. 31:11 (LXX).

[^600]: Tone 8 Sessional Hymn model: "Of Wisdom" (*Премꙋ́дрости* / Greek: *Τὴν Σοφίαν καὶ Λόγον*).

[^601]: Polyeleos Sessional Hymn to the Venerable Fathers of the Far Caves.

[^602]: Theotokion after the Sessional Hymn.

[^603]: Gradual (Hymns of Degrees), Tone 4, 1st Antiphon: *"From my youth up many passions war against me..."*

[^605]: Ps. 115:3 (LXX).

[^606]: Ps. 150:1 (LXX).

[^619]: St. Zeno the Faster of the Far Caves (14th c., commemorated Jan 30 / Aug 28).

[^620]: St. Makary the Deacon of the Far Caves (14th c., commemorated Jan 19 / Aug 28).

[^622]: St. Hypatius the Healer of the Far Caves (14th c., commemorated March 31 / Aug 28).

[^629]: St. Hilarion the Schemamonk of the Far Caves (11th c., Metropolitan of Kyiv and companion of St. Theodosius, commemorated Oct 21 / Aug 28).

[^630]: St. Dionysius the Recluse / Presbyter of the Far Caves (15th c., commemorated Oct 3 / Aug 28).

[^631]: St. Arsenius the Laborious of the Far Caves (14th c., commemorated May 8 / Aug 28).

[^654]: St. Martyrius the Deacon of the Far Caves (13th–14th c., commemorated Oct 25 / Aug 28).

[^655]: Heirmos of Ode 8, Tone 8 (*Цр҃ѧ̀ нб҃снагѡ* / Greek: *Τὸν Βασιλέα τῶν οὐρανῶν*).

[^656]: St. Evagrius the Silent of the Far Caves (14th c., commemorated Aug 28).

[^657]: St. Cassian the Recluse (Feb 29 / Aug 28) and St. Pior the Recluse (13th c., commemorated Oct 4 / Aug 28) of the Far Caves.

[^658]: St. Paphnutius the Recluse of the Far Caves, surnamed the Weeper.

[^659]: St. Joseph the Much-Sick / Patient of the Far Caves (14th c., commemorated April 4 / Aug 28).
